# Gymdesk Architecture

## TL;DR (read this first, skip the rest for small tasks)

**Stack**: Express API (`api/`) · Next.js admin app (`apps/admin/`, port 8081) · Next.js member PWA (`apps/member/`, port 8082; wrapped as an iOS/Android app by the Capacitor shell in `apps/mobile/`, see `docs/mobile-app.md`) · MySQL 8 (HeatWave in prod, schema `fitness`) · Clerk auth.

**Tenant isolation**: every domain table has `gym_id`. Every query filters by it. The `x-gym-id` request header carries the active gym; `tenantContext` middleware resolves it and attaches `req.tenantCtx`.

**Auth**: `requireAuth()` verifies the Clerk Bearer token → `userId` on `req.auth`. `tenantContext` then resolves the gym role from `gym_memberships`. Use `requireModuleAccess(module)` / `requireModuleWrite(module)` to gate at the router-mount and write-route level respectively. Use `requireRole('admin')` only for explicit last-admin guards or superadmin operations. Never use `getAuth()` — use `req.auth`.

**Roles**: `guest` (public) · `member` · `nutritionist` · `accountant` · `front_desk` · `trainer_performance` · `trainer_perf_nutrition` · `admin` (gym-scoped) · `superadmin` (platform, Clerk metadata).

**DB conventions**: auto-increment `INT UNSIGNED` PKs, `CHAR(36)` UUID for `gyms`, `UTC_TIMESTAMP()` not `NOW()`, `VARCHAR` not `TEXT` for indexed columns, soft-delete via `deleted_at DATETIME`, named CHECK constraints for statuses, no `RETURNING` (insert then SELECT by `insertId`), `db.transaction()` for multi-statement writes.

**Frontend**: all requests go through `apiFetch()` → `/api/proxy/` (Next.js proxy, Node runtime) → backend; a raw-bytes upload uses its sibling `uploadFetch()` (#824), which sends the same tenant headers with the file's own `Content-Type` — never a hand-rolled `fetch`, which is how theme logo uploads came to reach `tenantContext` with no `x-gym-id` and 401. Admin app uses `useGym()` / `GymContext`; member app uses `useApp()` / `AppContext`. Sidebar is config-driven via `config/navigationGroups.ts` — add nav items there, not in JSX.

**Reference implementations**: Members (staff-level CRUD + soft-delete) · Plans (`api/src/api/membership-plans.ts`, admin-only CRUD). Copy one of these for new features.

**Payments**: the two processes end to end — the member's first payment (CIT) and the nightly recurring charge (MIT) — are documented in `docs/payments.md`, together with the data model, the provider boundary and a manual test runbook. Read that before changing anything under `api/src/payments/`, `api/src/api/billing.ts`, `payment-requests.ts`, `payment-page.ts` or `webhooks.ts`.

**Architectural decisions**: see `docs/decisions.md` for the settled choices (MySQL, Clerk, no ORM, etc.) — don't re-litigate them.

---

## Overview

Multi-tenant Gym Management SaaS. One Express backend, two Next.js frontends (admin + member), one MySQL 8 database (Oracle HeatWave in deployed environments; schema `fitness`). Authentication via Clerk. Tenant isolation via `gym_id` on every domain table and the `x-gym-id` request header.

The domain now spans membership plans & billing, classes & scheduling, class packages/credits, promotions, workouts & training plans, team management, per-gym theming, and an audit log. See `docs/roadmap.md` for phase status.

---

## Monorepo Layout

```
gymdesk/
  api/src/                         # Express API (shared by both frontends)
    index.ts                       # App entrypoint, route registration, requireAuth()
    api/                           # One file per domain (members.ts, class-sessions.ts, …)
    lib/
      logger.ts                    # pino singleton (LOG_LEVEL env var; pino-pretty in dev)
    middleware/
      requestLogger.ts             # pino-http middleware — stamps requestId on req.log
    domain/types.ts                # Shared TypeScript interfaces
    infra/
      db.ts                        # mysql2 pool + query/transaction helpers
      tenantContext.ts             # Middleware: resolves gym role, requireRole(), requireSuperadmin
      audit.ts                     # recordAudit() — fire-and-forget audit_logs writer
      migrations/                  # Knex migration .js files (001_ … 195_; some numbers are reused across parallel branches)
      swagger.ts                   # OpenAPI spec served at GET /docs
      seed.ts                      # Dev seed (sets a Clerk user as platform superadmin)
    payments/                      # Payment provider abstraction layer (#179) — see docs/payments.md
      types.ts                     # Shared value types (PaymentStatus, CreatePaymentRequestParams, …)
      provider.ts                  # PaymentProvider interface (createPaymentRequest, parseWebhook, executeRecurring)
      money.ts                     # toMinorUnits() — every amount crosses the provider boundary in cents
      index.ts                     # getPaymentProvider() factory — reads PAYMENT_PROVIDER env var, caches singleton
                                   #   + SUPPORTED_PAYMENT_PROVIDER_KEYS / describePaymentDeployment() (#636)
      providers/
        monei/
          index.ts                 # MoneiProvider implements PaymentProvider
          client.ts                # fetch wrapper for https://api.monei.com/v1
          webhook.ts               # HMAC-SHA256 signature verification (timingSafeEqual first) + payload mapping
          types.ts                 # Monei-specific internal types
  apps/
    admin/src/                     # Staff/admin Next.js app (port :8081 both locally and deployed)
      app/[locale]/                # Next.js App Router pages (one folder per domain)
      app/api/webhooks/clerk/      # #1085: relays Clerk's webhook to the API (raw bytes + svix-* headers)
      app/api/internal/[...path]/  # #1086: relays the four nightly-run POSTs to the API
                                   #   (allowlisted paths, x-internal-secret, node:http — not fetch)
      components/                  # DataTable, CrudModal, ConfirmDialog, StatusBadge, StatusFilter,
                                   #   FilterBar, listChrome.ts,
                                   #   Toast, Sidebar, NavGroup, AppShell, TopHeader, GymSelector,
                                   #   LanguagePicker, ThemeProvider, ui.tsx
      config/navigationGroups.ts   # Sidebar structure: collapsible groups + role gating
      context/GymContext.tsx       # Active gym, role, isSuperadmin — loaded everywhere
      lib/apiClient.ts             # apiFetch() / uploadFetch() / pdfFetch() — attach Bearer token + x-gym-id
      middleware.ts                # Clerk auth + next-intl locale routing (both exempt /api/proxy,
                                   #   /api/webhooks/clerk and /api/internal)
      locales/base/{en,es,ca}.json
    member/src/                    # Member-facing PWA (port :8082 both locally and deployed)
      app/[locale]/                # Dashboard home, sign-in, schedule, calendar, membership, training, nutrition, profile
      app/api/proxy/[...path]/     # Proxy → backend (Node runtime — edge fetch can't reach port 3000)
      components/TopBar.tsx         # Back-to-Home + Notifications + Profile entry points (#361 — no bottom tab bar)
      context/AppContext.tsx        # gymId + linked member profile
      lib/apiClient.ts
      middleware.ts                # Public routes: /, /:locale, /classes, /sign-in, /api/proxy
      locales/base/{en,es,ca}.json
    payment/                       # Isolated PCI payment page (#204) — vanilla HTML/JS, no npm
      checkout.html                # Monei Card Input + MIT consent
      error.html                   # Expired/invalid token fallback
      js/checkout.js utils.js
      css/style.css
      templates/default.conf.template  # envsubst'd at container start (#1083): static
                                       # files, the /payment-page/ and /themes/ proxies,
                                       # and the POST /webhooks/payment relay to the API
      Dockerfile                   # nginx:alpine; published :8083 by #205
      SCRIPT-INVENTORY.md          # PCI DSS 6.4.3 third-party script inventory
  shared/
    charts/                        # @gymdesk/charts (#1037 stage 4) — the shared charting layer, an npm
                                   #   workspace both apps depend on and the only place that imports ECharts
      src/echartsRuntime.ts        # loadECharts() — dynamic import + the registration of what is drawn
      src/chartTheme.ts            # each colour role as the --gd-* variable that holds it, resolved per element
      src/lineChartOption.ts       # the option a line/area chart is drawn from (pure)
      src/series.ts                # ChartPoint/ChartSeries + segmentPoints() — one series per group
      src/EChartCanvas.tsx         # the one component that mounts, resizes, re-themes and disposes a canvas
      src/LineChart.tsx            # the first chart type; README.md says how to add another
  docs/                            # This folder
```

---

## Authentication & Authorization

### Auth (Clerk)
- `requireAuth()` in `index.ts` verifies the Clerk Bearer token on every protected route.
- Decoded `userId` is attached as `req.auth.userId`.
- **Required Clerk instance setting: Restricted mode.** Every user in this app — Members and Team roles alike — is meant to arrive via an invitation, never public self-registration. In the Clerk Dashboard, this is `Configure → Protect → Restrictions → Enable restricted mode`, and it must be **on** for every instance (dev + production). This is a dashboard-only toggle, not exposed via the Backend API (`GET`/`PATCH /instance` and `/instance/restrictions` only cover allowlist/blocklist and other flags — no sign-up-mode field), so it can't be enforced from code and isn't covered by CI. With it off, anyone can self-serve sign up with any email at `/sign-up`, independent of invitation state — see the Team invite flow note below for why that matters.

### Tenant context (`infra/tenantContext.ts`)
- Reads the `x-gym-id` header, looks up `gym_memberships` to get the user's `role` in that gym.
- Attaches `req.tenantCtx = { userId, gymId, role, gymMembershipId, isSuperadmin, actorName }`. `actorName` is derived from the Clerk user object already fetched in this middleware and stored for use by `recordAudit` (no extra query).
- Helper `getTenantContext(req)` retrieves it safely inside route handlers.
- Superadmins (Clerk metadata) are granted a synthetic `admin` role for any gym without a membership row.

### Roles

`AppRole` (the DB-backed role type, from `api/src/infra/permissions.ts`) is `admin | trainer_performance | trainer_perf_nutrition | front_desk | accountant | nutritionist | member`. `superadmin` is a **platform** role stored in Clerk, not a `gym_memberships` value. `guest` is anonymous (public routes only). Migration 075 renamed `coach → trainer_performance` and `staff → front_desk` and added 3 new roles.

| Role | Scope | Who | How identified |
|------|-------|-----|----------------|
| `superadmin` | Platform | Cordel internal | Clerk `publicMetadata.platform_role === 'superadmin'` |
| `admin` | Gym | Gym/studio owner | `gym_memberships.role` |
| `trainer_performance` | Gym | Performance trainer | `gym_memberships.role` |
| `trainer_perf_nutrition` | Gym | Trainer + nutrition | `gym_memberships.role` |
| `front_desk` | Gym | Receptionist | `gym_memberships.role` |
| `accountant` | Gym | Accountant | `gym_memberships.role` |
| `nutritionist` | Gym | Nutritionist | `gym_memberships.role` |
| `member` | Gym | Gym member/client | `gym_memberships.role` + `members.clerk_user_id` |
| `guest` | Public | Anonymous visitor | No auth — `/public/*` routes only |

### Permission model (#156)

Access is governed by a static `PERMISSION_MATRIX` in `api/src/infra/permissions.ts` (mirrored to `apps/admin/src/config/permissions.ts` for the frontend). The table below is the #156 spec, which the API matches; the admin copy drifted from it until #611 and `apps/admin/src/test/permission-matrix-parity.test.ts` now fails on any difference. **Read-only roles (`R` / `R_ASSIGNED`) see the module's menu and pages with write controls disabled** (#611/#613 — see `feature-patterns.md` → Read-only access); pages redirect only when the role has no access to the module at all (`useModuleAccess(module).canRead`). Each cell maps `AppModule × AppRole → PermissionLevel` (`RW | R | R_ASSIGNED | RW_ASSIGNED | R_OWN | NONE`). R_ASSIGNED/RW_ASSIGNED are treated as R/RW at the router layer until a `staff_member_assignments` table is added.

| Module | admin | trainer_performance | trainer_perf_nutrition | front_desk | accountant | nutritionist | member |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| MEMBERS | RW | R_ASSIGNED | R_ASSIGNED | RW | NONE | R_ASSIGNED | R_OWN |
| CALENDAR | RW | RW | RW | RW | NONE | R | NONE |
| ORGANIZATION | RW | R | R | R | NONE | R | NONE |
| TRAINING | RW | RW | RW | R | NONE | R_ASSIGNED | R_OWN |
| NUTRITION | RW | R_ASSIGNED | RW_ASSIGNED | R | NONE | RW_ASSIGNED | R_OWN |
| FINANCIALS | RW | NONE | NONE | R | R | NONE | NONE |
| PAYMENTS | RW | NONE | NONE | RW | R | NONE | R_OWN |
| SYSTEM | RW | NONE | NONE | NONE | NONE | NONE | NONE |
| CORDEL | NONE | NONE | NONE | NONE | NONE | NONE | NONE |

#### Feature-level permission overrides (#1070)

A module cell answers for every screen of its section, which is coarse on purpose. `FEATURE_PERMISSION_OVERRIDES` (`api/src/infra/permissions.ts`, mirrored in `apps/admin/src/config/permissions.ts`) is the exception: one entry per **feature key** — the same dot-separated keys `feature_flags` and the navigation use — saying that a role has that level on *that* feature whatever its module gives it.

* Resolution is `getFeaturePermission(role, module, featureKey)` = the override, else the module cell. It matches the **exact** key and never an ancestor, so `nutrition.personal_goals` says nothing about the rest of `nutrition`, and it *replaces* the module level in both directions (a `NONE` override takes a feature away from a role the module admits).
* Enforcement is `requireFeatureAccess(featureKey, module)` / `requireFeatureWrite(featureKey, module)` (`infra/tenantContext.ts`), given the same key the route is already mounted behind with `requireFeatureEnabled`. A route with no override declared for its key behaves exactly as the module guards did.
* The frontend asks `useModuleAccess(module, featureKey)`; the parity between the two declarations is `api/src/test/feature-permission-overrides.unit.test.ts` (in the API suite, since CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only).
* Cordel → Feature Flags reports them: `GET /platform/feature-flags/role-access` answers `access` (the module matrix, keyed by the key's first segment) **and** `overrides` (keyed by the full feature key), and the page draws an overridden value in the app's red with the inherited ones unchanged.

The same page names a flag after the section it gates (#1070 §1): `apps/admin/src/lib/featureFlagLabels.ts` resolves a feature key to the **navigation's own** `labelKey` (`config/navigationGroups.ts`), so `nutrition` reads *Nutrition & Goals* because #948 renamed that group — there is no second spelling of a section name, the stored key is untouched and still printed beside the label, and a key the navigation does not gate (the `member_web.*` flags) keeps the short key the page always showed.

The one override today: **`nutrition.personal_goals` × `trainer_performance` = `RW`** (#1070 §2). It covers the Personal Goals catalogue (`/personal-goals`) and the assignments (`/member-personal-goals`), both of which are mounted on that key, and deliberately not the rest of NUTRITION — changing the `NUTRITION` cell would hand a Personal Trainer the Nutrition Library, the plan templates and every member's nutrition plan.

**CALENDAR (#614, from #247)** gates `/calendar-events` and `/class-sessions` (mount + all their write routes); it used to ride on TRAINING, where front desk was read-only, so the admin app offered front desk calendar editing that the API rejected. Front desk now creates and edits events; nutritionist is read-only.

Routers are mounted with `requireModuleAccess(module)` (non-NONE, non-R_OWN gate); write routes inside them add `requireModuleWrite(module)` (RW or RW_ASSIGNED). Use `requireRole('admin')` only for explicit last-admin guards or admin-only operations within a module.

| Domain (router) | Module gate | Write guard | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Members (`members`) | MEMBERS | `requireModuleWrite('MEMBERS')` | Soft-delete + `/restore`; DELETE = `requireRole('admin')`. |
| Staff app access (`staff.ts` `/:id/access`) | ORGANIZATION | `requireRole('admin')` | Grant/resend/revoke a Staff record's login; also fired implicitly by Staff create/update/deactivate/delete. |
| Spaces, Specialities, Activity types, Class packages, Events, Trainers, Professional Services | ORGANIZATION | `requireRole('admin')` or `requireModuleWrite('ORGANIZATION')` | Admin-only writes. |
| Class sessions (`class-sessions`) | TRAINING | `requireModuleWrite('TRAINING')` | `POST /:id/cancel` also write-gated. `PUT /:id/sharing-authorized` requireModuleWrite. |
| Shared training requests (`shared-training-requests`) | TRAINING | `requireModuleWrite('TRAINING')` for POST; `requireRole admin/trainer_*` for approve/reject. |
| Bookings (`bookings`) | MEMBERS | `requireModuleWrite('MEMBERS')` | `POST /:id/attendance` = `requireRole('admin','front_desk','trainer_performance','trainer_perf_nutrition')`. `POST /bookings` accepts `force=true` for over-capacity walk-ins. Class-session attendance endpoints (`bulk-present`, `effective-trainer`, `complete`) use `requireRole` or `requireModuleWrite('TRAINING')`. |
| Exercises, Workout templates, Training plan templates | TRAINING | `requireModuleWrite('TRAINING')` | `POST /exercises/import` copies selected Base Exercises into the gym's catalog (#718, replaced `import-defaults`) and **re-importing one the gym already has restores its System media references** (#719 part 3); `GET /exercises/base` lists the library for the Import modal, read-only and gym-facing, with `media_refreshable` saying whether a re-import would move anything. A Base Exercise is not in `GET /exercises` until the gym has imported it (#804). Deletes are soft (#62). |
| Training plans (`training-plans` + `members/:id/…`) | TRAINING | `requireModuleWrite('TRAINING')` | See personalized plans notes (migration 054, 066). |
| Nutrition Library (`nutrition-library`) | NUTRITION | read: `requireModuleAccess('NUTRITION')`; `POST`/`PUT` (gym-owned items only): `requireModuleWrite('NUTRITION')` (#350) | `nutrition_library_items` has `gym_id CHAR(36) NULL` (migration 105 — NULL = Cordel-owned system item, read-only to gyms; a set value = gym-owned item that gym can create/edit), `status VARCHAR(20) DEFAULT 'active'`, `modified_at`. Functional unique index `COALESCE(gym_id,'')` + `name` handles NULL-safe uniqueness (migration 142 dropped `category` from this index — see below). Flat, paginated list (`GET /nutrition-library?search=&category_id=&quality_id=&limit=&offset=` → `{ items, total, limit, offset }`); query-building shared with `/platform/nutrition-library` via `api/src/domain/nutritionLibrary.ts`. Platform CRUD (system items) at `/platform/nutrition-library` (`requireSuperadmin`). **Multiple food categories (#501, migration 142)**: `category` was a single VARCHAR column; a food (e.g. peas) can belong to more than one category, so it's now a global `nutrition_library_categories` catalogue (`id`, `slug`) + `nutrition_library_item_categories` M2M junction — the same shape as `nutritional_qualities`/`nutrition_library_item_qualities` (#293). Both routers accept/return `category_ids: number[]` (required, at least one) instead of a scalar `category`; a platform-only `PUT /platform/nutrition-library/:id/categories` sub-resource mirrors the existing `/:id/qualities` one. `fat` and `fiber` were added to `nutritional_qualities` in the same migration. Both admin pages (`nutrition/nutrition-library` and `cordel/nutrition-library`) were converted from Modal CRUD to Inline row CRUD (Plans pattern) as part of the same ticket. **Quality assignments (#644, migration 167)**: the catalogue rows existed but `fat`/`fiber` had no item assignments at all, and `protein`/`carbohydrate` only the 20 that migration 127 (#350) backfilled. Since the item row stores no macro values, 167 seeds the classification by food name — the criterion and a per-item note live in the migration — covering all 32 system items plus any gym-created item sharing one of those names. It is additive (`INSERT IGNORE`), so a tag a gym set by hand is never deleted, and it is a one-time review — an item created afterwards is not touched. `down()` is deliberately narrower than `up()`: system items only, and never 127's pairs, because `INSERT IGNORE` keeps no record of which links it created and a name-and-slug delete cannot tell its own row from a hand-set one. **Image upload (#417 stage 3)**: migration 138 adds nullable `image_url VARCHAR(500)`, gym-owned items only (`nutrition-library.ts`'s `POST`/`PUT` accept/return it; `platform-nutrition-library.ts` is untouched, so system items stay image-less — they have no `storage_folder_prefix` to upload into). Admin gym-facing page uses the shared `ImageUploadField` (see Storage row) posting to `POST /storage/uploads/nutrition-image`. **Translated item names (#643, migration 166)**: `nutrition_library_item_translations` (`item_id` + `locale` PK, `name`, FK CASCADE) holds one row per translated locale — same catalogue+junction shape again. `nutrition_library_items.name` remains the base (English) value, the uniqueness key and the fallback when a locale has no row, so nothing renders blank. Reads resolve the name server-side through `localizedNameSql`/`localizedNameExpr` (`domain/nutritionLibrary.ts`) against the request locale (see Request Locale below): library list rows gain `display_name` (localized) and `translations` next to `name`, and the plan/template/member surfaces' `item_name` is localized in place. Search and ordering follow the displayed locale. Migration 166 seeds Spanish + Catalan for all 32 system items; gym-created items keep a single entered name shown in every locale. Superadmins author them via `translations` on `POST`/`PUT /platform/nutrition-library`, the `PUT /platform/nutrition-library/:id/translations` sub-resource (mirroring `/categories` and `/qualities`), and `GET /platform/nutrition-library/locales`. Category and quality names stay slug-keyed and are translated as UI labels in each app's locale files, not here. **Base food images (#715)**: a Base Nutrition Library food (`gym_id IS NULL`) belongs to no gym, so its image goes in the platform's own R2 folder — `cordel/nutrition/<food_id>-<sanitized food name>.png` (lowercase since 2026-09-27; #1035 replaced the per-upload UUID with the food's own id, so the key is deterministic; the pre-#1035 keys were `cordel/nutrition/<image_uuid>-<name>.png` and, before 2026-09-27, `cordel/Nutrition/<image_uuid>-<name>.png`, both of which still render from their stored URLs), built by `domain/baseNutritionImages.ts` off `PLATFORM_STORAGE_ROOT` (#732), never off `gyms.storage_folder_prefix`. No new column: the existing `nutrition_library_items.image_url` (migration 138) stores the resulting Cloudflare URL, the same column a gym-owned food already uses for its own upload — `<prefix>/nutrition/<food_id>-<name>.<ext>` since #1035, built by `buildGymNutritionImageKey()` beside the platform builder — and the row's `gym_id` is what decides the folder. The name sanitizer is nutrition's own (`Salmon, Atlantic` → `Salmon-Atlantic`) rather than `sanitizeStorageFolderName()`, which deletes spaces outright. `POST /platform/nutrition-library/:id/image` (superadmin, raw `image/png` body ≤ 2 MB) validates the **file**, not the request — PNG signature, exactly 512×512 read from the IHDR, and an alpha channel (or a `tRNS` chunk) — via `domain/pngImage.ts`; a rejection is a 400 carrying a `reason`, and nothing is uploaded or written, so the existing image survives. It then writes the `cordel/` + `cordel/nutrition/` markers, uploads, and updates the row last; since #1035 the key is deterministic, so a replacement normally rewrites the same object and the sweep below runs only when the key actually moved (a rename, a different format, or a pre-#1035 key) — the object the row pointed at before is deleted best-effort *after* the new one is stored (`storageKeyFromObjectUrl()`, the inverse of `buildStorageObjectUrl()`, so a URL this deployment did not build is never deleted). Storage failures reuse #417's conventions (503 unconfigured, 502 R2 failure). The image is shown on the **expanded** card and uploaded from the **Edit** form (`[locale]/cordel/nutrition-library/`; the button moved there in #799, below), over a checkerboard frame so a transparent background reads as transparency, with the required format stated and checked client-side as well. Existing foods are populated by the one-time operator script `npm run nutrition:base-images` (`src/scripts/backfill-base-nutrition-images.ts`), not by a migration — a migration is deterministic offline SQL and must not depend on R2: it discovers the foods from the database, uses artwork from `--from <dir>` when supplied and otherwise the deterministic offline illustration generator `domain/nutritionImageArt.ts` (a consistent 512×512 stylized form at 70–85% of the canvas, transparent background, no AI integration — `CLAUDE.md` forbids one), validates every image with the route's own rules, is idempotent (a food with an `image_url` is skipped unless `--force`, which also deletes each object it replaces), never aborts on one failure, and prints discovered/already present/generated/supplied/uploaded/failed with each failure's id and name. `--only <ids>`, `--limit` and `--dry-run` scope a run. **Read-only expanded row, Details modal, Edit behind `⋮` (#799, migration 196)**: the two libraries' rows now separate three interactions — expanding *reads* the complete item, `⋮ → Details` opens a read-only modal with the audit information, and `⋮ → Edit` is the only way anything changes (the image included). Migration 196 adds what the row could not answer: `description VARCHAR(1000) NULL` (§12 — a persisted field, not a UI-only one, accepted and returned by both routers' `POST`/`PUT`; absent from a `PUT` leaves it alone, an empty string clears it to NULL) and the audit snapshot `created_by_name`/`created_by_type`, `modified_by_name`/`modified_by_type`, `deleted_at`, `deleted_by_name`/`deleted_by_type`, each `*_type` under a `chk_nli_<prefix>_by_type` CHECK (`NULL`/`staff`/`superadmin`). The actor is **snapshotted at write time**, not joined: a base food is administered by a superadmin, who has no `gym_memberships` row to point at — the same reason `tax_rates.created_by_name` (126) and `themes.created_by_name` (178) exist. `domain/nutritionLibrary.ts` owns the pure rules (`normalizeDescription()`, `actorSnapshot()`, `itemDetailColumnsSql()`), so both routers write the same shape and every item-shaped response carries the columns the Details modal reads — there is **no detail endpoint**, the modal renders the list row (§25). `GET /nutrition-library` returns the shared system rows alongside a gym's own, so it asks for those columns with `maskPlatformActors: true`: a system row's actor is a Cordel employee, and the catalogue is shared but their name is not — the column is still projected under the same key (NULL), so the Details modal renders its em dash without a rule of its own, and `created_at`/`modified_at` are never masked. A gym-owned food has no delete route at all, so its Deleted At / Deleted By are always empty on that side. `status = 'deleted'` stays the flag every query filters on; `deleted_at` only records *when*, backfilled from `modified_at` for rows already deleted (the soft-delete UPDATE is its only writer and every other write path 409s on a deleted item). Existing rows keep NULL actor names: the obvious source would be `audit_logs`, but `/platform/*` runs behind `requireSuperadmin` with no `tenantContext`, so `recordAudit()` there is a no-op and there is nothing to reconstruct — a pre-existing gap this ticket did not widen, and the Details modal renders the em dash for it. Frontend: `apps/admin/src/components/nutritionLibrary/` holds the one declaration both pages import — `nutritionItemProfile.ts` (the row shape, the persisted-row → form-values mapping, the section order, the formatters and the chip style), `NutritionItemReadOnlyView.tsx` and `NutritionItemDetailsModal.tsx` (which takes the Audit Log `scope` as a prop, `platform` from Cordel). The expanded row renders Name → Description → Status → Translations → Categories → Nutritional Qualities → Media, in that order, with the whole category and quality catalogue shown and the assigned entries in the Edit form's selected blue — as spans, so there is nothing to click. A deleted base food keeps its `Details` menu entry, since that is where `Deleted At`/`Deleted By` are read. |
| Personal Goals (`personal-goals`), Nutrition Goals (`nutrition-goals`) | NUTRITION | read: `requireModuleAccess('NUTRITION')` + `requireFeatureEnabled('nutrition.personal_goals')` for Personal Goals and `requireFeatureEnabled('nutrition.nutrition_library')` for Nutrition Goals; `POST`/`PUT`/`DELETE` (gym-owned rows only): `requireModuleWrite('NUTRITION')`. Platform CRUD of the System rows at `/platform/personal-goals` and `/platform/nutrition-goals` (`requireSuperadmin`) | #947, #948. The gym administers three catalogues — **Foods** (`nutrition_library_items`, the row above), **Personal Goals** (what the member wants to achieve) and **Nutrition Goals** (what the plan should target nutritionally). #947 shipped all three as tabs of one page; **#948 stage 1 moved Personal Goals out to its own section** — `/{locale}/personal-goals` and Cordel's `/{locale}/cordel/personal-goals` (*Base Personal Goals*) — because a Personal Goal does not depend on Nutrition and is a different entity (§8), and the nav group it shares was renamed **Nutrition & Goals** (§1, a label only). Foods and Nutrition Goals stay the two tabs of the Nutrition Library page. **That move is why Personal Goals has its own feature flag**: `/personal-goals` was behind `nutrition.nutrition_library` only because the tab lived on that page, so hiding Foods would have 403'd a section of a different domain; migration 211 seeds `nutrition.personal_goals` from the Library's current value (migration 160's `financials.taxes` device, so nothing changes on deploy) and the `nutrition` group key still gates both. All four screens render the one `GoalLibrarySection`, so the move changed where the catalogue lives and nothing about how it behaves. **Two tables, not one with a `kind` column** (migration 206): §8 keeps the two concepts apart in the data model as well as the UI, and the two are on diverging paths, which is exactly what a discriminator column could not express — **since #1034 a Personal Goal is measurable** (`personal_goals.target_value DECIMAL(10,2)` + `target_unit VARCHAR(20)`, migration 218, with the same two CHECKs `member_personal_goals` already carries) while a Nutrition Goal's own target values (`2,000 kcal`) are still a later ticket's. `MEASURABLE_GOAL_KINDS` in `api/src/domain/goalLibrary.ts` is the one place that says which kinds have the pair, asked once by each router (`nutrition_goals` has no such columns, so projecting them there would answer ER_BAD_FIELD_ERROR the global handler turns into a bare 500, #966) and mirrored for the admin in `apps/admin/src/components/goalLibrary/goalProfile.ts` — so making a second kind measurable goes in **three** places: that declaration, the two columns plus their CHECKs on the kind's table, and the mirror. What a target *is* lives in `api/src/domain/goalTarget.ts`, shared with the assignment side rather than restated (#1034 §1's "do not introduce a second, incompatible unit system"): the unit is deliberately **free text**, because this codebase has no measurement-unit vocabulary to reuse (the only `*_UNITS` lists are billing periods) and declaring one would *be* that second system. Migration 218 seeds a target onto the four System Personal Goals whose meaning carries a magnitude (Weight Loss 3 kg, Weight Gain 3 kg, Muscle Gain 2 kg, Maintenance 0 kg — a change of zero *is* the goal) and deliberately **none** onto Performance, Recovery and Energy, since a number invented for a qualitative goal is the "same generic target" §2 forbids wearing a different value (§13 requires a unit only "for measurable goals"). The seed fills a row only while it has no target yet, so a value Cordel changed later is never overwritten. The gym's Personal Goals page also carries **`⋮ → Assign goal to member`** (#1034 §4/§5): an opt-in `onAssign` prop, so the action is **absent** from Cordel's Base library, which has no gym whose members it could name, and the dialog behind it is the page's (`AssignGoalToMemberModal`) rather than the shared section's. `personal_goals` and `nutrition_goals` are identical in shape — `gym_id CHAR(36) NULL` (NULL = a platform-owned **System** row, read-only to gyms; set = that gym's own, the `nutrition_library_items` convention from migration 105), `slug`, `name`, `description VARCHAR(1000)`, `status` (`active`/`deleted`), `created_at`/`modified_at`/`deleted_at` and the three `*_by_name`/`*_by_type` actor pairs (#799's snapshot shape, reusing `domain/nutritionLibrary.ts`'s `actorSnapshot()`, `normalizeDescription()` and `itemDetailColumnsSql()` rather than restating them) — so **one router factory per side serves both kinds**: `api/src/api/goal-library.ts` (gym) and `api/src/api/platform-goal-library.ts` (platform), each mounted twice. `api/src/domain/goalLibrary.ts` is the one place the kinds, their tables, their audit entity types (`personal_goal`, `nutrition_goal`, both in `AUDIT_ENTITY_REGISTRY`) and the seeded System rows are declared. **Uniqueness is among live rows**: a `live_name_key VARCHAR(300)` VIRTUAL generated column, non-NULL only while `status != 'deleted'`, carries the UNIQUE index — migration 183's `standing_promotion_key` device — so the index and the routers' `status != 'deleted'` duplicate check agree and a name a gym deleted is re-usable instead of reserved for ever. (Foods deliberately stays as it was: `nli_gym_name_unique` has no status component, so a soft-deleted food keeps its name reserved — the pre-existing router/DB disagreement #947 did not change in a table it does not own.) **`slug` is a System row's label handle and only a System row's** (`chk_<prefix>_slug_system_only`): migration 206 seeds seven of each kind (personal: weight_loss, weight_gain, muscle_gain, maintenance, performance, recovery, energy; nutrition: calories, protein, carbohydrates, fats, fiber, water, **fasting**, the one §4 adds) and the admin resolves `goal_library.<kind>_goal_<slug>` for them, falling back to the row's own `name` — the fixed-seeded-catalogue rule `result_types` already follows, which is why these catalogues need no per-locale junction table. The slugs are deliberately the `NUTRITION_GOALS` vocabulary the Nutrition Plan routers already validate `item_name` against, partitioned between the two concepts, so a later ticket can link a plan's goal to its catalogue row without renaming anything; the routers never write a slug, on either side. Both lists are `GET ?search=&limit=&offset=` → `{ items, total, limit, offset }`, search matching name **and** slug; the gym-facing list returns the System rows beside the gym's own with `maskPlatformActors: true` (a System row's actor is a Cordel employee), answers 403 `System goals are read-only` on a write to one, and the platform list adds `?status=deleted`. **One of the two catalogues is read, since #948 stage 2**: a Personal Goal can be assigned to a Member (`member_personal_goals`, the row below). **Nutrition Goals is still unread** — assigning one to a Nutrition Plan and defining its target values are out of scope (§9 is a future ticket), so `member_nutrition_plan_goals`/`nutrition_plan_template_goals` and their closed `NUTRITION_GOALS` vocabulary are untouched. Admin: `apps/admin/src/components/goalLibrary/` holds everything the four screens share — `goalProfile.ts` (the kinds, the four router roots, the tab declaration, the row shape, the row → form mapping, the payload builder and the `SYSTEM_GOAL_SLUGS` mirror), `LibraryTabs.tsx`, `GoalLibrarySection.tsx` (one section for all four screens: its own search, list, `+ Add <kind>`, inline create/edit and `⋮`) and `GoalDetailsModal.tsx`. Since #948 two of those screens are pages of their own (`app/[locale]/personal-goals/page.tsx`, `app/[locale]/cordel/personal-goals/page.tsx`), each ~40 lines: a title, the scope, the permissions and the label resolver. The section names no endpoint and decides no permission — `scope`, `canWrite` and the label resolver are the page's (#806) — and keeps #797–#800's model: expanding reads, `⋮ → Edit` writes inline in the row, `⋮ → Details` carries the audit information and the View Audit Log link. **Images (#1035 stage 2, migration 225)**: `personal_goals.image_url VARCHAR(1024) NULL` — Personal Goals only, decided by `IMAGE_GOAL_KINDS` in `domain/goalLibrary.ts` exactly as `MEASURABLE_GOAL_KINDS` decides the target pair, which also gates whether the two routes are registered (a Nutrition Goal's `/:id/image` is a 404 rather than a write to a missing column, #966). `domain/personalGoalImages.ts` is the one place that decides where the object goes, what is accepted and what may be deleted, and it is one module for both roots because this is one table holding both kinds of row: `<gym prefix>/goals/<goal_id>-<sanitized name>.png` and `cordel/goals/<goal_id>-<sanitized name>.png`, off `GOALS_FOLDER` / `PLATFORM_STORAGE_ROOT`, with the name capped at 80 chars so the URL cannot outgrow its column. `POST`/`DELETE /personal-goals/:id/image` (`requireFeatureWrite('nutrition.personal_goals', 'NUTRITION')`, raw `image/png` body ≤ 2 MB; 403 on a System row, 404 on another gym's, 409 on a deleted goal or a gym with no `storage_folder_prefix`, 503 unconfigured, 502 on an R2 failure) and the `/platform/personal-goals/:id/image` pair (`requireSuperadmin`, `gym_id IS NULL` rows, no 409 — a constant root has nothing to initialize, so it writes `cordel/` + `cordel/goals/` itself). Validation is the **file's** via `domain/pngImage.ts` — PNG signature and dimensions from the IHDR — and deliberately not #715's: **at most** 512×512 rather than an exact square, and no alpha requirement (#1035 `Q3`), so an opaque PNG is accepted. The key is deterministic, so a replacement overwrites its own object and the best-effort sweep runs only when the key moved (a rename, or a pre-#1035 key), guarded by `isGymOwnedPersonalGoalImageUrl()` / `isPlatformOwnedPersonalGoalImageUrl()` so neither side deletes the other's object or an external URL (#719 §19 both ways). Nothing is generated or backfilled (every row starts NULL) and there is no runtime fallback from a gym goal to the System goal's image. Admin: one `GoalImageField.tsx` for both screens, handed `basePath` and `requiresGymStorage` by the page (#806/#823) — the preview is a value in the read-only half and the control belongs to Edit mode (#797); the create form offers none, because the key needs the row's id. The 1:1 checkerboard frame moved into `components/imagePreviewFrame.ts`, which `ExerciseImageField` now reads too. |
| Assigned Personal Goals (`member-personal-goals`) | NUTRITION | read: `requireModuleAccess('NUTRITION')` + `requireFeatureEnabled('nutrition.personal_goals')`; `POST`/`PUT`/`DELETE`: `requireModuleWrite('NUTRITION')` | #948 §4, migration 212. The Personal Goals a member actually **holds**, as opposed to the ones the catalogue above offers — and the only thing that reads either catalogue. `member_personal_goals` carries `(gym_id, member_id, personal_goal_id)` plus what was agreed: `target_value DECIMAL(10,2)` + `target_unit`, `start_date`/`target_date`, `status` and `notes VARCHAR(1000)`, with #799's three actor pairs — and since #1034 (migration 218) a `goal_name` **snapshot**. **The assignment is an independent snapshot of the Gym Goal it was created from** (#1034 §7/§12): `POST /` reads the catalogue row it validates and writes its `name` onto the assignment, and a target the request does not mention inherits the catalogue's — so the snapshot is the *server's*, not the form's, and any client (the modal, a script, a test) takes one. An explicit `null` is still a clear, and the unit only inherits while the effective value is non-null, since a unit qualifying nothing is what `chk_mpgoal_target_unit` refuses. A submitted value is the per-member override §8 exists for, which is how one Gym Goal is assigned to three members at three targets. Reads project `COALESCE(mpg.goal_name, pg.name)`, never the live name alone: the fallback is for a row assigned **before** migration 218, which has nothing else to read (#635 §16's shape) — there is deliberately **no backfill**, because the catalogue's current name is not evidence of what it was called then. **`status` is progress, `deleted_at` is existence**, and the two are separate columns on purpose: `status IN ('in_progress','achieved','abandoned')` (`chk_mpgoal_status`) records what happened to the goal, while soft delete is `members`' own `deleted_at` shape — folding deletion into the vocabulary, as the catalogue's `status IN ('active','deleted')` does, would overwrite the one fact the row is about (the catalogue has no second axis to lose). `api/src/domain/personalGoalAssignment.ts` owns the vocabulary and every field rule: each normalizer answers `undefined` for a field the request did not mention (so the `PUT` is partial and a `{ status }` body cannot wipe a target), `null` for an explicit clear, and `goalAssignmentFieldError()` applies the two cross-field rules — a unit needs a value, a target date may not precede the start date — to the row the write *produces* rather than to the body, so a clear that contradicts a stored value is a 400 and not a CHECK violation the global handler would answer as a 500 (#966). The goal is validated with the catalogue's own gym-facing rule (`gym_id IS NULL OR gym_id = ?` and not deleted), so a System goal is assignable by every gym and a retired one by nobody; `member_id`/`personal_goal_id` are immutable (the `PUT` ignores them — re-pointing is a `DELETE` plus a `POST`). **Many per member, one live in-progress row per `(member, goal)`**: `mpgoal_live_goal_key`, a VIRTUAL generated column non-NULL only while `deleted_at IS NULL AND status = 'in_progress'`, carries the UNIQUE index (migration 183's device), so the index and the router's 409 agree and an achieved or abandoned goal may be assigned again. `GET ?member_id=&status=&search=&limit=&offset=` → `{ items, total, limit, offset }`, search matching the member, the goal's name and slug and the notes; every row reports the goal's `name`/`slug`/`gym_id` so the admin can resolve a System goal's locale label and render its `System` badge. `GET /statuses` serves the accepted set the writes validate against (registered before `/:id`). Audit entity `member_personal_goal`, a composed registry entry resolving `Member — Goal`. Admin: `apps/admin/src/components/personalGoals/` is the one declaration and the one editor for **two** screens (#806) — `/{locale}/assigned-personal-goals` (its own section under *Nutrition & Goals*, outside `/nutrition/` for the catalogue's reason) and the Member card's **PERSONAL GOALS** section, where every control belongs to the card's Edit mode and is absent outside it (#797/#957). A goal *is* assigned on a Member directly, which the issue thread settles explicitly — the opposite of #931's answer for Promotions. Nothing prices, projects or bills off an assignment. **Since #1036 (migration 222)** the row also carries `end_date DATE NULL` — *when* the assignment stopped, which neither `target_date` (when it was meant to be reached) nor `modified_at` records — written only by `endDateTransition()` in that same domain module and never submitted: it stamps the day an assignment stops being live-and-in-progress (removed, or moved to `achieved`/`abandoned`), keeps the first such date if it is removed again later, and clears when the goal comes back to `in_progress`. Nullable with no backfill, for migration 218's reason: a row that ended before the column existed has no end date to recover. The same migration widens the three `chk_mpgoal_*_by_type` CHECKs to admit **`member`**, since the member now writes their own assignments (`memberActorSnapshot()`; a superadmin impersonating one still records `superadmin`). The member-facing half is `/me/personal-goals` — see the Member app table below. **Since #1037 stage 2 (migration 224)** an assignment accumulates **readings**: `member_personal_goal_readings` holds one row per measurement (`value DECIMAL(10,2)` in the assignment's own unit, `recorded_at DATETIME`, `is_initial`, plus a `created_by` pair that admits `member`), append-only — there is no edit and no delete (§34), and the assignment's own soft delete never touches them (§28). **The five header fields are computed on read, never stored**: `initial_reading`, `initial_reading_at`, `latest_reading`, `latest_reading_at`, `progress_percent` and `reading_count` ride on every assignment-shaped response (the list, the single read and each mutation) through `withReadingSummaries()` in `api/src/api/goal-readings.ts`, which reads a whole page's readings in **one** query — the table's only read, which `mpgr_gym_goal_index` is shaped for, and the reason `is_initial` carries no index of its own — over the pure rules in `api/src/domain/goalReadings.ts`. **One table rather than the two §37 sketches**, because `is_initial` *is* the initial-reading period boundary: §1/§29 put the initial reading in the reading history, so a separate period row holding its own `value` and `effective_from` would store the same number twice and could contradict the measurement it was taken from. So the initial-reading history (§22) is `is_initial = 1` ordered by `recorded_at`, the **active** initial reading (§25) is its last row — falling back to the earliest reading when nothing is flagged (§2) — and which period a reading belongs to (§38) is `assignReadingPeriods()`, an ordering question with no join. **The direction is derived, never declared** (the thread's `Q2`): `progressPercent()` is `(initial - latest) / (initial - target) x 100` for both directions, since the two differences change sign together, `target == initial` is maintenance (100 while the latest reading is still the target, 0 otherwise) and the result is clamped to 0..100 with the raw value never reported (§27). `null` means not computable — no readings or no target — and the card shows `—` rather than `0%`. Two writers per surface, deliberately separate: `POST /:id/readings` records a measurement and `POST /:id/initial-reading` adds a new baseline, so a client cannot re-baseline a member's goal by passing a flag through and the audit rows (`add_reading` / `set_initial_reading`, against the assignment, which is the entity the registry already knows) say which happened. `GET /:id/readings` answers `{ readings, …summary }` oldest-first, since §17's chart and §19's history are the same rows read in opposite directions. `POST /` may establish the baseline as the goal is assigned (`initial_reading`, `initial_reading_at`), in the **same transaction** as the assignment, taking §4's "reuse the existing timestamp" from `start_date` when no time is named. **Since #1037 stage 3** those fields are on screen, and the rendering adds no rule: `apps/admin/src/components/personalGoals/goalReadings.ts` holds §5's five-field declaration, the formatters, the history ordering and the dialog's validation, `GoalReadingHeader.tsx` draws the header for both screens (the gym-wide list's expanded row and each card of the Member card's section) and `GoalReadingHistory.tsx` the collapsible newest-first log, with `AddReadingModal.tsx` serving both writers — which one it calls is the route, never a field. Nothing there computes a figure: the three reading values and the percentage come off the row, and `—` is what a non-computable one reads. The gym-wide list gains a **Progress** column and offers `⋮ → Add reading` / `Change initial reading` gated on write access (the expanded body stays control-free, #797), while the Member card's buttons belong to its Edit mode (#957). |
| Nutrition plan templates | NUTRITION | `requireModuleWrite('NUTRITION')` | `nutrition_plan_templates.gym_id` is nullable (migration 103 — NULL = Cordel base template). `cloned_from_id INT UNSIGNED FK→self` tracks lineage. `GET /nutrition-plan-templates?type=base\|gym\|all`; `owner_type` field on every row. `POST /:id/clone` deep-copies any template → gym-owned draft. `POST /:id/assign` (body: `{member_id, start_date}`) deep-clones → `member_nutrition_plans`. Platform CRUD at `/platform/nutrition-plan-templates` (`requireSuperadmin`). |
| Member nutrition plans (`member-nutrition-plans`) | NUTRITION | read: `requireModuleAccess('NUTRITION')`; mutations: `requireModuleWrite('NUTRITION')` | Created via `POST /nutrition-plan-templates/:id/assign` or, since #443, directly via `POST /member-nutrition-plans` (body: `{member_id, template_id?, name?, description?, start_date}` — `start_date` required here, YYYY-MM-DD; `name` required only when `template_id` is omitted). Both routes share the clone/insert transaction in `nutrition-plan-creation.ts`'s `createNutritionPlanTx()` (mirrors `training-plan-creation.ts`'s split for Training Plans). Tables: `member_nutrition_plans` → `member_nutrition_plan_days` → `member_nutrition_plan_meals` + `member_nutrition_plan_meal_items` + `member_nutrition_plan_restrictions` + `member_nutrition_plan_goals` (all with `gym_id`). `GET /member-nutrition-plans?member_id=`, `GET /:id`, `GET /:id/hierarchy`, `DELETE /:id` (soft-delete). #441 (matching Training Plans' Edit/Duplicate/Details/Complete): `PUT /:id` edits `name`/`description`/`start_date`; `POST /:id/complete` sets `status='completed'`; `POST /:id/duplicate` deep-clones the full tree into a new `active` plan for the same member. Lifecycle is just `active → completed\|deleted` (no `draft`/`expired`, unlike Training Plans) — a plan is mutable only while `active`, enforced per-route (403 once `completed`), same "immutable once done" rule as Training Plans. Full day/meal/item/restriction/goal CRUD under `/:id/days...` mirrors `nutrition-plan-templates.ts`'s routes one-for-one, scoped to `member_nutrition_plan_id` instead of `nutrition_plan_template_id` — note `member_nutrition_plan_meal_items.component_type` has a narrower CHECK (`main_dish\|side\|sauce\|additional`, no `drink`/`dessert`/`other`) than the template table. **Food Type options (#812)**: that difference is now served rather than assumed. The accepted sets live once in `api/src/domain/nutritionComponentTypes.ts` (`TEMPLATE_COMPONENT_TYPES`, seven — `chk_nptmi_component_type`, migration 111/#294; `MEMBER_PLAN_COMPONENT_TYPES`, four — `chk_mnpmi_comp`, migration 105), each validated through `isComponentType()` and exposed by `GET /component-types` on all three routers (`/nutrition-plan-templates`, `/member-nutrition-plans`, `/platform/nutrition-plan-templates`; registered **before** `/:id`, or Express reads the path as an id). Adding a value therefore takes **two** places — the list and the CHECK beside it. `NutritionPlanTree` fetches the set from `${apiBase}/component-types`, so the Food Type selector cannot offer a value the CHECK would refuse. The same component also reads a food's categories as the `categories: [{id, slug}]` list the library returns; it had kept reading the scalar `nutrition_library_items.category` that migration 142 (#501) dropped, which made every option render as `nutrition_plan_templates.tree_component_type_undefined`. Labels go through one `foodTypeLabel()` helper that falls back to the slug: next-intl's `t()` has no `defaultValue` option and prints a missing key verbatim, so the fallback that was there never fired. `member_nutrition_plan_days` has a `(member_nutrition_plan_id, weekday)` unique constraint (migration 136) mirroring the template table's, so `POST /:id/days` 409s on a duplicate weekday. Admin frontend (`apps/admin/.../nutrition/nutrition-plans/page.tsx`) reuses `nutrition-plan-templates/NutritionPlanTree.tsx` for the expand-in-place editor via its `apiBase` prop (now threaded through every child component) rather than a separate copy, and has a **+ New Nutrition Plan** action (`NewNutritionPlanDialog.tsx`) offering a from-template vs. from-scratch choice, mirroring Training Plans' `NewTrainingPlanDialog.tsx`. |
| Nutrition dashboard (`nutrition-dashboard`) | NUTRITION | read-only: `requireModuleAccess('NUTRITION')` + `requireFeatureEnabled('nutrition')` | **(#809)** `GET /nutrition/dashboard/nutrition-plans` — the `Nutrition → Dashboard` page's card wall. One card per **Nutrition Plan Template** (`{template_id, name, status, active_members}`) plus one bucket card (`template_id: null`, which the frontend labels *No base nutrition template plan*) for the plans created from scratch. Nothing here writes. Mounted on the **`nutrition` group flag**, not `nutrition.nutrition_plans`, so turning the Nutrition Plans page off leaves the Dashboard readable — the same choice #638's Finance Dashboard (`financials-dashboard.ts`, `GET /financials/dashboard/membership-plans`) made. One aggregate query, driven by the **assignments** rather than by the Templates (§11: no N+1), which is what gives the ticket's three filtering rules for free: a card exists only where a counted member exists (so a Template with none is never rendered as an empty `0` card), the Template's own status is never the filter (an `inactive` or `draft` Template shows while an active member is on it, and that status is the card's badge — a Template soft-deleted while members still hold plans from it keeps its card with `deleted`, since dropping it would silently remove those members from the overview), and a `member_nutrition_plans` row whose Template belongs to neither this gym nor the platform (`gym_id IS NULL`, a Cordel base template a gym may assign from) resolves to no Template and falls into the bucket, so no other gym's Template name can be read off the endpoint. **Active member** = the Members list's `enrollment_status = 'active'` (the member's latest `user_memberships` row) + `members.deleted_at IS NULL`; that derivation lives once in `api/src/domain/memberEnrollment.ts` (`latestEnrollmentStatusSql(alias)`) and is read by both `GET /members` and this endpoint, so the dashboard's count and the Members page's `Enrollment status: Active` filter can never mean different sets. Only `active` plans count (a `completed` plan is no longer currently assigned; a `deleted` one is gone everywhere else), and the count is `COUNT(DISTINCT member_id)` — a member holding two plans from the same Template counts once, which is what the card's `Active members` label says. |
| User memberships (`user-memberships`) | PAYMENTS | `requireModuleWrite('PAYMENTS')` | DELETE = `requireRole('admin')`. Status changes emit billing events. `created_by_name`/`created_by_type` are snapshotted by every creation path (#958, migration 215). |
| Billing ledger (`billing-events`) | PAYMENTS | `requireModuleWrite('PAYMENTS')` | Append-only. |
| Payments (`payments`) | PAYMENTS | `requireModuleWrite('PAYMENTS')` | Operational view; excludes `status_changed`. |
| Payment requests (`payment-requests`) | PAYMENTS | `requireModuleAccess('PAYMENTS')` write: `requireModuleWrite('PAYMENTS')` | Staff-raised requests for a member's Membership Fee: `GET /`, `GET /:id` (both excluding `source = 'card_update'`, #788), `POST /` → `{ id, checkoutUrl }`. The member's own equivalent is `GET`/`POST /me/payment-requests` (POST rate-limited 3/hour per Clerk user id). The amount is always `currentMembershipFee()` resolved on the cycle being paid, sent to the provider in minor units, and a cycle owing 0 is refused. **Process, field by field: `docs/payments.md` §A3.** |
| Stored cards (`payment-methods`) | PAYMENTS | `requireModuleAccess('PAYMENTS')` write: `requireModuleWrite('PAYMENTS')` | **(#788)** The card a member's recurring charges are taken from, and the one action that changes it. `GET /payment-methods?member_id=N` → `{ payment_method: { provider, card_brand, card_last4, since } | null, member_can_remove, removal_blocked_reason }` — never the `payment_token`/`sequence_id` pair, which is what charges the member. `POST /payment-methods/replace-requests` `{ member_id }` → `{ id, checkoutUrl }`: a **zero-amount card verification**, not a charge. Member side: `GET /me/payment-method`, `POST /me/payment-method/replace-requests` (3/hour, its own limiter), `DELETE /me/payment-method`. No route here accepts card data — PCI scope stays on `pay.vdicube.com`. **Why a verification is not a payment: `docs/payments.md` § Card replacement is not a payment.** |
| Payment page token (`payment-page`) | none | none (token auth) | No Clerk auth, 20 req/min per IP. `GET /payment-page/token/:token` — the browser's one bridge from the isolated `pay.vdicube.com` app into the API (since #1083 that app also relays Monei's webhook server-side, which is the only other path through it). Consumes the single-use token (`page_token` → NULL) — which, on a row still `pending`, is also the **record that the page was opened**, and this is its only writer, so `POST /billing/cleanup` can tell a link nobody followed from a checkout in flight (#789). Returns display-only fields plus the Monei `paymentId`; never a Gymdesk internal id. **Contract and field list: `docs/payments.md` §A4.** |
| Clerk webhooks (`/webhooks/clerk`) | none | Svix HMAC (Clerk signature) | Subscribed to `user.created` and `user.deleted` (#709). Mounted before `express.json()` with `express.raw` so the signature is verified against the exact bytes Clerk signed; `verifyWebhook()` is the route's **first** operation and a bad signature is a 400 with no DB query. `user.created` materialises the real Clerk `user_id` on an invited staff row (placeholder `invited_<timestamp>`), which `POST /staff/link` repairs at sign-in if the event is missed; `user.deleted` runs `unlinkClerkAccount(userId)` and is idempotent, so the echo of our own deletes is a no-op. **(#1085)** Clerk no longer posts here directly: its endpoint is the **admin app's** `POST /api/webhooks/clerk` (`https://admin.vdicube.com/api/webhooks/clerk` on dev, `https://admin.cordel.tech/…` on pro), a Next route handler that relays the request to this route at `CORDEL_FITNESS_API_URL` — the raw body as bytes and the three `svix-*` headers byte for byte, with the API's own status code relayed back. Nothing about verification moves: the relay checks nothing and `CLERK_WEBHOOK_SIGNING_SECRET` never leaves the API, so a tampered body is still a 400 *from the API*, relayed as such. What it forwards and where is `apps/admin/src/lib/clerkWebhookRelay.ts`' (pure); the route is the I/O half and decides nothing. It is deliberately **not** `/api/proxy`, whose header set carries `authorization`/`x-gym-id`/`x-locale` and none of the `svix-*` ones — a webhook sent through it would arrive unsigned. Two statuses are the relay's own and neither is a 200, because Clerk retries exactly what we report as failed: **502** when the API is unreachable, **500** when `CORDEL_FITNESS_API_URL` is unset. The path is exempt from `auth.protect()` **and** from the locale routing in `apps/admin/src/middleware.ts` (the early `return NextResponse.next()`, as `/api/proxy` is), or Clerk would get a 401 or a redirect to `/en/…`. The admin app, not the members app: the two have identical middleware and proxy, so there is no technical difference and a second endpoint would be a second thing to keep in step with the Clerk dashboard. Gate: `api/src/test/clerk-webhook-relay.unit.test.ts` (in the API suite, because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only), with the admin half's own in `apps/admin/src/test/clerk-webhook-relay.test.ts` (the pure module) and `clerk-webhook-relay-route.test.ts` (the route against a stubbed `fetch`). |
| Payment webhooks (`/webhooks/payment`) | none | HMAC-SHA256 (Monei signature) | Mounted before `express.json()` with `express.raw` so the signature is verified against the exact bytes Monei signed; `parseWebhook()` verifies it as its **first** operation and a bad signature is a 400 with no DB query. Idempotent: a row is processable only while `pending`, or when an **`expired`** one receives a `completed` payload (#789 — the provider is the source of truth about money; a `failed`/`expired` payload never revives a terminal row and an already-`completed` row is always skipped), rate-limited 60/min per IP; `GET` answers 200 for Monei's dashboard preflight. The `completed` branch is what creates a member's billable state. **Every branch and what each writes: `docs/payments.md` §A5.** **(#1083)** Monei no longer posts here directly: it posts to the isolated payment app (`PAYMENT_NOTIFICATION_URL` → `https://pay.…/webhooks/payment`), whose nginx relays the request to this route at the API's internal address — byte for byte, signature header included, with the API's own status code relayed back (see `decisions.md` #19). Nothing about verification moves: the relay checks nothing and the webhook secret never leaves the API. What does move is the **rate-limit key**: the chain in front of this one route is one hop longer than `trust proxy` accounts for, so the 60/min budget is keyed through `paymentWebhookClientKey()` (`domain/forwardedClient.ts`), which adds `PAYMENT_WEBHOOK_RELAY_HOPS` to `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS` and answers the client rather than the relay — key the relay and one gym's payments spend every gym's budget. The setting defaults to 0, where the key is `req.ip` exactly as before, and it is deliberately **not** a higher global `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS`: that would make Express trust one more caller-supplied `X-Forwarded-For` entry on every route that is still publicly reachable. |
| Internal billing cleanup (`/billing/cleanup`) | none | `X-Internal-Secret` header + internal-run limiter (#783) | POST writes off `pending` payment requests against **two** deadlines, never one (#789, `domain/paymentRequestExpiry.ts`): a row whose `page_token` is still set expires with that token (creation + 10 min), and one whose token the page load already cleared is kept for `abandonedRequestHours()` (`PAYMENT_REQUEST_ABANDONED_HOURS`, default 24, floored at 1) so a late terminal webhook can still land on it. Answers `{ expired, expired_unopened, expired_abandoned }` — `expired` stays the total because the workflow parses that field (#778). Run by the nightly workflow even when the charge step went red (`if: ${{ !cancelled() }}`). **See `docs/payments.md` §B8.** |
| Internal billing run (`/billing/run`) | none | `X-Internal-Secret` header + internal-run limiter (#783) | POST fires the nightly MIT recurring charges and answers `{ processed, succeeded, failed, waived, paused, receipts_issued }`. Guarded by the calendar, not a rolling window — one **completed** run per UTC date via `claimRun()`/`finishRun()` (#780, migration 193): today's run already done ⇒ `200 { skipped_reason: 'already_completed_today', … }` (never 429, so #781's 10:00 UTC second attempt is a green no-op), a genuine overlap ⇒ `429`, a crashed run blocks nothing. Prices each cycle through the one shared `resolveMembershipFee()` on the date `next_billing_date` names, charges in **minor units**, and escalates a rejected charge to `paused` on the second consecutive rejection (#785). **The selection query, all five outcomes, the dunning rule and the workflow's alerting contract: `docs/payments.md` §B.** |
| Internal recurring booking run (`/recurring-bookings/run`) | none | `X-Internal-Secret` header (`RECURRING_BOOKINGS_INTERNAL_SECRET`) + internal-run limiter (#783) | #647 stage 4. POST maintains the rolling 2-month Personal Training booking window. Walks `SELECT DISTINCT gym_id, member_id FROM member_recurring_slots` (skipping soft-deleted members *and* soft-deleted gyms — `member_recurring_slots` only cascades on a hard delete, so a closed gym would otherwise keep being filled) and, per member, re-projects the next 2 months from *now* and books every free date through the very same `bookSelectedSlots()` that `POST /members/:id/personal-training-slots/book` runs — so capacity, waitlist mode, #481 eligibility, package-credit debiting and the §5 Professional Service re-check are never re-implemented. Nothing tracks how far a member has been booked: "creates only missing bookings, never duplicates" falls out of the projection (a date the member already holds reads `already_booked` and is skipped). Dates it cannot take raise a `member_notifications` row of type `recurring_booking_skipped` (reasons `no_occurrence` / `not_scheduled` / `full` only), deduped on (slot, date) against the last 120 days so a rolling window does not re-alert nightly. Honours the `organization.professional_services` feature flag (403) — the same flag the member-facing routes are mounted behind. **(#780)** An **unscoped** run is guarded exactly as `POST /billing/run` is — one completed run per UTC date in the `recurring_booking_run_log` history (migration 193), `200 { skipped_reason: 'already_completed_today', … }` when today's is done, `429` only while another is genuinely `in_progress`, nothing blocked by a run that crashed. The field is `skipped_reason` rather than `skipped` because this run already reports a numeric `skipped` counter. A run scoped by `gym_id`/`member_id` in the body claims no row at all, so an operator can re-run one gym after a fix. Body: `{ gym_id?, member_id?, limit? (1..10000, default none), detail? }` — `limit` is a safety valve with no cursor behind it, so a capped run walks the same prefix every night; the nightly run passes none. One member's failure is caught, reported in its `members[]` entry and the run continues. **(#778)** `.github/workflows/recurring-booking-run.yml` reports the same way: red on `failed > 0`, on any `members[]` entry carrying an `error` (a member whose whole pass threw has zero counters, so `failed` alone would hide it), on a non-2xx (the 403 from the feature flag and the 429 from the guard included) and on a body with no counters; a `skipped_reason` body is green and reported as a no-op (#780). `skipped` stays green — a date that is a festivity, already booked or full is the expected outcome stage 4 was designed around and is already reported to the member as a `recurring_booking_skipped` notification. **(#781 §3)** Unlike the billing run this workflow keeps a **single** daily schedule: a night GitHub drops costs nothing lasting, because the run re-projects the rolling window from *now* and the next night books whatever is still free. |
| Internal Promotion expiry (`/promotion-lifecycle/run`) | none | `X-Internal-Secret` header (`BILLING_INTERNAL_SECRET`) + internal-run limiter (#783) | **(#900)** POST moves every `active` Promotion whose `ends_at` has passed to `lifecycle_status = 'expired'` and answers `{ expired }`. System-wide (one `UPDATE` across every gym, so it is mounted outside `tenantContext` rather than on the tenant-scoped `/promotions` router) and the third step of `.github/workflows/billing-run.yml`, which is why it shares that workflow's secret rather than introducing a third one — a job of its own would get a secret of its own. The rule is spelled once in `api/src/domain/promotionLifecycle.ts` (`promotionExpiryWhereSql()`): only `active` can expire (an `inactive` Promotion was switched off deliberately and stays so for ever, a `deleted` one is in the Recycle Bin, an `expired` one is already done — which is what makes the sweep idempotent), the end date is compared as `ends_at < UTC_TIMESTAMP()` so no DATETIME crosses a timezone conversion, and `ends_at IS NOT NULL` states the "no End Date ⇒ never expire" rule for a column that is NOT NULL today. Only `lifecycle_status` is written: the dates, the Billing & Duration months, the benefit sections, the targeted Plans and every `user_membership_promotions` application are untouched — an application prices from its own snapshot (#635 §16), so a Promotion expiring re-prices nobody. No run-log claim (`claimRun()`/`finishRun()` exist to stop two *charging* runs overlapping; this is one idempotent statement) and no audit row (`recordAudit()` needs a `tenantCtx` actor, and there is none — the same reason the nightly billing run records `billing_events` instead). |
| Internal booking reminder run (`/booking-reminders/run`) | none | `X-Internal-Secret` header (`BOOKING_REMINDERS_INTERNAL_SECRET`) + internal-run limiter (#783) | **(#1113 §2)** POST raises one `booking_reminder_2h` member notification per **active** booking whose occurrence starts within the next two hours and that has none yet, and answers `{ candidates, created, gyms, lead_minutes, capped }` (plus `failures[]` for a gym whose insert threw — one gym's failure costs that gym only). System-wide, so it is mounted outside `tenantContext`. The rules live once in `api/src/domain/bookingReminders.ts` (pure): `ceb.status = 'booked'` (a waitlisted member holds a place in a queue, not a training), `ce.status = 'scheduled'`, neither the occurrence nor the member soft-deleted, the window compared as `starts_at > UTC_TIMESTAMP()` *and* `<= DATE_ADD(UTC_TIMESTAMP(), INTERVAL 120 MINUTE)` so no DATETIME crosses a timezone conversion, with the per-pass cap **written into** the `LIMIT` rather than bound (mysql2's `execute()` refuses a bound one — see the `LIMIT` rule in `CLAUDE.md`), and a `NOT EXISTS` on `member_notifications` for that member and that occurrence — **which is the whole of the duplicate prevention**: nothing stores that a reminder was sent (#647 stage 4's device, and the reason the insert is `recordNotifications()` rather than fire-and-forget), so §6 falls out of it too — a cancelled booking simply stops being a candidate and the replacement occurrence is owed its own reminder. The window is "within the lead time", not "exactly at it", because a scheduled runner fires late: asking for 115–120 minutes would silently drop every member whose class fell between two passes, so a late pass costs punctuality rather than the alert. Three things it deliberately has not got: **no run-log claim** (#780's guard is *one completed run per UTC date*, which would silence every pass but the first of the day — this is the one scheduled run that is not nightly), **no audit row** (`recordAudit()` needs a `tenantCtx` actor) and **no feature-flag check** (a reminder is about a booking that already exists, not a capability a gym opts into). Its own secret, per the rule `promotion-lifecycle.ts` states: a job with a workflow of its own gets a secret with it. `.github/workflows/booking-reminder-run.yml` fires it every 15 minutes with `concurrency` set so two passes cannot overlap, goes red on a non-2xx, on a body with no counters and on any `failures[]` entry, and warns rather than fails on `capped` (the next pass picks up what `BOOKING_REMINDER_MAX_PER_RUN` left). |
| Run freshness (`/health/runs`) | none | none (public, read-only) | **(#782)** `GET /health/runs` answers `{ billing, recurring_bookings }`, each `{ last_completed_at, age_hours, stale }` — the latest `finished_at` of a `completed` row in `billing_run_log` / `recurring_booking_run_log` (`lastCompletedRun()`), and whether it is older than `RUN_FRESHNESS_THRESHOLD_HOURS` (default 26, `domain/runFreshness.ts`); a log with no completed run is `stale: true`. 200 whenever the DB answers (the prober reads `stale`), 503 when it does not. Unauthenticated and outside `/billing/` on purpose, so the internal-run secret and rate limiter never apply to the Grafana Cloud synthetic check that probes it; returns no counters and nothing tenant-scoped. Mounted in `app.ts` beside the plain `GET /health`. **See `docs/payments.md` → Observability today.** |
| Membership plans, Benefit types, Charge types, Promotions | FINANCIALS | `requireRole('admin')` | Admin-only within FINANCIALS module. |
| Tax Rates (`taxes`) | FINANCIALS | read: any FINANCIALS role; write: `requireRole('admin')` | **Flags (#610):** mounted behind the `financials` group flag only — Plans and Products load `GET /taxes` for their tax dropdown, so the Taxes page's own flag `financials.taxes` gates the five write routes in the router, not the list. (Before #610 the mount used `financials.products`, so hiding Products broke the tax dropdown on Plans.) Per-gym tax rate catalogue (migration 112, `description` + audit-actor columns added in migration 126, #388). One `is_system=1` "Standard VAT 21%" row auto-seeded per gym at creation. Custom rates: full CRUD with soft-delete. `tax_behavior` on `products` ('inclusive'/'exclusive') controls whether `amount` is gross or net. `computePriceFields()` in `products.ts` derives `amount_excl_tax`/`amount_incl_tax`/`applied_tax_rate`. `tax_rate_id FK→tax_rates` on both `products` and `membership_plans`. Taxes are **not** snapshotted onto a Membership at instantiation — a Tax edit's blast radius is always the *current* `products`/`membership_plans` rows referencing it, never past Membership instances. `created_by_name`/`created_by_type` and `modified_by_name`/`modified_by_type` snapshot the acting user's display name and `'staff'`/`'superadmin'` at write time (not a join to `gym_memberships`, which has no row for a superadmin acting directly — `*_membership_id` alone silently produced a blank actor in that case). `PUT /:id` computes the count of non-deleted `products`/`membership_plans` still referencing the tax before persisting; if either is non-zero and the request omits `confirmImpact: true`, it returns `409 { error: 'confirmation_required', impact }` without writing — the frontend re-submits with `confirmImpact: true` once the staff user confirms. Admin UI (`apps/admin/.../financials/taxes/`) uses inline create/edit (no modal), matching the `products` page's expand/collapse row pattern. |
| Finance dashboard (`/financials/dashboard`) | FINANCIALS | — | Read-only aggregation (#638), no writes. Mounted behind the `financials` **group** flag, not `financials.plans`, so the Dashboard keeps working when the Plans page is switched off. `GET /membership-plans` returns one card per plan (`id`, `name`, `lifecycle_status`, `assigned_members`): every `lifecycle_status='active'` plan plus any non-active plan that still has assignments. An assignment counts while it is neither `cancelled` nor `expired` under the #410 date-aware projection used by the Assigned Plans list, and assignments of soft-deleted members are excluded. |
| Payments dashboard (`/payments/dashboard`) | PAYMENTS | — | Read-only aggregation (#674), no writes and no change to Billing Event or payment-processing behaviour. Gated by its own `payments.dashboard` flag (migration 171 seeds it enabled) and mounted **before** `/payments` in `app.ts`, so `payments.transactions` being off never 403s it — and it can be switched off on its own. `GET /summary` returns `{current_month_start, current_month_end, previous_month_start, previous_month_end, scheduled_this_month, total_last_month, failed_last_month, successful_last_month, awaiting_action_count, awaiting_action_oldest_at}` (the last two are #779's, see *Failed payments awaiting action*). The three last-month cards come from one grouped query over `billing_events` (previous calendar month by `created_at`, one `WHERE be.gym_id = ?`) whose `(event_type, latest transaction status)` groups are mapped through `deriveBillingEventStatus()` (#640) rather than a CASE expression, so the cards can't drift from the Billing Events page; `total_last_month` counts every ledger row, so it is **not** necessarily failed + successful. `scheduled_this_month` reuses that page's projection from `user_memberships.next_billing_date` + `billing_policies.recurring_billing_interval` — projected dates inside the current month, skipping dates the nightly run hasn't caught up with. Month boundaries come from one exported `monthWindows()` in UTC so the SQL ranges and the JS projection share a clock, and the window is returned to the client so period labels aren't re-derived in the browser's time zone. |
| Audit log (`audit-logs`) | SYSTEM | — | Read-only; `?scope=all` for superadmin. |
| Themes (`/system/themes`) | SYSTEM | — | R/W within SYSTEM module. |
| Action types (`action-types`) | any role | — | Global lookup (no `gym_id`), seeded in migration. |
| Public (`public/*`) | guest | — | Gym landing + class list by slug. |
| Platform (`platform/*`) | `superadmin` | `superadmin` | Gym creation/list; superadmin management. |

**Internal run auth (#783).** The internal run routes — `POST /billing/run`, `POST /billing/cleanup`, `POST /promotion-lifecycle/run`, `POST /recurring-bookings/run` and, since #1113, `POST /booking-reminders/run`, fired by `.github/workflows/billing-run.yml`, `recurring-booking-run.yml` and `booking-reminder-run.yml` — are authenticated by a **shared secret and nothing else at the network layer**: each router's `checkInternalSecret()` compares the `X-Internal-Secret` header with `BILLING_INTERNAL_SECRET` / `RECURRING_BOOKINGS_INTERNAL_SECRET` / `BOOKING_REMINDERS_INTERNAL_SECRET` (unset ⇒ every call is 401). There is **no IP allowlist**: the nginx one on `location /billing/` (#306) was removed by #783 — it had never run anywhere, since there is no nginx in the API's request path (TLS and routing are Traefik on corfront, see *Infrastructure*). In its place `app.ts` mounts `internalRunLimiter` ahead of both routers — per client IP (`trust proxy`, below), `INTERNAL_RUN_RATE_LIMIT_MAX` (default 10) per `INTERNAL_RUN_RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_MINUTES` (default 15), one budget across the three routes, config parsed by `domain/internalRunRateLimit.ts`. **Only a 401 spends it** (`spendsInternalRunBudget()`), so it throttles guessing the secret without ever touching a caller that holds it — #781's two daily attempts, and the run guard's own `429`/`200 already_completed_today`, cost nothing. A spent budget answers `429` to every call from that address until the window ends, the right secret included. Do not reintroduce an allowlist that has to be refreshed by hand; a network-layer restriction comes back only with a concrete reason (a PCI/QSA requirement), decided in a ticket. See `docs/payments.md` §B1.

**(#1086)** Those routes are no longer called from GitHub's runners directly: the workflows call `$API_BASE_URL/<path>` where `API_BASE_URL` is the **admin app's relay prefix** (`https://admin.vdicube.com/api/internal` on dev, `https://admin.cordel.tech/api/internal` on pro), and `apps/admin/src/app/api/internal/[...path]/route.ts` forwards the POST to this API at `CORDEL_FITNESS_API_URL`. Which paths may be relayed, what crosses the boundary and what the relay answers when it cannot are `apps/admin/src/lib/internalRunRelay.ts`' (pure); `lib/internalRunUpstream.ts` is the transport and the route is the glue. Nothing about authentication moves: the relay compares no secret, neither internal secret leaves the API, and `checkInternalSecret()` still answers the `401` — relayed as such, and still spending the budget above. Four things are the relay's own.
