It carries an **explicit allowlist** of the API paths the workflows call (`INTERNAL_RUN_API_PATHS` — four until #1113 added `/booking-reminders/run`, which changes nothing about the relay: the allowlist is about which paths may be forwarded, never how often), so a path under that prefix which the workflows do not call is a `404` from the relay rather than a request this API has to judge. Its forwarded header set is `x-internal-secret`, `content-type` and `x-forwarded-for` and nothing else — deliberately **not** `/api/proxy`'s (`authorization`, `x-gym-id`, `x-center-id`, `x-impersonate-as`, `x-locale`), which is also why widening that route was rejected: it would have put the internal runners behind a route the browser app also uses. It answers **502** when it could not reach the API, **504** when the API outlived `INTERNAL_RUN_RELAY_TIMEOUT_MS` (default 660 000 ms, above the longest `--max-time` in `.github/workflows/`, so `curl` is always the first to give up) and **500** when `CORDEL_FITNESS_API_URL` is unset — never a blanket 200, because the workflows fail on a non-2xx and read the body (#778). And the upstream call is `node:http` rather than `fetch`: undici abandons a response whose headers take more than 300 s, and `POST /recurring-bookings/run` is allowed 600 s, so a long night would be cut off at five minutes — a run this API completed but the workflow saw as failed is the worst outcome available, since #780's guard then refuses the retry as `already_completed_today`. What *does* move is the **rate-limit key**: the chain in front of these routes is one hop longer than `trust proxy` accounts for, so the budget is keyed through `internalRunClientKey()` (`domain/forwardedClient.ts`), which adds `INTERNAL_RUN_RELAY_HOPS` to `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS` and answers the client rather than the relay — keyed on the relay, ten wrong guesses from anywhere lock the nightly run out of the window. The setting defaults to 0, where the key is `req.ip` exactly as before, and it is deliberately **not** a higher global `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS` (#1083's reason, unchanged). The path is exempt from `auth.protect()` **and** from the locale routing in `apps/admin/src/middleware.ts`, or the workflow would get a 401 or a redirect to `/en/…`. Gate: `api/src/test/internal-run-relay.unit.test.ts` (in the API suite, because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only), with the admin half's own in `apps/admin/src/test/internal-run-relay.test.ts` (the pure module), `internal-run-relay-route.test.ts` (the route against a stubbed transport) and `internal-run-upstream.test.ts` (the transport against a real local server), plus `api/src/test/internal-run-relay.test.ts` for the limiter key end to end.

Usage in routes:
```ts
// In app.ts — module-level read gate at mount:
app.use('/members', requireAuth(), tenantContext, requireModuleAccess('MEMBERS'), membersRouter);

// In a router — write gate on individual mutations:
router.post('/', requireModuleWrite('TRAINING'), handler);
router.delete('/:id', requireRole('admin'), handler);  // explicit last-admin guard

// Public routes — no Clerk session:
app.use('/public/gyms/:gymRef/registrations', publicRegistrationsRouter); // #599: per-gym API key + rate limits
app.use('/public', publicRouter);
```

**Trainers (#986).** A trainer is a **Staff record whose `employment_status` is `active`**, whatever their HR profile — not a coach role on the login row, which is what the `trainers` router filtered on until #986 (`role IN ('trainer_performance','trainer_perf_nutrition')`, i.e. only the *Personal Trainer* profiles), so a gym staffed by Gym Managers and Front Desk got an empty Default Trainer dropdown. `api/src/domain/trainerAssignment.ts` is the one place that rule and its ordering live (`ASSIGNABLE_TRAINERS_FROM`, `assignableTrainersSql()`, `isAssignableTrainer()`, `trainerWriteNeedsLookup()`); `GET /trainers`, `GET /me/trainers` and every writer that stores a trainer are projections of it — the Activity Type's `default_trainer_membership_id` (previously unvalidated), `validateSessionRefs()`, `PUT /class-sessions/:id/effective-trainer` and `POST /trainer-availability` (the last three previously carrying the coach-role filter themselves). `staff.current_status` (`on_vacation`, `suspended`, …) is deliberately not a filter, and the only structural condition is `staff.gym_membership_id`: every table that stores a trainer keys to `gym_memberships.id`, so a staff record whose login grant failed has no id to be stored under. The value a row already holds is not re-validated on write and is rendered as a disabled option, so an assignment survives the trainer leaving.

### Platform superadmin (Clerk metadata)
- `requireSuperadmin` middleware checks `user.publicMetadata.platform_role === 'superadmin'`.
- Used for `/platform/*` (gym creation, full gym list) and `/platform/superadmins` (grant/revoke platform role).
- `tenantContext` grants superadmins a synthetic `admin` role for any gym, so they can access all domain routes.
- Frontend: `GymContext` exposes `isSuperadmin` from Clerk's `useUser()`.
- `infra/seed.ts` bootstraps the first superadmin from `SEED_USER_ID` (local / dev only — production bootstrap is in `docs/go-to-production.md` §3).

### User Impersonation (#135, extended by #189)
Superadmins can impersonate any active gym user for support and debugging without a password change.

**How it works (separation of authentication and authorization):**
- The authenticated identity never changes. Only the *effective* identity used for authorization changes.
- The frontend stores an impersonation session in `sessionStorage` (survives refresh, cleared on tab close).
- `apiFetch()` appends `x-impersonate-as: <value>` to every request while impersonating. The value is `member:<id>` when impersonating a member (using `members.id`), or the Clerk user ID when impersonating a staff user.
- `tenantContext` detects this header and, after confirming the caller is a superadmin, resolves the effective identity. For `member:<id>` prefixed values it queries `members` directly (no `gym_memberships` join needed, so members without Clerk accounts are eligible). For Clerk user IDs it falls back to the existing `gym_memberships` lookup. Sets `effectiveType: 'member' | 'staff' | null` on the context.
- `TenantContext` carries `effectiveUserId` and `effectiveType`. For member impersonation `effectiveUserId` is the `members.id` as a string; for staff or normal usage it is the Clerk user ID. All `/me/*` handlers use the `resolveMemberId(gymId, ctx)` helper which routes to `WHERE members.id = ?` or `WHERE clerk_user_id = ?` depending on `effectiveType`.
- `centerContext` (runs after `tenantContext` on `/me/*`) follows the same rule when resolving a `member` role's assigned centers: it matches `member_centers` on `members.id = effectiveUserId` under member impersonation, or `members.clerk_user_id = userId` otherwise — never the impersonating superadmin's raw `userId` against `clerk_user_id`, which would resolve to zero centers and 403 every `x-center-id`-scoped request (#362).
- `recordAudit` encodes both identities in `actor_name` (e.g. `"Alice Johnson (impersonating John Smith)"`) so every business audit row remains traceable.
- **Gotcha — `centerContext` runs after `tenantContext` and must key off `effectiveUserId`, not `userId`.** `centerContext` (`api/src/infra/centerContext.ts`) resolves a `member`-role caller's `allowedCenterIds` via `member_centers`. Under member impersonation `userId` is still the impersonating superadmin's own Clerk ID — only `effectiveUserId`/`effectiveType` carry the impersonated member's identity — so any lookup keyed on `userId` (e.g. `clerk_user_id = ?`) silently resolves to zero centers instead of the impersonated member's. When `effectiveType === 'member'`, match `member_centers.member_id = effectiveUserId` directly instead of joining through `clerk_user_id`. `/me/*` handlers that scope by center (e.g. `GET /me/schedule` filtering `class_sessions.center_id`) must call `getCenterContext(req)` themselves — `centerContext` only populates `req.centerCtx`, it does not filter automatically like `tenantContext`'s `gym_id` scoping.
- **Gotcha — an empty `member_centers` result is not always "assigned to zero centers."** Many gyms have only one center and never populate `member_centers` at all. `centerContext` treats a `member` role's empty lookup as a single-center-gym fallback: if the gym has exactly one **active** center, `allowedCenterIds` resolves to that sole center rather than `[]`, mirroring `resolveCenterId()`'s existing single-center fallback for writes. Only a gym with 2+ active centers and a member genuinely unassigned in `member_centers` resolves to `[]` (sees nothing).
- **Sole-center defaulting counts active centers only.** Every "no center given → use the gym's only center" fallback (`resolveCenterId()`, `resolveMemberCenters()`, `resolveStaffCenters()`, the `centerContext` member fallback) goes through `soleActiveCenterId()` in `infra/centerContext.ts`. An inactive center never makes a gym multi-center; staff-side explicit center choices still accept inactive centers, because the admin pickers list them. Website registration no longer involves centers at all (#757).
- **Gotcha — `calendar_events.center_id` can be NULL and must stay visible regardless of center scoping (#478).** `materializeScheduleRule` (`domain/scheduleEngine.ts`) sets a materialized occurrence's `center_id` from its Activity Type's `default_center_id`, which is nullable and commonly unset. A strict `ce.center_id = ?` / `ce.center_id IN (...)` filter excludes every such row for every member (NULL never matches), which is what made schedule-rule-generated sessions vanish from the Member Calendar while the (center-unfiltered) Admin Calendar kept showing them. `GET /me/schedule`, `GET /me/bookings`, `GET /me/upcoming`, and `GET /me/activity-history` (`api/src/api/me.ts`) all wrap their center filter as `(ce.center_id IS NULL OR ce.center_id = ?)` / `(ce.center_id IS NULL OR ce.center_id IN (...))` — a NULL `center_id` means the occurrence isn't tied to any one center, so it must stay visible under every center context rather than being silently dropped. Any new center-scoped query against `calendar_events` should follow the same pattern.

**API surface** (`api/src/api/impersonation.ts`, mounted at `/platform/impersonation`, no `tenantContext` middleware — uses `requireSuperadmin` only):
- `GET /platform/impersonation/targets?q=&gym_id=&type=` — returns active staff (non-member roles in `gym_memberships`, name LIKE) + **all active members** (`members.deleted_at IS NULL`, no Clerk requirement); excludes the caller. Returns `[{ id, name, email, type, role, status?, gymId }]` where member `id` is `"member:<members.id>"` and staff `id` is the Clerk user ID. `type` (`staff` | `member`) is an optional filter — omitted, the endpoint returns both (original behavior, used internally); the Admin app's `ImpersonationDialog` always passes `type=staff` and the Member app's dialog conceptually wants `type=member` (#504 — the Admin dialog previously received Members mixed into its results because this filter didn't exist). A staff row with neither `name` nor `email` set falls back to its raw Clerk `user_id` as `name` for matching purposes — the Admin dialog detects this (`name === id`) and shows a translated placeholder instead of the technical ID. **#592:** staff targets are `gym_memberships` `LEFT JOIN staff ON staff.gym_membership_id = gm.id`, so `name` prefers the Staff record's `first_name last_name` and the response carries `profile`/`staffId`; the Admin dialog shows the profile as the secondary label. A login with no Staff record (the gym owner's admin membership created at gym creation) is still listed under its membership name; a Staff record with no login is not — there is no identity to impersonate. A **pending invitation** (`status = 'invited'`, `invited_<ts>` placeholder) is listed but greyed out as "Invitation pending", and `POST /:targetId` refuses it with 400: `tenantContext` resolves the target in Clerk on every request, so a session on a placeholder would fail on its first call (this reverses the POST half of #358; invited staff still appear, per #342).
- `POST /platform/impersonation/stop` — accepts `{ impersonated_user_id, impersonated_user_name?, impersonated_role?, duration_seconds? }`, returns 204. Must be declared before `/:targetId` in the router to avoid being swallowed by the dynamic segment.
- `POST /platform/impersonation/:targetId` — body `{ targetType: 'member' | 'staff' }`. For members: queries `members` by `id`; returns `{ id: 'member:<N>', name, role: 'member', gym_id, gymIds }`. For staff: `gym_memberships` check only (not yourself); returns `{ id: <clerkUserId>, name, role, gym_id, gymIds }`. `gymIds` restricts the gym selector in the frontend.
- **Staff discovery/validation is fully database-driven (#364)** — `impersonation.ts` makes no Clerk lookups at all (`createClerkClient` was removed from this router). Staff targets and validation come exclusively from `gym_memberships`; a staff member missing from Clerk still appears and can be impersonated. Superadmins are excluded implicitly because they carry no `gym_memberships` row (see `TenantContext.gymMembershipId`'s doc comment) — impersonating a superadmin's Clerk user ID simply fails the `gym_memberships` lookup with "no membership in this gym", the same as any other non-member user ID. This is a deliberate simplification (confirmed on the ticket): no `superadmins` DB table was introduced, since superadmin status remains Clerk-only and out of scope for this router. `requireSuperadmin` (the gate on *calling* these endpoints) is unaffected and still reads Clerk's `publicMetadata.platform_role`.

**Frontend — Admin app** (`apps/admin/src/`):
- `ImpersonationContext.tsx` — `ImpersonationSession` includes `gymIds: string[]`; `ImpersonationProvider` wraps the app and rehydrates from `sessionStorage` on mount.
- `TopHeader.tsx` — renders an "Impersonate" button (superadmin only, not while impersonating) and an `ImpersonationDialog`; replaces `UserButton` with a locked avatar div during impersonation.
- `ImpersonationDialog.tsx` — modal with debounced search calling `GET /targets`, results list with member/staff type badges, confirms via `POST /:targetId` with `{ targetType }` body.
- `GymContext.tsx` — while impersonating, filters visible gyms to `session.gymIds` and overrides `role` with `effectiveRole`. **Derives this from `useImpersonation()`, never from `sessionStorage` during render (#596)**: `GymProvider` receives `children` as a stable prop, so a parent `setSession` does not re-render it — reading storage in render left the previous role's buttons on screen after start/stop until a reload. `ImpersonationContext` exposes `ready` (same flag as the Member app, #415); until it flips, `GymProvider` falls back to the synchronous `readStoredImpersonationSession()` so a reload mid-impersonation never flashes the superadmin's own role.

**Frontend — Member app** (`apps/member/src/`):
- `ImpersonationContext.tsx` — same session shape as admin app, plus a `ready` flag that flips true after the initial `sessionStorage` read so `AppContext` does not fetch `/me/profile` as a bare superadmin on refresh (#415).
- `AdminBar.tsx` — superadmin-only top strip rendered above the `TopBar` in the layout; shows "Impersonate" button (via `MemberImpersonationDialog`) when not impersonating, or `ImpersonationBanner` when impersonating.
- `MemberImpersonationDialog.tsx` — uses raw `fetch` (not `apiFetch`) since `gymId` may not yet be resolved in `AppContext` when impersonation starts; calls `GET /targets` and `POST /:targetId` with `{ targetType }` body. The member app filters results to `type === 'member'` — impersonating staff would leave `tenantCtx.role` as the staff role and `/me/profile` (`requireRole('member')`) would 403 (#415).
- `ImpersonationBanner.tsx` — amber top bar with stop button; calls `POST /platform/impersonation/stop`.
- `AppContext.tsx` — exposes `isSuperadmin` (via `useUser()` + Clerk `publicMetadata`); consumes `useImpersonation()` reactively (`ImpersonationProvider` wraps `AppProvider` in `layout.tsx`, not the other way around) so its data-loading effect re-runs — and resets `loading`/`isLinked` — whenever impersonation starts, stops, or switches target. Adds `x-impersonate-as` to every `/me/*` call it makes (`/me/gyms`, `/me/profile`, `/me/centers`, `/me/notifications/count`), not just the initial gyms call (#362 fix — previously only `/me/gyms` carried the header and the effect never re-ran on impersonation change, leaving Home's Next Booking/Membership stuck on `Loading...` under impersonation). **Gotcha — a bare superadmin has no member identity (#415).** `GET /me/profile` is `requireRole('member')`; without `x-impersonate-as`, `tenantContext` assigns the superadmin `role: 'admin'` and the handler returns 403. `AppContext` therefore skips `/me/profile` until an impersonation session is active, prefers `session.gymId` when picking the gym, and Home renders a Support-mode prompt instead of infinite "Loading…" widgets. After impersonation starts, `/me/profile` runs with the header and `isLinked` becomes true — member tiles then behave like a genuine member session.
- `apiClient.ts` — appends `x-impersonate-as` header to all API calls when an impersonation session is stored.

**Restrictions**: cannot impersonate yourself; staff targets must have a `gym_memberships` row for the selected gym (superadmins are excluded by having none). No new DB tables.

---

## Multi-tenancy Pattern

Every domain table has `gym_id CHAR(36) REFERENCES gyms`. Every query filters by it:

```sql
SELECT * FROM members WHERE gym_id = ? AND deleted_at IS NULL
```

The frontend sends `x-gym-id` on every request via `apiFetch()`, which reads it from `GymContext.activeGymId`. Global lookup tables (`benefit_types`, `charge_types`, `action_types`, `result_types`) are the deliberate exception — they have no `gym_id` and are seeded in their migrations. `payment_providers` (#636, migration 175) is the same exception with CRUD on top: platform-level configuration administered from Cordel, pointed at from the tenant side by `gyms.payment_provider_id` (NOT NULL). It is deliberately *not* the nullable-`gym_id` hybrid `themes` and `nutrition_library_items` use — a gym can own a theme, but never a payment provider — and the joined row is served only on the superadmin `/platform/gyms` reads, never on the member-readable `GET /gyms`, since a provider's status and default flag are platform state. The nutrition-library junction tables (`nutrition_library_item_categories`, `nutrition_library_item_qualities`, `nutrition_library_item_translations`) are the same exception one level down: they hang off `nutrition_library_items` by FK with `ON DELETE CASCADE`, so tenant isolation holds transitively and a `gym_id` of their own would only be able to disagree with the item's. `nutrition_library_items` is a hybrid: `gym_id` is nullable — `NULL` marks a Cordel-owned system item (global, read-only to gyms), a set value marks a gym-owned item that gym can edit (#350).

---

## Request Locale (`infra/locale.ts`, #643)

Translated **UI labels** live in each app's `locales/base/{en,es,ca}.json` and never reach the API. Translated **data** — content stored in the database — has to be resolved server-side, so the API needs to know the caller's language.

`getRequestLocale(req)` resolves it from, in order: the `x-locale` header (both apps send it from next-intl's `useLocale()`; both `/api/proxy` routes allowlist it), then `Accept-Language` (q-value ordered, with `es-ES` falling back to `es`), then the base locale. It never throws and never returns a value outside the allowlist.

- `SUPPORTED_LOCALES` (env, default `en,es,ca`) is the allowlist; entries that aren't well-formed BCP-47 tags are dropped at boot, and an allowlist that parses to nothing throws.
- `DEFAULT_LOCALE` (env, default `en`) is the base locale: the language base columns are written in, and the fallback whenever a locale has no translation row. Untranslated never renders blank.

**A Member's own default language (#1039, migration 220).** The above resolves one *request*; `members.preferred_locale VARCHAR(10) NULL` is what makes a member's choice survive the session, since next-intl's `NEXT_LOCALE` cookie lives in one browser. `NULL` means **no preference** — the request-level resolution above applies unchanged, which is what every member written before the migration means, so there is no backfill and no seeded default (writing today's default in would turn "follow the app's default" into a snapshot of it). There is deliberately **no CHECK**: the accepted set is `SUPPORTED_LOCALES`, i.e. configuration, so a deployment adding `fr` would otherwise store a locale the application accepts and the database refuses. The gate is the write path — `parseMemberPreferredLocaleInput()` (`domain/memberPreferredLocale.ts`) is the only place a request's value is judged, and it reuses `normalizeLocale()` rather than declaring a second list, so the **allowlist's own string** is stored and `es-ES` folds onto `es`. Its three answers are the partial-write ones: a field the request does not *mention* keeps the stored value (every client older than the ticket patches the phone alone), an explicit `null`/`''` **clears** the preference, and anything else is a 400 rather than a coercion. `PATCH /me/profile` therefore writes `preferred_locale = IF(?, ?, preferred_locale)` keyed on whether the field was supplied — a `COALESCE` on the value would make clearing it unpersistable — and both `/me/profile` reads narrow the column through `toMemberPreferredLocale()`, so a locale the deployment no longer configures reads as "no preference" without touching what is stored. Ownership is structural: both routes resolve the member from the authenticated context, so no member id is on the wire. The Members App applies it in exactly one place — see "Member app: the stored language preference" below.

**Translating a column.** Add a junction table keyed `(entity_id, locale)` holding the translated value — the same catalogue+junction shape used for categories and qualities — and leave the base column on the entity as the fallback and the uniqueness key. Resolve reads through a `COALESCE` subquery, which collapses to a plain column reference for the base locale so English pays nothing. Since #967 the rules are **one module**, `domain/nameTranslations.ts`: a `TranslatedNameConfig` names the junction table, its FK column and its subquery alias, and the module builds the `COALESCE` expression, the `?q=` clause that matches any language, the `JSON_OBJECTAGG` of the stored names, and the replace-all / copy / validate writes over it. An entity adds a thin wrapper (`domain/nutritionLibrary.ts` for foods, `domain/exerciseTranslations.ts` for exercises) so no query carries the configuration around with it, and a second copy of the SQL — which is how two surfaces come to resolve one name two ways — cannot appear. The locale is interpolated into that SQL rather than parameterised, so the helper embeds **the matching `SUPPORTED_LOCALES` entry**, not the locale it was handed — the argument is compared against the allowlist and otherwise unused, and anything with no match resolves to the base column. Request bytes therefore never reach the query text, regardless of what upstream validation did.

Expose the localized value as a **separate field** (`display_name`) rather than overwriting the base one: edit forms submit the base value back, so a form prefilled from a translation would overwrite the original on save. A **read-only consumer** surface is the exception that proves the rule — it has no form, so it takes the localized value in the field it already reads (`item_name`, `exercise_name`). Project the stored map (`translations`) on **every** read of the entity, list reads included: `⋮ → Edit` seeds its form from the row the page already holds (#800), so a form seeded without the translations would submit an empty replace-all set and clear them on the first save. On the way in, a payload that *omits* `translations` leaves the stored rows alone and an explicit `{}` clears them — the same distinction every replace-all section `PUT` in the codebase draws.

**Translated exercise names (#967, migration 210).** `exercise_translations` (`exercise_id` + `locale` PK, `name VARCHAR(200)` in `exercises.name`'s own charset/collation, FK CASCADE, `KEY (locale, name)`) holds one row per translated locale, for **both** kinds of exercise — a Base Exercise (`gym_id IS NULL`) and a gym's own — because they are one table and one contract, so nothing downstream branches on where an exercise came from. `exercises.name` stays the base value, the routers' duplicate check and the fallback. `domain/exerciseTranslations.ts` is the wrapper: `localizedExerciseNameSql/Expr`, `exerciseNameSearchSql` (base name **or** any stored translation, so `Press de Banca` finds `Bench Press` whatever the reader's language), `exerciseTranslationsExpr`, `parse`/`replace`/`copy`. Both routers accept `translations` on `POST`/`PUT` and return `name` + `display_name` + `translations`; `GET /exercises/locales` and `GET /platform/exercises/lookups` carry the language list, so the shared editor renders one Name input per locale without declaring one. Every consumer read is localized in place — the plan tree (`planTreeSelect(locale)`, shared by the staff, gym-template and member reads), workout templates and their platform twin, training plans, training plan templates, exercise logs, `GET /me/exercise-logs` and the Recycle Bin. Duplicate, Clone and `POST /exercises/import` **copy** the translations (which is how an imported Base Exercise reads in a member's own language from day one); a *re-import* refreshes media only and never a translation the gym has corrected. Nothing is seeded or backfilled: an existing exercise keeps its one name and a translation is never invented for it.

---

## Database Conventions

- **Migrations**: Knex JS files in `infra/migrations/`. Numbered sequentially (`001_` … `071_`). Run with `npm run db:migrate`. ⚠️ MySQL DDL is **non-transactional** — a failed migration leaves partial state, so keep migrations small and re-runnable (guard `ALTER`s with `hasColumn`/information_schema checks — see `030_gym_theme.js`).
- **Primary keys**: auto-increment `INT UNSIGNED` for domain tables, `CHAR(36)` UUID for `gyms` (tenant root, `DEFAULT (UUID())`).
- **Timestamps**: `DATETIME`, always UTC (the mysql2 pool uses `timezone: 'Z'`; use `UTC_TIMESTAMP()` in SQL, never `NOW()`).
- **Indexed text columns**: `VARCHAR(n)`, not `TEXT` (MySQL cannot index TEXT without a prefix length).
- **Soft deletes**: Add `deleted_at DATETIME` and filter `WHERE deleted_at IS NULL`. Used for members, workout templates (which also set `status='deleted'`), and exercises (`status='deleted'` + `deleted_at`; the `(gym_id, name)` unique index was dropped so a deleted name can be reused — uniqueness among non-deleted rows is enforced in the router). Consider for other user-facing entities.
- **Cascade**: FK `ON DELETE CASCADE` when the child has no meaning without the parent. Use `ON DELETE SET NULL` when the reference is optional.
- **Duplicates**: unique-key violations surface as `err.code === 'ER_DUP_ENTRY'` (errno 1062) → return 409.
- **Statuses**: `VARCHAR` + a **named** CHECK constraint (so the allowed set can evolve without an `ALTER TYPE` dance).
- **No partial/filtered indexes** in MySQL: for "unique among non-deleted/active rows" use a generated column + unique index.
- **Inserts**: no `RETURNING` in MySQL — insert, then `SELECT` by `insertId` (returned by the `db.query` helper). Upserts use `INSERT ... AS new ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE col = new.col`; "insert if absent" uses `INSERT IGNORE`.
- **Transactions**: multi-statement writes use `db.transaction(async (tx) => { … })` — never `BEGIN`/`COMMIT` through `db.query` (pooled connections).

### `gym_memberships` (the "users in a gym" table)

Beyond `user_id`, `gym_id`, `role`, this table now carries invitation state (migrations 031–034):
- `status` — `active | invited` (named CHECK).
- `email` — set for invited rows before a Clerk user exists; cleared to `NULL` on link.
- `name` — display name (editable for invited rows).
- `invitation_id` — the Clerk invitation id, used to revoke on removal.

Invited rows use a placeholder `user_id` of the form `invited_<timestamp>` until the invitee signs in and `/staff/link` (or the Clerk `user.created` webhook) materializes the real Clerk `user_id`. Since #592 every non-member row is expected to be owned by a Staff record via `staff.gym_membership_id` (FK `ON DELETE SET NULL`); the exception is the gym owner's admin row inserted at gym creation.

### `members` (portal-invite tracking, migration 051)

`members.invitation_id` (nullable, migration 051) mirrors `gym_memberships.invitation_id` — the pending Clerk invitation id, used to revoke on removal or explicit un-invite. Unlike `gym_memberships`, there's no `status` column: a member row is always "real" (it's the billing/plan record) independent of portal access, so portal state is derived as `clerk_user_id IS NULL AND invitation_id IS NOT NULL` (invited) vs `clerk_user_id` set (linked) vs neither (never invited). See "Member invite + auto-link flow" below.

### `user_memberships` — billing columns (migration 111)

Two nullable columns added for recurring MIT billing:
- `next_billing_date DATE NULL` — set by the payment webhook on the first successful charge to the first `starts_at + n·cadence` strictly after UTC today (#790, `stampFirstNextBillingDate()` in `domain/nextBillingDateStamp.ts` — so a back-dated assignment is never charged the cycles that elapsed before it paid); advanced by the billing run after each subsequent charge; walked forward the same way when a transition back to `active` finds it in the past (`rollStaleNextBillingDateForward()` — a pause is not a debt).
- `last_billed_at DATETIME NULL` — stamped by the billing run on success.

**Dunning state (#785, migration 194)** — two more columns the nightly run owns:
- `failed_attempts INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` — consecutive provider rejections of the cycle `next_billing_date` names. `2` is the pause threshold (`MAX_BILLING_RUN_ATTEMPTS`); `domain/billingDunning.ts` clamps whatever it reads, because `user_memberships` carries no CHECK.
- `last_failed_at DATETIME NULL` — when the last rejection was recorded, and the run-**day** marker the escalation reads (`DATE(last_failed_at) = UTC_DATE()`).

Both are **run state, not editable fields**: they are written by the rejection branch of `POST /billing/run` and cleared by every path where money arrives or the assignment resumes — the run's own success and waived branches, the payment webhook's `completed` branch, `clearDunningState()` behind the two staff payment actions, and a transition to `active` (`/reactivate` or a direct `PUT` status flip, so a resumed assignment gets the full two attempts again). No route accepts them as input. They are not part of the Assigned Plan snapshot (`has_billing_snapshot`) for migration 192's reason, and because the Assigned Plan reads select `um.*` they do appear in list/detail responses and in `recordAudit` snapshots — a diff of two audit entries around a rejection can therefore differ by a field no human touched.

### `billing_run_log` (migration 111, reshaped by migration 193)

Not a per-tenant table — intentional exception to the `gym_id` convention, because it tracks a platform-wide job rather than tenant data.

Migration 111 created it as a single-row singleton (id=1, CHECK id=1, `last_run_at DATETIME NULL`) holding the 23-hour rate limit on `POST /billing/run`. **#780 (migration 193) turns it into a history**: one row per run, `id INT UNSIGNED AUTO_INCREMENT`, plus

| column | meaning |
|---|---|
| `run_date DATE` | the UTC calendar date the guard counts |
| `status VARCHAR(20)` | `in_progress` \| `completed` \| `failed`, bounded by `chk_billing_run_log_status` |
| `started_at DATETIME` | when the run claimed the row |
| `finished_at DATETIME NULL` | NULL while in progress |
| `processed` / `succeeded` / `failed` / `waived` | `INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` — the counters the endpoint returns |

Indexed on `(run_date, status)` and `(status, started_at)`, which is exactly the guard's read ("anything in progress, or on the last two dates"). A new run status goes in **two** places: `RunLogStatus` in `domain/runGuard.ts` and that CHECK.

The rule the history buys is **at most one *completed* run per UTC date** — see the endpoint's row in the table above for what each outcome answers. `infra/run-log.ts` is the only writer: `claimRun()` reads and inserts inside one short transaction with `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` (so two simultaneous calls cannot both decide the table is free) and `finishRun()` closes the row, from the success path *and* the `catch`. The transaction covers the claim only, never the run — a run holds no row lock while it charges cards.

`billing_events.event_type` CHECK extended with `recurring_payment` and `failed_billing`. `payment_requests.source` CHECK extended with `billing_run`. Migration 185 (#635 stage 11) adds `waived_billing` — a cycle the assignment's Free Period, Bonus Duration or an applied Promotion's free month covered, recorded at amount 0 with no Payment Transaction behind it, so `deriveBillingEventStatus` reads it as `recorded` (informational, never payment-actionable). A new event type goes in **two** places: the CHECK and whatever writes it; `billing.ts` is the only writer of this one.

### `recurring_booking_run_log` (#647 stage 4, migration 170)

The same shape as `billing_run_log`, and reshaped with it by migration 193 (#780) into a history — same `run_date` / `status` / `started_at` / `finished_at` / `chk_…_status` columns, same two indexes, same `infra/run-log.ts` writer, and its own counters (`processed`, `created`, `skipped`, `failed`, `notified`: what the endpoint returns). Also not per-tenant — the second deliberate exception to the `gym_id` convention, and for the same reason.

Migration 170 created it as a `last_run_at` singleton for the 23-hour guard on `POST /recurring-bookings/run`, stamped at the *start* of a run. That had the same two defects as billing's (a cron 22.5 h after a late one was refused; a crashed run held the lock for the day), so the guard is now the same calendar rule. What stays different is *why* it exists: a second run here would not double-book anyone — the job is idempotent by construction — so the guard stops two runs walking the same members at once, which is the `in_progress` half of it. A run scoped by `gym_id`/`member_id` is a manual re-run and claims nothing.

Migration 170 also rewrites `chk_member_notifications_type` (migration 087) to add `recurring_booking_skipped`, and — while the constraint is being re-added anyway — `shared_training_approved` / `shared_training_rejected`, which `infra/notifications.ts` has emitted since #575 without ever being added to the CHECK, so those inserts were failing invisibly (`sendNotification()` is fire-and-forget and only `console.error`s). **Adding a notification type now means editing that CHECK**, not just the `NotificationType` union.

### Billing Event payment actions (#640, migration 165)

A **Billing Event** (`billing_events`) is the charge; a **Payment Transaction** (`payment_requests` pointing at it through `billing_event_id`) is an attempt to settle it. An event carries 0..N transactions, and its **status is the status of the latest one** — `domain/billingEventStatus.ts` (pure, unit-tested) maps `completed`→`paid`, `failed`/`expired`→`failed`, `pending`→`pending`, and falls back to the `event_type` mapping (`recurring_payment`/`payment_recorded`→`paid`, `failed_billing`→`failed`, everything else→`recorded`) for an event with no transaction at all. Both the list endpoint and the Details endpoint derive from that one module, never inline.

Migration 165 adds `modified_at` + `modified_by_user_id` to `billing_events` and `payment_requests`, and `failure_code` / `failure_message` / `notes` / `attempt` to `payment_requests`; `chk_payment_requests_source` widens to `admin|customer|billing_run|retry|manual`. The actor columns are Clerk user ids (matching `billing_events.actor_user_id`, not the `modified_by_membership_id` FK the newer catalog tables use), resolved for display through `gym_memberships.name` — the same lookup the audit registry's `gym_user` entity uses.

Three routes on `paymentsRouter`, all under the existing PAYMENTS gates:
- `GET /payments/billing-events/:id` — read-only Details (§2 of the issue): id, member, plan, amount, currency, derived status, event/charge type, source, Created At/By, Modified At/By, Next Payment, failure reason (transaction-level wins over the ledger row's `notes`), receipt number, and the `can_retry` / `can_record_manual_payment` / `can_issue_receipt` (#787) flags the UI keys its actions on.
- `POST /payments/billing-events/:id/retry` — Retry Payment. Creates a **new transaction against the same event** (never a second ledger row), fires `executeRecurring`, and on rejection fires once more. Two rejections pause the assigned plan via `recordStatusChange`, so the pause is explicable from the ledger like any other transition.
- `POST /payments/billing-events/:id/manual-payment` — the front-desk settlement the issue first called "Flag as Paid". Records a completed transaction (`source='manual'`, `provider='manual'`), no provider call.

Invariants both actions share (`domain/billingEventPayments.ts`): the event must be `failed`, must hang off a membership (`payment_requests.user_membership_id` is NOT NULL) and must have a positive amount; an event that already has a **completed** transaction returns 409, which is also what stops one charge advancing the schedule twice. A settled charge advances `next_billing_date`/`last_billed_at` exactly as the nightly run does, and only while the membership is still waiting on that charge (`next_billing_date <= today`). `audit_logs` carries the manual intervention — actions `retry_payment` and `manual_payment` with previous/new status and the per-attempt results.

### Failed payments awaiting action (#779)

A staff-facing to-do, not a statistic: a Billing Event is **awaiting action** when its derived status is `failed` (`isAwaitingAction()` in `domain/billingEventStatus.ts`) — the charge was rejected or expired and neither Retry Payment nor Manual payment has since appended a `completed` transaction to it. The queue is therefore exactly the Billing Events list filtered by `status=failed`; there is no status column and no second definition.

- `GET /payments/billing-events/attention` → `{ count, oldest_created_at }`, gated by PAYMENTS read access like the rest of `paymentsRouter`. `loadFailedPaymentsAttention()` groups by `(event_type, latest transaction status)` and maps the groups through the shared predicate; its WHERE only narrows candidates to rows that *can* derive to `failed` (a `failed_billing` row, or one with a failed/expired transaction), so settled history is never scanned.
- The Payments Dashboard summary carries the same pair as `awaiting_action_count` / `awaiting_action_oldest_at` — all-time, not bounded by the month windows.
- `GET /payments/billing-events` takes `order=asc|desc` (default `desc`); `?status=failed&order=asc` is the work queue both the sidebar badge (`NavGroup`'s `badge`, polled every 60 s by `useFailedPaymentsAttention()` only while Payments → Billing Events is visible) and the dashboard card link to.
- It counts **events, not memberships**, so the badge matches the row count of the list it opens; since #785 one assignment contributes at most two before the run pauses it. A rejected *first* payment writes no Billing Event (the webhook inserts `payment_recorded` only on `completed`) and is not counted. The member is not notified.

### Receipts (#114, widened by #787)

A receipt is a *factura simplificada* PDF, numbered from a gapless per-gym, per-year sequence (`receipt_sequences`, migration 114) and stamped onto the Billing Event as `receipt_number` + `receipt_issued_at`. The PDF itself is generated on demand from the row (`lib/receipt-pdf.ts`) — nothing is stored.

**Which events may carry one** is `isReceiptableEvent()` in `domain/billingEventStatus.ts`, and nothing else re-derives it. The rule is *money actually received*: the event type must be one that records a payment (`payment_recorded`, `recurring_payment`, `failed_billing`) **and** its derived status must be `paid`. That single predicate gives all three real cases without a special branch — a front-desk cash `payment_recorded` (no transaction, so the event type answers), the nightly run's settled `recurring_payment`, and a `failed_billing` that the staff's Retry or Manual payment later settled (those actions deliberately never append a second ledger row, #640, so the money sits on the rejected event and there is nothing else to issue against). A `waived_billing`, an `adjustment`, a `charge_created`, a `status_changed`, and any of the three above whose latest transaction is `failed`/`expired`/`pending`, are refused with a `400` naming the reason. Before #787 the rule was `event_type === 'payment_recorded'`, so **no automatically charged fee could ever be receipted** — once a gym bills monthly that is every payment except the first.

**Who allocates a number.** Exactly two callers, both through `issueReceiptNumber()` (`domain/receiptNumbers.ts`), which owns the sequence arithmetic and is idempotent: it reads the event `FOR UPDATE` and returns the existing number rather than spending the next one, so a retried request, a concurrent click and a re-run all produce one receipt for one payment.
- `POST /payments/:id/receipt` — staff, on demand. Allocates on first call, then returns the PDF. `GET /payments/:id/receipt` and `GET /me/receipts/:billingEventId` are pure reads: they `404` until a number exists and need no eligibility check of their own, because a number can only exist if the predicate allowed it.
- `POST /billing/run` — automatically, for each charge it settles, so the member finds the receipt on their own billing history without a staff member having to click first. Deliberately **after** the charge transaction commits, in one of its own, and with the error swallowed and logged: that transaction records money the provider has already taken, and a failure inside it rolls the record back while the charge stands. A receipt that fails to auto-issue is still issuable on demand, so it is never worth risking the charge record for. The run reports `receipts_issued` alongside its other counters; it is not a run-log column and never turns a run red.

`GET /members` derives `payment_status` from the latest transaction of **any** membership covering the member (joined through `user_membership_members`), not only from transactions raised against them personally: a two-person or family Membership bills once, against its owner, so every covered member has to follow that charge's outcome.

### Stored card replacement (#788, migration 195)

A member's card lives in `payment_methods` (one row per member and gym, `payment_token` + `sequence_id` from Monei). Until #788 nothing wrote that row after the first customer-initiated payment's webhook: a member whose card expired had no way to replace it except the **Start payment** button on My Membership, which raises a full membership fee — so updating a card cost a whole fee, and only worked on a cycle that happened to owe one.

**Replacing a card is a zero-amount verification, never a charge.** `PaymentProvider.createCardVerificationRequest()` takes **no amount at all**, so no caller can charge through this path; the Monei adapter sends `amount: 0`, `transactionType: 'VERIF'` and `generatePaymentToken: true` (MONEI's documented tokenisation, docs.monei.com/guides/save-payment-method), and the `completed` webhook carries the same `paymentToken`/`sequenceId` a first real payment does — which is why one upsert serves both. Some acquirers answer a zero-amount verification with a small authorisation they then void; that is the acquirer's behaviour, not a second code path, and confirming it against the live account is a go-to-production item.

**The row it writes is not a financial row.** The hosted page loads by `page_token` and the webhook reconciles by `provider_order`, both columns of `payment_requests`, so a verification needs a row there — with `source = 'card_update'` (migration 195 widens `chk_payment_requests_source` for the fourth time), `amount = 0.00` and `charge_type_id` **NULL** (the same migration makes the column nullable: a verification bills nothing, and inventing a `card_verification` charge type would put a non-charge in the cash-payment picker and in charge-type reporting). Three surfaces exclude it by that value — `GET /members`' derived `payment_status`, the staff `GET /payment-requests` list and the member's own `GET /me/payment-requests` — so a pending verification can never read as an unpaid fee, and the member app's return page cannot mistake one for a payment. `domain/storedCards.ts` owns the constant and the rules (pure, unit-tested); `api/card-updates.ts` owns the provider call, the insert and the reads, shared by the staff router and `/me` so the two surfaces cannot drift.

**Removal is allowed only once nothing is scheduled to be charged** (`cardRemovalBlock()`): while any of the member's assignments is `active` or `paused` with a `next_billing_date`, `DELETE /me/payment-method` answers `409 billable_membership` and the app offers **Replace card** instead. The reason is that the nightly run skips an assignment with no `payment_methods` row *silently* — it writes nothing at all — so a member under a live contract could stop paying with nobody the wiser; the surfaces that would make that visible to the staff (#779) do not exist yet. Replacing is never blocked, in any status, and a member who wants to stop paying cancels the membership. Once #779 lands, this is the decision to revisit.

**Where the member sees it.** My Membership gains a *Payment method* section (brand, last four, when the card on file was stored — `COALESCE(payment_methods.updated_at, created_at)`) with **Replace card** / **Add card** and, when allowed, **Remove card**. There is no consent modal on that side: the hosted page's own consent checkbox is what authorises the future recurring charges, and it names them. `PAYMENT_OK_URL`/`PAYMENT_KO_URL` carry `purpose=card_update` back, so the member app's payment return page polls `GET /me/payment-method`'s `last_update` instead of the payment history a verification deliberately never joins. Staff see the same card read-only in Members → Payments, beside a **Send replace-card link** action.

### Assigned Plans — expanded detail + Billing Events (#511 stage 3, migration 150)

`GET /user-memberships/:id` is now the "expanded card" endpoint for an Assigned Plan (mirrors `enrichPlan()`'s pattern in `membership-plans.ts` — everything the Details modal needs in one call, rather than several separate per-section requests). Beyond the plain `user_memberships` row + audit metadata (stage 2), it also embeds:
- `members` — every covered Member (owner + additional, #374), via the existing `MEMBERS_SELECT`.
- `billing_policy` — the plan's `billing_policies` row (the Billing frequency `recurring_billing_*` + auto-renew; the initial/service interval pairs were retired by migration 189, #635 stage 13).
- `promotions` — applied promotions with their stage-2 snapshot overlay, via the same `fetchAppliedPromotions()` also used by `GET /:id/promotions`.
- `additional_services` — the Additional Periodic Services attached to the assignment (#631, below).
- `billing_events` — the Billing Events view (below).
- `example_timeline` — the **Membership Fee Simulation** (#924 stage 3, below).
- `billing_event_simulation` — the **Billing Event Forecast** (#924 stage 4, below).

`GET /user-memberships/:id/billing-events` exposes that last section on its own (a lighter, single-section fetch), computed by the same `computeBillingEventsView()` the `:id` response embeds it from.

**Billing Events range rule** (per the #511 issue thread's Q2 answer, implemented in `domain/assignedPlanBillingEvents.ts` — pure, unit-tested, no DB dependency): show all billing events affected by a promotion applied to the plan, plus the events covering the following two calendar months after the last one; with no applicable promotion, the next two calendar months from the plan's `starts_at`. Clamped to `ends_at` when the plan ends first.
- The view queries the real, persisted `billing_events` ledger and only ever tags (`promotion_affected: true/false`) and filters it — amounts are never recomputed, preserving historical values.
- #511 also gave a **`draft`** plan a non-persisted projection (`projected: true`), since a draft had no ledger yet. #786 retired the `draft` status, and #854 removed the projection and its `projected` flag (view-level and per event) with it. #1108 stage 1 brought the **status** back without bringing the projection back: a Draft's ledger is genuinely empty, and what a Draft would bill is the card's existing Billing Event Forecast (`assignmentBillingEventSimulation.ts`, #924 stage 4), which a Draft reaches because `draft` is in `SIMULATED_STATUSES` — not a second, status-specific projection inside this view.

`user_membership_promotions.revoked_at` (nullable `DATETIME`, migration 150) is stamped when a promotion is revoked (`DELETE /user-memberships/:id/promotions/:promotionId`) — together with `applied_at`, it defines the `[applied_at, revoked_at]` window used to tag whether a given billing event (or projected cycle) was affected by that promotion, independent of the row's `status`. Migration 150 also adds a `billing_events (user_membership_id, created_at)` composite index, since the range calculation reads every ledger row for one membership ordered by `created_at`.

The Close action's unused-value check warns on a pending `next_billing_date`. #511 stage 3 also warned about a `session_count` allowance with sessions left in its window; #635 stage 4 (part 2) retired Included Services, so that half is gone — a Plan's Session Benefits are billed up front rather than consumed per booking, so closing forfeits nothing.

### Draft Assigned Plans (#1108 stage 1, migration 227)

Assigning a Membership Plan no longer makes it the member's membership. All three insert
paths write `ASSIGNMENT_CREATION_STATUS` — **`draft`** — and `POST /user-memberships/:id/activate`
is the one `draft → active` transition; `PUT /user-memberships/:id` refuses that flip and
names the route, the way a cancellation is refused and routed to `DELETE`. Migration 227
widens `user_memberships_status_check` to five values, which is the widening migration 198
(#786) said a payment gate before activation would need. `awaiting_payment` stays retired:
#1108's second pre-activation state is *Pending Payment*, and it arrives with stage 2's
**Save & Pay** rather than as a value nothing can write for a second time.

| Question | Answer, and where it lives |
|---|---|
| Is a Draft the member's plan? | **No.** `draft` is outside `LIVE_ASSIGNMENT_STATUSES` (`domain/oneActivePlan.ts`), so one Active plan plus one Draft replacement is a legal state — which is what makes a replacement configurable before it is committed. #956's check, its `confirm` and `supersedeLiveAssignments()` therefore run in the activate transaction rather than in the four insert paths. Migration 213's UNIQUE index is untouched (`active_member_key` is `IF(status = 'active', member_id, NULL)`) and still backstops the commit, because the `draft → active` UPDATE populates that column. |
| Does it bill or book? | **No.** `api/src/api/billing.ts` and `api/src/api/activity-eligibility.ts` both read `um.status = 'active'`. A Draft not being bookable *is* a behaviour change — an unpaid assignment was bookable from creation under #786. |
| Is it editable? | **Fully** (§2–§4). `draft` is in `SNAPSHOT_EDITABLE_STATUSES` (every snapshot section), `ATTACHABLE_STATUSES` (Additional Products) and `CLOSEABLE_FROM`; Promotions were never status-gated. |
| Is it projected? | **Yes** — `SIMULATED_STATUSES` and the Member card's own `LIVE_STATUSES` include it, so the Assigned Plan card's existing **Billing Event Forecast** answers what committing it would bill. #1108 Q3's answer is to read it there rather than grow a fourth name beside *Membership Fee Simulation*, *Billing Event Forecast* and the **Billing Events** ledger. |
| Where is it invisible? | `latestEnrollmentStatusSql()` (it reads the *latest* row, so a Draft would overwrite a currently Active member's enrollment status and drop them out of the Nutrition Dashboard's count), `countsAsRecentMembership()` (#927's window — a Draft's `starts_at` is by definition inside it), the Financials dashboard's assigned-plan join, and the member's own reads through `MEMBER_CURRENT_ASSIGNMENT_FILTER` beside `MEMBER_CURRENT_ASSIGNMENT_ORDER` — both halves in both callers, because `FIELD()` answers 0 for an unlisted status and would sort a Draft *first*. |
| What if nobody commits it? | It sits there (Q1a — no expiry sweep). Staff discard it with `POST /:id/close`, which is warning-free because a Draft has never had a `next_billing_date`. |

Admin: `lifecycle_status` reports `draft` (a stored status outranks the date-aware part of
`LIFECYCLE_STATUS_SQL`, so a future-dated Draft reads `draft` and not `pending`), the
Assigned Plans filter offers it, `StatusBadge` already had the tone, and the Assigned Plan
card offers `⋮ → Activate` — which raises the shared `ReplacePlanDialog` from the 409, since
that is where the conflict comes from now. The gate is
`api/src/test/draft-assignment-status.unit.test.ts`.

### Membership Fee Simulation — the Assigned Plan's Example Timeline (#924 stage 3 — no migration)

`GET /user-memberships/:id/example-timeline`, also embedded as `example_timeline` on `GET /user-memberships/:id`, is the Membership Plan card's **Example Timeline** (#818) for a contract that really exists: one row per billing period, with Period / Dates / Status / Billing. Read-only, recomputed on every request, persisted nowhere, charges nothing.

§7 of #924 forbids the shortcut — *"the simulation should not implement a separate calculation engine"* — so the projection is shared and each entity owns only an adapter, exactly as #922 arranged the Billing Event Simulation:

- `api/src/domain/exampleTimeline.ts` — the walk: period stepping over a cadence, the horizon (`TRAILING_REGULAR_PERIODS = 2` regular periods after everything configured has run out), the `MAX_TIMELINE_PERIODS = 60` cap and the open-ended final row. It prices nothing and classifies nothing.
- `api/src/domain/planExampleTimeline.ts` — the Plan adapter (#818): one price for every period, each row classified by `classifyPlanDurationPeriod()`.
- `api/src/domain/assignmentExampleTimeline.ts` — the assignment adapter: every row priced by `resolveMembershipFee()`, the one implementation the nightly run, `GET /me/membership` and every staff surface already read (#635 stage 12), so the table cannot advertise a charge the run does not make in either direction.
- `api/src/api/assigned-plan-fee-timeline.ts` — the reads. It hands the adapter the very `MembershipFeeContext` `priceMembershipFeeOn()` builds (`membershipFeeContextFor()` in `membership-fee-pricing.ts`, extracted beside `negotiatedFeeLapsed()` and `standingFeePromotions()`), so the assignment's frozen Billing & Duration, its standing Promotion applications and its Personal Membership Fee Benefit (#772) are all in the numbers by construction.

What the assignment contributes that a Plan preview cannot: real dates counted from its own `starts_at` in periods of its own cadence (`ASSIGNMENT_CADENCE`, #892); its applied Promotions, which outrank the Plan's durations wherever they govern a date (#635's Q2 answer); and a negotiated fee that lapses (`discount_expires_at`), resolved as the two numbers `regularMembershipFee()` can answer and picked per period by the same `negotiatedFeeLapsed()` predicate the nightly run asks.

Two rules are load-bearing:

- **The Status is reported, never re-derived.** `resolveMembershipFee()` returns a `periodStatus` (`free_plan` … `pay_regular`, or `free_promotion` … when a Promotion governs), because the precedence between the two is its decision alone. A caller classifying the period itself would label a cycle `free_plan` that the governing Promotion is charging the regular price for.
- **Rows start at the period containing today**, while the period *numbers* count from `starts_at`: an assignment three years old has three years of elapsed cycles and the section answers what will be charged from here on. An assignment with no billing frequency at all reports `available: false` with a reason, exactly as a Plan with no `billing_policies` row does.

Admin: `components/assignedPlan/AssignedPlanExpandedRow.tsx` renders `MEMBERSHIP FEE SIMULATION` between the applied Promotions and the Additional Periodic Services (§11's order), through the shared `components/ExampleTimeline.tsx` — the same table the Plan and Promotion cards use. The formatting rules are shared too (`apps/admin/src/lib/exampleTimeline.ts`: the Billing cell and the three row tones, which `plans/planProfile.ts` now delegates to); the page contributes only labels, and unlike the Plan card's — which may only say *(benefit)* — these may say *(promotion)*, because an Assigned Plan really can be inside one.

### Billing Event Forecast — the Assigned Plan's Billing Event Simulation (#924 stage 4 — no migration)

`GET /user-memberships/:id/billing-event-simulation`, also embedded as `billing_event_simulation` on `GET /user-memberships/:id`, is the Membership Plan card's **Billing Event Simulation** (#915) for a contract that really exists: one group per billing *date*, listing every line that falls on it — the Membership Fee plus each Product and Additional Periodic Service the assignment carries — with a per-date total. Read-only, recomputed on every request, persisted nowhere, charges nothing.

It answers a different question from the Membership Fee Simulation above it on the same card, and neither may grow into the other: that one is one row per billing **period** and is about the fee alone, this one is one group per billing **date** and lists every line.

§8 forbids the shortcut — *"do not implement a separate simulation engine for Assigned Plans"* — so this is the **third adapter** over the shared projection, beside the Plan's and the Promotion's (#922):

- `api/src/domain/billingEventSimulation.ts` — the result shape, the `SIMULATED_CYCLES = 2` horizon floor and the group-by-date re-grouping. Since stage 4 the grouping also takes an optional `from`, the earliest date a group may carry.
- `api/src/domain/assignmentBillingEventSimulation.ts` — the assignment adapter. Unlike the other two it does not *invent* an assignment: it is handed the real one, the very `SimulationAssignment` the Member-level Billing Simulation (#629) is built from, so every snapshot rule (#635 §13–§17), every standing Promotion and every Additional Periodic Service is already in it and there is nothing here to disagree with billing about.
- `api/src/api/assigned-plan-billing-forecast.ts` — the reads, which are only `loadSimulationAssignments()` scoped to one assignment (`SimulationScope`, extracted from `computeMemberBillingSimulation()` in `billing-simulation.ts`), so the two surfaces cannot read the configuration differently.

The adapter adds exactly two things, both about *where the projection runs* and neither about what anything costs:

- **The horizon is anchored on today** (`horizonFrom` on `BillingSimulationInput`). The engine's streams start at the contract's real `starts_at`, so without it both the two-cycle floor and the engine's 36-month safety cap would be measured from a 2023 signup and the forecast would be empty. The floor is counted from each stream's first occurrence at or after that anchor, which for a hypothetical assignment (every stream of a Plan or Promotion preview starts today) is the same date as before — the two existing adapters are unaffected.
- **The dates already behind us are dropped** (`from`). They are what the assignment has been charged on, and the **Billing Events** section two sections below is the ledger of those. The engine still walks the contract from its own `starts_at`, because that is what decides which period a future date falls in and therefore what it costs.

Everything else is the engine's: the dates, the amounts, which benefit applies and which period status a cycle has. Two consequences worth stating:

- **The amounts are the ones billed.** Unlike the Plan and Promotion adapters, which gross a catalogue price up before handing it over, this projection is denominated in the assignment's own frozen figures — the Membership Fee `priceMembershipFeeOn()` prices each cycle from, and each benefit line's frozen `unit_price` — which is the same number the Membership Fee Simulation above quotes and the same one the nightly run charges. No tax arithmetic happens in the projection or in the page (#817).
- **No line is flagged Mandatory.** That is a Membership Plan's question (#832/#893): the flag says a catalogue item must be part of every Plan benefit section. An assignment bills what it was agreed with, frozen, so a live flag is never claimed about a frozen line.

An assignment that bills nothing (no fee, no cadence, no benefits), one whose every remaining date is behind us (an `ends_at` already passed), and one that is `cancelled`/`expired` all report `available: false` with a reason, so the card says so rather than rendering an empty table.

Admin: `AssignedPlanExpandedRow.tsx` renders `BILLING EVENT FORECAST` below the Membership Fee Simulation and above the Additional Periodic Services, through the shared `components/BillingEventSimulation.tsx` — the same component the Plan and Promotion cards render. The page contributes only its labels (resolved in the `assigned_plans_page` namespace, where `simulation_example_note` reads *Billing events from {date}* rather than the Plan card's *Example: a member starting on…*) and its date formatter.

### Additional Periodic Services — *Additional Products* in the UI (#631, migration 164; #957)

`user_membership_services` attaches recurring Products (`products`) directly to an Assigned Plan (`user_memberships`) — independent of the Membership Plan's included benefits and of Promotion benefits (#631 §7), which keep their own tables. The Product stays the source of truth for the name, price and `billing_frequency` (read live on every request, never copied onto the attachment), so the table stores only the assignment-specific facts: `product_id`, `quantity`, `starts_at` and `ends_at`. `product_id` has no `ON DELETE CASCADE` (the same reasoning migration 130 used for the charge-benefit snapshot, retired in stage 4): items are only ever soft-deleted, and an attached service must survive one being retired — a service whose item was later retired keeps billing and is flagged `product_retired` on read. A `VIRTUAL` generated column (`open_service_key`, non-NULL only while `ends_at IS NULL`) carries a unique key, so two concurrent POSTs can't both slip past the overlap check and leave the same item attached twice.

Routes (mounted at `/user-memberships/:id/services` in `app.ts` — three path segments, so `userMembershipsRouter`'s own `/:id` never matches — behind the same `PAYMENTS` gate and `payments.transactions` flag as the rest of Assigned Plans):
- `GET /user-memberships/:id/services` — the attached services, oldest window first. Also embedded in `GET /user-memberships/:id` as `additional_services`.
- `POST /user-memberships/:id/services` — attaches one. Only items `classifyProduct()` (#550) classifies as `periodical` are accepted, and only while the plan still bills (`active`/`paused`); `starts_at` defaults to today, or to the plan's start date when the plan hasn't started yet, and can never precede it. Attaching an item that already has an open window on the same plan is a 409 — quantity is what expresses "two of them".
- `DELETE /user-memberships/:id/services/:serviceId` — **future-only removal** (#631 §3): it stamps `ends_at = today` instead of deleting, so charges the service already produced stand and only future ones stop. A service whose billing has not started yet (`starts_at` in the future) is deleted outright — an `ends_at` before `starts_at` would violate `chk_ums_ends_at`.

The Billing Simulation projects each attached service as its own stream (below). The admin frontend renders `ADDITIONAL PERIODIC SERVICES` as inline row CRUD with no modal (`components/assignedPlan/AdditionalPeriodicServices.tsx`), in the Assigned Plan expanded row and in the Member page's current plan card — where an add/remove also remounts the Billing Simulation, so it recalculates immediately (#631 §6).

**The UI says *Product*, and the add action is Edit mode's (#957).** The section's copy is the final Product terminology the Service → Product rename (#949) lands everywhere else — `Additional Products`, `Additional Periodic Products`, `+ Add Product`, *Select a product…* — so no screen shows an intermediate "Add Service" state. Only the locale **values** moved: the `services_*` / `section_additional_services` keys are identifiers and renaming those is #949's, and the audit action `add_service` is persisted data. *Professional Services* is a different concept that merely shares the word and is untouched. The add action is a real button wearing the Theme's Buttons group through `primaryBtnSmall()` (#912/#954) rather than the lilac text link it was, and the shared editor takes `canAdd` (default `true`) to decide whether to render it at all: the Member card passes its Edit-mode flag, so the read-only expanded row carries **no** Add affordance rather than a disabled one (#797), while the Assigned Plans card — which has no edit mode — passes nothing and keeps the action it always had. Everything else about the section is unchanged: the table, the removal action and the needs-a-plan line read in both modes, and no endpoint, payload or billing rule moved.

### Assigned Plan snapshot (#635 stages 2–3 + 6–13, migrations 174, 183, 184, 185, 186 and 189)

An Assigned Plan owns the commercial configuration it was assigned with, so a later edit to the Membership Plan, a Promotion or a Product can never move an assignment that already exists (#635 §11–§17). What is captured, all inside the assignment's own transaction:

- **On `user_memberships`** — `free_periods` / `paid_periods` / `bonus_periods` / `pay_beforehand_periods` (the Plan's Billing & Duration, migrations 173 and 189, renamed by migration 201 when #892 made them counts of Billing Frequency periods rather than of calendar months), `recurring_billing_interval` / `recurring_billing_unit` (the Plan's `billing_policies` cadence) and `membership_fee_price` (the *regular*, pre-Promotion fee: the price window covering the start date). A Plan with no price window leaves it `NULL`. Since #635 stage 15 it is also where a **negotiated** fee lives — `final_price`, the separate "agreed price after promotions and discounts" column, is gone (migration 191), so an assignment stores one price and every cycle is resolved from it.
- **`user_membership_session` / `_oneoff` / `_periodical`** — the assignment's copy of the Plan's three benefit sections, each row carrying `quantity` plus the item's `item_name`, `item_type`, `item_billing_frequency`, `unit_price` and `currency` as they were. `product_id` has **no** `ON DELETE CASCADE` (unlike the Plan-side tables of migration 173): the row is the historical record, so it outlives a retired item — same reasoning as migrations 130 and 164.
- **`user_membership_promotion_{session,oneoff,periodical}_snapshot`** — created empty by migration 156 and finally written here, when a Promotion is applied (`snapshotPromotionGrants()` in `membership-promotions.ts`). Migration 174 adds the pricing columns they lacked; a name and a quantity cannot reproduce a charge.
- **`user_membership_services`** — Additional Periodic Services gain the same five snapshot columns, captured at attach time. `NULL` marks an attachment made before migration 174.

`api/src/api/assigned-plan-snapshot.ts` owns the write (`snapshotAssignedPlan()`, called from all three assignment entry points: `POST /user-memberships`, `POST /user-memberships/:id/assign-new-plan`, `POST /membership-plans/:id/assign`) and the read (`loadAssignedPlanSnapshot()`, embedded in `GET /user-memberships/:id` as `snapshot`). `snapshot_captured: false` marks an assignment that predates the migration and has nothing to read back.

**Editing the snapshot (stage 6, no migration).** §9/§10/§15: the Assigned Plan exposes the Membership Plan's own structure and each section is saved on its own, against *this* assignment:

- `PUT /user-memberships/:id/billing-duration` — the six `user_memberships` snapshot columns. Only the keys present in the body are written (so one section's editor can leave the others alone), a blank clears back to `NULL` ("not configured", distinct from `0`), and the cadence is validated as a pair: half of one would let `ASSIGNMENT_CADENCE`'s per-column `COALESCE` mix the assignment's interval with the Plan's unit.
- `PUT /user-memberships/:id/fee-benefit` (#772) — the **Personal Membership Fee Benefit**, `{action, value}` replace-all. See its own section below; it is edited here but it is *not* part of the snapshot, so it is the one section editor that does **not** materialise.
- `GET`/`PUT /user-memberships/:id/{session,oneoff,periodical}-benefits` — the same replace-all `{items:[{product_id,quantity}]}` contract as the Plan and Promotion sections (the admin editors are shared), writing the assignment's own rows via `writeAssignedPlanBenefitSection()`. A line already in the section keeps the commercial facts it was captured with and only changes quantity — a *newly* added line freezes the Product as it is now, so editing one line never silently reprices its neighbours (§17); re-pricing a kept line is a deliberate remove-then-add. Only a new line is held to the catalogue (gym-owned, non-deleted, `active`, and of the section's category), so a line whose item was since retired stays saveable.

All three are `requireModuleWrite('PAYMENTS')` and refuse a `cancelled`/`expired` assignment — a terminal row bills nothing further, so rewriting what it was agreed at would only falsify the record. The first two call `materialiseAssignedPlanSnapshot()`: for an assignment with `snapshot_captured: false` the live values it resolves today are written down before the edit applies, because stage 3's fallback is all-or-nothing — the first edit would otherwise make the assignment "captured" and blank the sections it never mentioned. Since stage 3 these rows *are* what the assignment bills, so a saved section moves its Billing Simulation immediately; the Plan, its other assignments and the Products are untouched. Admin UI: `components/assignedPlan/AssignedPlanConfiguration.tsx` renders `BILLING & DURATION` above `ONE-OFF`, `SESSION` and `PERIOD BENEFITS` inside the expanded row, each with its own Edit/Save/Cancel and no modal (#627's section-at-a-time editing), reusing the shared `components/ProductBenefits.tsx` editor. Since **#924 stage 1** the read-only half reuses that module's grid too — `ProductBenefitView` over `PRODUCT_BENEFIT_COLUMNS`, the same six columns at the same positions as the Membership Plan and Promotion cards (#916, #919/#920), replacing the card's own `<table>`; the Session section's Frequency cell shows the **agreed renewal Frequency** (#918) while the other two show the item's own, and the Benefit column reports the frozen `(action, value)` pair in the Plan's option set. What stays different from the Plan's own sections is where the amounts come from: `loadAssignedPlanSnapshot()` / `loadAssignedPlanBenefitSection()` report `original_price_incl_tax` / `final_price_incl_tax` plus the two line totals from each line's **frozen** `unit_price` and pair, through the one shared `productBenefitPrices()` (`api/src/api/product-benefit-pricing.ts`) so the card cannot quote a line differently from what the assignment bills. The only live column it reads is the Product's tax treatment (`tax_behavior` + `tax_rates.rate_percent`, LEFT JOINed) because the snapshot never captured a statutory rate and #924 §4 asks these figures to be VAT-inclusive; the frozen price itself is never re-read from `products`. Nothing is stored — the four amounts are computed on every read, like `example_timeline`.

### Personal Membership Fee Benefit (#772, migration 192)

Everything else that changes what the Membership Fee costs is bounded in time: a Promotion's Membership Fee Benefit lives inside that Promotion's own Free/Paid/Bonus timeline and ends with it (#635 stage 12), and the Plan's Free / Pre-paid / Bonus durations are counted from `starts_at` and run out. This one is bounded by nothing — it belongs to the Assigned Plan, lasts its whole lifetime, and only an explicit edit changes or removes it.

- **Stored** as `user_memberships.personal_fee_benefit_action` (`VARCHAR(30) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'no_benefit'`) + `personal_fee_benefit_value` (`DECIMAL(10,2) NULL`). The action vocabulary is shared with the Promotion benefits so `applyPeriodBenefit()` stays the one place a pair becomes an amount, but `domain/personalFeeBenefit.ts` restricts *this* surface to the ticket's two options — `no_benefit` and `percentage_discount` — and `toPersonalFeeBenefit()` clamps whatever it reads to 0..100, because `user_memberships` carries no CHECK (migrations 174 and 189 declined one on that table: `ADD CONSTRAINT` rebuilds it under `ALGORITHM=COPY`).
- **Not part of the snapshot.** It is agreed with the member rather than captured from the catalogue, it has no live counterpart to fall back to, and its columns are NOT NULL with a default — so it is deliberately absent from the `has_billing_snapshot` expression and from `snapshot_captured`, and `PUT /:id/fee-benefit` does **not** call `materialiseAssignedPlanSnapshot()`. Folding it in would answer "captured" for every row in the table and freeze the duration fallback for assignments that never captured anything.
- **Not a negotiated price.** `membership_fee_price` is still the regular fee this contract discounts *from*, and `discount_reason` still marks a negotiated one. The two are separate decisions, so a percentage keeps following the fee if the fee is later renegotiated.
- **Read on every cycle** by `resolveMembershipFee()`, as the last step and via a required `personalFeeBenefit` on `MembershipFeeContext` — required so a new pricing path cannot silently forget it. Every fee surface therefore carries it: the Assigned Plans list and card, the Billing Simulation, the Billing Events projection, `GET /me/membership` and the nightly run.
- **Not carried across a plan change.** `POST /user-memberships/:id/assign-new-plan` leaves the successor at the column default: it is a different Assigned Plan, at a price renegotiated in the same request, so a percentage agreed against the old Plan's fee would apply a discount nobody agreed to the new number.
- Admin UI: a `MEMBERSHIP FEE BENEFIT` section in `AssignedPlanConfiguration.tsx`, between Billing & Duration and the three Product sections, with the same Edit/Save/Cancel shape as its neighbours.

**The applied Promotions (stage 7, no migration).** §16: an applied Promotion is part of the agreement, so both what the card *shows* and what the assignment *charges* come from the application rather than from the Promotion's current definition.

- `fetchAppliedPromotions()` (`membership-promotions.ts`, shared by `GET /user-memberships/:id/promotions`, the expanded card's `GET /user-memberships/:id` and the Member configuration read) returns, per application: `applied_by_name` (resolved from `gym_memberships.user_id = ump.applied_by` as a subquery, so a staff member since removed still leaves the record readable), `display_status`, and `session_grants` / `oneoff_grants` / `periodical_grants` — the frozen Products, loaded through the same `loadPromotionGrantSnapshots()` the Billing Simulation uses, so the two can never disagree about what was granted. An application with no grant rows at all predates the snapshot flow and falls back to the Promotion's live benefits. Since **#924 stage 2** each grant row also carries its frozen `(action, value)` pair — read in the **Promotion**'s option set, so all five actions can appear where a Plan benefit has only three (#896 §16) — plus `original_price_incl_tax` / `final_price_incl_tax` and the two line totals, computed by the one shared `productBenefitPrices()` (`api/src/api/product-benefit-pricing.ts`) from the line's **frozen** `unit_price`, never `products.amount`. The only live column it reads is the Product's tax treatment (`tax_behavior` + `tax_rates.rate_percent`, one lookup per card), because the snapshot never captured a statutory rate and #924 §4 asks these figures to be VAT-inclusive; an item deleted since is simply absent from that lookup, which leaves the frozen amount as the honest gross. Nothing is stored — the amounts are computed on every read.
- `display_status` is decided by `domain/promotionApplicationStatus.ts` (pure, unit-tested): `inactive` once the application is no longer standing, `expired` while applied but past the end of its **agreed** window (the snapshot's `ends_at`, not the Promotion's current one), `active` otherwise. The issue thread also names "incompatible"; nothing can persist in that state, because a Promotion that does not stack with one already applied is refused at apply time.
- The fee resolution reads each applied Promotion's Membership Fee Benefit out of its own snapshot (`membershipFeeBenefitsFromSnapshot()`) instead of joining `promotion_membership_fee_benefits` live. That join was the last path by which editing a Promotion could still move an existing assignment's price: the fee is re-resolved on every read, so a Promotion repriced in between used to reprice everything already applied (§13). The `duration_months` gate stays in SQL (`applied_at + INTERVAL n MONTH > NOW()`, one round trip for the whole set) so month arithmetic still clamps the way it always did. Only a snapshot-less application still reads the Promotion's current definition — it has nothing else to read.
- Admin UI: `components/assignedPlan/AssignedPlanPromotions.tsx` renders one expandable card per application — checkbox, name, applied at, applied by, `StatusBadge` — which opens onto the agreed window, Billing & Duration, the Membership Fee Benefit and the three grant sections at their frozen prices. Since **#924 stage 2** those three render from the shared `ProductBenefitView` over `PRODUCT_BENEFIT_COLUMNS` (#916, #919/#920) rather than the card's own `<table>` — the same six columns at the same horizontal positions as the Plan Benefit sections above them, `showFrequency` for all three so a One-off grant keeps its cell with a `—`, `benefitContext="promotion"` for the Promotion's own option set, and `frequencyColumn` left on the item's own frequency since #918's renewal Frequency is a Membership Plan Session Benefit's.
