Both halves of this card live in one locale namespace, so the key carries the voice: the Plan sections keep `item_action_*` (*Benefit*) and these resolve `promo_item_action_*` (*Promotion*), through the page's own `grantT` wrapper (#896 §3/§4). A revoked application reads as `inactive`: its checkbox is cleared and its card does not expand. Clearing a standing one's checkbox revokes it (`DELETE /user-memberships/:id/promotions/:promotionId`, behind a confirmation), which recomputes the price and moves the Billing Events section with it. Ticking a spent one back applies the Promotion again — see stage 9 below.

**Billing & Duration bills (stage 8, no migration).** §7 asks the Plan's Free Period / Paid Duration / Bonus Duration to use "the same semantics as the Promotion configuration"; stages 1 and 2 stored them and nothing read them, so a Plan sold with a free first month billed its full fee on day one. `api/src/domain/planDuration.ts` (pure, unit-tested) classifies a date as `free_plan` / `pay_plan` / `bonus_plan` / `pay_regular`, and the Billing Simulation waives the Membership Fee in the first and third, reporting it as a `source: 'membership_plan'` benefit line carrying the period. Three rules worth knowing:

- **The Promotion wins any overlap** — the thread's Q2 answer, *"in case of conflict, prioritize the promotion"*. Where an applied Promotion governs the date (inside its own timeline, or applying its Membership Fee Benefit) it decides the fee alone; the Plan's own period applies only where no Promotion does, including after one is revoked. Stacking them would also let a Promotion's *paid* month be overridden by the Plan's free one, which inverts the answer.
- **The periods are counted from the assignment's `starts_at`**, not from the first of its month the way `computePromotionTimeline` anchors a Promotion's illustrative preview — a plan starting on the 31st must not be free for 30 days it was never given. Every boundary is measured from that anchor in a single `advanceBillingDate` step, so its end-of-month clamping (31 Jan + 1 month = 3 Mar) cannot accumulate and put a later boundary before an earlier one.
- **Only the Membership Fee is waived.** The Plan's Period Benefits are still charged inside a free month, exactly as they are inside a Promotion's. The Paid Duration charges the regular price and produces no benefit line — it *is* the regular charge, so the horizon stops there unless a Bonus Duration behind it still has to be shown.

The durations are read from the assignment's own columns, with the Plan's live ones as the fallback for an assignment that captured no snapshot at all — the same all-or-nothing rule as the benefit loader, and for a sharper reason: the columns are nullable, so a per-column `COALESCE` would let a Free Period *added to the Plan later* start waiving an existing assignment's fee (§13). Nothing else changes: the nightly run charges `final_price` on its own cadence, as it does for a Promotion's free months too.

**A Promotion can be deselected and selected again (stage 9, migration 183).** The thread's Q2 answer asks for Promotions that are *"selectable and deselectable adjusting automatically the simulation"*. Stage 7 only revoked: `ump_unique_pair` (migration 022) reserved `(user_membership_id, promotion_id)` across every status, so a revoked application held the pair for good and a second apply answered 409. Migration 183 narrows the uniqueness to the applications that are still **standing** — a `standing_promotion_key` VIRTUAL generated column, non-NULL only while `status = 'applied'`, under a UNIQUE KEY (`ump_one_standing_per_promotion`), the same shape migration 164 gives Additional Periodic Services. The plain index `ump_membership_promotion_index` is created *before* the old unique key is dropped, because that key was also the index MySQL used for the `user_membership_id` foreign key.

- **`applied_at` and `revoked_at` share one clock.** Migration 022 defaulted `applied_at` to `CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` (the server's session time zone) while the revoke path stamps `revoked_at = UTC_TIMESTAMP()`. The two bound the same window, and since stage 9 a replacement application's `applied_at` is compared against the `revoked_at` of the one it replaced, so migration 183 moves the default to `UTC_TIMESTAMP()` — a non-UTC server would otherwise mis-tag which persisted charges a Promotion affected.
- **A re-apply is a new application, not a resurrection.** The row owns the snapshot of the Promotion it was agreed with (§16) and its `[applied_at, revoked_at]` window is what tags the Billing Events ledger (#511 stage 3), so reusing it would rewrite what the member was already billed under. The spent application therefore stays on the list as history, and the new one snapshots the Promotion **as it stands today** — a new agreement, made now, the same rule stage 6 applies to a newly added benefit line. A Promotion repriced between the revoke and the re-apply moves the new application only (§13 keeps it off the spent one).
- **Every eligibility rule stays on the apply path**, which is unchanged: the Promotion must be `active` and inside its own window, must target the Plan, must satisfy stacking, and must honour `only_applicable_for_new_members`. The one addition is an explicit "a standing application already holds this pair" check, so the database's unique key surfaces as a 409 with a message rather than as a duplicate-key error.
- **`can_reapply`** (per application, from `GET /user-memberships/:id/promotions` and the expanded card) is what the checkbox reads, decided by `canReapplyPromotion()` in `domain/promotionApplicationStatus.ts` (pure, unit-tested): the application must be `inactive`, no other application of the same Promotion may be standing, and the Promotion itself must be active and inside its window today. It is deliberately narrower than the apply path — stacking, plan targeting and new-member eligibility are not re-run on a read, and surface as the server's message if the apply is refused. Admin UI: ticking a spent card's checkbox confirms, then `POST`s to the apply endpoint; the confirmation says the Promotion is agreed as it stands today.

**The Member sees the snapshot too (stage 10, migration 184).** `GET /me/membership` — the Member app's My Membership page — was the last surface still reading an assignment's commercial configuration live. Its `BENEFITS` list came from `membership_plan_benefits` (migration 006's plan-keyed benefit vocabulary, joined to `benefit_types`), a table no code has written since it was created and which is keyed to the *Plan*, not to the assignment; its billing cadence came straight from `billing_policies`, so a Plan re-cadenced after enrollment moved the dates the member was shown. Both now resolve the assignment's own rows: the cadence through `ASSIGNMENT_CADENCE` (as every other billing query does) and the benefits through `loadPlanBenefitsForSimulation()`, so the Member reads exactly what the Billing Simulation and the nightly run bill — the Products the assignment carries, at the quantity, frequency and price frozen with them, with the Plan's live sections as the fallback only for an assignment that captured no snapshot at all. Migration 184 drops `membership_plan_benefits` (its last reader is gone); `benefit_types` stays, as a global vocabulary with its own read-only router. The member app renders the lines grouped one-off → session → period (`membership.benefit_group.*`) with each line's frozen price and frequency (`membership.frequency.*`), replacing the fixed `membership.benefit.<code>` label set. Nothing about what is *charged* moved at that stage: `upcoming_payments` still projected the stored agreed price, which is what the nightly run charged, only now on the assignment's own cadence.

**The nightly run stops charging a waived cycle (stage 11, migration 185).** Stage 8 made the Billing Simulation bill from the assignment's own Billing & Duration and stage 10 put the same numbers in front of the Member — but `POST /billing/run` still charged `user_memberships.final_price` flat, so a Plan sold with a free first month was shown €0 and charged for it that night. The run now resolves, for the date it is billing (`next_billing_date`, not "today" — a run missed for two days must still price the cycle it is settling), whether the fee is waived, through `priceDueMembershipFee()` in `api/src/api/billing-run-pricing.ts`. That function calls `resolveMembershipFee()` — the Billing Simulation's own resolver, exported for the purpose — so the run cannot disagree with what the member was shown, precedence rule included: where an applied Promotion governs the date it decides alone, and the Plan's Free Period applies only where none does. A waived cycle calls no payment provider and writes no `payment_requests` row; it records a **`waived_billing`** billing event (amount 0, `source = 'system'`, its `notes` naming the period: `free_plan`, `bonus_plan`, `free_promotion`, `bonus_promotion`) and advances `next_billing_date` exactly as a charged cycle would. `last_billed_at` deliberately does not move — nothing was billed. `POST /billing/run` answers with a fourth counter, `waived`.

Two things stage 11 left alone (stage 12 below closes the first). A cycle that is *not* waived was charged `final_price` — that column carries no date, so a Promotion's Membership Fee Benefit which had run out kept discounting later cycles. An assignment that captured no snapshot reads its **Plan's live** Billing & Duration, all-or-nothing, the same rule the benefit loader and the simulation apply (§13). And a €0 `final_price` is now treated as waived rather than sent to the provider as a zero-amount MIT charge. Supporting change: `advanceBillingDate` moved into its own leaf module, `api/src/domain/billingDate.ts`, because the run importing the engine would otherwise have closed an `api/billing → domain/billingSimulation → domain/planDuration → api/billing` import cycle; `api/src/api/billing.ts` re-exports it, so every existing importer is unchanged.

**One date-aware Membership Fee rule, everywhere (stage 12, migration 186).** The thread's stage 12 answer (a) — *a Promotion's own Free/Paid/Bonus months determine when its Membership Fee Benefit ends* — settles §16's last gap. Three code paths priced a fee and they disagreed: `final_price` (what the run charged) and `computeMembershipFeePriceAt()` (the Billing Events projection) applied the benefit for as long as the application stood, gated only by its own `duration_months`, while the Billing Simulation and My Membership stopped it at the end of the Promotion's timeline. For the same cycle the Member was *shown* one price and *charged* another, in both directions.

`resolveMembershipFee()` is now the only implementation of the rule, and everything asks it:

- The Billing Events projection's `computeMembershipFeePriceAt()` delegated to it until #854 removed it — the projection served only the `draft` status #786 retired.
- `priceMembershipFeeOn()` / `currentMembershipFee()` / `currentMembershipFees()` (`api/src/api/membership-fee-pricing.ts`) wrap it for every staff screen and payment request that shows or raises a fee.
- Applying or revoking a Promotion (`membership-promotions.ts`) resolves the cycle `next_billing_date` names — never a date already past, never before `starts_at` — instead of discounting a date-less number.
- `GET /me/membership` prices each of its two `upcoming_payments` on its own date (`computeUpcomingPayments`'s `priceOn` callback) rather than repeating one stored number, so the second can legitimately cost more than the first.
- `POST /billing/run` charges what the resolver returns for the cycle, not a stored price flat.

To make that possible without duplicating the rule, `MembershipFeeBenefit`, `AppliedPromotionForBilling` and `promotionCoversDate` moved into a new leaf module, `api/src/domain/promotionApplication.ts` (and `AppliedPromotionForBilling` now carries the Promotion's Free/Paid/Bonus months, because every caller needs them to bound the benefit). `domain/billingSimulation.ts` imports that instead of `domain/assignedPlanBillingEvents.ts`, which inverts the edge that had kept the two rules apart; both old modules re-export what they used to declare, so no importer changed. `regularMembershipFee()` likewise moved to `api/user-memberships.ts`, so the number every path discounts *from* has one chain as well: the assignment's frozen `membership_fee_price` → its Plan's `membership_plan_prices` window at `starts_at` → a non-zero `base_price`.

**It shipped switchable and off, and stage 15 removed the switch.** The correction *raises* the charge of a member whose promotional months already elapsed, which the thread asked not to let happen silently — so stage 12 gated the two writers that move money on a `billing.date_aware_membership_fee` feature flag seeded **disabled** (migration 186), and stage 14 gave the impact report a page of its own (**Payments → Membership Fee Drift**, its own `payments.membership_fee_drift` key, migration 190) so a gym admin could review what switching it on would change. Every read-only surface resolved dates through the rule regardless — showing the agreed timeline correctly charges nobody.

The thread's answer to that review was to stop switching it: *"please remove the billing.date_aware_membership_fee feature flag entirely, as well as the stored final_price approach. Date-aware Membership Fee pricing should be standard system behaviour, with pricing calculated dynamically in the Assigned Membership Plan simulation."*

**Date-aware pricing is standard, and nothing stores a price (stage 15, migration 191).** Both flag rows and `user_memberships.final_price` are dropped, the drift report and its page are deleted (empty by construction once nothing is stored to drift from), and the run's `drift` counter is gone with them. There is no second rule left to disagree with, and nothing to keep in sync at apply/revoke time — which is the point: `final_price` was a number with no date in it, recomputed only on mutation, so a Promotion whose months had elapsed kept discounting until someone happened to touch the assignment again.

What replaces it is one shared module, **`api/src/api/membership-fee-pricing.ts`** (the former `billing-run-pricing.ts`, renamed because it is no longer the run's private helper):

| Export | What it answers |
|---|---|
| `priceMembershipFeeOn(row, date)` | The fee owed on one date: `{ amount, waived, periodStatus }`, resolved through `resolveMembershipFee()` over the assignment's own regular fee, Billing & Duration and standing applications. |
| `priceMembershipFeesFor(rows, dateFor)` | The same over many assignments and dates, with one query for every row's applications — what a list page needs instead of N round trips. |
| `currentCycleDate(row)` | The date "what does this member pay **now**" means: `max(next_billing_date, today)`, never before `starts_at` and never a cycle already past. |
| `currentMembershipFee(gymId, umId)` / `currentMembershipFees(gymId, ids)` | That cycle's fee, for one assignment or a list. |
| `FEE_ASSIGNMENT_COLUMNS` / `FEE_ASSIGNMENT_FROM` / `loadFeeAssignment(s)` | The columns and joins any of the above needs, so no caller re-derives the snapshot/fallback SQL. |

Every surface that used to read the column now reports a computed **`membership_fee`** instead: `GET /user-memberships` and `/:id` (beside `snapshot.membership_fee_price`, the *regular* fee an edit writes), the create / renew / lifecycle responses, `POST /user-memberships/:id/promotions` and its DELETE, `GET /members/:id/membership-configuration`, `GET /me/membership`, and the Payments dashboard's projected rows — which are now priced **per projected date**, so a Free Period reads €0 and the cycle after a Promotion's timeline ends reads the regular price. A **payment request** (staff-raised, `POST /payment-requests`, or member-raised) asks for that same resolved number and is refused with 400 when the cycle owes nothing, rather than asking a member to pay for a cycle the run would waive.

Two knock-on changes worth knowing:

- **A negotiated fee lives in `membership_fee_price`.** There is no second column for it, so `POST /user-memberships` and `POST /:id/assign-new-plan` take `membership_fee_price` (with `discount_reason`, as before) in place of `final_price`, and freeze it through `snapshotAssignedPlan()`. `PUT /:id` no longer takes a price at all — editing one is a snapshot edit, which goes through `PUT /:id/billing-duration` so the snapshot is materialised first. `discount_reason` still marks an assignment as negotiated, which is what keeps `POST /membership-plans/:id/pricing/apply-to-assigned-plans` off it; a past `discount_expires_at` is the one case that reads the Plan's price window instead of the frozen number (`regularMembershipFee`'s `ignoreFrozenFee`). Migration 191 therefore runs **two** backfills before the `DROP COLUMN`, not one: the narrow *last-resort* pass (the fee is written from `final_price` only where the old chain would have returned `final_price` anyway, materialising an uncaptured candidate in full first), and a *negotiated-price* pass (#777) for an assignment created with a price override **before stage 15**, whose Plan had a price window covering `starts_at`: the override went to `final_price` while `membership_fee_price` took the **catalogue** price (from `snapshotAssignedPlan()`, or from migration 174's backfill for an older row), so without the second pass the member would be charged the catalogue fee from the first nightly run after the deploy. The pass keys on a non-empty `discount_reason`, a frozen fee that disagrees with `final_price` and a non-terminal status, and it deliberately **skips an assignment that ever had a Promotion applied, in any status**: a standing one has the discount baked into `final_price`, and a revoked one had `final_price` recomputed from scratch at the revoke (pre-stage-15 apply/revoke never started from the previous value — under the legacy rule it started from `base_price`, a constant 0), so the agreed number is in neither column and a `status = 'applied'` filter would have frozen a `0.00` as a 100 % override. Those rows keep the catalogue fee; the migration logs how many it left behind and `docs/go-to-production.md` lists them for a staff re-negotiation.
- **Pushing a Plan's price onto its assignments now writes `membership_fee_price`.** `apply-to-assigned-plans` wrote `base_price`/`plan_price_id`/`final_price`, none of which the resolver reads first — so under date-aware pricing it would have changed nothing an assignment bills. It materialises each affected assignment's snapshot (the write makes the row "captured", and the all-or-nothing rule would otherwise blank its benefit sections) and then moves the frozen fee.

The migration's backfill is deliberately narrow: `membership_fee_price` is written from `final_price` only where `regularMembershipFee()`'s chain would have returned `final_price` anyway — no frozen fee, no Plan price window covering `starts_at`, no positive `base_price`. Copying it more widely would freeze a *promotional* discount into the contract as its regular fee, which is the bug stage 12 existed to fix.

It cannot write that fee on its own, though, and that is the subtle part. `membership_fee_price IS NOT NULL` is one of the seven disjuncts that *define* `has_billing_snapshot`, and the fallback it turns off is all-or-nothing: setting it alone would flip an uncaptured assignment to captured, and its Free Period, Pre-paid and Bonus Durations, cadence and three Plan benefit sections would then read back as *nothing* instead of falling through to the Plan — a member inside a free month would start being charged for it. The two row sets overlap by construction rather than by accident: the rows migration 174's backfill left uncaptured are exactly those whose Plan had no durations, no billing policy *and* no price window covering `starts_at`, and that last condition is the one this backfill keys on. So 191 materialises an uncaptured candidate in full first — the same values `materialiseAssignedPlanSnapshot()` and 174 write, benefit rows included, with the candidate id set frozen in a scratch table because inserting those rows is itself a capture — and writes the fee on top. `api/src/test/billing-run-membership-fee.test.ts` pins the rule, so a future path that writes the fee alone fails there rather than in a billing run. Two operational consequences: `up()` is guarded on `hasColumn('final_price')`, because the `DROP COLUMN` commits implicitly and a retry must skip the statements that read it; and the materialisation is one-way — `down()` restores the column but those rows stay captured, which changes nothing they bill (the values are what they already resolved to live) but means the pair is not a round trip.

**Billing reads the snapshot (stage 3).** Everything that prices or schedules an existing assignment resolves its own row first and only falls back to the live catalogue when it has none: the Billing Simulation (regular fee, cadence, the Plan's three benefit sections, each applied Promotion's grants, each attached service's price), the nightly run in `billing.ts`, the Billing Events projection, the Billing Events page's scheduled rows, the Payments dashboard, the schedule advance after a settled payment and the webhook that stamps the first `next_billing_date`. The cadence half is one shared SQL fragment, `ASSIGNMENT_CADENCE` in `assigned-plan-snapshot.ts` (`COALESCE(um.recurring_billing_*, bp.recurring_billing_*)`) — every one of those queries therefore LEFT JOINs `billing_policies`, so a Plan whose policy is deleted no longer strands its assignments. The Plan-side benefit loader falls back to the live sections only for an assignment with no snapshot at all (`hasBillingSnapshot` false *and* no benefit rows), so a Plan that gained benefits after an assignment was made cannot reach it. **Stage 4 (part 1)** then retired Charge Benefits outright — `plan_charge_benefits` and `user_membership_charge_benefits` are dropped by migration 176, their endpoints and both payload fields are gone, and the #485 Plan Billing Forecast carries no benefit lines any more. Nothing billed off either table after stage 3, so no assignment changed what it charges. **Stage 4 (part 2)** retired Included Services: migration 177 drops `plan_allowances`, and the `allowances` / `activity_allowances` fields, the four `/membership-plans/:id/allowances` endpoints and the `INCLUDED SERVICES` section go with it. It was never commercial configuration — it was booking access — and the issue answered where that belongs: *"Activity type should flag [which] membership plans are enabled to attend [a] certain activity type"*. That relation already existed as `activity_type_eligible_plans` (#481, migration 139), so `activity-eligibility.ts` is now the only plan-based booking gate, and the check is configured from one side instead of two. Two things moved rather than disappeared: the plan **center coverage** check that shared the retired hook's file now lives in `plan-center-access.ts`, and the **class-package claim** (a member without a covering plan paying out of prepaid sessions) is made by the eligibility gate itself via `tryClaimPackageCredit()`. What genuinely goes away is the per-window `session_count` cap, which eligibility has no equivalent for and §1 forbade relocating. Existing assignments were backfilled from the live catalogue by migration 174 rather than deleted (the #635 Q3 answer permitted deleting them): the values captured are the ones those rows already resolved to, so nothing they bill changed. Benefit rows are copied only for assignments that still bill — a `cancelled`/`expired` one would otherwise be stamped with today's catalogue under the heading "what was agreed". Every copy of a Product's name and type resolves `COALESCE(gc.name, ct.name, …)` / `COALESCE(gc.type, 'other')`, because a *system* charge carries neither of its own (both `products` columns are nullable) and the snapshot columns are `NOT NULL`.

### Product pricing treatment inside a Promotion or a Plan (#896 stages 1–2, migration 203)

A Product configured in a Promotion section or a Membership Plan section carries an `(action, value)` pair beside its `quantity` — the **Promotion** in the Promotions UI, the **Benefit** in the Membership Plans UI, one stored vocabulary under two labels (#896 §3/§4). The configuration belongs to the *relationship* and never to `products` (§12), so the same item may be waived by one Plan and discounted 20% by a Promotion.

- **Twelve tables.** `promotion_{session,oneoff,periodical}` (155) and `membership_plan_{session,oneoff,periodical}` (173), plus the six assignment-side snapshots that have to carry it for the Assigned Plan rule — `user_membership_promotion_{…}_snapshot` (156/174) and `user_membership_{session,oneoff,periodical}` (174). A pair that reached only the catalogue tables would be a configuration no existing assignment could have been agreed with.
- **Two option sets, declared once.** `api/src/domain/productBenefitActions.ts`: a Promotion may configure all five actions (`no_benefit`, `waive`, `percentage_discount`, `fixed_discount`, `fixed_price` — the `PromotionBenefitAction` vocabulary that already exists, not a second enum), a Membership Plan only the first **two** since #997 (the first three until then), because §16 states outright that Plans cannot configure *Fixed discount* or *Fixed Price*. The `chk_<table>_action` CHECK beside each table enforces that rather than the dropdown, so a new action goes in **two** places — and see *Plan benefit `% Discount` retired* below for why *removing* one from the Plan side touches the module and not the CHECK.
- **`chk_<table>_value`** is `benefitConfigError()`'s rule in SQL: the two valueless actions must carry no value, a percentage is 0..100, an amount is non-negative. The upper bound is the `DECIMAL(10,2)` column itself; `MAX_BENEFIT_AMOUNT` is that limit spelled out so the API answers 400 rather than letting MySQL raise an out-of-range error.
- **Neutral default, corrective backfill.** New rows default to `no_benefit` (§13 — a row written by a path that does not know about the column is charged at the normal price and can never read as discounted). Rows that already existed are a different question: a Promotion grant is an implicit *waive* today (`buildItemSingleCharge()` charges `unit × (quantity − covered)`), so migration 203 writes `waive` onto every promotion-side row, while the Plan side is already described by the default.
- **Nothing prices from it yet.** Stage 3 is the pricing cutover in `domain/billingSimulation.ts`, where `applyLineBenefit()` — the line, not the unit, per the ticket thread — delegates to `applyPeriodBenefit()` rather than growing a second copy of the arithmetic (§11).

**Stage 2 — the API (no migration).** The six replace-all `PUT`s (`/promotions/:id/{session,oneoff,periodical}-benefits`, `/membership-plans/:id/{…}-benefits`) take the pair beside `product_id` and `quantity`, and every read reports it:

- **One validation, per context.** `parseProductBenefitInput(context, item)` (`domain/productBenefitActions.ts`) wraps `benefitConfigError()` and is the only place a request's pair is judged: an action outside the context's set is a 400 (so `fixed_discount`/`fixed_price` are refused on a Plan section before SQL ever sees them, §16), a value-requiring action without a value is a 400, a value on `no_benefit`/`waive` is a 400, and a `value` with **no** action is a 400 rather than silently dropped. A rejected line fails the whole save, so a section can never be half-written.
- **A request that does not name the pair keeps the stored one.** `benefit: null` from the parser means *unmentioned*, which is not `no_benefit`. Both `PUT`s read the section's current `(action, value)` under the transaction that is about to delete it and re-apply it to every line the request left neutral. This is what protects migration 203's `waive` backfill: the replace-all payload is how quantity has always been edited, and every client until stage 4 sends no pair at all, so a neutral default here would quietly turn a waived Promotion grant into a charged one the moment stage 3 lands. Clearing a treatment is still possible and still explicit — send `action: 'no_benefit'`.
- **Reads are normalized.** `shapeProductBenefitRow(context, row)` reports `value` as a number rather than the `DECIMAL(10,2)` string mysql2 returns, and reads an action the context may not **store** back as the neutral default, so the Plan editor is never handed an option it cannot offer. It covers the Plan sections' `GET`, `enrichPlan`'s three embedded sections, the Promotion sections' `GET`, and both `PUT` responses; `AssignedPlanBenefitRow` carries the pair the assignment was agreed with the same way.
- **Copies stay copies.** `POST /membership-plans/:id/duplicate` and `POST /promotions/:id/duplicate` copy the pair verbatim — Duplicate is a copy, not a re-configuration. The snapshot writers (`snapshotAssignedPlan()`, `writeAssignedPlanBenefitSection()`, `snapshotPromotionGrants()`) already carried it from stage 1.
- **#893's mandatory rule is unchanged by it.** A mandatory item a Plan has no row for reads as `no_benefit` (Mandatory says the item must be *there*, never what it costs), and one the client dropped is re-added by `withMandatoryBenefits()` naming no treatment — so the preservation rule above keeps whatever it was configured with.

### Plan benefit `% Discount` retired (#997, no migration)

A Membership Plan Product benefit is **`No benefit` or `Waive`**. The `% Discount` the Plan side could configure since #896 is gone from the dropdown and from the `PUT` — but not from the database, and not from any row that already holds it.

- **Offered vs. stored, as two declarations.** `api/src/domain/productBenefitActions.ts` now exports `PLAN_BENEFIT_ACTIONS` (`no_benefit`, `waive`), `LEGACY_PLAN_BENEFIT_ACTIONS` (`percentage_discount`) and `STORED_PLAN_BENEFIT_ACTIONS` (both), with `benefitActionsFor()` / `isBenefitActionAllowed()` answering the write question and `storedBenefitActionsFor()` / `isStoredBenefitAction()` the read one. `toProductBenefit()` and therefore `shapeProductBenefitRow()` ask the **stored** set: a reader that asked the offered one would normalize a stored percentage to `no_benefit` and start charging the full price for a discounted line. It is the same split `domain/productFrequency.ts` draws for a Product's retired Billing Frequencies (#945/#821).
- **No migration, no backfill, no coercion.** Migration 203's `chk_<table>_action` keeps permitting all three on the six Plan-side tables, so every stored row stays valid and the route is what refuses a new one. §6 forbids both of the easy answers: converting a percentage to `Waive` makes a €20 item free, and reading it as `No benefit` charges €20 for a line agreed at €16. So a legacy line keeps pricing (`applyLineBenefit()`), displaying (`planBenefitPrices()`), snapshotting (`snapshotAssignedPlan()`) and billing exactly as stored until a human corrects it.
- **Kept, never re-configured.** `benefitConfigError(context, action, value, current)` takes the pair the line is stored with, and `keepsRetiredBenefit()` is the rule: a retired action passes only when action *and* value are unchanged. So a legacy line's quantity stays editable, a new percentage is a 400 naming the two that remain, and moving a stored 20% to 50% is a 400 too. The section `PUT` reads those pairs with `loadStoredPlanBenefitPairs()` before validating; the `FOR UPDATE` read inside the transaction is still the only thing that decides what a *kept* line is written with, so a concurrent save can at worst let a stale pair validate and never get stored.
- **The editor shows it without offering it.** `ProductBenefits.tsx` appends the stored retired action as a **disabled** `<option>` while the draft holds it, and renders its number as read-only text where the percentage input used to be (§2 — no percentage input inside a Membership Plan). Dropping the option would make the select read *No benefit* for a line that bills a discount, and the first unrelated save would make that true. The read-only card still reports it through `benefitTreatmentLabel()`.
- **Identification, per §6.** `npm run plans:percentage-benefits` (`api/src/scripts/report-plan-percentage-benefits.ts`) is a read-only report listing every `membership_plan_{session,oneoff,periodical}` line still carrying one — correctable in the Plan's Benefit section — and, separately, every `user_membership_{session,oneoff,periodical}` snapshot line, which is what those members were *agreed* at and is left alone.
- **Promotions are untouched (§8).** They configure all five actions; `isRetiredBenefitAction()` answers `false` in that context, and the stored vocabulary is still one, so a Plan benefit and a Promotion grant stay comparable.

### Session Benefit renewal Frequency (#918, migration 205)

A Membership Plan's **Session Benefit** carries a Frequency beside its quantity — the period on which the included sessions are renewed, so `Personal Training Class | 2 | Weekly | Waive` reads "2 included classes every week". The column is `frequency` on `membership_plan_session` and on the assignment-side `user_membership_session`, and on nothing else.

- **Its own accepted set, not the Product's.** `api/src/domain/sessionBenefitFrequency.ts` declares `once, week, four_weeks, month, year`, mirrored by `chk_<table>_frequency` and by `apps/admin/src/lib/sessionBenefitFrequency.ts` — so a new value goes in **three** places. `week` is offered here although #821 retired it from `products.billing_frequency`: how often an allowance *renews* and how often an item is *priced* are different questions (the #918 thread's Q2 answer picks weekly as the case the field exists for). `per_session` is not a period and is not offered.
- **`NULL` is a real state.** The column is nullable with no default and no backfill: `NULL` is the dropdown's `—` and is what every Session Benefit written before the ticket means — a one-time allowance of N sessions on the start date. An explicit `once` behaves identically but records that someone chose it, which is why the two are not collapsed.
- **Replace-all keeps what it does not mention.** `parseSessionBenefitFrequencyInput()` answers *keep* / *set* / *400*, exactly as `parseProductBenefitInput()` does for the `(action, value)` pair and for the same reason: the section `PUT` is replace-all, so a quantity-only save (the other two sections, the Assigned Plan snapshot editor, any client written before #918, a mandatory item re-added by `withMandatoryBenefits()`) must not clear a configured Frequency. Clearing it is explicit — `frequency: null` or `''`.
- **It is part of the agreement.** `snapshotAssignedPlan()` copies it onto the assignment and `writeAssignedPlanBenefitSection()` keeps it on a line it keeps, so a Plan switched from Weekly to Monthly afterwards cannot change what an existing member was agreed (#635 §13–§17). `loadPlanBenefitsForSimulation()` reads it from the snapshot, and from the live Plan only for an assignment that captured none.
- **The simulation summarises it; it does not multiply billing events.** `sessionFrequency` is a required field of `SimulationPlanBenefit`, and a renewing one turns the item into a stream at the **assignment's own billing cadence** (`buildSessionAllowanceStream()` in `domain/billingSimulation.ts`), each occurrence reporting `quantity × renewalsInPeriod(...)` — the renewals of one schedule anchored at the assignment's start date that fall inside that cycle. So a 4-weekly Plan with `2 | Weekly` shows **8 sessions** on each billing date rather than one line per week (the #918 thread's Q1 answer), a monthly one shows the 5 renewals a 31-day cycle really contains and the 4 of the next, and a cycle containing no renewal shows no line at all rather than "0 sessions". `once` and `NULL` keep the single charge on the start date, as does an assignment with no cadence to summarise onto. A Promotion's session grant still grants its units **in total**, not per cycle: `allocateSessionUnits()` allocates the line's units as intervals and takes an offset for everything the earlier cycles consumed, which for a single charge (offset 0) is the pre-#918 rule to the cent.
- **Admin.** One column, not a second one: the shared `PRODUCT_BENEFIT_COLUMNS` Frequency cell takes a `frequencyColumn` prop (`'item'` — the Product's own, read-only, the default everywhere — or `'benefit'`), and the Session Benefits section of the Plans page is its only `'benefit'` caller. The Promotion sections are untouched: a Promotion's session grant has no renewal Frequency of its own.
- **Out of scope, deliberately.** There is still no consumable allowance ledger: nothing tracks how many of the renewed sessions a member has used, and booking eligibility is unchanged (`activity_type_eligible_plans` plus the class-package claim). The Frequency says what the contract grants and how the projection reports it.

### Promotion item Requirement — Mandatory / Optional (#959, migration 207)

A Product configured inside a **Promotion** carries a `requirement` beside its quantity and its `(action, value)` pair: `mandatory` (the member takes it with the Promotion) or `optional` (the member may decline it when the Promotion is assigned). `Gym Membership Fee | 50% | Mandatory` beside `Locker Fee | 50% | Optional` is the ticket's own example.

- **Not `products.mandatory`.** That flag (#832/#893) is a property of the gym's **catalogue** item and means "this item is forced into every Membership Plan benefit section" (`domain/mandatoryPlanBenefits.ts`). This one is a property of the **relationship** between one Promotion and one Product — exactly where #896 put the pricing pair, and for the same reason: the same item may be mandatory in one Promotion and optional in another. The two never share a grid, since a Promotion section passes no `enforceMandatory`.
- **Six tables, Promotion side only.** `promotion_{session,oneoff,periodical}` (155) and the three `user_membership_promotion_{…}_snapshot` tables (156/174). The snapshot side is not optional: an application reports and prices from its own snapshot and never from the live row (#635 §16), so the screen that will offer the member the choice must read what *that* application was agreed with. `membership_plan_*`, `user_membership_{session,oneoff,periodical}` and `promotion_membership_fee_benefits` are deliberately untouched — the issue thread excludes Membership Plans from the ticket ("Exclude Membership plans from this ticket") and answers "No" to a Requirement on the Membership Fee, which has no Product to attach one to.
- **One accepted set, three places.** `api/src/domain/promotionItemRequirement.ts` declares `mandatory, optional`, mirrored by `chk_<table>_requirement` (migration 207) and by `apps/admin/src/lib/promotionItemRequirement.ts` — a new value goes in all three, which `promotion-item-requirement.unit.test.ts` and `promotion-item-requirement-ui.test.ts` assert.
- **NOT NULL, defaulted and backfilled to `mandatory`.** That is what every row written before the ticket means: an applied Promotion grants everything it configures, and nothing offers the member a choice today. A default of `optional` would announce a choice the assignment process has not implemented, for every stored row. Letting an item be declined is the deliberate act, so it is the configured one — unlike #918's Frequency there is no `—`, because the column has no "unanswered" state.
- **Replace-all keeps what it does not mention.** `parsePromotionItemRequirementInput()` answers *keep* / *set* / *400*, as `parseProductBenefitInput()` and `parseSessionBenefitFrequencyInput()` do and for their reason: the three section `PUT`s are replace-all, so a quantity-only save — any client written before this ticket — must not reset an optional item to mandatory. An unknown value is a 400, never a coercion: `optional` and `mandatory` are opposite promises to the member.
- **Copies stay copies.** `POST /promotions/:id/duplicate` and `snapshotPromotionGrants()` carry the flag verbatim.
- **Nothing reads it yet, and that is the ticket's shape.** No pricing path, no projection, no apply path and no member-facing surface consumes `requirement`; an applied Promotion's optional grant is granted and priced exactly as a mandatory one. The member's own enabled/disabled choice, its billing effect and the Membership Plan side of the issue are the assignment process's and need their own ticket — a code path that acts on the flag beyond configuring, reporting and snapshotting it is deciding that behaviour.
- **Admin.** One more entry in the shared `PRODUCT_BENEFIT_COLUMNS` (after Benefit, before the two prices), rendered only where a page opts in with `showRequirement` — the Promotions page, in both halves of its card, through the same two wrappers that name the Promotion's benefit context. The control is the grid's existing inline `<select>`; no new colour, badge or pattern (the ticket: "Do not introduce a custom visual pattern if an existing segmented control, radio group, select, or similar component can be reused"). Labels are `promotions.item_requirement_*`, which keeps #896 §3 (nothing in that namespace says "Benefit").

### The Member's "New Member" status, and Promotion eligibility (#634 §3, #927 — no migration)

`promotions.only_applicable_for_new_members` (`NOT NULL DEFAULT 1`, stored since #633/migration 163) is enforced from #634's follow-up on. The #634 thread defined "new" as *a member that books his/her first membership plan in 12 months*; **#927 shortened that window to six months** — "a member is considered a New Member when they have not had an active Membership Plan during the previous 6 months" — and the answer on its thread (*"6 months here and in promotions"*) settled that the same number governs both surfaces. So `NEW_MEMBER_WINDOW_MONTHS = 6` is the one window: a Member who left between six and twelve months ago now reads as new **and** qualifies for a new-members-only Promotion. There is no second window and no display-only variant; #927 §5 ("avoid implementing a separate New Member calculation inside Promotions") is that rule in both directions.

- `api/src/domain/newMemberEligibility.ts` — pure, unit-tested, no DB. An Assigned Plan disqualifies its Member when it is **live** (`active`/`paused` — the same list the configuration read and the simulation use), when it **started** on or after the cutoff, or when it **ended** (`ends_at`) or was **closed** (`closed_at`) on or after it. A row superseded by Assign New Plan carries neither end date (the supersede stamps only `status = 'expired'`), so it is dated by the assignment that replaced it — otherwise a membership renewed through that action for years would read as "new".
- `api/src/api/new-member-eligibility.ts` — reads the Member's own assignments (`user_memberships.member_id`; being *covered* by somebody else's family plan is not booking one) and applies the rule.

The assignment the Promotion is being applied to **never counts against its own Member**: a Plan is assigned first and its Promotions applied right after, so without that exclusion a brand-new Member would be disqualified by the very plan being configured. Enforcement points, all excluding it: `POST /user-memberships/:id/promotions`, `applyPromotionToMembership()` (assign-new-plan, `POST /membership-plans/:id/assign`'s best-effort auto-apply, `POST /payments/apply-promotion`) and #628's up-front `validatePromotionSelection()`, which takes the `member_id` and excludes nothing, because the assignment it validates for does not exist yet.

`GET /user-memberships/member/:memberId/configuration` reports the answer per Assigned Plan as `new_member_eligible` (a read of the same rule, without attempting an apply — the enforcement point is the apply path). It fed the Member card's `PROMOTIONS` picker until #931 removed that section; the field stays, the rule is unchanged. Since the column defaults to 1, every Promotion created before this carries the flag: test fixtures that are not about the rule set it to `0` explicitly.

**The Member's own status (#927).** `GET /members` projects `is_new_member` for every listed Member and `GET /members/:id` reports it for one, both through `api/src/api/new-member-eligibility.ts` — `newMemberStatusByMember()` for the list (**one** query for the page, never one per row, and never a second SQL copy of the rule: the mistake `latestEnrollmentStatusSql()` exists to prevent) and `isNewMemberStatus()` for the single read. Neither excludes anything, because a Member-level answer has no assignment being configured: a Member holding a live plan reads as not new, and a Member with no assignment at all reads as new. Nothing is stored — there is no column, no migration and no writer, so the status tracks both the Membership history and the passing of the window on its own, and `PUT /members/:id` (an explicit field list) cannot write it.

On the admin side it is a **calculated field of the Member Profile**: `MEMBER_PROFILE_FIELDS` carries it with `calculated: true`, and `MemberProfileLayout` renders a calculated field through its own `renderCalculated` callback in *both* modes — so the Edit form has no way to put a control under that label, the field is absent from `MemberEditFormValues` and from the `PUT` payload, and the read-only view and the form draw the same cell (`NewMemberValue`). **#960** made that cell a compact `Yes` / `No` chip on the label's own row rather than #927's ticked box on a row of its own: the chip is the list's own metadata pill — `listNameBadgeAccentStyle` for `Yes`, `listNameBadgeStyle` for `No`, no colour or size declared in the Profile — and its `aria-label` keeps the full sentence (*New Member: yes*), since the label beside it is not programmatically tied to the chip. The inline row is `MemberProfileLayout`'s treatment of a `calculated` field only; an editable field keeps the stacked label-above-control shape, because that box is where its `<input>` sits in the other mode (#929). The Members list shows the same `is_new_member` as an accent name-cell badge (#913's voice), so a new Member is identifiable without expanding the row and the two places cannot disagree.

### Billing Simulation (#629 stage 1 — no migration)

`GET /user-memberships/member/:memberId/billing-simulation` returns a read-only, **never persisted** forecast of what a Member will actually be charged: the concrete dates, the regular price, the Promotion/Benefit that changed it, and the resulting amount. It creates no `billing_events`, invoices, charges or payment records, and is recomputed on every request, so it always reflects the current configuration.

It is **Member-level and consolidated**, not per Assigned Plan (the #629 thread's Q4 answer): every simulated assignment of that Member contributes to one chronological simulation, which is the shape #634 asks for. The route is mounted separately in `app.ts` (three path segments, so `userMembershipsRouter` never matches it) behind the same `PAYMENTS` module gate and `payments.transactions` feature flag as the rest of Assigned Plans.

Split in two, the same way the Billing Events view is:
- `api/src/domain/promotionApplication.ts` — pure, no DB, no imports but `promotionBenefits`: what one **application** of a Promotion is (`AppliedPromotionForBilling` — its window, its Free/Paid/Bonus months, its Membership Fee Benefits) and `promotionCoversDate`. A leaf module so the fee rule and the Billing Events projection can share one definition (#635 stage 12); re-exported from `domain/assignedPlanBillingEvents.ts`.
- `api/src/domain/billingSimulation.ts` — pure, unit-tested, no DB. Resolves *regular price → promotion → benefit → actual charge* per event and groups events into sections (`one_off`, `year`, `month`, `four_weeks`, `week`, `session`, `other` — the first four in the order #629 §3 fixes; the rest exist because `products.billing_frequency` and `billing_policies` can produce them).
- `api/src/api/billing-simulation.ts` — the reads, plus the router. `loadPromotionApplications` (exported from `user-memberships.ts`, shared with the Billing Events range calculation) supplies each applied Promotion's window, duration and Membership Fee benefits.

Rules the engine implements:
- **Inputs** (#629 thread Q1): the membership price, one-off prices, periodic benefits, session benefits, the applied Promotions and (#631) the Additional Periodic Services attached to each assignment. Plan Charge Benefits are not an input: they were decommissioned entirely by #635 stage 4 (migration 176). Since #635 stage 3 every one of those is read from the assignment's snapshot, with the live catalogue as the fallback for a row that captured none.
- **Membership Plan benefits** (#635 stage 3): the Plan's One-off / Session / Period Benefits, frozen onto the assignment, are *charged* items — the Plan grants the member a locker, not a free locker. A Period Benefit is a stream at the item's own frequency, priced `unit_price × quantity`; the other two are a single line on the start date. A Product carried by both the Plan and an applied Promotion produces **one** merged stream (keyed on `product_id`), the Promotion covering periods or units of it — two streams would bill the same locker twice. A one-off/session grant covers up to the quantity the Plan bills and the remainder is charged (10 sessions with 4 granted = 6 charged); an item only a Promotion carries is covered in full, exactly as before. Since #896 stage 3 "charged" and "free" are both configuration: the Plan row's own `(action, value)` pair prices the whole line (`applyLineBenefit()`) for the life of the assignment, which is why it never marks a charge `promotional` — `no_benefit`, the column default and what every pre-migration-203 row holds, is the full price this bullet describes.
- **Additional Periodic Services** (#631): one stream each, billed at the Product's own `billing_frequency` and price × quantity, from the later of the assignment's start and the service's `starts_at` to the earlier of the assignment's end and its `ends_at`. They are not Promotion benefits, so no benefit ever resolves against them — every occurrence charges the regular price, including inside a promotional period.
- **Regular Membership Fee**: `user_memberships.membership_fee_price`, frozen at assignment time (#635 stage 3); for an assignment without it, the Plan's `membership_plan_prices` window covering `starts_at`. There is no further fallback since #635 stage 15 — an assignment neither of those can price has no fee anyone agreed. `user_memberships.base_price` is *not* usable — `effectivePrice()` has snapshotted a constant `0` into it since `membership_plans.base_price` was dropped in migration 058.
- **Promotion periods** (#629 §5): each projected charge is classified through `computePromotionTimeline` (the same projection the Promotion screen renders) — Free and Bonus periods waive the fee, Pay/Prepaid periods apply the Membership Fee (Period) Benefit, and nothing applies outside the Promotion's own timeline — since #635 stage 12 that is the rule *everywhere*, the nightly run, My Membership and the Promotion apply/revoke adjustment included, so a Promotion configured with no Free/Paid/Bonus months changes no cycle at all (its timeline is one open-ended Pay (regular) period, and #625 caps the benefit at free + paid + bonus = 0). `resolveMembershipFee()` is that single implementation.
- **The assignment's own Billing & Duration** (#635 stage 8, stage 13): its Free Period, **Pre-paid Duration** and Bonus Duration charge no Membership Fee (`source: 'membership_plan'`, `period_status: 'free_plan'` / `'prepaid_plan'` / `'bonus_plan'`), counted from `starts_at` by `domain/planDuration.ts`, in periods of the assignment's own Billing Frequency (#892 — `PlanDuration` carries the cadence, resolved through `ASSIGNMENT_CADENCE`) — a pre-paid period is one of the Paid Duration's periods that was already paid up front (`pay_beforehand_periods`, the Promotion's own column since migration 141), so it is labelled pre-paid rather than waived. **(#946)** The Pre-paid Duration is where that money is actually *collected*: `prepaidPeriodsDueOn()` (same module) says the whole duration is owed **once, on the first of its periods** — `€70/month` with 3 pre-paid periods charges `€210` there, and the periods it covers generate no Membership Fee event at all (`quantity: 0`, `walkStream()`'s existing "not a billing event"). Before it, every prepaid period priced at 0 with a `waive` benefit, so the Billing Event Simulation read `Waived · €0.00` where the gym had sold €210 and — because `POST /payment-requests` refuses a cycle that owes nothing — a brand-new prepaid assignment could not raise its first payment, never stored a card and was skipped by the nightly run for ever. The lump is the regular price of the periods it pays for (no benefit line, `prepaid_periods` on the line instead), it is still `promotional` in the horizon's sense so the projection runs on to the first ordinary charge, a governing Promotion still decides the fee alone, and the Personal Membership Fee Benefit still discounts it. Every path reaches it through `resolveMembershipFee()`, so the nightly run, a staff or member payment request, the Payments dashboard, My Membership, the Example Timeline and the Billing Event Simulation all answer €210 for that one cycle. A Promotion governing the same date decides the fee alone (§7 + the #635 thread's Q2 answer) — see "Assigned Plan snapshot" above.
- **The assignment's own Personal Membership Fee Benefit** (#772): a `percentage_discount` applied to the Membership Fee **last and on every cycle** (`source: 'personal'`, `period_status: null`). It stacks with everything above rather than competing with it — a Promotion that halves the fee and a personal 10% together charge 45% of the regular price — and it never expires, so the cycle after the Promotion's timeline ends still carries it. It deliberately does not mark a charge `promotional`: a benefit with no end would otherwise push the horizon below to its 36-month cap for every discounted assignment, and a cycle whose only benefit is the personal one *is* this contract's regular charge. See "Personal Membership Fee Benefit" above.
- **Granted Products**: `promotion_periodical` grants are projected at the item's own `billing_frequency` (4-week means 28 days, never a month), **treated** for the granted number of periods and at the regular price afterwards — since #896 stage 3 the treatment is the grant's own `(action, value)` pair folded onto the line through `applyPeriodBenefit()` (migration 203 backfilled every existing grant to `waive`, so "free for the granted periods" is what they all still do, and the line reports `waive` where it used to report `included`); a grant configured `no_benefit` changes neither the price nor the horizon; `promotion_oneoff` and `promotion_session` grants are a single line on the assignment's start date covering the granted quantity. Read from the `user_membership_promotion_*_snapshot` rows written when the Promotion was applied (#635 stage 3), keyed on `user_membership_promotions.id`; an application with no snapshot rows at all falls back to the live `promotion_session`/`_oneoff`/`_periodical` definitions, so editing or deleting a Promotion cannot move an assignment that has one (§16).
- **Horizon** (#629 §6 / thread Q3): every item is projected until it has been charged once at its *regular* price, and the whole simulation then runs to the latest of those dates — a yearly item pulls the monthly ones out with it. "Regular" means no Promotion governs the charge *and* none is still ahead of it: a paid promotional period with no benefit charges the regular price but is still inside the Promotion, and a charge that predates a Promotion applied mid-assignment comes before it. Since #635 stage 8 the assignment's own Billing & Duration counts the same way — a free or bonus month is never the regular charge, and a Paid Duration month is one only when no Bonus Duration follows it. Capped at 36 months, which sets `truncated: true` for a benefit with no end date. Charges a year or more after the item started carry `price_may_change: true`, flagging a possible annual price revision.
- **Statuses**: `active` and `paused` assignments are simulated; `cancelled`/`expired` bill nothing further.

The admin frontend renders it as its own `BILLING SIMULATION` section of the Member expanded row (`[locale]/members/MemberBillingSimulation.tsx`) — never inside a Membership Plan card (#634 §13) — and remounts it whenever the Member's plans change.

---

## Backend Route Registration (`index.ts`)

```ts
// Public — no auth, no tenant context (identified by gym slug)
app.use('/public', publicRouter);

// Auth but no tenant context (gymId not known yet)
app.use('/gyms',                  requireAuth(), gymsRouter);
app.use('/platform',              requireAuth(), platformRouter);            // superadmin only
app.use('/platform/superadmins',  requireAuth(), superadminsRouter);         // superadmin only
app.use('/platform/orphaned-accounts', requireAuth(), orphanedAccountsRouter); // superadmin only (#709)

// Link routes must run BEFORE tenantContext (no membership row exists yet on first link)
app.use('/me/link',        requireAuth(), meLinkRouter);
app.use('/staff/link', requireAuth(), staffLinkRouter);

// Member self-service — auth + tenant context (member role)
app.use('/me',             requireAuth(), tenantContext, meRouter);

// Team + all domain routes — auth + tenant context
app.use('/members',                requireAuth(), tenantContext, membersRouter);
app.use('/bookings',               requireAuth(), tenantContext, bookingsRouter);
app.use('/user-memberships',       requireAuth(), tenantContext, userMembershipsRouter);
app.use('/user-memberships/:id/promotions', requireAuth(), tenantContext, membershipPromotionsRouter);
app.use('/user-memberships/:id/services', requireAuth(), tenantContext, userMembershipServicesRouter);
app.use('/user-memberships/member/:memberId/billing-simulation', requireAuth(), tenantContext, memberBillingSimulationRouter);
app.use('/muscles',                requireAuth(), tenantContext, musclesRouter);
app.use('/result-types',           requireAuth(), tenantContext, resultTypesRouter);
app.use('/exercises',              requireAuth(), tenantContext, exercisesRouter);
app.use('/workouts',               requireAuth(), tenantContext, workoutsRouter);
app.use('/training-plan-templates',requireAuth(), tenantContext, trainingPlanTemplatesRouter);
app.use('/training-plans',         requireAuth(), tenantContext, gymTrainingPlansRouter);        // #67
app.use('/members/:memberId/training-plans',        requireAuth(), tenantContext, trainingPlansRouter);
app.use('/members/:memberId/member-training-plans', requireAuth(), tenantContext, memberTrainingPlansRouter);
app.use('/members/:memberId/workout-logs',   requireAuth(), tenantContext, memberWorkoutLogsRouter);
app.use('/audit-logs',             requireAuth(), tenantContext, auditLogsRouter);
app.use('/membership-plans',       requireAuth(), tenantContext, membershipPlansRouter);
app.use('/benefit-types',          requireAuth(), tenantContext, benefitTypesRouter);
app.use('/charge-types',           requireAuth(), tenantContext, chargeTypesRouter);
app.use('/products',         requireAuth(), tenantContext, productsRouter);
app.use('/taxes',                  requireAuth(), tenantContext, taxesRouter);
app.use('/billing-events',         requireAuth(), tenantContext, billingEventsRouter);
app.use('/spaces',                 requireAuth(), tenantContext, spacesRouter);
app.use('/trainers',               requireAuth(), tenantContext, trainersRouter);
app.use('/activity-types',         requireAuth(), tenantContext, activityTypesRouter);
app.use('/class-sessions',         requireAuth(), tenantContext, classSessionsRouter);
app.use('/class-packages',         requireAuth(), tenantContext, classPackagesRouter);
app.use('/action-types',           requireAuth(), tenantContext, actionTypesRouter);
app.use('/promotions',             requireAuth(), tenantContext, promotionsRouter);
app.use('/promotions/:id',         requireAuth(), tenantContext, promotionDetailsRouter);
app.use('/members/:memberId/class-packages', requireAuth(), tenantContext, userClassPackagesRouter);
app.use('/nutrition-plan-templates', requireAuth(), tenantContext, nutritionPlanTemplatesRouter);
app.use('/nutrition-library', requireAuth(), tenantContext, requireModuleAccess('NUTRITION'), nutritionLibraryRouter);
app.use('/member-nutrition-plans', requireAuth(), tenantContext, requireModuleAccess('NUTRITION'), memberNutritionPlansRouter);
app.use('/platform/nutrition-library', requireAuth(), platformNutritionLibraryRouter);
app.use('/platform/nutrition-plan-templates', requireAuth(), platformNutritionPlanTemplatesRouter);

// activity-eligibility and plan-center-access register booking-lifecycle hooks as
// side-effect imports. Since #635 stage 4 activity-eligibility is the only plan-based
// gate (it also claims a class-package credit when the plan does not cover the activity,
// via package-credits.ts), so their order no longer matters: each rejects on its own
// grounds — the plan is not eligible, or the plan does not cover the center.
```

Legacy `/fares` and `/subscriptions` routers are fully removed (migrations 004, 007, 009).

### A workout block's result is untyped free text (#1009)

Migration 074 (#154) moved the result type from the block down to the exercise
instance. Migration **209** completes that move on the logging side: it drops
`workout_block_logs.result_type` and its `wbl_result_type_check`, so the table is

```
workout_block_logs(id, gym_id, member_id, workout_block_id, logged_date,
                   started_at, finished_at, result_value, notes,
                   created_at, modified_at, modified_by_member_id)
```

`POST /me/workout-block-logs` read `wb.result_type` to snapshot into that column
and therefore answered `500 Unknown column 'wb.result_type' in 'field list'` for
every member who marked a block complete — the endpoint had no test. Its
pre-insert `SELECT` is now `SELECT 1`, which is the ownership probe behind its
403; the three other queries on the table are `SELECT *` and needed no edit.

The column is **dropped rather than re-vocabularied** because nothing read it:
every reader is a blanket projection (`SELECT *` here, `SELECT wbl.*` in
`exercise-logs.ts`), there is no caller in `apps/admin` for
`GET /members/:memberId/workout-block-logs`, none for `GET /me/workout-block-logs`,
and no report. A block has no single result type after #154 — each of its
exercises has its own — so keeping the column would have meant inventing what it
means when they disagree, and backfilling the rows holding `Rounds`/`Score`
(which have no member of the `result_types` slug vocabulary) with that
invention: a guess written into a member's training history.

So the contract is: **`result_value` is one optional, untyped free-text value**
(varchar(60)) the member typed for the block — `21:04`, `7 rounds + 3` — and an
empty input is `null`. Do not give it a type column, a derived type, a coerced
legacy value, or a per-block "takes no result" flag. Typed, per-set results are
the exercise instance's, in `exercise_logs` + `exercise_log_sets`.

`cleanupTestGyms()` deletes `workout_block_logs` **before** `members`:
`workout_block_id` is `ON DELETE RESTRICT` while deleting a member cascades
`training_plans` → `workouts` → `workout_blocks`, and MySQL does not order the
cascades one DELETE fans out into, so the block could go while its log still
pointed at it.

#### The gate on 074's dropped columns

Three queries were found reading one of them, one ticket at a time: #966's
Training Plan Template tree, this endpoint, and the Members App's My Training
page — where `block.result_type` was `undefined` (the server stopped sending it
at #154), so `undefined !== 'None'` passed and `undefined.toLowerCase()` threw
**while rendering any block**, taking the page down ahead of the 500 that was
reported. `api/src/test/migration-074-dropped-columns.unit.test.ts` is the gate:
no production `.ts`/`.tsx` under `api/src` or `apps/member/src` may name
`result_type`, `exercise_type`, `distance_value` or `distance_unit`, because
after migration 209 **no table has a column with any of those names**. There is
no per-file exemption — in particular none for the two routers and the page that
shipped the defect — and a test asserts all three are inside the scan, so it
cannot become a vacuous pass. `duration_seconds` is deliberately outside the
set: 074 dropped it from `workout_template_exercises` only, and it is still a
real column of `workout_blocks`, `workout_template_blocks` and `exercise_logs`.
The gate scans the Members App from the API suite because **CI runs `npm test` in
`api/` only** — the admin job type-checks and builds, so neither app's own
`src/test` runs there.

### What the global error handler answers (#966)

The handler at the bottom of `app.ts` logs the real error (`console.error`) and
then answers through `api/src/domain/httpErrorResponse.ts`, which is the one
place that decides what a client is told:

* an error carrying an explicit HTTP `status` — `Object.assign(new Error(m),
  { status: 400 })` as `centerContext.ts` throws, body-parser's own 400/413 — is
  a **deliberate** answer and keeps its own message;
* anything else is an unexpected failure, so the client gets `500
  { error: 'Internal server error' }`.

It used to answer `err?.message` verbatim with a 500, so a mysql2 error reached
the browser as a toast reading `Unknown column 'b.result_type' in 'field list'`
— a database implementation detail, and one naming a column and a table alias
to anyone who can provoke it. Routes that build their own error body are
unaffected: `handleDupEntry()` responds 409 itself, and the storage routes
answer with their own `stage`/`path`/`details` shape (#828) without reaching
this handler. A route that wants a specific message for a specific failure gives
the error a status; it must not rely on the handler forwarding a driver's words.

### Member invite + auto-link flow (`members` + `me`)

Unlike Team, a `members` row is the record of truth (name, contact, plan, billing) whether or not the member ever gets portal access — invite is a separate, optional action layered on top, not part of creating the member. Portal state is derived from two columns, never a `status` enum: `clerk_user_id` (set once linked) and `invitation_id` (the pending Clerk invitation, if any).

1. Staff calls `POST /members/:id/invite` (also `admin`,`staff`) → Clerk sends an invitation email with redirect to the member app (`CORDEL_FITNESS_MEMBERS_URL`), and the returned invitation id is stored in `members.invitation_id`. 409s if the member already has a linked `clerk_user_id`.
2. `POST /members/:id/reinvite` resends it (only valid while `clerk_user_id IS NULL AND invitation_id IS NOT NULL`) and **overwrites** `invitation_id` with the new one — this matters because the old id is no longer revocable once superseded (see the Team reinvite note below for the bug this avoids).
3. `POST /members/:id/revoke-invite` cancels a pending invitation without touching the member record — for when staff invited someone by mistake or changed their mind, but the member stays on the roster.
4. Member signs in via Clerk in `apps/member`.
5. On first sign-in the app calls `POST /me/link` (no `gym_memberships` row yet) — backend matches by email + gym_id (requiring `clerk_user_id IS NULL AND deleted_at IS NULL`), sets `members.clerk_user_id`, clears `invitation_id`, and inserts `gym_memberships(role='member')`.
6. Subsequent requests use `/me/*` routes with `tenantContext` resolving the member role.

**Orphaned Clerk accounts (#709).** A Clerk login is *linked* to Gymdesk when it is a superadmin (`publicMetadata.platform_role`), has a staff `gym_memberships` row (any role but `member`), or has an **active** `members` row (`clerk_user_id`, `deleted_at IS NULL`); a member-role `gym_memberships` row on its own does not count. That single definition lives in `infra/clerk-account-links.ts` (`loadAccountLinks`, pure `classifyAccount`, `unlinkClerkAccount`) and is used by all three places that could otherwise leave an account linked to nothing:
- **`DELETE /members/:id`**: when the member's login has no other link, the Clerk user is deleted **first** (502 and no DB change if Clerk fails, mirroring staff `revokeAccess({ deleteClerkUser: true })`), then this gym's member-role `gym_memberships` row is removed and `members.clerk_user_id` nulled; the same unlink happens when Clerk no longer has the account. A login still linked elsewhere (staff in another gym, superadmin) is kept **and the member stays linked to it**, so a Recycle Bin restore (`POST /recycle-bin/member/:id/recover`) reconnects them as before; a member whose login was deleted is restored unlinked and invited again.
- **Clerk `user.deleted` webhook** (delivered through the admin app's relay since #1085) → `unlinkClerkAccount(userId)`: every `gym_memberships` row for the user is deleted and `members.clerk_user_id` nulled (rows kept). Idempotent, so the echo of our own deletes is a no-op. Audited with `gym_id` NULL, source `system`. The event must be enabled on the Clerk webhook endpoint.
- **`/platform/orphaned-accounts`** (superadmin, *Cordel → Orphaned accounts* in the admin): `GET` pages through every Clerk user (`getUserList`, 500 per call) and returns the unlinked ones with a reason (`member_deleted`, `signup_incomplete`, `signup_in_progress`, `no_links`) and the gyms they used to belong to; `DELETE /:userId` re-classifies (409 if linked, or a sign-up younger than 24 h), deletes in Clerk first, then `unlinkClerkAccount`, audited as `clerk_account` via `recordPlatformAudit` (`infra/audit.ts`, the gym-less audit writer).

Every FK to `gym_memberships.id` is `ON DELETE SET NULL` or `CASCADE` (checked for #709), so removing member-role rows never fails on a reference.

**Removing a member with a pending, not-yet-accepted invitation revokes it.** `DELETE /members/:id` (soft-delete) checks `clerk_user_id IS NULL AND invitation_id IS NOT NULL` before setting `deleted_at`, and best-effort calls `clerkClient.invitations.revokeInvitation()` first (a revoke failure doesn't block the soft-delete) — same race this closes as the Team flow: `/me/link` already refuses to link into a soft-deleted (`deleted_at IS NOT NULL`) row, so without the revoke the only leftover risk was an orphaned, still-valid Clerk invitation email and a wasted invitation slot, not an actual access breach. Revoking it is cleanup, not a security fix, in this case — the DB-level `deleted_at` guard was already sufficient. This still depends on the Clerk instance being in **Restricted mode** (see "Auth (Clerk)" above) to stop a fresh, uninvited self-registration with the same email.

### Website self-registration (`public-registrations.ts` + `website-integration.ts` + `me`) — #599, #645

The one **write** route under `/public`: `POST /public/gyms/:gymRef/registrations` lets a gym's own website (WordPress — setup in `docs/wordpress-integration.md`) register a person by `{ name, email, locale? }` — to the **gym** only: no center (#757; a `center_id` sent by an older site is dropped by the schema, not rejected). It has no Clerk session; it is authenticated by a **per-gym API key** in `x-api-key` and is meant to be called server-to-server, so the key never reaches a browser and the API needs no CORS.

- **Key storage.** `gyms.website_api_key_hash` (salted scrypt, stored as `scrypt$<salt>$<digest>`), `website_api_key_prefix` (first 12 chars — shown in the UI) and `website_api_key_created_at` (migration 159). `infra/website-api-key.ts` generates/hashes/verifies (`timingSafeEqual`). scrypt is slow by design and this runs on an unauthenticated route, so `verifyWebsiteApiKey` compares the stored prefix **first**: a caller without the key never reaches the digest, and junk traffic cannot burn CPU. (A fast hash would be cryptographically sufficient for a 256-bit random key; scrypt is what CodeQL's `js/insufficient-password-hash` accepts, and the pre-filter makes it free.) Managed at **Cordel → Gyms → [Gym] → Website Integration** (#1052 — it was an item of the gym's own *Configuration* group until then) via the unchanged `GET /system/website-integration`, `POST …/key` (generate or rotate — returns the plaintext key **once**) and `DELETE …/key`; SYSTEM module, feature flag `system.website_integration`, audited as `website_api_key`. Those are tenant-scoped routes, and the section is on a **superadmin** screen, so it names the gym the card belongs to rather than the selected one: `apiFetch(path, { gymId })` (`apps/admin/src/lib/apiClient.ts`) is the one place that override exists, and `tenantContext` still decides whether the caller may use it — a superadmin gets admin on any gym it is handed, anyone else needs a `gym_memberships` row for it. The UI is `apps/admin/src/components/gyms/GymWebsiteIntegrationSection.tsx`, loaded when a gym's card is expanded (the key state is not in the list's row, and the endpoint URL is the API's to build, #645). Gym rows are read with `SELECT g.*` in `gyms.ts`, so `stripGymSecrets` there drops the hash from every response and audit payload — **any new secret column on `gyms` must be added to it.**
- **No `members` row at request time.** The handler only creates a Clerk invitation carrying `publicMetadata.gym_signup = { gym_id, name }` (plus `lang` for `ca`/`en`, #701) and the usual `…/<locale>/link?gym_id=` redirect. Clerk copies that metadata onto the new user, and `POST /me/link` — when no unlinked `members` row matches and `gym_signup.gym_id` equals `x-gym-id` — creates the `members` row (with **no** `member_centers` row — #757; a `center_id` left in older invitations' metadata is ignored) and `gym_memberships(role='member')` in one transaction, returns 201, then clears the metadata. The metadata is server-set, so it is the proof that our backend invited that email to that gym. A fake request therefore costs one unused invitation and never reaches the roster. If staff had already added the person (unlinked, never invited row), the endpoint behaves like the staff **Invite** button instead: no metadata, `members.invitation_id` stored.
- **Gym reference (#645).** `:gymRef` is `{gymId}-{gym-name}`: `parseGymRef` takes the leading `CHAR(36)` UUID and resolves the gym **by id**, so two gyms with the same name can never share an endpoint and a rename never breaks a configured website (the name half is never matched against anything). A reference that doesn't start with a gym id falls back to `gyms.slug`, the pre-#645 format, so WordPress installs configured before the change keep registering; the only way the two forms could overlap is a slug shaped like a UUID followed by a hyphen, which slugified gym names don't produce. `website-integration.ts` builds the advertised URL in one place (`gym_ref` / `endpoint_path` / `endpoint_url`), URL-encoding the name half because a slug set by hand is not guaranteed to be URL-safe.
- **Health check (#645).** Exactly `{ name: 'test', email: '' }` is a connectivity probe, not a registration: after the key check and gym resolution it answers `200 { ok: true, health_check: true }` and returns before validation — no member, no invitation, no email, nothing written. It is recognised by `isHealthCheckBody` (trimmed, case-insensitive name; empty email), which cannot collide with a real registration because the schema requires a non-empty email — the name `test` with a real address is a normal `202`. A probe skips the per-gym daily limiter (it writes nothing, so it must not spend a gym's registration quota) but not the per-IP one.
- **No enumeration.** Once the key and body are valid the answer is always `202 { ok: true }` — for a new email, a staff login (#594 guard, `isStaffLoginEmail`), an already linked/invited/soft-deleted member, an email on another gym's member record (`members.email` is unique platform-wide), or a Clerk `422` (invitation pending / account exists). Swallowing the `422` is also the per-email throttle: a repeat request sends no second email. Unknown gym reference and wrong key share one `401`. `400` is reserved for caller mistakes (invalid body), `502` for a Clerk outage.
- **Flood protection.** A per-IP limiter runs before any DB work (`PUBLIC_REGISTRATION_IP_LIMIT_PER_HOUR`, default 60) and a per-gym limiter runs **after** the key check (`PUBLIC_REGISTRATION_GYM_LIMIT_PER_DAY`, default 200) so only authenticated calls spend a gym's quota. CAPTCHA belongs on the website's form, not in the API — a platform-wide Turnstile secret would have to enumerate every gym's domain, and the key already proves the caller is the gym's server.
- **`trust proxy`.** The API runs behind a reverse proxy (Traefik on corfront; there is no nginx in front of it), so `app.ts` sets `trust proxy` to `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS` (default `1`). Without it `req.ip` is the proxy's address for every request and every per-IP limiter — including the global 500/15 min one — collapses into a single bucket shared by all clients.
- **Known limits (v1).** An email that already has a Clerk account gets no email (Clerk refuses to invite existing users — same as the staff Invite button), and the website cannot trigger a resend while an invitation is pending.

### Staff app access (`staff.ts` + `infra/staff-access.ts`) — #592

The **Staff** page is the only place an admin manages who can sign in to the admin app. The old Team page (`gym-users.ts`, `/team`, `/link-team`) was dropped from navigation in #77 and removed entirely in #592; its invite/grant/revoke mechanics moved, behavior intact, into `api/src/infra/staff-access.ts` (`grantAccess`, `resendInvitation`, `revokeAccess`, `linkGymInvite`).

Every Staff record owns at most one `gym_memberships` row in its gym via `staff.gym_membership_id`. The RBAC role is **derived from the HR `profile`** through `PROFILE_ROLE_MAP` in `infra/permissions.ts` (Gym Manager→`admin`, Personal Trainer→`trainer_performance`, Personal Trainer & Nutritionist→`trainer_perf_nutrition`, Front Desk→`front_desk`, Accountant→`accountant`, Nutritionist→`nutritionist`); an unknown profile is a 400. There is no separate role picker — changing a profile changes what the person can access.

- `POST /staff` (active employee) → `grantAccess`: if Clerk already knows the email, insert/update the membership row (status `active`, `name` = first + last, `COALESCE(?, name)` on update — #504); otherwise create a Clerk invitation carrying `publicMetadata.gym_invite = { gym_id, role }` and insert an `invited_<ts>` placeholder row. The link is stored immediately in both cases. A Clerk failure does **not** roll back the staff row — the 201 carries `access: { status: 'error', error }` and the admin retries from the row. An employee created `inactive` gets no login (`access.status = 'not_enrolled'`).
