- `PUT /staff/:id` → the login follows the record: profile change syncs `gm.role` (self-edit and last-admin guards → 400, same as the old Team role edit), name change syncs `gm.name`, and a changed email on a still-pending invite revokes the old invitation and issues a new one. **Save also keeps login and status in step:** an `active` record with no login gets one exactly as on create (records that predate #592 heal on their next save; re-activating restores the login), and saving a record as `inactive` removes its login like `PATCH /deactivate`. The response carries `access` whenever a login was granted or removed; the form warns before saving that an invitation email may be sent.
- `PATCH /staff/:id/deactivate` → `revokeAccess({ deleteClerkUser: false })`: pending invite revoked (best-effort) / membership row deleted, Clerk account kept; `gym_membership_id` cleared. Re-activation just needs *Send invitation* again.
- `DELETE /staff/:id` → `revokeAccess({ deleteClerkUser: true })`: as above, plus the Clerk user is deleted when this was their last gym membership anywhere — that delete must succeed first (502 otherwise), so a failure never orphans the account.
- `POST /staff/:id/access` → idempotent retry/resend: not enrolled → grant/invite (201); pending → resend and **overwrite** `invitation_id` (the superseded id is no longer revocable, so a later revoke must target the live one); active → `{ status: 'already_granted' }`.
- `DELETE /staff/:id/access` → revoke only, staff row untouched (204).
- `POST /staff/link` (mounted before `tenantContext`, like `/me/link`) → `linkGymInvite(userId)`: on the invitee's first sign-in the admin app's `GymContext` self-heal calls it; it flips the placeholder row to the real Clerk `user_id` (the Staff link keeps pointing at the same row) and clears the metadata. The Clerk `user.created` webhook calls the same function so activation happens whichever fires first.

**One email is never both member and staff of the same gym (#594).** A staff member signs in with their work account; if the same person is also a member, that membership lives on a separate personal account. Because `gym_memberships` holds a single role per `(user_id, gym_id)`, letting both flows write it would silently flip the role and break whichever app lost (every `/me/*` route is `requireRole('member')`; `/me/link` can't recreate a deleted member row because `clerk_user_id` is already set). So both sides refuse with **409** instead: `assertNotMemberEmail` (staff side — active `members` row on that email in the gym, or the Clerk account already holding a `member` row there) runs in `POST /staff` **before** the insert, on `PUT /staff/:id` when the email changes, and inside `grantAccess`; `isStaffLoginEmail` (member side — a Staff record with a linked login, or a pending staff-invite placeholder on that email) guards `POST /members` and `POST /members/:id/invite`; and `POST /me/link` refuses when the signing-in account already holds a non-member row in the gym (this also covers logins with no Staff record, e.g. the gym owner). Messages: `MEMBER_EMAIL_CONFLICT` / `STAFF_EMAIL_CONFLICT` in `staff-access.ts`.

`GET /staff/:id/clerk-status` is unchanged and drives the row's **App access** section (`not_enrolled` / `invited` / `active` / `suspended` / `error`). All access mutations are audited under the existing `gym_user` entity (the audit registry entry stays for historical rows).

**Removing an invited (not-yet-accepted) user is a revoke, not a delete.** This closes a race: an admin invites someone, then removes them before they click the email link — without revocation, the `gym_memberships` row would be gone from the app but the Clerk invitation would still be live, so accepting it later would silently recreate access. `revokeAccess` therefore calls `clerkClient.invitations.revokeInvitation(invitation_id)` before deleting a pending row (best-effort — a revoke failure doesn't block the row deletion).

Revoking the invitation closes the race for the *link itself* (Clerk shows "The invitation was revoked" if they click it), but does **not** by itself stop that email from just self-registering fresh at `/sign-up` — that's a separate, instance-wide Clerk setting, not something this endpoint can control. See "Required Clerk instance setting: Restricted mode" above; with it enabled, sign-up requires a currently-valid invitation, so a revoked one is rejected outright instead of letting a brand-new, gym-less account through.

---

## Audit Logging (`infra/audit.ts` + `audit_logs`)

`recordAudit(req, { action, entityType, entityId?, entityName?, previous?, next? })` is a **fire-and-forget** writer: it never fails the calling request (a rejected INSERT just logs to `console.error`). Each row is a self-contained historical snapshot:

- **`actor_name`** — display name captured from the Clerk user object already fetched in `tenantContext` (zero extra queries). Populated on every write; survives later Clerk user renames.
- **`entity_name`** — resolved by `infra/audit-registry.ts` at write time. Named entities (those with a `name` column) use a registry lookup; M-N/link entities (e.g. `user_membership`) get a composed label from their FK parents. Callers may pass `entityName` directly to skip the lookup. The UI shows "Unknown" (translated) when the field is null.
- **FK enrichment** — `previous_values`/`new_values` are enriched before storage: `foo_id: value` becomes `foo: { id, name }` using the FK map in `audit-registry.ts`.

The registry (`AUDIT_ENTITY_REGISTRY`) and action list (`AUDIT_ACTIONS`) are served by `GET /audit-logs/meta` for frontend dropdown population. High-value mutations call `recordAudit` after the business write; `class-types.ts` and `promotions.ts` were added in #69.

**A new `entityType` belongs in the registry, not only in the `recordAudit` call.** An unregistered type still writes rows, but they resolve no `entity_name` and the type is absent from `GET /audit-logs/meta` — so the Audit Log's entity-type dropdown cannot show it, and a Details view deep-linking to it (below) lands on a filter whose select has no matching option. #675 registered the nine types the Details views needed (`activity_type`, `theme`, `tax_rate`, `product`, `professional_service`, `nutrition_library_item`, `nutrition_plan_template`, `member_nutrition_plan`, `staff`).

Two read views share one endpoint and one React component (`AuditLogView`): **System → Audit log** (`/audit`, admin+) is scoped to the active gym; **Cordel → Audit log** (`/cordel/audit`, superadmin) sends `?scope=all` to see every gym's events with a Gym column joined in (#66). Filters: `entity_type` (dropdown), `entity_id` (exact match), `entity_name` (LIKE on stored snapshot), `actor` (name LIKE or Clerk ID exact), `action` (dropdown), `source` (dropdown), `from`/`to` date range.

`entity_type`, `entity_id` and `entity_name` are also read from the query string on mount, so any page can deep-link to one record's history — the Members → Details modal's **View Audit Log** button opens `/{locale}/audit?entity_type=member&entity_id=<id>` (#642). Link by id, never by name: `entity_name` is a snapshot and is neither unique nor stable. The `entity_id` filter is served by the existing `(gym_id, entity_type, entity_id)` index.

**Every** Details view offers that deep link (#675), through one component — `apps/admin/src/components/ViewAuditLogButton.tsx`. It owns the permission rule (superadmin, or a gym `admin` with the `system` and `system.audit` flags on — the same rule the sidebar applies, re-checked in `AuditLogView` and in the API) and the URL shape, and renders nothing when the viewer may not read the log or the record has no id. `scope="platform"` targets `/cordel/audit` instead of `/audit`, for entities administered outside a single gym (Gyms, base Themes, the Cordel Nutrition Library); `size="small"` suits an inline expanded row rather than a modal footer. In a `CrudModal` it goes in `extraFooter`. Its label is `common.action_view_audit_log`. See `docs/feature-patterns.md` → *Details view → View Audit Log*.

---

## Frontend Patterns

Both frontends follow the same proxy + context pattern. The admin app uses `GymContext`; the member app uses `AppContext`.

### API calls
All requests go through `apiFetch()` (from `useApiClient()`), which:
1. Gets a fresh Clerk token.
2. Attaches `Authorization: Bearer <token>`.
3. Attaches `x-gym-id: <activeGymId>`.
4. Hits `/api/proxy/<path>` → Next.js proxy route → backend. The proxy MUST stay on the Node runtime (no `runtime = 'edge'`). The API has no CORS — it is reachable only through these proxies. The proxy target is `CORDEL_FITNESS_API_URL` (deployed: `https://api.vdicube.com`; local dev: `http://localhost:3000`).

**Body passthrough gotcha (#830)**: both proxy routes read the backend's response with `await res.arrayBuffer()` and forward the bytes — never `res.text()`. Most responses are JSON, but not all: `GET /themes/:id/logo` answers raw image bytes (how a blob-backed logo, i.e. any **Base Theme**'s, is rendered) and `POST /payments/:id/receipt` answers a PDF (`pdfFetch()` reads it with `res.blob()`). A UTF-8 decode replaces every byte that is not valid UTF-8 with U+FFFD, and because the response still carries the right `Content-Type` and status nothing errors — the browser simply cannot decode the image, which is what rendered the Theme editor's `logo preview` as a broken image until #830. `ArrayBuffer` is byte-exact for JSON too, so there is no reason to branch on the content type.

**Header allow-list gotcha (#362)**: the proxy route (`app/api/proxy/[...path]/route.ts` in both `apps/admin` and `apps/member`) only forwards headers on an explicit allow-list (`authorization`, `x-gym-id`, `x-center-id`, `x-impersonate-as`, `content-type`). Any new request header the frontend needs the backend to see (e.g. a future context header) must be added to this allow-list in **both** apps' proxy routes, or it is silently dropped and the backend never sees it — this is exactly what broke impersonation end-to-end until #362 fixed the member app's proxy.

### Backoffice: GymContext
Available via `useGym()`. Key fields:
```ts
activeGymId: string | null
activeGym: { id, name, slug, role, theme_key? } | null
isSuperadmin: boolean
loading: boolean
```

### Backoffice: CenterContext (#930 — its one control is the Calendar's)
Available via `useCenter()` (`context/CenterContext.tsx`): the gym's active centers, the selected `activeCenterId` (`null` = all centers, persisted per gym in `localStorage`), and `refreshCenters()`. `apiClient` sends the selection as `x-center-id` on every call, where it does exactly one thing: `resolveCenterId()` defaults the center of a **write** that names none (class sessions, spaces, rooms, trainer availability). It has never filtered a read on the admin side — `getCenterContext()` is read only by `centers.ts` (a member's `allowedCenterIds`) and `me.ts` — so a read that wants one center passes `center_id` as a query param.

The control that sets it used to be `CenterSelector` in `TopHeader`, on every page. **#930 removed it**: a gym-wide header dropdown read as a selection every section obeyed, and the Members list never did (it has had its own `All centers` filter all along). The context stayed, and its one control is now `[locale]/calendar/CalendarCenterFilter.tsx` in the Calendar's filter bar — center selection is a Calendar concern. Choosing a center there also narrows the calendar itself, which the header dropdown never did: the page passes `center_id` to `GET /calendar-events` and `GET /class-sessions`. Nothing renders for a gym with one center, since `resolveCenterId()`'s sole-active-center fallback already decides it. Do not reintroduce a global/top-header center control, and do not fork the context into a second per-page selection — the center theme `ThemeProvider` paints and the write defaults above both read this one value.

### Member app: AppContext
Available via `useApp()`. Key fields:
```ts
gymId: string | null      // from URL param or localStorage
member: MemberProfile | null
isLinked: boolean
loading: boolean
```

### Admin navigation (grouped sidebar)
The sidebar is **config-driven** from `config/navigationGroups.ts`. Groups are collapsible; each group and item can declare a `requiredRole` (`staff | admin | superadmin`, hierarchical) and `filterNavGroups()` hides anything above the user's role. Expanded state persists in `sessionStorage`; the group containing the active route auto-expands (that effect compared `pathname` against the config's unresolved `/{{locale}}/…` hrefs until #1003, so it never fired — it goes through the locale-resolved items now, like the rest of the component). **Impersonation precedence (#439)**: `Sidebar.tsx` computes `filterNavGroups`'s `userRole` as `(isSuperadmin && !isImpersonating) ? 'superadmin' : (activeGym?.role ?? 'member')` — `isImpersonating` from `useImpersonation()`. The native-superadmin bypass (both the `requiredRole: 'superadmin'` Cordel group and every `featureKey` check) does not apply while impersonating; nav reflects the impersonated user's own role and flags, matching the backend's `requireFeatureEnabled` (see "Member Web feature flags" above). `FeatureFlagsContext.tsx` polls every 30s (matching the backend's in-memory cache TTL) so a toggle in Cordel → Feature Flags reaches an already-open session without a reload. Current groups:

**First-level icons (#884)**: every group carries a required `icon: NavIconName` in the same config, and `NavGroup` renders it through `<NavIcon name={group.icon} />` — nothing branches on a group id or a label to pick one, and a group added without an icon does not compile. The set lives in `apps/admin/src/components/icons/` (`navIconNames.ts`, the JSX-free name list the config imports its type from, plus `NavIcons.tsx`, the nine drawings and the `NAV_ICONS` registry); a new name goes in **both**. Every icon is drawn on one 24×24 grid through a single shared `IconShell` at `NAV_ICON_SIZE` (16px, the chevron's own box) and `NAV_ICON_STROKE` (1.75), stroked in `currentColor` and filled with nothing, which is what makes it follow the header's active/hover colour instead of carrying a state of its own; each `<svg>` is `aria-hidden` and `focusable="false"`, so the accessible name stays the label. The icon sits **beside** the expand/collapse chevron, never instead of it, and the group header's padding is 16px with a 6px gap so the longest uppercase label (`ENTRENAMIENTO`, `CONFIGURACIÓN` — 196px of the 250px sidebar) still fits on one line. Second-level items stay text-only. No icon library is installed: for nine icons a dependency costs more than it saves.

**Collapsing it on desktop (#1003)**: the sidebar has two states — expanded (labels and subsections, 250px since #1033) and collapsed (the group icons alone, 64px) — and `apps/admin/src/lib/sidebarCollapse.ts` is the one place that decides all of it: the two widths (`sidebarWidth()`), the breakpoint it applies at (`SIDEBAR_DESKTOP_MEDIA_QUERY`, `(min-width: 769px)`, mirroring AppShell's own split), the stored preference (`readSidebarCollapsed()`/`writeSidebarCollapsed()`, `localStorage`, every access in a `try/catch`) and — the load-bearing part — `navGroupContainsActivePath()`, the single rule for *which group holds the open page*. The active navigation item is always a **subsection**, so collapsed there is nothing left to highlight but its parent section's icon; if the two modes asked that question separately they could disagree, and collapsing the sidebar would move the highlight. The collapsed icon takes the treatment an active item already has (`--gd-sidebar-selected-bg`/`-text` plus the 3px `--brand` rail) and no colour of its own, and it is still **not** an active item: clicking it navigates nowhere — it brings the sidebar back and opens that group (`expandIntoGroup()`), which is what keeps every option reachable without a second flyout menu. The expanded header is untouched (`collapsed && isAnyChildActive` gates the highlight), the label becomes the icon's `title` and `aria-label` since the `<svg>` is `aria-hidden`, and `NavGroup` renders the same one `<NavIcon>` at the same `NAV_ICON_SIZE` in both modes. The width lives on `.sidebar-panel` with a 150ms transition, so `main` — the row's flexible item — takes the space back by itself and AppShell needed no change at all. The two states are **independent** of which groups are open: collapsing never writes `navGroupsExpanded` (`sessionStorage`, per tab) and the preference is `sidebarCollapsed` (`localStorage`, a lasting layout choice). Collapsing is desktop-only (§5): below the breakpoint the panel is the drawer, open at full width or off-screen, so the preference is ignored rather than shrinking the drawer to a strip. Frontend-only — no migration, no API, no new Theme setting — plus two locale keys (`nav.collapse_sidebar`, `nav.expand_sidebar`). Covered by `apps/admin/src/test/sidebar-collapse.test.ts`.

**Its dividers (#1020)**: two, declared once in `apps/admin/src/components/navChrome.ts` — the 1px hairline `NavItem.separatorAbove` draws *inside* a group, and the 2px rule above a first-level **section** (`NavGroup.separatorAbove`, carried today by Cordel alone). Both widths sit beside each other there, so "thicker than the standard separator" is a relationship in code rather than two literals in the JSX, and both draw in the same `rgba(255,255,255,0.15)` the rest of the sidebar's chrome uses: the `--gd-sidebar-*` set is the background, the text and the selected/hover pair, so there is no separator token a Theme can move and a divider must not invent a colour of its own. Which section carries the rule is navigation config beside `icon` (#884's rule: nothing branches on a group's id or label), and `Sidebar` is the only place that suppresses it for the **first visible** group — a leading rule separates a section from nothing, and which groups a role can see is the sidebar's to know. Collapsed (#1003), the rule stays and only its inset narrows (16px → 12px), since a 64px strip would show a stub. Covered by `apps/admin/src/test/sidebar-cordel-separator.test.ts`.

| Group | requiredRole | Items |
|-------|--------------|-------|
| Membership | — | Dashboard, Members (→ Deleted) |
| Organization | `admin` | Dashboard, Team, Centers, Rooms, Resources, Trainers, Specialities, Class types, Class packages, Events |
| Training | — | Dashboard, Exercises, Workout Templates, Training Plan Templates, ‹divider›, Assigned Training Plans (#67, renamed #970) |
| Nutrition | — | Dashboard (placeholder) |
| Payments | — | Transactions |
| Financials | `admin` | Dashboard, Plans, Promotions, Products, Assigned Plans, Taxes |
| System | `admin` | Audit log (gym-scoped) |
| Cordel | `superadmin` | Gyms, Users, Audit log (platform-wide, `/cordel/audit`), Themes, Payment Providers (#636) |

The per-group "Dashboard" pages (Organization, Training, Nutrition, Financials, plus the singular `/membership`) are **placeholder shells** ("coming soon") today. Some functional pages (e.g. `/memberships` — the user-membership manager with ledger + promotion-apply modals) exist and are reachable but are not yet linked from a nav group.

### AppShell (admin)
Wraps all admin pages. Hides sidebar + header for sign-in/sign-up and the unauthenticated home page.

**The navigation drawer and its one scroll container (#883).** Below 769px the sidebar is a fixed drawer that slides in from the left. Three elements share the job and each owns exactly one part of it:

* `.sidebar-wrapper` (AppShell's `<style>` block) **bounds** the drawer — `height: calc(100dvh - var(--gd-top-bar-h))`, with the `100vh` form declared first as the fallback for browsers without dynamic viewport units, plus `overflow: hidden`. It is a `flex-direction: column` container on both breakpoints.
* `<aside class="sidebar-panel">` (`Sidebar.tsx`) **fills** the wrapper (`flex: 1; min-height: 0`) and declares no height of its own. It used to carry `min-height: 100vh`, which on mobile made the panel taller than the drawer that contains it: the bottom groups were pushed past the viewport with nothing able to scroll them back, and expanding a group made it worse.
* `<nav>` is the **one** scroll container (`flex: 1; min-height: 0; overflow-y: auto`), with `overscroll-behavior: contain` so a swipe reaching either end does not chain to the page behind, and `-webkit-overflow-scrolling: touch` for native momentum. It is a block container, not a column flex box — flex children would shrink to fit instead of overflowing, so an expanded group would squeeze the others rather than scroll.

While the drawer is open AppShell toggles `sidebar-drawer-open` on `<body>`, whose `overflow: hidden` rule lives inside the mobile media query only, so the desktop sidebar is untouched. Desktop keeps `position: relative` and imposes no height or overflow of its own; the panel stretches to the shell's `min-height: 100vh` flex line exactly as before. Covered by `apps/admin/src/test/mobile-sidebar-scroll.test.ts`.

### Theming (`ThemeProvider` + per-gym `theme_id`)

**Two tiers of themes (migration 065):**
- **Base Themes** — `gym_id IS NULL`. Platform-owned, managed by superadmins via **Cordel → Base Themes**. Never duplicated into customer data. Exactly one Base Theme carries `is_system_default = 1` (migration 072) — the factory default assigned when a new organization is created.
- **Customer Themes** — `gym_id = <gymId>`. Gym-owned, created by cloning any Base or Customer Theme. Editable and soft-deletable by gym admins via **System → Themes**.

Each gym references an optional **Theme** entity (`gyms.theme_id`, migration 057, FK on `themes.id`). Themes carry a versioned `tokens` JSON column (`v: 4` as of migration 157) covering typography (5 levels × `fontFamily` + `color`) and 33 named colors grouped as: **Application** (`pageBackground`), **Cards** (`cardBackground`, `cardBorder`), **Text** (`textColor` — labeled "Primary Text Color" in the editor, `secondaryTextColor`, `mutedTextColor`, `sectionHeadingTextColor`), **Separators** (`separatorColor` — application-wide, distinct from `headerSeparatorColor`), **Inputs** (`inputBackgroundColor`, `inputBorderColor`), **Header** (`headerBackground`, `headerText`, `headerSeparatorColor`, `headerSeparatorHeight`), **Sidebar** (`sidebarBackground`, `sidebarText`, `sidebarSelectedItemBackground`, `sidebarSelectedItemText`, `sidebarHoverBackground`), **Navigation** (`dropdownBackground`, `dropdownText`, `dropdownHoverBackground`), **Buttons** (`primaryButton`, `primaryButtonText`, `secondaryButton`, `secondaryButtonText`), **Status** (`statusSuccess`, `statusWarning`, `statusError`, `statusInfo`), **Links** (`linkColor`, `linkHoverColor`). `sectionHeadingTextColor` (#558, migration 157) drives `--gd-section-heading-text`, used by the theme editors' own accordion section titles and the `ThemeColorsEditor`/`renderReadOnly` per-group headings (e.g. "APLICACIÓN") — the theme card's other two previously-hardcoded elements named in #558 (empty-state text, inner card dividers) reuse existing tokens instead of adding new ones: `mutedTextColor`/`--gd-text-muted` (same one `DataTable`'s empty-rows message already uses) and `separatorColor`/`--gd-border` (same one several other admin pages' inline-editor row dividers already use) respectively — per #558's own "do not introduce duplicate properties if an existing token can correctly control the same UI element" requirement. Validation (`validateTokens`/`defaultTokens`, `HEX_RE`, `FONT_STACKS`) lives in the shared `api/src/domain/themeTokens.ts` (#489 stage 1), used by both `api/src/api/themes.ts` (superadmin base themes) and `api/src/api/gym-themes.ts` (gym-admin customer themes). Covered by unit tests (`api/src/test/theme-tokens.test.ts`) and, for the actual HTTP round trip through both routers — including that a legacy/partial `tokens.colors` shape and an existing `tokens.advanced` map persist unchanged — by integration tests in `api/src/test/themes.test.ts`/`gym-themes.test.ts` (#489 stage 5). ~52 finer-grained design tokens (layout, shape, shadows, per-component colors, animations) live in the `advanced` JSONB map; the Admin theme editors no longer group them under a standalone "Advanced" section — each attribute's `group` (`apps/admin/src/lib/themeTokens.ts`'s `ADVANCED_ATTRIBUTES`) matches one of the color groups above (plus **Cards** and a colorless **Tables** group) so it renders inline with that component's colors (#489 stage 2). Most of them are still admin-editable but not applied to any CSS variable anywhere — a pre-existing gap noted during #489's audit and explicitly out of scope for stages 1–4 (wiring it would be redone once the Advanced section's fate was settled; #489 stage 2 already deleted the standalone Advanced section by redistributing these attributes into the per-component groups above). Two groups have since been wired: the six `calendar*` attributes (#559 stage 2, below) and the Cards group's `cardBorderRadius` (#677) — see **Card surfaces** below.

**Card surfaces (#677):** the Cards group's two settings now reach the cards. `cardBorder` was already written as `--gd-card-border`, but `cardBorderRadius` was editable and persisted while never reaching a CSS variable at all, so configuring the Card Border Radius did nothing; `applyTokens()` now writes it as `--gd-card-radius` through the exported `CARD_ADVANCED_VARS` map (same shape as `CALENDAR_ADVANCED_VARS`, falling back to `DEFAULT_ADVANCED` for a theme with no `advanced` map or a blank value, via the shared `cssLengthValue()`). Independently of the variable, most admin card surfaces restated a border colour (`#e2e2e6`, `#ececf0`, `#e0e0e8`) and a `borderRadius` of their own, so even the border colour only applied on the pages that happened to use the variable. All of them — every `cardStyle`/`cardSt` plus the inline list cards on Themes, Staff and Centers — now spread one shared `cardSurfaceStyle` from `apps/admin/src/components/ui.tsx` (`border` + `borderRadius` + `background`, each `var()` with its default token value as the CSS fallback); a highlighted or being-edited card overrides `border` on top of the spread and keeps the themed radius. The base ↔ custom relationship is unchanged: both kinds of theme are applied through the same `applyTokens()`, so a base theme's values paint the cards when it is the applied theme and a custom theme's values override them when it is. Frontend-only — no schema, API or validation change (`themes.tokens` is schema-less JSON and `validateTokens()` does not format-check `advanced`). One deliberate visual change: card surfaces that hardcoded `borderRadius: 10` now follow the token, whose default is `8px`. Covered by `apps/admin/src/test/theme-card-css.test.ts` (variable emission for configured/legacy/blank values, the CSS fallback matching the default token, and a source scan asserting no card style declares a border radius of its own).

**Primary action buttons (#901):** the Buttons group's `primaryButton`/`primaryButtonText` were written as `--gd-primary-btn`/`--gd-primary-btn-text` by both apps' `applyTokens()` and consumed by nothing — editable, persisted, inert, the same gap `cardBorderRadius` had before #677. The subsection `Edit` button of the Membership Plan and Promotion cards is the first consumer: `apps/admin/src/components/SectionEditButton.tsx` is the one component both pages render for that action, and its background and label colour are those two variables, with the old hardcoded lilac (`#6c63ff`, which is also `DEFAULT_TOKENS`' value for `primaryButton`) kept only as the CSS `var()` fallback for the frames before `applyTokens()` has run. Before the ticket the same action was styled twice and differently — Promotions passed the literal to `btnSmall()`, Plans used a page-local `linkBtn` text link off `--brand` (which is `sidebarSelectedItemBackground`, not an action colour) — so neither followed the Theme's Buttons group. Frontend-only: no schema, API, validation or locale change, and no new Theme setting (#901 §4 asks explicitly that an existing one be reused). Each page still owns the label, the `disabled` decision and whether the button is rendered at all — it stays **absent** outside `⋮ → Edit` (#816/#897). Covered by `apps/admin/src/test/section-edit-button.test.ts`. **#963** decided *where* that action sits: both cards laid their section header out with `justifyContent: 'space-between'`, so on a wide screen a card's width of empty space separated `BILLING & DURATION` from the `[ Edit ]` that opens it, and each section editor's Save/Cancel sat at the far right under its fields rather than in the header of the section it commits. `apps/admin/src/components/CardSectionHeader.tsx` is the one header row for both cards now — title first (a child, not a slot, so an action can never precede it), actions immediately after, `flexWrap: 'wrap'` on the row and on the actions group for phone width, and the heading taken from `formChrome.ts`'s `cardSectionLabelStyle`, which retires both pages' local `sectionLabelSt` copies. `Edit` and the Save/Cancel pair are the two states of that slot; the editor's error line stays in the body; Plans' *Apply new price to assigned plans* and the Promotion card's *Retry* for Suitable Membership Plans moved with them, since both are subsection actions. A card's own form pair (Plans' GENERAL, the Promotion card's main configuration) deliberately stays at the end of the fields it commits. Layout only — no handler, label, gate, locale key, API call or calculation moved — and the Assigned Plan card is explicitly out of scope. Covered by `apps/admin/src/test/section-action-placement.test.ts`. **#1028** answered the remaining half of that question — *which side* a form's pair sits on — and made all three rows agree: `formActionsRowStyle` was right-aligned while `inlineActionsRowStyle` was not, and about thirty entity forms spelled a `justifyContent: 'flex-end'` of their own, so Spaces' `[Cancel] [Save changes]` sat at the far right of a card whose fields start at the content margin, Products' pair at the left, and Staff's read `[Save] [Cancel]`. None of the rows declares a justification now — flex's own default is the rule — a third row (`modalActionsRowStyle`) covers the Modal CRUD shape's footer and the hand-rolled `New <entity>` dialogs, and every entity create/edit form spreads one of them instead of a layout of its own. Layout only, as #963 was: no label, handler, permission gate, `disabled` state, locale key, payload or endpoint moved, and the button colours stay #912/#954's. `ConfirmDialog`, `DependencyDialog` and `CrudModal`'s `hideSave` branch keep a right-aligned footer on purpose — a destructive confirmation's pair and a read-only Details view's `Close` are not form actions — and `apps/admin/src/test/form-actions-alignment.test.ts` asserts that set rather than consulting an allowlist, so a fourth one fails the build. **#912** extended that to the Theme editor's own primary actions — `+ Assign Centers…` and `Save changes` on Custom Themes, `Save changes` and `+ Add` on Base Themes — which were still painted in the hardcoded lilac, so a gym could set *Primary Button = red* in the very editor whose buttons ignored it. The `var()` fallback pair is declared **once**, as `primaryActionColors` in `apps/admin/src/components/ui.tsx`, with `primaryBtnStyle()` / `primaryBtnSmall()` spreading it over `btnStyle()` / `btnSmall()`'s geometry; `sectionEditButtonStyle` is now `primaryBtnSmall()` and `SectionEditButton.tsx` carries no colour literal at all. Note that spreading the pair is *not* the same as dropping `btnStyle()` / `btnSmall()`'s argument — their own default is `--brand`, which `applyTokens()` maps to `sidebarSelectedItemBackground`, a different Theme setting and not an action colour. A new primary action calls one of the two helpers rather than spelling `--gd-primary-btn` again; secondary (`#888`), neutral-upload (`#444`) and destructive (`#c0392b`) buttons are deliberately untouched, as is the *show all centers* disclosure text link (a link, whose token would be the Links group's `--gd-link`) and `CrudModal`'s shared Save. Covered by `apps/admin/src/test/theme-primary-buttons.test.ts`. **#954** finished the job app-wide. #912 had themed the Theme editor, so a gym that set *Primary Button* saw it on `Save changes` there and on the subsection `Edit` buttons — and nowhere else: 56 call sites across 27 files still passed the lilac literal to `btnStyle()`/`btnSmall()`, and the two Calendar detail panels spelled `background: '#6c63ff', color: '#fff'` inline on their own full-width geometry. Every one of them is a *filled primary action* — Save, Add, Create, New, Assign, Apply, a modal's submit, `CrudModal`'s shared Save — and now derives from `primaryBtnStyle()`/`primaryBtnSmall()`, or, where the geometry is the button's own, spreads `primaryActionColors` in place of that colour pair so the `opacity`/`cursor` overrides after it still win. Nothing else moved: no token, no helper, no geometry, no label, no `disabled` decision, no permission gate (a `readOnlyStyle(primaryBtnStyle(), !canWrite)` dims the themed colours exactly as it dimmed the lilac), and no locale key. Deliberately left lilac: the three file-picker controls (`ImageUploadField`, `ExerciseImageField`, `ExerciseVideoField`) — a picker is not its form's primary action, its Save is — plus text links (whose token is the Links group's `--gd-link`), selected filter chips and selection/accent borders, which carry state rather than an action. The enforcement is a scan: `theme-primary-buttons.test.ts` walks every `.ts`/`.tsx` under `apps/admin/src` outside `src/test` and fails on a `btnStyle('#6c63ff')`, a `btnSmall('#6c63ff')` or a flat `background: '#6c63ff'` outside that named list, and on a module other than `ui.tsx` (and `themeTokens.ts`, which writes them) naming `--gd-primary-btn` for itself. **#968 decided two of those pickers the other way:** `ExerciseImageField` and `ExerciseVideoField` take `primaryBtnSmall()`, because the Base Exercise form's `Upload Image`/`Upload Video` were the only lilac left in a view whose Save already followed the Theme — `ImageUploadField`, which that form does not render, keeps the exception and is still the file the scan names. The same ticket moved the one Exercise editor's Save/Cancel pair onto `inlineActionsRowStyle` + `secondaryBtnSmall` (left-aligned, the shape every other inline editor uses) and the Base Exercises page's `+ New Exercise` from `btnStyle()` — i.e. `--brand`, the sidebar's colour — onto `primaryBtnStyle()`; `apps/admin/src/test/exercise-form-actions.test.ts` pins all of it.

**Calendar section (#559, complete — stages 1–4):** a `group_calendar` section in both theme editors, covering 18 `calendar*` colors on `tokens.colors` (`calendarBackground`, `calendarSurfaceBackground`, `calendarHeaderBackground`/`calendarHeaderText`, `calendarDayText`, `calendarMutedDayText`, `calendarTodayBackground`, `calendarSelectionBackground`, `calendarGridBorder`, `calendarTimeAxisBackground`/`calendarTimeAxisText`, `calendarWeekendBackground`, `calendarDisabledSlotBackground`, `calendarEventBackground`/`calendarEventBorder`/`calendarEventText`, `calendarNavButtonBackground`/`calendarNavButtonText`) plus 6 `calendar*` entries in the `advanced` map (`calendarEventBorderRadius`, `calendarEventSelectedOverlay`, `calendarEventHoverBackground`, `calendarSlotHeight`, `calendarNavButtonHoverBackground`, `calendarNavButtonBorderRadius`). The last three colors and `calendarEventHoverBackground` arrived in stage 3; stage 1 shipped the rest. The field list is scoped to what the admin Calendar (`@fullcalendar/react`, views `timeGridDay`/`timeGridWeek`/`dayGridMonth`) actually renders, so two things the ticket listed are deliberately **not** tokens: a now-indicator color (`nowIndicator` isn't enabled on the calendar) and an empty-state color (dayGrid/timeGrid have no empty state — that's FullCalendar's list view, which isn't used). Defaults mirror FullCalendar's own built-in values, so a theme that has never configured them looks exactly as before. **No migration**: `themes.tokens` is schema-less JSON, and every consumer falls back to `DEFAULT_TOKENS` for an absent key (`applyTokens()`, and `ThemeColorsEditor`'s pickers since #559) — pre-#559 themes keep saving and rendering unchanged, and `validateTokens()` treats the calendar keys as optional. The canonical field list lives in `api/src/domain/themeTokens.ts`'s exported `CALENDAR_COLOR_FIELDS`, consumed by the validator and the tests. The **Base ↔ Custom Theme relationship is unchanged** by this ticket: there is still no live sparse inheritance for any section, and `POST /system/themes/clone/:sourceId` copies the calendar tokens along with the rest of the blob at clone time, exactly as it already did for every other token. Covered by `apps/admin/src/test/theme-calendar-tokens.test.ts` (group registration, defaults, en/es/ca × `themes`/`gym_themes` translation parity, and that both editor pages still render the shared `ThemeColorsEditor`), plus validation/persistence/clone/tenant-isolation cases in `api/src/test/theme-tokens.test.ts` and `api/src/test/gym-themes.test.ts`.

**Stage 2 — CSS variables + FullCalendar application (frontend only, no API or schema change).** `applyTokens()` writes one `--gd-calendar-*` variable per calendar token, driven by two exported maps (`CALENDAR_COLOR_VARS`, `CALENDAR_ADVANCED_VARS` in `apps/admin/src/lib/themeTokens.ts`, mirrored in `apps/member/src/lib/themeTokens.ts`) that are the single source for the variable names — shared by `applyTokens()`, the stylesheet and the tests. Each key falls back to `DEFAULT_TOKENS`/`DEFAULT_ADVANCED`, so a theme saved before #559 (no calendar keys at all) still resolves the full variable set. The only consumer is `components/CalendarThemeStyles.tsx` — a `<style>` block rendered inside the calendar page (same in-body pattern as `Toast`/`AppShell`, which also puts it after the stylesheet FullCalendar injects into `<head>`, so equal-specificity rules win on document order). Where FullCalendar v6 exposes a variable of its own the token is mapped onto it (`--fc-border-color`, `--fc-page-bg-color`, `--fc-today-bg-color`, `--fc-highlight-color`, `--fc-non-business-color`, and the six `--fc-button-*` entries), so one declaration covers every view and state; the surfaces FullCalendar hardcodes get an explicit rule (toolbar title, `.fc-col-header-cell`, month-view date numbers, other-month dates, the time axis and its cushions, slot height, weekend columns). Every rule is scoped to a `gd-calendar` wrapper class — on the calendar container in `apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/calendar/page.tsx` and `apps/member/src/app/[locale]/calendar/page.tsx` — so nothing leaks into the rest of the app, and every `var()` repeats its default token value as the CSS fallback for the unthemed case. Member Web reads the same tokens (it renders the same FullCalendar views off the same theme; the tokens are only ever *edited* in the Admin theme editors), so its `ThemeTokens`/`DEFAULT_TOKENS` gained the 16 calendar colors and its `CalendarThemeStyles` is a byte-identical copy of Admin's sheet, guarded by `apps/member/src/test/calendar-theme-css.test.ts`. Two deliberate pixel-level changes: other-month dates use `calendarMutedDayText` at full opacity instead of FullCalendar's `opacity: .3` (otherwise the token would be barely visible), and non-business slots paint `calendarDisabledSlotBackground` opaquely instead of FullCalendar's translucent grey — the stage-1 default `#f7f7f7` is what that grey renders as over white, and an opaque fill is what an admin picking a color expects. Event colors were left to stage 3 (below). Covered by `apps/admin/src/test/theme-calendar-css.test.ts` (variable-map completeness, emission for configured/legacy/partially-configured themes, that the sheet consumes every variable it emits, that each CSS fallback equals the default token value, and that every selector is `.gd-calendar`-scoped).

**Stage 3 — event colors + the status pill badge (frontend only, no API or schema change beyond two more tokens).** An event's fill no longer encodes its status. Every event takes the theme's `calendarEventBackground`/`calendarEventBorder`/`calendarEventText`, and the booking status is carried by a pill-shaped badge rendered inside the event (`apps/admin/src/components/CalendarStatusBadge.tsx`) — the shape and palette the app's other status pills use (`StatusBadge`), at a size a calendar event can afford. This is #559's answer to the conflict with #541 ("event color depends only on `status`"): status still decides a color and nothing else does, it just paints the badge instead of the box, which is what frees the box to be themeable. `apps/admin/src/lib/calendarEventColors.ts` stays the single status → color mapping and now returns a `{ bg, fg }` pair per status, plus `full` — a status derived in the UI for a `scheduled` session whose bookings reached capacity, which takes the badge over `scheduled` as the more actionable of the two. The badge labels come from `calendar.status_*` (the raw uppercased DB value was shown before), with `status_full` added in en/es/ca. The admin calendar page stops setting a per-event `backgroundColor`/`borderColor`, since FullCalendar writes those as inline styles that would beat the theme. In the sheet, the tokens map onto `--fc-event-bg-color`/`--fc-event-border-color`/`--fc-event-text-color`, so one declaration each reaches `.fc-h-event`/`.fc-v-event` in every view; `--fc-bg-event-color` is a separate variable, so the greyed-out closed-hours blocks (#418) are untouched. Three rules are added on top: the event radius (which re-asserts FullCalendar's corner-flattening for continued multi-day events, since the radius selector is at least as specific), a hover background — FullCalendar has none of its own, hence the one default that isn't a FullCalendar built-in — and the selected/focused overlay, applied as the configured color at `opacity: .25` because the token is a plain color where FullCalendar bakes an `rgba()`. **Member Web is deliberately unaffected:** its events are colored by the session's *availability* to that member, set per event as an inline style that wins over these rules, so the event tokens only supply the base for an event that asks for no color of its own; the badge belongs to the Admin calendar, whose events convey a booking status. Event background/border default to `#6c63ff` — the purple `scheduled` events already had — so an unconfigured calendar still looks like the one admins had. **#976 changed Member Web's half of that sentence, and only Member Web's:** its events no longer carry an availability colour either — they carry the *event's* own colour, so no inline style encodes anybody's booking state in either app, and an event that configures no colour now falls through to these same event tokens. #976's scope note was explicit that admin behaviour was out of scope, so the badge, the tokens and the no-inline-colour rule stood until **#975 answered the open question with `Q1 event colour`** — see the paragraph below; the badge and the tokens still stand, the no-inline-colour rule does not. Covered by `apps/admin/src/test/calendar-status-badge.test.ts` (no per-event inline color, the `--fc-event-*` mappings, background events left alone, the corner-flattening re-assertion and its ordering, the overlay weight, one badge per view, the badge holding no palette of its own, `full` winning over `scheduled`, and `status_*` translation parity) and by the reworked `apps/admin/src/test/calendar-event-colors.test.ts`.

**#975 — an event's colour is the event's, in the Admin app too (frontend only, no API or schema change).** The Members app got this in #976; `Q1 event colour` on the thread extends it to the Admin calendar, which is the third design in this place in three tickets, so it is worth saying what each decided. #541 painted the event *by status*. #559 stage 3 overturned that, moved the status into `CalendarStatusBadge` and gave the box the theme's event tokens — and in doing so left `calendar_events.color` editable and read by nothing, the #677 defect shape (the event editor's picker pre-fills from the Activity Type and `PUT /calendar-events/:id` persists it). #975 restores the *configured* colour only: the status stays in the badge where stage 3 put it. `apps/admin/src/lib/calendarEventPaint.ts` is the one place that decides it, and three of its answers are the rule rather than the implementation. The colour is `COALESCE(calendar_events.color, activity_types.color)` — the event's own over its Activity Type's, the order the API already applies for `GET /me/schedule` and the order the picker assumes — resolved in the browser from columns both reads already project (`ce.*` plus `at.color AS activity_type_color` in `SESSION_SELECT`/`EVENT_SELECT`), which is why this ticket touches no route. A colour that is absent, blank or **not a usable hex** answers `null`, and `null` means the caller sets *no* inline `backgroundColor`/`borderColor`/`textColor` at all — the only way the theme's `calendarEventBackground`/`Border`/`Text` can still paint the box, since FullCalendar writes those as inline styles. And the **foreground** is scored rather than assumed: the theme's `calendarEventText` is chosen against the theme's *own* event background (that is the pair `calendarContrast.ts` scores), so both candidates (`#111827`, `#ffffff` — no third) are run through that module's own WCAG 2.1 `contrastRatio()` and the better taken. Two affordances an inline background would otherwise swallow are restored in `CalendarThemeStyles` behind the `gd-event-colored` class the paint module adds: hover **darkens** the configured colour (`filter: brightness(.92)`) instead of replacing it with `calendarEventHoverBackground`, because swapping an event's identity colour under the pointer is status-as-colour in another guise; and the focus ring switches to `currentColor`, which is the foreground that was just scored against this very background. An event that configures no colour is untouched in every respect. Note the stage-4 contrast report keeps scoring the `event_text`/`event_surface` token pairs, which from here describe the *unconfigured* case — a free hex field cannot be promised AA (white on the app's own `#6c63ff` is 4.32:1, which is exactly what that advisory report tells an admin). Covered by `apps/admin/src/test/calendar-event-paint.test.ts` (the COALESCE order, shorthand hex, the six unusable forms, border = background, the two-candidate scoring and that it never reads worse than plain white, the module seeing no status/booking/capacity field, and both CSS affordances), with the two #559/#977 guards rewritten from "no inline colour" to "no colour the page spells itself, and no status palette in the event mapping".

**#981 — the trainer and the space, on the event box itself (frontend only, no API or schema change).** An admin reading the week could not tell who was delivering a session or where it ran without opening it, and the Members app's box named the trainer and never the room. Both apps show both now, in the Day and Week views, and the values are the **occurrence's own**: `GET /calendar-events`, `GET /class-sessions` and `GET /me/schedule` all project `space_name`/`trainer_name` by joining `ce.space_id`/`ce.trainer_membership_id`, so a session retargeted away from its Activity Type's defaults reads as *Jane Smith · Studio 2* and not as the Activity's *John Smith · Studio 1* — which is why this ticket touches no route. One module per app decides it, as #975/#976 already established for the colour (the two apps share no frontend module): `apps/admin/src/lib/calendarEventMeta.ts` and `memberEventMeta()`/`memberEventMetaLine()` in `apps/member/src/lib/calendarEventDisplay.ts`. Three of their answers are the rule rather than the implementation. A name that is **absent, blank or whitespace resolves to `null`, and `null` renders no line at all** — no `N/A`, no `Unknown`, no `Not assigned` (§6) — which is also why the module returns the *lines* rather than a pre-joined string: a trailing `·` is exactly the empty placeholder the ticket forbids. The **layout follows the view**, because a FullCalendar event cannot measure itself: `full` (a line each, §4's own mock) is the admin Day view, where a day column is full-width, and `compact` (one truncated `Trainer · Space`) is the admin Week view and *both* member time-grid views — §5's example is compact on a single line and the Members app is phone-first. And the admin Day view's occupancy counts keep a line of their own rather than being folded in, so nothing the box already carried was dropped. The **trainer is the effective one where there is one** (`effective_trainer_name ?? trainer_name` — who actually delivered the occurrence, #193; only a session read projects it, so a manual entry falls through to its own), which is the choice the day view was already making inline. Every other view is untouched (§1): the admin month cell still carries title, time and badge, and the member month cell still carries the trainer-alone line it has always had. It is **text and only text** (§8): the box stays painted by `calendarEventPaint()`/`eventBackgroundColor()` from the event's own configured colour, neither module declares a hue or resolves a locale key (a person's name and a room's name are data, not copy), and a caller that turned either into a colour would be reintroducing #541. Covered by `apps/admin/src/test/calendar-event-meta.test.ts` (16) and `apps/member/src/test/calendar-event-meta.test.ts` (10) — the occurrence-not-default rule and the absence of any field to pass a default in through, every unusable name, the no-dangling-separator cases, the two layouts and the trainer-first order, each page asking the module instead of reading the columns itself, the month cells left alone, the counts line kept separate, the nowrap/ellipsis truncation, and the hue-and-key freedom of both modules.

**Stage 4 — accessibility pass (frontend only, no API or schema change).** Three things close the ticket's Accessibility requirements. (1) **A contrast report** in the Calendar section of both theme editors: `apps/admin/src/lib/calendarContrast.ts` scores each combination the eye actually has to separate (`CALENDAR_CONTRAST_PAIRS` — header/day/muted/time-axis/event/nav text against their backgrounds at WCAG AA 4.5:1, and the event box against the grid at the 3:1 non-text bar) with the WCAG 2.1 contrast formula, resolving each token the same way `applyTokens()` does so it scores what the calendar will paint. `CalendarContrastReport` in `ThemeTokensEditor.tsx` renders it under the section — **advisory only, never a save blocker**: a gym's branding is its own and the API-side `validateTokens()` stays a pure format check. The grid border and disabled-slot background are deliberately out of the pairs (decorative boundaries whose FullCalendar defaults sit below 3:1; scoring them would flag the stock calendar). The one shipped default that falls short is `calendarEventText` on `calendarEventBackground` (white on `#6c63ff`, 4.32:1) — kept because the ticket requires an unconfigured calendar to look unchanged, and reported instead. (2) **Focus indicators** the themed calendar has to keep (WCAG 2.4.7): a `:focus-visible` ring on events drawn inside the box in `--gd-calendar-event-text` (which already reads against the event fill, so it survives any surface), and a themed outline replacing FullCalendar's nav-button focus box-shadow (which was keyed to FullCalendar's own default button color and washed out once the buttons were themed). Both are added identically to Admin's and Member Web's `CalendarThemeStyles.tsx`. (3) **Fallback hardening** (the ticket's "sensible defaults and fallbacks when a theme property is missing or invalid"): `calendarVarValue()` in both `themeTokens.ts` files now falls back to the default for a persisted value that is *unusable*, not just missing — an empty string or a non-hex color would otherwise reach the CSS variable and, being invalid at computed-value time, drop the whole declaration rather than trigger the `var()` literal fallback (a `validateTokens()` gap for the un-format-checked `advanced` map and for rows written before #559). Covered by `apps/admin/src/test/calendar-a11y.test.ts` (the WCAG math, the pair definitions and their label/translation coverage, the report's advisory placement, the focus rules and their Member Web mirror, and the unusable-value fallback) with the Member Web mirror checks in `apps/member/src/test/calendar-theme-css.test.ts`.


**Members App settings with Admin inheritance (#833):** the Theme editor also configures the **Members App**'s own appearance, in five collapsible sections — `Header (Members App)`, `Background (Members App)`, `Section Cards (Members App)`, `Text (Members App)`, `Calendar (Members App)` — under a `Members App` card on both Theme screens. The 18 settings and their Admin inheritance sources are declared **once**, in `apps/admin/src/lib/membersAppTokens.ts`; `apps/admin/src/components/ThemeMembersAppEditor.tsx` renders them and both screens (`[locale]/themes`, `[locale]/system/themes`) import it, so Base Themes and Custom Themes get the same settings with no second editor. **No schema change and no migration**: `tokens.membersApp` is an **overrides-only** map — a key that is absent means the setting follows the *current* value of its Admin source, and `Restore inherited value` deletes the key (dropping `membersApp` entirely when it empties) rather than writing the Admin value into it. That is what keeps inheritance dynamic rather than a snapshot taken when the Theme was created: an Admin colour edited later moves every Members App setting still inherited from it and none that was overridden, and inheritance is decided per setting, so overriding Header Color cannot implicitly override Header Text. `validateMembersApp()` (`api/src/domain/membersAppTokens.ts`, called from `validateTokens()`, so both Theme routers apply it) rejects an unknown key, a colour that is not `#rrggbb`, a font outside `FONT_STACKS`, a separator width outside 0–20 and a blank CSS length; `HEX_RE`/`FONT_STACKS` moved into `domain/themeTokenFormats.ts` so the two validators need not import each other. **Two Admin sources were missing and were added** as `advanced` attributes beside the settings they belong to — `headerTextFont` (`group_header`, a `FONT_STACKS` select → `--gd-header-font`, read by `TopHeader`) and `cardBorderWidth` (`group_cards` → `--gd-card-border-width`, read by `cardSurfaceStyle`) — each wired to a real CSS variable, because an attribute the editor persists that no variable carries is the #677 defect. The two §14 flagged as possibly missing were **not** added: `Application Surface` is the existing `advanced.modalBackground` and `Input Background` the existing `colors.inputBackgroundColor`, so the Calendar Modal settings inherit from those rather than from duplicates (and never from `calendarBackground` — a window layered over the calendar is a separate surface, as is the time column). On the reading side, `apps/member/src/lib/membersAppTokens.ts` mirrors the declaration and `ThemeProvider` calls `applyMembersAppTokens()` **after** `applyTokens()`, so a Members App setting sharing a variable with its source (`--gd-app-bg`, `--gd-calendar-*`, `--gd-color-h1..h3`, `--gd-header-sep-*`) wins; the rest get `--gd-members-*` variables of their own, read by `TopBar` (header colour/text/font — it used to borrow the Admin *sidebar* colour), `MembersSectionCard` (the border, on the one component every navigation card renders through) and the member Calendar page (the event window and its dropdowns). An unusable stored value falls back to the inherited one for `calendarVarValue()`'s reason. There is deliberately **no Members App preview** in the Admin editor, and the editor holds no save state of its own: it only hands `onChange` a new token draft, so the Theme editor's existing Save/Cancel and dirty state cover these settings, restoring inheritance included.

**One place the Members App spells a visual value (#983):** #833 wired the 18 Members App settings to CSS variables but only four surfaces read them, so every other member screen carried colour literals — and literals are values a Theme cannot move. `apps/member/src/lib/memberChrome.ts` is the Members App's counterpart to the Admin's `listChrome.ts`/`formChrome.ts`: it declares each **role** as the variable that holds it (`memberTheme.pageBackground` → `--gd-app-bg`, `surface` → `--gd-card-bg`, `text`/`textSecondary`/`textMuted`, `separator`, `title1..3` → `--gd-color-h1..h3`, `cardBorderColor`/`cardBorderWidth` → the two Section Card settings, `inputBackground`/`inputBorder`, `primaryButton`/`primaryButtonText`, the four `status*` colours, and the Calendar page's own `calendarButton`/`calendarModalBackground`/`calendarModalInputBackground`), with the value `applyTokens()` writes as each `var()` fallback — so the fallback covers only the frames before `ThemeProvider`'s effect runs. Pages spread the objects built from it (`sectionCardStyle`, `rowDividerStyle`, `inputStyle`, `primaryButtonStyle`/`secondaryButtonStyle`/`destructiveButtonStyle`, `statusPillStyle(statusTone(status))`, `noticeStyle(tone)`), never a hex: `sectionCardStyle` is what gives the content cards of My Membership, My Training Plan, My Bookings, My Nutrition, Packages, Profile and the dashboard the same Section Cards border `MembersSectionCard` gives the navigation tiles, which is also why that component now imports `sectionCardBorder` instead of declaring it. A status pill's tint is `color-mix()` of the theme's own status colour over the card surface (mixed over `surface`, not `transparent`, so it stays opaque on a Section Card carrying artwork), and one `statusTone()` answers for the dashboard, My Membership and My Bookings, which each held a copy of the same four-state map. Two things the sweep fixed rather than moved: `MembersBackground` resolved its scrim from `tokens.colors.pageBackground`, so a Theme *overriding* the Members App background tinted its artwork with the Admin colour — it goes through `membersAppVarValue()` now — and the member Calendar's filter bar and its Apply button read `--gd-sidebar-selected-bg`, the Admin **sidebar's** colour, which no Members App setting can move; they take the Calendar Buttons setting (`--gd-calendar-nav-btn-bg`) that FullCalendar's own navigation buttons already follow, while an Activity Type's own colour stays its own (#975). Three files keep a literal, each with a product reason and each named in the gate: `app/[locale]/layout.tsx`'s static `theme-color` meta (it tints the browser's chrome, is read from the document head before any gym resolves, and cannot be a CSS variable), and `AdminBar`/`ImpersonationBanner`, the two bars that tell a superadmin whose account they are looking at — deliberately outside the Theme, since a gym able to repaint them could make them disappear into the page it designed. The gate is `api/src/test/members-app-theme-consumption.unit.test.ts`, in the **API** suite for #1009's reason (CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only): it walks every `.tsx` under the member app's `app/` and `components/`, strips comments and `var(--token, fallback)` expressions, and fails on a remaining colour, on any surface reading a `--gd-sidebar-*` variable, and on the chrome module not reading one of the variables it must. Frontend-only — no migration, no API change, no new setting — and #983 also restored the byte-identity `apps/member/src/components/CalendarThemeStyles.tsx` is supposed to have with the Admin's, which had drifted when #975 added its two `.gd-event-colored` rules to the Admin copy alone; those rules are inert in this app, since a member's events are coloured inline (#976) and nothing adds that class here.

Each center may optionally override the gym's default theme via `centers.theme_id` (migration 065).

**Theme ownership and lifecycle:** `gym_id IS NULL` = system/base theme (Cordel-owned); `gym_id = <id>` = customer theme. Status lifecycle: `draft → active → inactive → deleted` (migration 079 widens the CHECK constraint). Deleting or setting a theme to `draft`/`inactive` is blocked (409) when it is assigned as the gym's default (`gyms.theme_id`) or to any center (`centers.theme_id`). Only `active` themes can be assigned as gym default or to centers.

**Theme header metadata (#712, migration 178):** `themes` carries a `created_by_name`/`created_by_type` (`'staff'`/`'superadmin'`) actor snapshot, written once at creation — the same immutable-snapshot shape as `themes.deleted_by_name` (migration 101) and `tax_rates.created_by_name` (migration 126), chosen over an actor FK for the same reason #388 gave: a superadmin acting directly has no `gym_memberships` row to join to. `POST /system/themes/clone/:sourceId` is the **only** path that creates a Customer Theme, so it is the only writer; a future creation path must capture the snapshot too, or the theme reads back with no creator forever. Migration 178 backfills existing rows from the earliest `create`/`clone` `audit_logs` entry per theme (`created_by_type` stays null there — an audit row snapshots the name, not whether the actor was staff or superadmin), and the base themes seeded by migrations 056/065 stay unattributed because no person created them. Reads take the column, never the audit log — `GET /platform/themes/:id` (superadmin Base Theme details) is the one place still resolving actor names out of `audit_logs`, for its modified/deleted lines. Every `/system/themes` response also carries **`is_gym_theme`**, computed in `shapeTheme()` against the *requesting* gym's `gyms.theme_id` (fetched by `getGymThemeId()`), so the same Base Theme row is flagged for the gym that selected it and not for any other. The Customer Themes card header (`apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/themes/page.tsx`) renders Created by · Created at for Custom Themes only and a ★ Gym theme badge on the flagged row; Base Theme rows, the expandable editor and its Assignments/Branding/Colors/Typography sections are unchanged. Covered by `api/src/test/gym-themes.test.ts` (snapshot on clone, list shape, `is_gym_theme` for the assigned/unassigned/other-gym cases) and `apps/admin/src/test/theme-header-metadata.test.ts`.

`ThemeProvider` in both the admin app (`context/GymContext` → `activeGym.theme.tokens`) and the member app (`context/AppContext` → resolved via `GET /me/gym`) writes the same full `--gd-*` CSS variable set to `<html>` on gym switch (`apps/member/src/lib/themeTokens.ts` mirrors `apps/admin/src/lib/themeTokens.ts`'s `applyTokens()`/`DEFAULT_TOKENS` — #489 stage 4; before that, Member Web only ever set ~10 of the ~30 variables); legacy `--brand`/`--chrome`/`--accent` aliases are preserved. Unlike Admin, Member Web has no center-level theme override (`/me/centers` doesn't return `theme_tokens`), so it only resolves gym-theme-then-default. Logos are ≤ 512 KB (allow-list: PNG, SVG, JPEG, WEBP) and served public at `GET /themes/:id/logo` (`Cache-Control: immutable`). Since #713 a **Custom Theme** logo is stored in the gym's Cloudflare R2 folder and the row keeps only `themes.logo_object_key` (migration 180); since #824 the key is the theme's own — `<gyms.storage_folder_prefix>/themes/<theme_id>-<sanitized name>/logo/logo.<ext>` (`domain/themeLogo.ts`'s `buildThemeLogoKey()`), the sibling of the `Members/` leaf #725 writes — so each theme of a gym carries its own logo and the obsolete `Branding/` tree is written by nothing; since #829 a **Base Theme** logo is the same shape one root over — `cordel/themes/<theme_id>-<sanitized name>/logo/logo.<ext>`, built off `PLATFORM_STORAGE_ROOT` because a `gym_id IS NULL` row has no `storage_folder_prefix` to hang off — so `themes.logo_bytes` has no writer left: a logo uploaded before #829 (a Base Theme's, or a Custom Theme's from before #713) stays a MEDIUMBLOB until it is replaced, which writes the key and clears the blob in one statement. #829 also lowercased the whole theme tree: `themes/`, `logo/` and `members_app/`, the gym-level `themes/` root included. Nothing rewrites a key a row already holds, so objects stored under the earlier `Themes/…/Logo/` and `Themes/…/Members/` keep rendering from their stored keys and are swept by hand (`docs/go-to-production.md`). `CHECK chk_themes_logo_storage` keeps the two modes mutually exclusive. Every theme-shaped response gained `logo_url`: the public R2 URL (`${CLOUDFLARE_R2_ENDPOINT}/${CLOUDFLARE_R2_BUCKET}/<key>?v=<logo_updated_at>`, built by `domain/themeLogo.ts`'s `themeLogoUrl()`), or null for a blob-backed logo. Both app headers and the Custom Themes page prefer `logo_url` and fall back to `GET /themes/:id/logo`, which serves either mode — for an R2-backed logo it reads the object and returns the bytes rather than redirecting, because the Payment app loads that URL under `img-src 'self'` and CSP matches a redirect's host too. The member app resolves its gym and theme in one call via `GET /me/gym` (no `x-gym-id` header required). #725/#732/#1038: the theme also carries `members_images`, the seven Members App background images (a Custom Theme's live in the gym's folder, a Base Theme's under `cordel/themes/…`; the member app sees only URLs either way); `components/MembersBackground.tsx` sits next to `ThemeProvider` in the member layout and paints the slot the current path maps to (`/training`, `/nutrition`, `/schedule` → *bookings*, `/membership`, everything else — **`/calendar` since #984** → *background*) onto `<body>` as `cover`/centred artwork under a scrim of the theme's own `pageBackground` at 72 % — so no page is redesigned and text keeps its contrast. A `null` slot clears the property and the theme background colour shows through; the member app resolves nothing else — no storage path, no theme id, no object key, no second source. **#984 takes the Calendar page out of that table**: `slotForPathname()` no longer names `/calendar`, so the Calendar page falls through to the general *background* slot like any other unnamed route — the `calendar` image is the dashboard tile's alone (`MembersSectionCard`, below), and a 512×512 tile stretched to `cover` behind a dense time grid read as the tile leaking onto the page. The page therefore follows the global Members App background image when one is configured and the resolved Members App background colour when none is, changing the tile cannot move it, and the Calendar's own theme settings (header, buttons, time column, modal) are what paint that screen. #728 then puts the same artwork on the **Home sections** themselves: `components/MembersSectionCard.tsx` renders the element the page already rendered (a tile `<button>`, the My Membership `<div role="button">`) with the caller's own styles and replaces only its `background`, so the grid, the card dimensions, the icons, the labels and the navigation are untouched — Calendar → *calendar*, My Training Plan → *training*, My Bookings → *bookings*, My Nutrition → *nutrition*, My Membership → *membership*, while the page behind them keeps the *background* slot. A card's artwork is `scroll`, not `fixed` (a viewport-anchored image would show a different crop in every card and slide under them as the page scrolls). **Since #982 it carries no scrim**: `cardBackgroundStyleValue()` takes the URL alone, so an uploaded image is painted at full opacity with its own colours and contrast — #728's `cardBackground`-at-82 % overlay washed a black photograph out to grey — while `MembersBackground` keeps the page's 72 % scrim, because that one surface sits under every page's text and controls at once. #982 also makes a configured slot **replace** the tile's default emoji: `useSectionImageUrl()` is the one place that answers whether a slot has artwork, `NavTile` renders the icon only when it answers `null` (no placeholder, no second asset, no `<img>`) and keeps the tile's height so the picture fills the same box, and clearing the image brings the default icon back with no further change. The Next Booking card is deliberately not one of them. **#1038 adds the `personal_goals` slot** for the My Goals artwork; `slotForPathname()` does not name it, because the My Goals page is #1036's and a page is added to that map when it exists. Both components read `theme.members_images` off the payload the app already has, so no section costs a request. The isolated Payment app (`pay.vdicube.com`) is unauthenticated and has no `ThemeProvider`; `GET /payment-page/token/:token` returns a curated `themeColors` subset instead (see the API surface table above), applied by `apps/payment/js/checkout.js`'s `applyThemeColors()` directly onto `style.css`'s own (non-`--gd-*`) CSS variables (#489 stage 4).

**Admin Theme editors — draft / live preview / Save-Cancel (#492):** both the Base Themes (`[locale]/system/themes/`) and Customer Themes (`[locale]/themes/`) editors edit a local draft, never the persisted Theme, until the user clicks Save; Cancel discards the draft and calls `applyTokens()` with `lib/themeTokens.ts`'s `getLiveTokens()` (same center-then-gym-then-default resolution `ThemeProvider` uses) to restore whatever tokens are actually driving the app chrome, independent of which theme was being edited. Every Colors/Typography/Advanced field change updates the draft and calls `applyTokens()` directly on `document.documentElement` for immediate visual feedback — there is no sandboxed preview surface. Save persists the whole draft (branding fields + `tokens`) in one `PUT`, plus a deferred logo upload/removal: clicking "clear logo" only sets a pending-removal flag on the draft (`DELETE /logo` fires on Save, never immediately). `tokensEqual()` (JSON-equality) drives the dirty state that gates the Save button and an unsaved-changes guard (`beforeunload` + a confirm-discard dialog before switching to another theme or navigating away), following the same `guardUnsaved`/`pendingAction` pattern used by Members/Training Plans. The Colors-group list, per-group color pickers, inline `ThemeAdvancedSection`, and the Typography grid are shared between both editors via `apps/admin/src/components/ThemeTokensEditor.tsx` (`COLOR_GROUPS`, `ThemeColorsEditor`, `ThemeTypographyEditor`) instead of being duplicated per page. On the Customer Themes page, Base Themes shown in the "System" section render `ThemeColorsEditor` with `readOnly` (their `PUT` 404s server-side since `gym-themes.ts` only matches `gym_id`-owned rows, so edits were previously silently discarded). Since #632 each Colors group inside `ThemeColorsEditor` is a **collapsible card** — a full-width header `<button>` (the group's existing label, `aria-expanded`, a rotating `▾`) over a body that is unmounted while collapsed — with every group collapsed initially. The open set is a component-local `Set<string>` of group keys, deliberately outside the token draft: groups collapse independently (a `Set`, not an accordion's single key) and toggling one never calls `onChange`, so it can't dirty a theme or change a color. Everything inside a group is unchanged (pickers, the Header separator-height input, the inline `ThemeAdvancedSection`, the Calendar contrast report), and because both editors render the Colors section through this one shared component, both get the cards. Covered by `apps/admin/src/test/theme-colors-collapsible.test.ts`.

**One editor model for Base and Customer Themes (#678):** both screens expand a row straight into the same structured editor — collapsible sections over a Save/Cancel footer — instead of the Base Themes page opening a read-only grid of color swatches that hid every non-color setting behind a separate *Edit* step. On `[locale]/system/themes/` a click on the row now opens the editor itself (sections **Branding / Colors / Typography**; the platform has no `Assignments` counterpart, since a theme is assigned to a *gym's* centers), Cancel collapses the card, and the row menu's *Edit* entry lands in the same place. Persistence, the status workflow and the theme engine are untouched: same `PUT /platform/themes/:id`, same `EDITABLE_STATUSES` select (kept inside Branding — on the Customer Themes page status is driven from the row menu instead), same draft/live-preview/dirty-guard machinery from #492. The section wrapper and the Branding block (name, description, logo upload/removal, `logo_contains_gym_name`) are now shared through `apps/admin/src/components/ThemeSectionEditor.tsx` (`ThemeSection`, `ThemeBrandingEditor`) the way Colors and Typography already were through `ThemeTokensEditor.tsx`, so a future theme setting is added once. On the Customer Themes page a Base Theme now exposes **all four** sections rather than Assignments + Colors, with Branding, Colors and Typography rendered read-only (its `PUT` 404s server-side — see above); read-only means *disabled controls*, never a different set of controls, so `ThemeAdvancedSection` is rendered disabled instead of being omitted, and `ThemeTypographyEditor` gained the same `readOnly` prop `ThemeColorsEditor` already had. Frontend-only: no migration, no API change; `themes.label_description` was added to `es.json`/`ca.json`, where it was missing (it rendered as its raw key). Covered by `apps/admin/src/test/theme-editor-unification.test.ts`.

**`logo_contains_gym_name` (migration 146, #488):** boolean on `themes`, default `false`, one source of truth shared by Base and Customer Themes (both live in the same `themes` row). Editable via a checkbox directly under the Logo field on both the Base Theme and Customer Theme admin screens. When `true`, the configured logo already contains the gym's name, so the header in Admin (`TopHeader.tsx`), Member Web (`TopBar.tsx`, home screen), and the Payment app (`checkout.html`/`checkout.js`) render the logo only and suppress the separate gym-name text; when `false` (or no logo is configured), the gym name renders next to/instead of the logo as before. Exposed on every theme-shaped API response (`/platform/themes`, `/system/themes`, `/me/gym`, `/me/gyms`, the admin gym list) and on the public `GET /payment-page/token/:token` response (`logoUrl`, `logoContainsGymName`). The Payment app's nginx config proxies `/themes/` (in addition to the existing `/payment-page/` proxy) to `api.vdicube.com` so the logo `<img>` stays same-origin under its CSP `img-src 'self'`.

**Theme resolution order (per center):**
1. `centers.theme_id` (explicit center assignment)
2. `gyms.theme_id` (gym default)
3. First theme alphabetically

**API surface:**
- `GET|POST|PUT|DELETE /platform/themes` — superadmin Base Themes CRUD only (`gym_id IS NULL`). `PUT` accepts `status` in `['draft','active','inactive']`; setting `draft`/`inactive` is blocked (409) when assigned as gym default or to centers. `DELETE` is also blocked (409) for the same reason.
- `PUT /platform/themes/:id/set-system-default` — atomically marks one Base Theme as `is_system_default = 1` and clears all others (superadmin only).
- `POST /platform/themes/clone/:id` — clone a Base Theme into a new Base Theme (superadmin only).
- `GET /system/themes` — gym admin: lists Base Themes + Customer Themes for the active gym. Each row carries `created_by_name`/`created_by_type` and `is_gym_theme` (#712).
- `POST /system/themes/clone/:id` — clone any accessible theme into a new Customer Theme.
- `PUT|DELETE /system/themes/:id` — update/soft-delete a Customer Theme (same protection rules as above).
- `GET /system/themes/:id/assignments` — returns `{ is_gym_default, centers: [{id, name, is_assigned, is_inherited}] }`. Since #985 it reports **every** non-deleted Center of the gym, not only the ones using this theme: the Assignments section is a checkbox list, so an unassigned Center has to be a box you can tick. `is_assigned` is the Center's own `centers.theme_id` (null-safe `<=>`), `is_inherited` says the theme reaches it through `gyms.theme_id` instead — reported so the list can say so beside the name, never as a ticked box.
- `PUT /system/themes/:id/set-default` — sets `gyms.theme_id`; rejects non-active themes (400).
- `PUT /system/themes/:id/centers` — **replace-all** (#985): body `{ center_ids }` (an empty array is a legitimate save; `centers.id` is an auto-increment **integer**, so an id is accepted as a number *or* a string and every comparison runs through `centerKey()` — the response reports the stored form), the submitted Centers get `centers.theme_id = :id` and a Center the request leaves out and that currently holds this theme goes back to NULL (inheriting the Gym Default). Both UPDATEs run in one transaction, so a half-saved list is impossible; what moves is decided by `api/src/domain/themeCenterAssignments.ts` (`themeCenterAssignmentPlan`, `unknownCenterIds`), the Active rule guards *assigning* rather than the request (a theme taken out of service can still be removed from Centers), an unknown or non-string id is a 400 that writes nothing, a Center pointing at another theme and left out is untouched, and the change is audited as `previous`/`next` `assigned_center_ids`. It replaces the three routes the picker needed — `GET /:id/unassigned-centers`, `POST /:id/assign-centers` and `DELETE /:id/centers/:centerId` ("Restore Inheritance"), all removed: a set edited as a set has one writer.

### Charts (`@gymdesk/charts`, #1037 stage 4)

Both apps draw charts through **one** workspace package, `shared/charts`, and neither imports Apache ECharts itself:

```text
Admin app  ─┐
            ├─▶  @gymdesk/charts  ─▶  Apache ECharts
Members app ─┘
```

The package owns ECharts (loaded by `loadECharts()`, **dynamically**, so it never runs during SSR and never lands in a first-load bundle; only the chart and component modules actually drawn are registered, never the `echarts` barrel), the pure option builder per chart type (`lineChartOption.ts`), the colour resolution (`chartTheme.ts`) and the canvas's lifetime (`EChartCanvas.tsx` — mount, resize, dispose, and a re-read of the Theme when `applyTokens()` writes it, since that effect may land after the chart's). Each app owns only the adapter that turns its rows into `ChartPoint`s with its **own** locale and labels — the layer resolves no locale key, formats no date and knows no entity.

**Theming**: ECharts paints a canvas and cannot read a CSS variable, so each role is declared as the `--gd-*` variable that holds it (the same names `applyTokens()` writes in both apps) and resolved off the element the chart sits in, so a Center's own Theme reaches it; a variable resolving blank falls back rather than letting ECharts use its own palette. `segmentPoints()` turns points carrying a `group` into one series per group, each with its own palette entry and bridged to the previous one so the line does not break — #1037 §23's per-period colouring is that one rule.

`api/src/test/charts-layer.unit.test.ts` is the gate (in the API suite, because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only): no `echarts` import in either app, no quoted colour in a chart component, no `t()` inside the layer, and the workspace wired into both apps' `package.json`, `next.config.js` and `Dockerfile`. Adding a chart type is four steps, in `shared/charts/README.md`.

### Middleware (both apps)
Both apps use `clerkMiddleware` + `next-intl` middleware together. Public routes bypass `auth.protect()`. Both require `NEXT_PUBLIC_CLERK_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` (baked at build time) and `CLERK_SECRET_KEY` (runtime).

### i18n
All strings live in each app's `locales/base/{en,es,ca}.json`, namespaced by feature. Use `useTranslations()` in every page/component. The admin app has a `LanguagePicker`.

#### Member app: the stored language preference (#1039)
