It never writes back to `status` and doesn't change any existing business logic (the one-active-Membership-per-Member index, billing, etc.) — those all keep reading the stored `status` column as before. **Multi-member Memberships (migration 129, #374)**: `user_membership_members` (gym_id, `user_membership_id FK→user_memberships` CASCADE, `member_id FK→members` CASCADE, `is_owner`, UNIQUE on `(user_membership_id, member_id)`) records every Member covered by a Membership; `POST /user-memberships` always inserts the paying Member as `is_owner=1` in the same transaction as the Membership row, and existing Memberships were backfilled the same way. `GET /:id/members` → `{ member_limit, members: [{member_id, is_owner, name, email}] }`; `POST /:id/members` `{member_id}` adds a covered Member (404 unknown/other-gym member, 400 at the plan's `member_limit`, 409 already covered); `DELETE /:id/members/:memberId` removes one (400 on the owner, 404 if not covered) — all `requireModuleWrite('PAYMENTS')`. Covered Members (not just the owner) get the plan's booking-time entitlements: `plan-allowances.ts` and `plan-class-types.ts`'s `registerBookingAccessHook`s and `GET /me/schedule`'s `access_locked` flag all resolve a Member's active Membership via `um.member_id = ? OR EXISTS (... user_membership_members ...)`. Admin UI: Plans page gets a "Members per Membership" select (1/2/Family); Memberships page gets a "Members" action opening `MembershipMembersModal` (list + add/remove, capped, owner protected). **Assign Plan to Member(s) (migration 130, #376)**: `POST /membership-plans/:id/assign` (in `membership-plans.ts`, admin-only) instantiates a Plan into a new Membership for one or more existing Members in one call — body `{member_ids[], owner_member_id, starts_at}`, validated against the plan's `member_limit` (exact count for `'1'`/`'2'`, any count ≥1 for `'family'`). In one transaction: inserts the `user_memberships` row via the same `effectivePrice()` price-window lookup as `POST /user-memberships`, writes the `status_changed` billing ledger row, inserts one `user_membership_members` row per selected Member (owner flagged), and snapshots the plan's current `plan_charge_benefits` onto a new `user_membership_charge_benefits` table (gym_id, `user_membership_id FK→user_memberships` CASCADE, `product_id FK→products` no cascade — survives a charge being soft-deleted, `action`/`value` mirroring the source row, `no_benefit` rows excluded, UNIQUE on `(user_membership_id, product_id)`) so a later edit to the Plan's benefits never retroactively changes an already-instantiated Membership. After the transaction commits, any Promotion currently targeting the plan (`promotion_membership_plans`, active + within its date window) is auto-applied best-effort via the existing `applyPromotionToMembership()` — a non-stackable conflict is skipped rather than failing the assignment. Reuses the existing unique-constraint `ER_DUP_ENTRY` → 409 handling ("already has an active Membership Plan" since #956, which also added the `409 active_plan_exists` / `confirm: true` replacement path for every selected Member at once). Admin UI: Plans page's row context menu gains "Assign Plan to Member", opening `AssignPlanModal.tsx` (member search/multi-select via the existing `MemberSearchInput`, radio-select owner, capacity warning when over the plan's `member_limit`, starts-on date). **Plan history + transition workflows (#412)**: `POST /:id/assign-new-plan` (admin-only, same gate as the cancel `DELETE`) atomically supersedes a Membership — in one transaction it **cancels** the current row only if it's still `active`/`paused` (a row already `cancelled`/`expired` is left alone), stamping `closed_at` and `ends_at` through the shared `supersedeLiveAssignments()` (#956 — it wrote `expired` and no date at all until then), then inserts the new `active` row for the same Member via `effectivePrice()` (same discount-override/`discount_reason` validation as `POST /`) plus its owner row in `user_membership_members`. Cancelling the old row before inserting the new one in the same transaction avoids colliding with the `active_member_key` unique index (one active Membership per Member, which migration 213 restored — the ordering is still what keeps a same-Plan supersede legal). Replacement is this route's contract, so it needs no `confirm`; a Member *additionally* covered by another live assignment does, and gets the same `409 active_plan_exists`. Admin Members page (`MemberExpandedRow.tsx`) now renders a Member's **full** plan history (`GET /user-memberships?member_id=` is already sorted `starts_at DESC`, #410/#411) instead of only the latest row, with a per-row admin-only `ContextMenu`: "Cancel Plan" (the existing `DELETE /:id`) and "Assign New Plan" (**#628**: `AssignPlanInlineEditor.tsx`, an inline editor rendered inside the plan card it supersedes — plan picker, start date and a **Promotions** section; the former `AssignNewPlanModal.tsx` is gone, and only one assignment editor may be open at a time). The Promotions section lists `GET /promotions?lifecycle_status=active&active_on=<today>&membership_plan_id=<plan>` (the `membership_plan_id` filter is #628's addition — an `EXISTS` over `promotion_membership_plans`, so the list is exactly what the apply path accepts for that plan) and enforces the `stackable` rule live: a selected non-stackable Promotion disables every other one, any selection disables the non-stackable ones, clearing it re-enables everything. The picked ids are submitted as `promotion_ids` on `POST /:id/assign-new-plan`, which re-validates them server-side — `validatePromotionSelection()` (`membership-promotions.ts`) re-states the per-Promotion apply checks (exists / active / inside its window / targets this plan) over the whole set plus the pure `validatePromotionStacking()` (`domain/promotionStacking.ts`) cross-Promotion rule, **before** the assignment transaction runs, so an invalid combination never leaves a half-configured assignment behind. Each Promotion is then applied via `applyPromotionToMembership()` after the assignment commits (it opens its own transaction), exactly as applying one from the Assigned Plans page would, its `adjustment` ledger entry included. Benefits and next-billing-date display stay scoped to the most recent (first) row. **Assigned Plans lifecycle (migration 148, #511 stage 1 of 5)**: `STATUSES` gains two pre-activation values, `draft` and `awaiting_payment` — the ticket's "Closed" action maps onto the existing `cancelled` value, no new terminal status. A new `ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS` table enforces the ticket's §10 rules (`draft`→`awaiting_payment`/`cancelled`, `awaiting_payment`→`active`/`cancelled`, `active`↔`paused`/→`cancelled`, `paused`→`cancelled`; `expired` has no forward transitions, only ever reached by `assign-new-plan`'s supersede logic above); `PUT /:id` validates any direct `status` flip against it (400 on an invalid transition) in addition to its existing admin-only `cancelled` guard. `lifecycle_status`'s `CASE` projection was updated to pass `draft`/`awaiting_payment` through unchanged (previously unhandled, since they didn't exist), and both are added to the `lifecycle_status` filter's enum. Four new action endpoints on `/user-memberships/:id`, each recording the same `status_changed` billing event + audit log entry as every other status flip in this router: `POST /submit` (`draft`→`awaiting_payment`, `requireModuleWrite('PAYMENTS')`) — a no-op for billing today, since nothing in the running system pre-creates future `billing_events` rows yet (that lands with this ticket's Billing Events stage); `POST /pause` (`active`→`paused`); `POST /reactivate` (`paused`→`active`); and `POST /close` (`requireRole('admin')`, mirroring `DELETE`'s existing cancel restriction since both permanently end a Membership — `awaiting_payment`/`active`/`paused`→`cancelled`, stamps a new nullable `closed_at DATETIME` column kept separate from the pre-existing, admin-settable `ends_at`). Close first checks for unused value that would be lost — today limited to a scheduled MIT charge (`next_billing_date >= CURDATE()`), since per-membership benefit/usage accounting doesn't exist yet — and returns `409 {error: 'unused_value_impacted', message, warnings}` unless the request body includes `confirm: true` (same warn-then-confirm contract as `activity-type-schedule-rules.ts`'s `confirm_cancel_booked`, see the Scheduling row below). No creation entrypoint ever wrote a `draft` row and no payment path ever moved `awaiting_payment` to `active`, so **#786 retired both statuses** (migration 198 narrows the CHECK back to `active`/`paused`/`cancelled`/`expired`, refusing to run while any row holds a retired one) together with `POST /submit`: an assignment is `active` from creation, and `ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS` is `active`↔`paused`/→`cancelled`, `paused`→`cancelled`. See "Assigned Plan status model" in `docs/payments.md`. **Promotion snapshot + audit-derived Modified by/at (migration 149, #511 stage 2 of 5)**: `user_membership_promotions` gains a nullable `snapshot` JSON column, populated by a new `buildPromotionSnapshot()` in `membership-promotions.ts` at the moment a promotion is applied (both `applyPromotionToMembership()` and `POST /user-memberships/:id/promotions`) — it captures the promotion's name/description/`stackable`/dates/free-paid-bonus months plus its full `promotion_charge_benefits`/`promotion_period_benefits`/`promotion_included_benefits` rows (joined to `charge_types` for a stable code/name), the same assignment-time-snapshot idea as `user_membership_charge_benefits` (#376 above) but as one JSON column rather than relational rows, since this is a display/history concern only — it never feeds `computeFinalPrice`, which continues to read the live `promotion_charge_benefits`/`promotion_period_benefits` tables (recomputation already only happens at existing mutation points, and `final_price`/`billing_events` already capture the historical monetary result immutably). A new `withSnapshot()` overlays this onto `GET /user-memberships/:id/promotions` rows in place of the live `promotions` join whenever present, so renaming a promotion or editing its benefits after the fact no longer changes what an already-applied Assigned Plan displays; rows applied before this migration (`snapshot IS NULL`) keep falling back to the live join. Separately, `GET /user-memberships/:id` gains `created_by_name`/`modified_by_name`/`modified_at`, derived from `audit_logs` by a new `loadAuditMetadata()` helper (mirrors the existing `promotions.ts`/`themes.ts` `:id` pattern) rather than stored columns — "created" is the earliest row with `action IN ('create','assign_new_plan')` (both are creation entry points for a `user_memberships` row); "modified" is the latest row with any other action (edit, submit, close, pause, reactivate, apply/revoke promotion, add/remove member), `null` until the first one happens. Deliberately not added to `LIST_SELECT` or the list endpoint: the ticket requires this audit metadata only in the Details modal, not the list/expanded-card view. **Billing Simulation (#629)**: `GET /user-memberships/member/:memberId/billing-simulation` — a read-only, non-persisted consolidated forecast for one Member, computed by `domain/billingSimulation.ts` (see the Billing Simulation section above). **One Member, one Membership Plan (migration 213, #956)**: a Member holds **zero or one live Membership Plan** — `active` or `paused`, a future-dated row included — and assigning a new one **cancels** the one they have. Migration 213 restores migration 007's `user_memberships_one_active` unique index on `(active_member_key)` alone, reversing migration 172 (#634 §6/§14), which had narrowed it to `(active_member_key, membership_plan_id)` for "several plans in parallel, but only one of each type". The rules are declared once in `domain/oneActivePlan.ts` and the SQL half — the locked read and the supersede — in `api/one-active-plan.ts`; all three creation paths (`POST /user-memberships`, `POST /user-memberships/:id/assign-new-plan`, `POST /membership-plans/:id/assign`) call them **inside their own insert transaction**, so there is no window between the check and the insert. A caller who has not confirmed gets **`409 { error: 'active_plan_exists', message, current_plan, conflicts }`** carrying both plan names and the current plan's dates, so the frontend's confirmation dialog needs no second read (stage 2: the shared `ReplacePlanDialog`, see "Shared admin components" below — raised by all four admin entry points, never per page); `confirm: true` proceeds, and the superseded row becomes `cancelled` with `closed_at` stamped now and `ends_at` set to the new plan's `starts_at` (so the two never overlap), plus a `status_changed` ledger row. A `starts_at` earlier than the plan being replaced is a 400. "Already has a plan" is read through `user_membership_members`, not `user_memberships.member_id`, so a **covered member of someone else's family plan already has one**; the index is keyed on the owner, so that overlap is the application's half of the invariant and two concurrent assignments of a Member with *no* plan are the index's. `POST /user-memberships/:id/members` is the fourth path and the only one with **no** `confirm`: covering a Member who already has a live plan is a 409 naming it, because a replacement ends the old plan on the new one's `starts_at` and coverage added to a plan that started months ago has no such date. Migration 213 also sweeps the Members who owned more than one live row (`active` before `paused`, then latest `starts_at`, then latest `id` survives; the rest are cancelled and dated like a replacement), which `npm run memberships:multi-active` reports **before** it runs. `POST /me/payment-requests`'s `409 multiple_active_memberships` is gone with the state that produced it. `POST /user-memberships` and `POST /:id/assign-new-plan` now also reject a Plan that is not **Active + Public** (`planAssignabilityError()`, §2 — "must not be assignable through the API"), and `GET /membership-plans` accepts an `enrollment_status` filter so the pickers offer exactly what the API accepts. New read-only endpoint `GET /user-memberships/member/:memberId/configuration` (`member-membership-configuration.ts`) returns `{plans, services}` in one call: every assignment newest-first with `is_live` (active/paused since #786 — deliberately the same set the Billing Simulation consolidates) and its `activity_allowances`, plus the Additional Services of the **live** assignments, each row carrying its `user_membership_id` and `plan_name`. It holds no business logic — every write stays on the existing per-Assigned-Plan routes. Admin `MemberExpandedRow.tsx` is restructured into the ticket's sections: `MEMBERSHIP PLANS` (`MemberMembershipPlans.tsx` — all plans, inline "+ Add Membership Plan" with radio-button Plan selection; "Assign New Plan" remains as the explicit supersede action), `ADDITIONAL SERVICES` (`MemberAdditionalServices.tsx` — reuses #631's `AdditionalPeriodicServices` once per live Plan) and `BILLING SIMULATION`. Any change in the first two re-reads the configuration and remounts the simulation (§12), whose lines now name the Plan that generated each charge (§7). **Promotions are not one of them (#931)**: the `PROMOTIONS` section the ticket's §13 put here (`MemberPromotions.tsx`, Member-level, "+ Add Promotion" by picking Plan then Promotion) is gone, and the configuration payload carries no `promotions` key — a Promotion applies to a **Membership Plan** or a **Product**, never to a Member, so it is configured on the Promotions page and reaches a Member through the Plan they are assigned (#628's Promotions section of the Assign New Plan editor, and `POST /membership-plans/:id/assign`'s auto-apply). Nothing about applying, revoking, snapshotting or pricing an application changed: `POST`/`DELETE /user-memberships/:id/promotions` are untouched, every application and its snapshot stays in `user_membership_promotions`, the Assigned Plans card still lists them (`AssignedPlanPromotions.tsx`, from each application's own snapshot, #635 §16) and the Member's Billing Simulation still shows the discounts they produce. Knock-on: `POST /me/payment-requests` no longer picks "the" active membership with `LIMIT 1` — it accepts an optional `user_membership_id` (#956 removed the `409 multiple_active_memberships` beside it, since a Member has only one live assignment again). **Created by / Created at (#958, migration 215)**: the creation actor is a snapshot pair on the row (`created_by_name` + `created_by_type`, the #799 shape), written by the three paths that insert a `user_memberships` row — `POST /user-memberships`, `POST /:id/assign-new-plan` and `POST /membership-plans/:id/assign` — and backfilled from the audit rows it replaces — all three creation actions, `create`, `assign_new_plan` and the Plans page's `assign_plan`, with `created_by_type` left NULL because `audit_logs` cannot say whether the actor was a gym admin or a superadmin acting on the gym (`sourceForRole()` maps both to `admin`), and a surface that renders the type degrades to the bare name. #511 derived it from `audit_logs` in the single-row read only, deliberately, because a correlated subquery per list row was too much; the ticket's Q4 answer is that this metadata belongs on the entity, and the column is what lets the Member's plan cards each show the actor. `loadAuditMetadata()` keeps deriving the *modified* pair, which has no actor to snapshot: the nightly run, the payment webhook and the dunning escalation all modify an assignment, and `audit_logs` records them with their own `source`. `GET /user-memberships/member/:memberId/configuration` reports `created_at` (as a bare date, like every other date in that payload) plus the pair per plan. |
| Billing ledger | `billing-events.ts` | Ledger drawer in `[locale]/memberships/` | Append-only (GET + POST). `status_changed` rows are system-emitted. |
| Payments | `payments.ts` | `[locale]/payments/` (Transactions + Billing Events) | PAYMENTS module (#129). Operational view of `billing_events` excluding `status_changed`. Exposes `POST /payments/apply-promotion` which delegates to `applyPromotionToMembership` from `membership-promotions.ts`. **Billing Events admin view (#349)**: `GET /payments/billing-events` — combined past+future list; past rows sourced from `billing_events` DB, future rows computed from `user_memberships.next_billing_date` as a rolling 5-date window per active membership, merged+paginated in JS; filters `member_id`/`from`/`to`. **Status filter (#416)**: `status` is a derived field (`paid`/`failed`/`recorded`/`scheduled`, computed from `event_type` — see the mapping above), not a stored column, so the endpoint accepts `status` as a multi-value query param (repeated `status=a&status=b` or comma-separated, same `z.preprocess` pattern as `user-memberships.ts`'s `lifecycle_status`) and filters the already-merged past+future array in JS before pagination, rather than pushing it into SQL; when the selected statuses exclude `scheduled`, the future-row query/generation is skipped entirely. Admin UI: `MultiSelectFilter` in the filter bar, synced to a `?status=` URL param via the page's existing `pushParams` pattern. **Created / Next Payment (#639)**: every row also carries `created_at` — the ledger row's own creation instant as a full ISO datetime (`null` on projected rows, which aren't persisted yet) — and `next_payment_date`, the `YYYY-MM-DD` the next payment is scheduled to run for the membership the event belongs to, i.e. `user_memberships.next_billing_date`, the same value the nightly billing run charges on. It is `null` (the admin table renders `—`) when the event has no membership, the membership is no longer `active`, or nothing is scheduled; being a DATE column it carries no time of day, so the UI renders it at `00:00` in the ticket's `DD/MM/YYYY HH:mm` format without parsing it as an instant. Note mysql2 returns DATE columns as JS `Date` objects (pool `timezone: 'Z'`), so a `String(d).slice(0, 10)` yields `"Wed Oct 21"`; `toDateOnly()` in `payments.ts` normalises either representation and is what the future-row projection now uses to seed its rolling window. `GET /payments/billing-events/:id/transactions` — `payment_requests` rows with `billing_event_id = :id`, JOINs `payment_methods` for card info. Domain invariant: every `payment_requests` row has `billing_event_id` (nullable FK→`billing_events.id` ON DELETE SET NULL); `billing_events` rows can exist without a payment. `billing.ts` inserts `billing_events` first then `payment_requests(billing_event_id=…)`; `webhooks.ts` inserts `billing_events(payment_recorded)` then UPDATEs `payment_requests.billing_event_id`. Migration 119 adds the FK column. **Context menu, Details and payment actions (#640)**: status is re-derived from the latest linked Payment Transaction (`domain/billingEventStatus.ts`, `pending` added to the filter vocabulary); `GET /payments/billing-events/:id` serves the read-only Details view and `POST .../retry` + `POST .../manual-payment` the two audited actions (`domain/billingEventPayments.ts` — see the Billing Event payment actions section above). Rows carry `payment_actions_available` so the admin `ContextMenu` shows the two write items only where they apply; Details expands the same row read-only and Manual payment is an inline card inside it (no modal). |
| Payment Providers | — | `[locale]/financials/payment-providers/` | FINANCIALS module (#159). Server component (no API call) reading `PAYMENT_PROVIDER`, `PAYMENT_ENV`, `MONEI_API_KEY`, `MONEI_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, `CORDEL_FITNESS_API_URL` from admin's process.env. Shows active provider, key/secret presence (masked), environment badge, and the webhook URL to register in the provider dashboard. `/payments/payment-providers` redirects here. |
| Charge types | `charge-types.ts` | (inside ledger's Record-payment modal) | Global lookup (no `gym_id`), seeded. Extended in migration 088 with `name VARCHAR(100)` and `is_product TINYINT(1)` flag; 4 new codes added (`locker_rental`, `parking_fee`, `access_key`, `premium_fitness_app`). |
| Products | `products.ts` | `[locale]/financials/products/` (redirects from `/gym-charges`, `/sellable-items` and `/class-packages`) | #271. Unified model for all products — consolidates the 6 system gym charges and migrated class packages. `products` table extended (migrations 102–103) with: `name VARCHAR(255)`, `type ENUM(fee/service/sessions/merchandise/other)`, `units INT UNSIGNED`, `status ENUM(active/inactive)`, `is_system TINYINT(1)`, `deleted_at/deleted_by_membership_id/deleted_by_name` (soft-delete), `package_information TEXT`, `validity_days INT UNSIGNED`; `billing_frequency` CHECK extended with `'per_session'` (migration 102) and `'four_weeks'` (migration 123, #369 — a fixed 28-day period, distinct from `'month'`; `billing_frequency` is a descriptive label only, entitlement length is tracked separately via `validity_days`). #370 (migration 122) added `enrollment_status ENUM(public/staff_only)` — mirrors the `status`/`enrollment_status` split already used by `membership_plans` (see Plans row above), except with only two enrollment values (no `closed`/draft-like concept for Products): `status` answers "is this item active?", `enrollment_status` answers "who can enroll in it?" (`public` = member-facing purchase flows; `staff_only` = internal/admin sale only). `status='inactive'` always overrides `enrollment_status` at the application layer — enforced wherever a future member-facing catalog/purchase endpoint reads this table; there is no such endpoint yet, so today `enrollment_status` is exposed on the admin CRUD only (validated, filterable via `?enrollment_status=`, defaults to `public` on create). System items (`is_system=1`): auto-created at gym init, no delete, limited edits (description/amount/frequency/notes/status/enrollment_status). #371 (migration 124 + `gyms.ts` `seedSystemPtPackage()`) adds a 7th system item, seeded per-gym alongside the 6 charge-type rows: "Personal Training Class Package (10 Sessions)" (`type='sessions'`, `units=10`, `status='active'`, `enrollment_status='staff_only'`, `validity_days=182` ≈ 6 months, `tax_rate_id` = the gym's system tax rate) — staff-sellable via internal flows, not member-purchasable; price left `NULL` (set via the normal edit flow). Custom items (`is_system=0`): full CRUD with soft-delete. `GET /` filters: `?type`, `?status`, `?enrollment_status`, `?q` (name+description search). `POST /`: create custom item (requireRole admin). `PUT /:id`: system/custom split — system items: description/amount/billing_frequency/notes/status/enrollment_status only; custom: all fields including name/type/units/package_information/validity_days. `POST /:id/activate` + `POST /:id/deactivate`: set status + keep legacy `availability` in sync (`enrollment_status` untouched). `DELETE /:id`: 403 on is_system=1, soft-delete otherwise; Recycle Bin restore resets `status='active'` but leaves `enrollment_status` as-is (same convention as `membership_plans` restore not resetting `enrollment_status`). All endpoints fire audit via `recordAudit()`. `membership-plans.ts` and `promotion-details.ts` charge-benefit queries changed: LEFT JOIN charge_types, `COALESCE(gc.name, ct.name)` for name, filter by `status='active' AND deleted_at IS NULL` (unaffected by `enrollment_status`, since charge-benefit selection is an internal/admin flow). Class packages migrated as `type='sessions'` custom items in migration 103; `class_packages` table kept (FK constraint). Admin UI: inline creation row, inline edit, type/status filters, name search, system/custom context menus (system: Details/Edit/Duplicate/Activate|Deactivate; custom: +Delete), delete confirm dialog with Recycle Bin note; Status and Enrollment Status shown as separate badges/fields in the row, inline edit form, and Details modal. **(#545) Duplicate**: `POST /:id/duplicate` (requireRole admin) copies any visible item (system or custom) into a new custom (`is_system=0`), current-gym, current-user-created row named `"Copy of <original>"` — copies name/type/units/description/amount/billing_frequency/status/enrollment_status/notes/package_information/validity_days/tax_rate_id/tax_behavior, but deliberately drops `charge_type_id` (system-only concept, not part of custom-item creation) and the legacy `class_package_id` traceability FK, so the duplicate has no lineage back to the source beyond the audit log. Frontend: single immediate action (no confirmation/review step), matching the `membership-plans.ts` Duplicate convention — `handleDuplicate()` calls the endpoint then reloads the list. **(#546) Professional Services linkage**: a Product with `type='sessions'` may be linked to one or more Professional Services (#484) — a session package can bundle sessions delivered by different services, so it's many-to-many via the new `product_professional_services` join table (migration 153: `gym_id`, `product_id FK→products`, `professional_service_id FK→professional_services`, unique on `(product_id, professional_service_id)`), the same shape as `nutrition_library_item_categories` (#501). Domain helpers live in `domain/productProfessionalServices.ts` (`loadProfessionalServicesMap`, `validateProfessionalServiceIds`, `replaceProfessionalServices` — replace-all within the caller's transaction). `GET /`/`GET /:id` embed `professional_services: [{id, name, is_system}]`; `POST /`/`PUT /:id` accept `professional_service_ids: number[]`, validated against Professional Services visible to the requesting gym (global system row or this gym's own custom row, not soft-deleted) and persisted transactionally alongside the product write. Not required — an item may have zero linked services (no existing domain rule mandates at least one, per #546's own guidance not to invent one). Type-gating: the field/relationship only applies while the item's (post-update) type is `'sessions'`; `PUT` computes the *effective* type (request's `type` for custom items, unchanged for system items, which can never change type) and **always clears** the join rows when the effective type isn't `'sessions'` — a plain, unconditional clear-on-save with no confirmation step, since the join table is only a catalog association (never a booking/purchase/billing record, so nothing downstream can be left dangling). Omitting `professional_service_ids` on a `PUT` that keeps the item at `'sessions'` leaves the existing selection untouched (normal partial-update semantics); sending an array (including `[]`) replaces it. `POST /:id/duplicate` copies the source's linked services onto the new row only when the source is `'sessions'`-typed — safe by construction, since duplication always happens within the same gym the links were already validated against. Admin UI: `[locale]/financials/products/page.tsx` adds a "Professional Services" chip-checkbox multi-select (mirrors the Nutrition Library category-checkbox pattern, #501) to the inline create/edit forms, shown only when `type === 'sessions'`; the read-only expanded row and the Details modal render the linked service names when applicable. See `docs/feature-patterns.md`'s "Type-Gated M2M Relationship" entry for the reusable template. **(#821, #945) Billing Frequency is four choices**: `once`, `four_weeks`, `month`, `year` — `'week'` left the product surface with #821 and `'per_session'` with #945 (a session package's size is its **Units** and its frequency is when the whole package is billed, so *Per Session* named a usage-based model the Product does not have; `cadenceForProduct()` has always given it no schedule, i.e. it billed exactly like `once`). Nothing was migrated either time: `products_billing_frequency_check` (migration 123) still permits all six, so an item configured weekly or per-session before the ticket keeps its value, keeps reading back, keeps classifying into the same benefit section (`isRecurringFrequency()` still counts `week`; `per_session` is non-recurring, as `once` is) and keeps billing. #945 declines a `per_session → once` backfill although the two bill alike: what a gym *meant* by the value is not knowable from the row, so §3's "flag the value for correction rather than guessing" applies and the editor's notice is that flag. The accepted set lives in `domain/productFrequency.ts`, mirrored for the form in `apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/financials/products/productFrequency.ts`, and `productFrequencyWriteError(next, current)` is the one rule the routes apply: `POST /` refuses a retired value outright, `PUT /:id` accepts one **only** when it is the value the row already stores (so editing another field of a legacy item neither 400s nor silently rewrites its frequency) and refuses moving any item onto it — including moving a `week` item onto `per_session` or back. `POST /:id/duplicate` copies the stored value verbatim, legacy included — a duplicate is a faithful copy, not a re-selection. Admin UI: the retired value renders as a **disabled** option, shown only while the form holds it, plus a one-line notice under the select **naming that value** (`frequency_legacy_notice` interpolates it, since #945 there are two) and saying that choosing another replaces it; both the inline create card and the inline editor build their options from the same `frequencyOptions()` (#805). Every other surface that shows a Product's frequency (the three Benefit sections' `item` Frequency column, Additional Periodic Services, the Assigned Plan cards) only ever *displays* it through `t(`frequency_<value>`)`, so a legacy row still reads *Per Session* where it is stored — the offered list has exactly one consumer, asserted by `apps/admin/src/test/product-frequency.test.ts`. **(#832) Mandatory**: `products.mandatory` (migration 200, `tinyint(1) NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`) — one flag serving both kinds of row the page holds, the per-gym System items (`is_system=1`, the ticket's "Base Products") and the gym's own (`is_system=0`, "Custom"), since both live in this one table. Attribute and UI only in #832; since **#893** exactly one consumer exists — a mandatory item is part of every **Membership Plan** benefit section (see the Plans row and `domain/mandatoryPlanBenefits.ts`). Everything else is still out of scope: automatic inclusion in a **Billing** Plan, preventing removal from one, Billing Plan pricing and any Insurance Fee special case belong to a later ticket that consumes them. `POST /` defaults it to `0` when absent; `POST /:id/duplicate` copies it verbatim; `PUT /:id` writes it as `mandatory = IF(?, ?, mandatory)` keyed on whether the request supplied the field (`mandatoryProvided`, the shape `PUT /promotions/:id` uses for `only_applicable_for_new_members`) — a `COALESCE` would make `false` unpersistable, and the write sits **outside** the `IF(? = 0, …)` `is_system` guard the catalogue-shape columns carry, because a System item's flag is editable even though its name/type/units are not. No CHECK: the router coerces to `? 1 : 0`, and adding one would rebuild `products` under `ALGORITHM=COPY`. Admin UI: a checkbox in the inline create card and the inline editor (never hidden for a System item), plus a Yes/No row on the read-only expanded card and in the Details modal. **(#974) One card body, two modes**: the expanded read-only card and the inline editor render from one declaration (`productProfile.ts` — the five sections in order, each section's fields, the `type`/`status`/`enrollment_status` option sets, and the persisted-row → form-values mapping) through one layout (`ProductLayout.tsx`, over `CardSectionHeader` + `formChrome`'s section/control/value styles). Before it the read-only half was a 160px-label `Label: value` list that omitted Name and joined the linked Professional Services into a sentence, while the editor was a fixed two-column grid — so the two agreed on the section names and nothing else, and every field added since had to be added twice in two shapes. `visibleProductSections({ isSystem, isSessionType })` is the one function both halves ask: it drops the session-only section for a non-session item (off the *draft's* Type while the editor is open, so choosing Sessions reveals it before saving), drops Package information and Validity for a System row, and returns `editable: false` for a System row's frozen name/type/units — which the layout renders as **values in both modes**, the mechanism #927 added for a calculated field, since `PUT /:id` writes those three only inside its `is_system` guard while the card must still report them. Mandatory stays editable there (#832). The editor's Save/Cancel moved to the shared left-aligned `inlineActionsRowStyle` with `secondaryBtnSmall`/`primaryBtnSmall()` (#929/#968/#974 §4), the create card's pair with it; the page's own `SectionHeader`, `DetailRow`, `inlineInputStyle`, `inlineSelectStyle`, `inlineLabelStyle`, `checkboxLabelStyle` and `errorStyle` are gone, and the list's search and Type controls take `filterControlStyle` (#724) rather than a hardcoded `#ccc`, so every control on the screen follows the Theme's input pair. Presentation only: no endpoint, no payload, no column and no locale key changed, and the Details modal keeps its own field list and the audit metadata (#974 §7). Test: `apps/admin/src/test/products-expanded-read-only.test.ts`. **#949 stage 3 — the wire and the schema (migration 214)**: the router is mounted at **`/products`** (there is no `/sellable-items` alias; the owner answered `Q1 C` and "a complete refactor to leave just products" on the thread), the table is **`products`**, its FK column **`product_id`** across the thirteen tables that key to it, the three promotion snapshots' name column **`product_name`**, the join table **`product_professional_services`**, the eight CHECKs **`products_*`** / `chk_products_enrollment_status`, and `charge_types.is_gym_charge` → **`is_product`** (the **Charge Type** itself keeps its name — a different concept that merely shares a word, per `CLAUDE.md`'s hard constraint and `Q3`). Three stored values moved with them: `promotions.applies_to` `'sellable_item'` → **`'product'`** with `chk_promotions_applies_to` rebuilt (so `promotion-target.unit.test.ts` now reads migration 214's exported set, not 204's), the feature-flag key `financials.gym_charges` → **`financials.products`** (one UPDATE, so the gym's own choice travels with the key), and `audit_logs.entity_type` `'gym_charge'` → **`'product'`** — the history's `entity_type` is the key `AUDIT_ENTITY_REGISTRY` and the Audit Log's filter are built from, while the audited values (`previous_values`/`new_values`) are left exactly as written. The response fields follow (`products` on an enriched Plan and on a tax rate's delete impact, `product_id`/`product_name`/`product_status`/`product_deleted_at`/`product_retired`), as do the Billing Simulation line `kind: 'product'` and the recycle bin's `product` entity type. Two admin routes stay as `permanentRedirect`s to `financials/products` — `financials/sellable-items` and `financials/gym-charges`, one per earlier name — and the old migrations keep their own file names and SQL, because they are the history of a schema that really did carry those names. `product-identifiers.unit.test.ts` now bans the `snake_case`, `gym_charge*` and `gymCharge*` shapes stage 2 left to stage 3, so it is a flat ban again with no allowlist; a line naming a migration **file** is read with that name removed. Test: `api/src/test/products.test.ts` (renamed from `gym-charges.test.ts`). |
| Assigned Plans | — (reuses `user-memberships.ts`) | `[locale]/financials/assigned-plans/` | #410. Lists every Membership assignment across the gym — Member Name, Plan Name, Start Date, End Date ("Open-ended" when `ends_at` is null), and a `lifecycle_status` badge (pending/active/paused/expired/cancelled, see the User memberships row above). Calls the existing `GET /user-memberships` (PAYMENTS module + `payments.transactions` flag) directly — no dedicated list endpoint. Nav entry under the Financials group, `financials.assigned_plans` feature key (not seeded in `feature_flags` — defaults to enabled). #411 added an advanced filter bar: multi-select `lifecycle_status` (`MultiSelectFilter`), a debounced member-search filter, and `start_date`/`end_date` range filters — `GET /user-memberships` accepts `lifecycle_status` (repeatable or comma-separated), `start_date`, `end_date` alongside its existing `status`/`member_id` params; `lifecycle_status` is filtered via an outer query over a derived table since it's a computed SELECT alias. **(#511 stage 4 of 5) Expandable card + actions**: rows are now expandable via the shared `DataTable` (Members-page pattern) instead of a flat table; expanding a row mounts `AssignedPlanExpandedRow.tsx`, which fetches the stage-3 `GET /user-memberships/:id` expanded detail (members, pricing/billing config, benefit usage, applied promotions, the Billing Events view) and renders it directly in the card — Created/Modified audit fields are deliberately left out of the card and shown only in `AssignedPlanDetailsModal.tsx` (mirrors `PromotionDetailModal`'s field/value + Audit-section layout, fed from the already-loaded detail rather than a second fetch). **#786 removed the Edit and Submit items described next, and `awaiting_payment` from `StatusBadge` and the `status.*` locales — both were gated on the retired pre-activation statuses and could never be reached; the menu is Details, Pause, Reactivate and Close (`active`/`paused`).** A per-row `ContextMenu` exposed Details (always), Edit (`draft`/`awaiting_payment` only, `requireModuleWrite('PAYMENTS')` — extended past the ticket's original Awaiting-Payment-only scope since `draft` didn't exist when that matrix was written, and the issue thread's Q1 answer explicitly allows configuring a draft before Submit), Submit (`draft` only), Pause (`active` only), Reactivate (`paused` only), and Close (`awaiting_payment`/`active`/`paused`, admin-only, matching `POST /:id/close`'s `requireRole('admin')`). Edit switches the card into an inline form (starts_at/ends_at/discount_reason/discount_expires_at — since #635 stage 15 the Membership Fee is not among them: it belongs to the snapshot and is edited in `BILLING & DURATION` — the fields `PUT /:id` actually accepts) with standard Save/Cancel, matching `MemberExpandedRow`'s session-package inline-edit shape rather than a modal. Close always asks for confirmation first (`ConfirmDialog`), then — if `POST /:id/close` responds `409 unused_value_impacted` — shows a second confirmation listing the returned `warnings` before resending with `confirm: true`; every action re-fetches the row's detail and calls the list's `onChanged` to refresh the parent table. `StatusBadge` and the `status.*` locale namespace gained an `awaiting_payment` entry (all 3 locales); a new `apps/admin/src/test/assigned-plans-locales.test.ts` (mirrors `member-locales.test.ts` from #540/#563) guards the `assigned_plans_page.*` key set staying complete across en/es/ca. **(#958)** The card body is `components/assignedPlan/` now, not a page directory: `AssignedPlanExpandedRow.tsx` (with `AssignedPlanConfiguration`, `AssignedPlanPromotions`, `AdditionalPeriodicServices`, `AssignedPlanDetailsModal`, `assignedPlanProfile.ts` and `types.ts`) is rendered by this page *and* by the Member card's MEMBERSHIP PLANS section, which expands a plan card into exactly these sections — one section list, one set of `assigned_plans_page` keys, one `GET /user-memberships/:id` read (#806's rule applied to a read surface). The Member card passes `embedded`, which drops the body's own summary header and its `⋮`, and therefore its Edit mode: `⋮ → Edit` is the single entry point into one (#797), so expanding a plan on the Member page reads it and editing stays on this page. `AssignedPlanDetailsDialog.tsx` beside it is the one thing #958 added: it loads an assignment and hands it to the same Details modal, for the Member card's `⋮ → Details`, which has no detail in hand. **(#1051)** The *list* around that body is shared the same way: `AssignedPlansTable.tsx` holds the `Column<…>[]` declaration, the `DataTable`, the chevron, the Status badge and the expanded body, and this page supplies only its rows and its filter bar — it keeps every column, header and behaviour it had, because `scope` defaults to `gym`. The Member card renders the same component with `scope="member"` (the Plan names the row instead of the Member) and `rowActions` (its own `⋮`); neither screen declares a column, a date formatter or a status badge of its own any more. |
| Class packages | `class-packages.ts`, `user-class-packages.ts`, `package-credits.ts` | `[locale]/class-packages/` (catalog); Members page → expanded row "Session Packages" section (per-member) | Catalog + per-member packages + credit transactions; credits consumed/refunded on booking lifecycle (see Bookings row for the #372 same-day-vs-advance refund rule and manual refund/walk-in endpoints). **#372**: `MemberExpandedRow.tsx` fetches `GET /members/:memberId/class-packages` and renders each package (name, purchased_at, total/used/remaining sessions, expires_at, status) as a card; PAYMENTS-write staff see an "Extend expiration" action. `PUT /members/:memberId/class-packages/:id/extend-expiration` (requireModuleWrite PAYMENTS) sets a new `expires_at` — 400 unless strictly later than the current value — and records the change via `recordAudit` plus new `user_class_packages.modified_at`/`modified_by_membership_id` columns (migration 128); it does not touch `sessions_remaining`/`status`, which stay driven entirely by the booking/attendance lifecycle. |
| Promotions | `promotions.ts`, `promotion-details.ts` | `[locale]/promotions/` | Admin-only. Plan targeting, Session / One-off / Periodical Benefits (migration 155, keyed to a Product) and the Membership Fee Benefit. Soft delete via `lifecycle_status='deleted'` + `deleted_at` + `deleted_by_membership_id` (migration 093). Billing lifecycle config (migration 102): `free_months` (initial free period before billing), `paid_months` (months at promotional price), `bonus_months` (bonus months after paid period) on the `promotions` table. **Membership Fee Benefit (#551, #635 stage 5, #814)**: `promotion_membership_fee_benefits` (migration 179) — one row per Promotion (`UNIQUE promotion_id`), with `action VARCHAR(30)` (no_benefit/waive/percentage_discount/fixed_discount/fixed_price), `value`, `duration_months` (nullable = the whole Promotion, capped at it by #625) and `enabled`. **Those four fields are the whole benefit**, and they are exactly what `MembershipFeeBenefit` (`domain/promotionApplication.ts`) carries into `resolveMembershipFee()`. It has **no recurrence**: `quantity`, `frequency_interval` and `frequency_unit` were inherited from the `promotion_period_benefits` shape the section was edited in ("2 sessions every 3 months") and dropped by migration 199 (#814) together with `chk_pmfb_positive` and `chk_pmfb_frequency_unit` — there is one membership fee per assignment and its cadence is the assignment's own Billing frequency (`ASSIGNMENT_CADENCE`), never a Promotion's. Nothing priced on them, so no assignment re-prices; snapshots written before #814 keep those keys and no caller reads them. Do not reintroduce a recurrence, a quantity or a frequency on this benefit. It also carries no item reference: the item is always the membership fee. Singleton `GET`/`PUT /promotions/:id/membership-fee-benefit` (an upsert, not replace-all). The three `charge_types`-keyed tables it replaced — `promotion_charge_benefits` (020/092), `promotion_period_benefits` (021/070/093) and `promotion_included_benefits` (102) — and their endpoints are gone (migration 179); a Promotion's other benefits are the Product-keyed `promotion_{session,oneoff,periodical}` tables. Charge_type seeds (`is_product=0`): `nutrition_service`, `personal_training`, `duet_personal_training`, `group_class`. Admin page: expandable-row UX, one section editable at a time (#627); edit form has Billing & Duration section (four inputs, see #486 below) + live example timeline table + Session / One-off / Periodical Benefits + the Membership Fee Benefit with Duration, Action, Value and Enabled (#814 removed its Quantity / Every / Unit controls; the Product sections keep their own). **(#486) Pay Beforehand + server-side Forecast**: `pay_beforehand_months` (migration 141, unsigned int, `NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`) selects how many of a Promotion's `paid_months` are already paid beforehand; validated on POST/PUT (`validatePayBeforehandMonths` in the new pure module `domain/promotionTimeline.ts`) as `0 <= pay_beforehand_months <= paid_months` — a PUT that only patches one of the two fields validates against the other's current DB value. The Free/Pay/Prepaid/Bonus/Regular forecast classification (previously duplicated client-side) now lives entirely in `domain/promotionTimeline.ts`'s `computePromotionTimeline`: given `{freeMonths, paidMonths, payBeforehandMonths, bonusMonths}` it returns one `pay_regular`-terminated period per month (`free_promotion` → `prepaid_promotion`/`pay_promotion` → `bonus_promotion` → one open-ended `pay_regular`), each with a stable status key and `startsOn`/`endsOn` (`YYYY-MM-DD`, `endsOn: null` for the open-ended last period) computed from an `anchorDate` (defaults to the first of the current month — a hypothetical enrollment date for illustration; never persisted). Exposed as `GET /promotions/timeline?free_months&paid_months&pay_beforehand_months&bonus_months&anchor_date` — a config-only preview endpoint (not tied to a saved Promotion id, registered before `/:id`) so the admin page's live timeline, which must recalculate on every unsaved keystroke, can call the backend instead of re-implementing the classification rule (per the "no duplicated business logic in the frontend" hard constraint). The frontend renders `t(STATUS_LABEL_KEYS[status])`/`t(STATUS_BILLING_KEYS[status])` off the returned status key rather than comparing translated strings. User-facing labels: `Free (promotion)`, `Pay (promotion)`, `Prepaid (promotion)`, `Bonus (promotion)`, `Pay (regular)` (the old `Paid (promotion)`/`Paid (regular)` wording is gone). **(#487 stage 1) Configurable Membership Fee benefit on Period Benefits**: `promotion_period_benefits` gains nullable `action VARCHAR(30)` + `value DECIMAL(10,2)` (migration 144), mirroring `promotion_charge_benefits`'s action/value convention exactly (same 5-value CHECK: `no_benefit`/`waive`/`percentage_discount`/`fixed_discount`/`fixed_price`; `value` only persisted for the 3 actions that need one, `0<=value<=100` for `percentage_discount`, `value>=0` for `fixed_discount`/`fixed_price`). Validated in `validatePeriodBenefit()` across all four write paths (`PUT`/`POST`/`PUT /:pbId` replace/create/update, `GET` just returns the columns) plus the `POST /:id/duplicate` copy (which also now copies `duration_months`, previously silently dropped). Stage 1 is display/config only — zero effect on real billing (`computeFinalPrice` in `membership-promotions.ts`) or the Forecast/timeline endpoint; that wiring is stage 2/3 of #487, separate future PRs. Admin UI: the Period Benefits row whose charge type is Membership Fee (`charge_type_code === 'membership_fee'`) gets the same inline Action selector + dynamic Value input (percentage/currency, switching actions clears the value) as Charge Benefits, reusing its `cb_action_*` i18n keys; every other Period Benefit row is unaffected and keeps `action`/`value` as `null`. **(#487 stage 2) shared benefit calc + real-billing fixes**: `domain/promotionBenefits.ts`'s `applyPeriodBenefit(amount, action, value)` is now the single place that turns a Charge/Period Benefit action into a resulting amount (clamped at 0) — `computeFinalPrice` (`membership-promotions.ts`) calls it instead of inlining `if`/`else`, which also adds the `fixed_price` branch it was missing. Do not confuse this with `domain/billingForecast.ts`'s `applyChargeBenefit`, a separate function for the unrelated Plan Charge Benefits forecast (#485) that explicitly excludes promotions. Fixed two pre-existing bugs found while wiring this up: `computeFinalPrice` selected the long-dropped `membership_plans.base_price` (migration 058) instead of the `user_memberships.base_price` snapshot, 500ing on every call; and `promotion_charge_benefits`'s `action` CHECK was never actually widened for `fixed_price` — migration 102's `DROP CHECK pcb_action_check` targeted the wrong constraint name (the real one, from migration 092, was `chk_prcb_action`), so the stale 4-value constraint silently rejected every `fixed_price` row until migration 145 dropped it. **(#487 stage 3) wiring Period Benefits into real billing**: `computeFinalPrice` now also queries `promotion_period_benefits` (filtered to `charge_types.code = 'membership_fee'` and `enabled = 1`) for the membership's applied promotion(s) and applies each via `applyPeriodBenefit`, after the existing Charge Benefit loop — the two stack. Unlike Charge Benefits (unconditional for as long as the promotion is applied), a Period Benefit is gated by its `duration_months` window counted from `user_membership_promotions.applied_at` (`applied_at + duration_months months > NOW()`; `NULL` never expires); `quantity`/`frequency_interval`/`frequency_unit` are not part of this gate — they describe how a count-based benefit recurs, not whether the Membership Fee action is in effect. `computeFinalPrice` still only runs at existing mutation points (assignment, promotion apply/revoke) — nothing reverts `final_price` on its own the instant a `duration_months` window lapses without a new mutation. The Forecast/timeline surface remains stage 4, a separate future PR. **(#551) Membership Fee Benefits — dedicated category**: the Membership Fee `promotion_period_benefits` row (previously mixed into the generic Period Benefits list, singled out only by `charge_type_code === 'membership_fee'` in the frontend per #487 stage 1) now has its own singleton sub-resource, `GET`/`PUT /promotions/:id/membership-fee-benefit`, reusing `validatePeriodBenefit()`/`periodBenefitValue()` unchanged — the only difference is `charge_type_id` is always server-resolved to `membership_fee`, never accepted from the client, and the row is upserted (looked up by `(promotion_id, charge_type_id)`, no separate create/delete). The generic `/period-benefits` endpoints (`GET`/`PUT`/`POST`/`PUT :pbId`/`DELETE :pbId`) now all exclude or reject the `membership_fee` charge type, so the two UI sections can never write the same row — `PUT /period-benefits`'s bulk replace-all scopes its `DELETE` to `charge_type_id != membership_fee's id` specifically so saving the generic list can't wipe the Membership Fee row out from under it. No schema change — the two categories are still one table underneath, purely a routing/UI split; `POST /:id/duplicate`'s existing blanket copy of `promotion_period_benefits` needed no change. Admin UI: Period Benefits' Action/Value columns are gone (they could only ever apply to the now-carved-out Membership Fee row) and its item picker no longer offers Membership Fee; a new "Membership Fee Benefits" section shows the same Quantity/Every/Unit/Duration/Action/Value/Enabled fields as a single, always-present row with the item fixed to Membership Fee (no select). **(#552, the #487-stage-4 Forecast/timeline wiring)**: `GET /promotions/timeline` and `domain/promotionTimeline.ts`'s `computePromotionTimeline` gained four optional inputs mirroring the Membership Fee Benefit's shape (`membershipFeeAction`/`membershipFeeValue`/`membershipFeeEnabled`/`membershipFeeDurationMonths`, sent as unsaved draft values from the admin form — never a persisted lookup, consistent with the timeline's existing config-only/non-persisted nature) and each returned period gained `billingAction`/`billingValue`: on `pay_promotion`/`prepaid_promotion` periods, set to the benefit's action/value while it's enabled and (if `durationMonths` is set) not yet expired counting from the timeline's own anchor date — otherwise `null`, meaning that period bills at the regular price. `free_promotion`/`bonus_promotion`/`pay_regular` periods never carry a billing action (their Billing column text is fixed). Admin UI: the Example Timeline moved from inside Billing & Duration to the bottom of the Promotion edit form (below Membership Fee Benefits), with an added disclaimer that the simulation assumes a monthly billing period; its Billing column now reads the benefit's action to render `No charge` (`waive`), `Regular price` (`no_benefit`/none), or `Promotional price (N%)`/`(N€)`/`(-N€)` (percentage/fixed price/fixed discount) instead of the previous generic static-per-status text; the four Billing & Duration field labels dropped their "(months)" suffix. **(#554) Suitable Membership Plans hardening**: `PUT /:id/plans` (plan targeting, `promotion_membership_plans`, originally #26) now requires every *newly added* plan id to be active (`lifecycle_status='active'`, `deleted_at IS NULL`) and gym-owned — an id the promotion was already associated with is let through even if that plan has since gone inactive elsewhere, so an unrelated re-save never drops a pre-existing association; duplicate ids in the request are deduped rather than hitting the table's unique constraint. No schema change — the association, its endpoints, and the apply-time eligibility check (`applyPromotionToMembership`/`POST /user-memberships/:id/promotions`, both already rejecting a promotion that doesn't target the membership's plan) all predate this ticket. Admin UI: the "Applicable Plans" section is renamed "Suitable Membership Plans" and gains a Select All checkbox (checked/unchecked/indeterminate — pure logic in `apps/admin/src/lib/suitablePlansSelection.ts`, unit tested, only ever acting on the *displayed* active plans so a hidden legacy association is never touched), its own loading/empty/error states (split out of the page's other best-effort lookups), and secondary info (current price or enrollment status) per plan; the read-only view now renders directly off `GET /:id/plans`' own `{id, name}` rows instead of resolving against the active-only plans list, fixing a bug where an inactive-but-associated plan displayed as `#<id>`. **(#550 stage 1) Schema for Product-keyed benefit reclassification**: `promotion_period_benefits`/`promotion_included_benefits` key off the `charge_types` pseudo-catalog (`is_product=0` rows like `nutrition_service`/`personal_training` that were never real per-gym Products), which the ticket's classification (Session/One-off/Periodical, based on a real Product's `type`/`billing_frequency`) can't be backfilled onto. Migration 155 adds three new tables keyed to `product_id FK→products` instead — `promotion_session` (`type='sessions'`), `promotion_oneoff` (non-recurring `billing_frequency`), `promotion_periodical` (recurring `billing_frequency`, read live off the Product's own frequency, not stored per-benefit) — each `(id, gym_id, promotion_id FK→promotions CASCADE, product_id FK→products CASCADE, quantity, created_at, created_by_membership_id)`, unique on `(promotion_id, product_id)`. The classification itself lives in the new pure module `domain/productClassification.ts` (`classifyProduct()`, `isRecurringFrequency()`, `benefitTableForCategory()`) — the single source of truth so the frontend never re-derives it (per the "no duplicated business logic in the frontend" hard constraint). Migration 156 adds three parallel, denormalized (`product_name` copied at write time) snapshot tables (`user_membership_promotion_{session,oneoff,periodical}_snapshot`, FK'd to `user_membership_promotions`) per the issue's request to "leave the system ready" for a future ticket's assignment-time snapshot; not yet written to or read anywhere. This is schema + classification helper only (stage 1 of #550's staged plan) — `promotion_period_benefits`/`promotion_included_benefits` and their `promotion-details.ts` endpoints are deliberately left untouched and still live; the API cutover and the frontend's `/charge-types`-as-"Products" replacement land with stage 2+ rather than break the app mid-migration. **Migration 158** does complete the ticket's explicit "hard delete all promotions [benefit data] and start from scratch" clarification now rather than deferring it to the cutover: it `TRUNCATE`s (not drops — `promotion-details.ts`/`promotions.ts`/the admin Promotions page still read/write these two tables) `promotion_period_benefits` and `promotion_included_benefits`, mirroring the existing clean-slate precedent in migration 055 (`TRUNCATE TABLE audit_logs`). Any Period/Included Benefit an admin saves through the still-live old UI between this migration and the stage-2 cutover will simply repopulate those two (now-empty) tables under the old `charge_types` scheme, same as before this ticket touched them. `promotion_charge_benefits` (discount-only, already keyed to `product_id` since migration 092) is a separate concern, untouched by this ticket. **(#550 stage 2) Backend validation + endpoints**: three new sub-resources, `GET`/`PUT /promotions/:id/session-benefits`, `/oneoff-benefits`, `/periodical-benefits`, backed by migration 155's `promotion_session`/`promotion_oneoff`/`promotion_periodical` tables — same one-time-grant shape as `/included-benefits` (`{product_id, quantity}`, replace-all `PUT`), but keyed to a real Product. `PUT` validates, per item: `product_id` resolves to an `active`, non-deleted Product owned by this gym; no duplicate `product_id` within one request; `quantity` a positive integer; and — the new "section-classification enforcement" the ticket asked for — `classifyProduct()` on the looked-up Product's `type`/`billing_frequency` must match the endpoint's own category, so e.g. a `type='sessions'` item can never be saved via `/oneoff-benefits`. `POST /:id/duplicate` (`promotions.ts`) now also copies all three tables' rows, alongside its existing Charge/Period/Included Benefits copy. This stage deliberately leaves the legacy `/period-benefits` (non-Membership-Fee) and `/included-benefits` endpoints from stage 1 untouched and still live, addressable in parallel with the three new ones — the admin frontend hasn't been repointed yet (stage 3), so retiring the old endpoints/tables now would break the currently-live Promotions UI mid-migration; that retirement happens together with the stage-3 frontend cutover. Assignment-flow wiring (populating migration 156's snapshot tables when a Promotion is applied to a Membership) remains explicitly out of scope, per the issue owner's clarification, and is a separate future ticket. **(#550 stage 3) Frontend cutover + retiring the legacy endpoints**: the admin Promotions page's Included Benefits and generic Period Benefits sections are replaced by three Product-keyed sections — Session Benefits, One-off Benefits, Period Benefits — wired to stage 2's `/session-benefits`/`/oneoff-benefits`/`/periodical-benefits`. New selections are sourced from `GET /products?availability=available`, whose response now carries a server-computed `benefit_category` (`'session'|'oneoff'|'periodical'`, via `classifyProduct()` in `products.ts`'s `attachBenefitCategory()`) so the frontend groups the picker by that field rather than re-deriving the type/frequency rule itself. The old `charge_types`-pseudo-catalog-driven `/period-benefits` (bulk + single-row) and `/included-benefits` endpoints in `promotion-details.ts` are removed outright — `promotion_period_benefits` stays in the schema (still the sole backing table for the untouched `/membership-fee-benefit` singleton, #551) and `promotion_included_benefits` stays too but is now unused (no write path remains; its historical rows, already emptied by migration 158, are not backfilled anywhere, per the issue owner's "start from scratch" instruction). `PUT` on the three stage-2 endpoints was also loosened to fix a gap stage 2 had flagged: only a *newly* selected `product_id` must be `active` — one already associated with the promotion is let through even if it has since gone inactive, mirroring the Suitable Membership Plans `/plans` PUT pattern, so "existing selected items remain visible when editing" (the ticket's explicit requirement) holds without needing the client to silently drop a since-deactivated selection. Stage 4 (tests/docs polish) is folded into this PR rather than landing separately, since CLAUDE.md's finishing checklist already requires tests/docs on every PR. **(#625) Membership Fee Benefit duration capped at the Promotion duration**: a Promotion Period Benefit belongs to the Promotion lifecycle and can never outlast it, so the Membership Fee Benefit's `duration_months` is bounded by the total Promotion duration `free_months + paid_months + bonus_months` (Pay Beforehand excluded — it only reclassifies paid months as prepaid, never lengthens the Promotion). Two pure helpers in `domain/promotionBenefits.ts` are the single source of truth: `promotionDurationMonths(free, paid, bonus)` and `effectiveBenefitDurationMonths(configured, promotionDuration)` = `min(configured, promotionDuration)`, with a `null` (unbounded) configured duration resolving to the full Promotion duration. `PUT /promotions/:id/membership-fee-benefit` now loads the promotion's free/paid/bonus and rejects (400) an explicit `duration_months` greater than that sum (a `null` duration is still allowed). `computePromotionTimeline` applies the *effective* (clamped) duration when deciding which `pay_promotion`/`prepaid_promotion` periods carry the benefit's billing action, so the forecast never shows the benefit in the `pay_regular` period even for an over-long or unbounded configured duration. Admin UI: the Membership Fee Benefit Duration input is `max`-capped at the live Promotion duration (computed from the edit form) and clamps on entry, and an effect re-constrains an already-entered duration when the Promotion is shortened. Migration 162 is a one-off data cleanup clamping existing `promotion_period_benefits` `membership_fee` rows whose `duration_months` exceeds their promotion's duration. **(#626) Charge Benefits removed from the Promotion UI**: the Promotion editor no longer displays or configures Charge Benefits — the edit form's per-Product action/value grid and the read-only view's benefit list are both gone, together with the page's `cbDraft`/`cachedCb` state and its `GET`/`PUT /promotions/:id/charge-benefits` calls, and the `promotions` i18n namespace's `section_charge_benefits`/`no_charge_benefits`/`col_charge`/`add_charge_benefit`/`save_charge_benefits` keys. Deliberately frontend-only: `promotion_charge_benefits`, its endpoints and the `POST /:id/duplicate` copy all stay, because the table is not exclusively required by the removed UI — `membership-promotions.ts` still reads it when a Promotion is applied to a Membership (the `charge_benefits` snapshot and `computeFinalPrice`'s Charge Benefit loop, #487 stage 2), and Assigned Plans still renders that snapshot. Promotion benefits are now configured only through the Session / One-off / Period (#550) and Membership Fee (#551) sections. The Membership Plans editor's own Charge Benefits section (`plan_charge_benefits`, see the Plans row) is unrelated and unaffected. **(#627) Promotion editing split by section**: the Promotion card is never editable as a whole. A single `editingSection` (`'main' | 'session' | 'oneoff' | 'periodical' | 'membership_fee'`) alongside `editingId` says which one part of which card is in edit mode — the context-menu **Edit** action sets `'main'` (General, Suitable Membership Plans, Billing & Duration only), and each Benefit section has its own **Edit** button (`enterSectionEdit`) that seeds only that section's draft from freshly reloaded saved values. `handleSaveMain` writes `PUT /promotions/:id` + `PUT /:id/plans` and nothing else; `handleSaveBenefitSection` writes only that section's endpoint (`/session-benefits`, `/oneoff-benefits`, `/periodical-benefits` or `/membership-fee-benefit`); Cancel discards only the open section's draft. Every other section's Edit button (and the context-menu Edit) is disabled while one section is open, since the drafts are single-valued. Creating a Promotion is the one exception and stays a single form covering every section (`handleCreate`) — there is no Promotion id yet to hang per-section saves off. Frontend-only: no schema, endpoint or Membership-Plan-status change, and no modal (the inline/expandable card is preserved). #625's cap is upheld across the split by `clampSavedMembershipFeeDuration`, which re-PUTs an already-saved Membership Fee Benefit whose `duration_months` the main save has just shortened the Promotion past (to the new duration, or to `null` when the Promotion has no duration at all) — otherwise that benefit would be left permanently rejected (400) by the endpoint's own `duration_months <= promotion duration` check. **(#633) `only_applicable_for_new_members`** (migration 163, `tinyint(1) NOT NULL DEFAULT 1`, after `stackable`): a second main-configuration boolean, rendered as a checkbox directly below "Stackable with other promotions" and checked by default on create. Stored and returned only — `POST`/`PUT`/`GET`/duplicate carry it, `POST` defaults it to `1` when the caller omits it or sends `null`, and existing rows were backfilled to `1` by the column default. **Nothing reads it yet**: Promotion eligibility, stacking (`domain/promotionStacking.ts`), the apply path in `membership-promotions.ts` and the Assign-Plan-to-Member flow are all deliberately unchanged, and the `user_membership_promotions` snapshot does not capture it — the behaviour that consumes the flag is a separate ticket, which will need to add it to `buildPromotionSnapshot()` at that point. **(#900) `expired` is a Promotion status** (migration 202 widens `chk_promotions_lifecycle_status` to `active`/`inactive`/`expired`/`deleted`): `inactive` means somebody switched the Promotion off, `expired` means its validity period ran out on its own, and the two are never collapsed. Before this ticket a past-dated Promotion sat at `active` for ever while every apply path already refused it (`validatePromotionSelection` / `applyPromotionToMembership`: *outside its active window*), so the status column and the behaviour disagreed; `expired` is exactly "no longer applicable", and because those paths require `lifecycle_status = 'active'` it is non-applicable for free. The status list the API accepts is declared once in `api/src/domain/promotionLifecycle.ts` (`PROMOTION_LIFECYCLE_STATUSES` — `deleted` deliberately absent, it is reached only through DELETE and restored only through the Recycle Bin) and is what `GET /promotions?lifecycle_status=` and the write validation both read. `expired` *is* accepted on a write because `PUT /promotions/:id` is a partial update that carries the status back unchanged — refusing it would make an expired Promotion un-editable — but the editor never offers it: the page has a filter list (all three, §10) and an edit list (`active`/`inactive`), and a row already `expired` renders that value as a **disabled** option so saving its other fields cannot quietly reactivate it. Setting it back to Active is how a gym revives one, and the sweep expires it again unless its End Date moved. Nothing is backfilled by the migration (the sweep does it on its first run, counted in the workflow log) and `POST /:id/duplicate` still copies the source's status and dates verbatim, so duplicating an expired Promotion yields an expired copy — Duplicate is a copy, not a new Promotion. `StatusBadge` already had a colour for `expired` and `status.expired` already existed in en/es/ca, so §9's "reuse the existing badge" needed no new key. **#919/#920 (sections read as one table, with prices):** the three Product sections (`GET`/`PUT /promotions/:id/{session,oneoff,periodical}-benefits`) now report the same `original_price_incl_tax` / `final_price_incl_tax` pair plus the two `*_line_*` totals that the Membership Plan sections have carried since #916, labelled *Regular Price* / *Final Price* in the `promotions` namespace. The wiring is shared rather than copied: #916's `grossBenefitUnitPrice()` and the `planBenefitPrices()` call that wraps it moved out of `membership-plans.ts` into `api/src/api/product-benefit-pricing.ts` (`withProductBenefitPrices(context, rows, catalogue?)`), and `promotion-details.ts` is its second caller with `context: 'promotion'` — which is what prices `fixed_discount` and `fixed_price`, the two actions a Plan may not configure (#896 §16); for those two the configured amount is taken at face value as the VAT-inclusive figure the gym typed and is never grossed up a second time. The section `SELECT`s gained `gc.amount`, `gc.tax_behavior` and a `LEFT JOIN tax_rates`, from the benefit row's own join, so a Promotion still prices an item that has since been deactivated. Nothing is stored and nothing is charged — an *applied* Promotion is still priced from that application's own snapshot (#635 §16); these columns are a catalogue-side preview. Admin: `showFrequency` is true for all three sections (so Session and One-off rows hold a `—` instead of shifting the columns after them, needing `promotions.frequency_once` / `frequency_per_session`), and the Membership Fee Promotion's `DURATION (MONTHS)` column is relabelled `DURATION` (`promotions.col_duration`) in both halves of the section — terminology only, the stored `duration_months` is unchanged. **#922 (Billing Event Simulation):** the Promotion card gains the Membership Plan card's third projection (#915), over the items a Promotion affects — one group per **billing date**, each line carrying what it would otherwise have cost beside what it costs with the Promotion applied, plus a per-date total, VAT included. It is the *same* projection rather than a second one, which is the ticket's own requirement ("do not create a second pricing engine [...] the only difference should be the input/context"): #915's shared parts — the result shape, the `SIMULATED_CYCLES` horizon floor, the group-by-date re-grouping — moved into the new pure `api/src/domain/billingEventSimulation.ts`, and each entity now has only its adapter beside it (`planBillingEventSimulation.ts` re-exports the shared names, so #915's own importers are unchanged). The new `api/src/domain/promotionBillingEventSimulation.ts` expresses the Promotion as the hypothetical assignment of a member given it today — grants only, open-ended (a Promotion reaching its End Date never revokes a standing application, #900), anchored on its own `starts_at` when that is still in the future — and hands it to `computeBillingSimulation()`, so every date, every covered unit or period and every amount is the engine's `buildItemStream()` / `buildItemSingleCharge()` over `applyLineBenefit()`: the very code the nightly run prices an applied Promotion with. A `Once` or `Per Session` grant is one line on the anchor date for its whole quantity; a periodical grant recurs at its own frequency, waived or discounted for the periods its quantity covers and at the regular price after that (the ticket's "no applicable promotion" case); frequencies are never converted to a common cadence, and two that land on one date simply share a group. **There is no Membership Fee line**: a Promotion carries no fee of its own — it belongs to whichever Membership Plan the Promotion is applied alongside — so inventing one would advertise a price no member is promised (the same reason #915's hypothetical carries no Promotions); what a Promotion does to a fee is the Example Timeline beside it, and the section's disclaimer says so in all three locales. `GET /promotions/:id/billing-event-simulation` serves it, loaded with the card's other sub-resources and reloaded whenever a Benefit section is saved; the grant prices are `grossBenefitUnitPrice()`'s, shared with #919/#920's section pair, so the two halves of one card cannot quote an item two ways and no tax arithmetic reaches the page (#817). Nothing is persisted, nothing is charged, no migration. Admin: the section is read-only and renders through the shared `apps/admin/src/components/BillingEventSimulation.tsx` — #915's `plans/PlanBillingEventSimulation.tsx` moved there and both cards render it, with the wire shape and the `simulationPriceLabelKey()` mapping in the JSX-free `apps/admin/src/lib/billingEventSimulation.ts` and every label resolved in the calling page's own namespace. **Collapsible billing-period cards (#955)**: that one component renders the section as one collapsible card per billing date — the header carries the date, the number of lines on it and that date's total (the per-date `Total` row under the lines is gone, the total moved into the header), the lines appear underneath when the card is open, and a single *Expand all* / *Collapse all* control sits above them, rendered only when there is more than one period, with the first period open and the rest closed. The detail table is the Example Timeline's own language: `ExampleTimeline.tsx` now exports `timelineThStyle`/`timelineTdStyle` and its three tones (`TIMELINE_TONE_BACKGROUND`/`TIMELINE_TONE_TEXT`) and this component imports them, so the two tables cannot drift and no new colour is declared for a billing state. The three pure decisions live beside the wire shape in `lib/billingEventSimulation.ts` — `simulationLineTone()` (free for a `waive`d/`included` line, the promotional tone for any other action and for #946's `prepaid_periods` lump, grey otherwise; read off the treatment the server reported, never off the amount, because €0.00 is also what an item with no price costs), `initialExpandedPeriods()` and `allExpandedPeriods()`/`everyPeriodExpanded()` for the global control. Expanding fetches nothing and recomputes nothing: every date, amount and total is still the server's, so a fourth consumer of the projection inherits the cards without a layout of its own. **Applies To (#926, migration 204)**: `promotions.applies_to VARCHAR(32) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'membership_plan'` + `chk_promotions_applies_to`, declared once in `api/src/domain/promotionTarget.ts` (`PROMOTION_TARGETS`, mirrored for the radio group in `apps/admin/src/lib/promotionTargets.ts`) — a new target goes in those two places plus the CHECK, as a new lifecycle status does. It says whether the Promotion is about a Membership Plan or about a **Product bought on its own**, and in this ticket it is *configuration only*: no apply, pricing, snapshot or eligibility path reads it, so an existing Promotion keeps behaving exactly as before (the default backfills every row to `membership_plan`). What it decides is which configuration the editor shows — the **Membership Fee Promotion** section and **Suitable Membership Plans** are absent, not disabled, while the target is `product`, in both halves of the card (`targetsMembershipPlan()` is asked by the read-only view and by the form, so a section can never be visible in one and missing from the other), and the radio updates the card at once without saving. Switching the target **migrates and reinterprets nothing**: the `promotion_membership_fee_benefits` row and the `promotion_membership_plans` rows stay exactly as stored, which is why `PUT /promotions/:id` writes this column alone and why the admin's main save *skips* the replace-all `PUT /promotions/:id/plans` while the target is `product` rather than sending the hidden draft — a `[]` there would silently discard the gym's Plan selection. `POST /:id/duplicate` copies the target like every other configured value. Out of scope and deliberately not built: multiple Products on one Promotion, bundles, a per-item benefit for the target item, and any Billing Event Simulation or pricing change for a Product Promotion. **(#962) The section reads *Membership Fee Simulation***: a label rename only — the heading and its two empty-state hints (`timeline_unavailable` / `timeline_empty`) say *Membership Fee Simulation* in en/es/ca, the wording the Assigned Plan card has used since #924 stage 3, and the key is `section_fee_simulation` in `plans`, `promotions` and `assigned_plans_page` alike (`plans.section_example_timeline` and `promotions.section_timeline` are retired). No endpoint, field, module, date or amount moved: `planExampleTimeline.ts`, `example_timeline`, `/example-timeline` and `components/ExampleTimeline.tsx` keep the projection's own names. |
| Exercises / Muscles / Result Types | `exercises.ts`, `result-types.ts` | `[locale]/exercises/` | Per-gym exercise catalog; admin/coach CRUD. **Import from the Base Exercises library (#718)**: `GET /exercises/base` lists the platform library (`gym_id IS NULL`, `status='active'`) for the gym's Import Exercises modal — gym-facing, because `/platform/exercises` is superadmin-only — with `imported_exercise_id` naming the gym's own copy when it has one (matched on `cloned_from_id` *or* name, so a pre-#718 copy is not offered again). `POST /exercises/import` takes `{ baseExerciseIds }` and copies each one in a single transaction, keeping the base name (no "(Copy)" suffix) and recording provenance in `cloned_from_id`; ids that are not active base exercises are rejected 400 with `invalid_ids`, ids the gym already has come back under `skipped`. It replaced `POST /exercises/import-defaults`, which seeded eight hardcoded names unrelated to the library. The list's source badge reads **System sourced** (`gym_id IS NULL` or `cloned_from_id` set) or **Custom** — a label, not a change to the ownership model. **Import is the visibility boundary (#804)**: `GET /exercises` answers with the gym's own rows only (`e.gym_id = ?`).
