It used to include the library (`OR e.gym_id IS NULL`), so adding a Base Exercise made it appear on every gym's Exercises page, badged *System sourced*, before anyone had imported it — existing in the library is not the same as having imported it. **Filtered server-side, from the one declaration (#969)**: `GET /exercises` and `GET /exercises/base` read `domain/exerciseListFilters.ts` exactly as `GET /platform/exercises` does — `?q=` (name or any stored translation), `?slug=`, `?status=`, `?equipment=`/`?category=`/`?level=`/`?mechanic=`/`?source=`, and `?muscle=`/`?primary_muscle=`/`?secondary_muscle=` with `?muscle_match=any|all` — so one toolbar serves all three screens and a parameter cannot mean three things. `GET /exercises` passes `withSlug: false`, because a gym's own exercises carry no slug (no editor writes one), and each screen has its own facets read for what its dropdowns offer plus the unfiltered total `Showing 42 of 612` counts against: `GET /exercises/facets` (the gym's own rows) and `GET /exercises/base/facets` (the importable library), both gym-facing for the reason `/base` is, and both registered before `/:id`. Only `status` and `muscle_match` refuse an unknown value; an unrecognised muscle key is a filter that matches nothing, since #964 §8 lets the importer store a key outside `MUSCLE_KEYS` rather than fail. No new state was introduced for the distinction (#804 §14): the gym's copy *is* the import state, written by `POST /exercises/import` with `cloned_from_id`, so the badge's `gym_id IS NULL` arm is simply unreachable from the list now. The filter sits in the list query rather than on any one control, which is what keeps `?q=`, `?status=` and the ordering from reaching a non-imported row; `GET /exercises/base` still lists the library for the Import modal, and `GET /exercises/:id` still answers for a base row. The write paths already agreed with the rule: `workout-templates.ts` and `training-plans.ts` validate an `exercise_id` with `gym_id = ?`, so the library rows this list used to hand the exercise pickers were rejected the moment one was picked. Soft delete (`status='deleted'` + `deleted_at`). Muscles are a static in-app catalog (`domain/muscles.ts`, slug keys on `exercise_muscles.muscle`, labels via admin i18n) — no muscles table or CRUD. `GET /exercises/:id/references` powers the dependency dialog (#62). **Result types (#154)**: `result_types` is a global catalog table (9 types: repetitions, weight, distance, duration, pace, speed, calories, rpe, rest_time) with no `gym_id`. `exercise_allowed_result_types` is a join table (exercise_id + result_type_id composite PK, no `gym_id`, FKs CASCADE from both sides). `GET /exercises` returns `allowed_result_types` as a JSON array; PUT/POST accept `allowed_result_type_ids`. `GET /result-types` returns the full catalog (auth + tenant context required, no role gate). **Image upload (#417 stage 2)**: `exercises.image_url` was originally populated by uploading a file rather than pasting a URL, through the shared `ImageUploadField` → `POST /storage/uploads/exercise-image` (see Storage row below), which writes one `<uuid>.png` and no thumbnail. **Gym Exercise image, master + thumbnail (#719 part 1)**: the Exercises page no longer uses that route. `exercises.image_thumbnail_url` (migration 187) joins `image_url`, both VARCHAR(1024) — a thumbnail URL is always ten characters longer than its master's, and the row is written after the objects are in R2, so an overflow would mean an orphan and a 500 —, and `POST /exercises/:id/image` is the only writer of the pair: the body is JSON `{ image, thumbnail }` (base64, because the API has no multipart parser and the two files must succeed or fail together), the **browser** produces the 512×512 thumbnail from the 2048×2048 master (#719 Q2 — no `sharp`/`ffmpeg` in the API image, `lib/exerciseImageUpload.ts`), and the server re-validates each file from its own bytes — PNG signature, exact square, alpha channel (`domain/exerciseImages.ts`) — never from the `Content-Type` header or the file name. Keys are derived from the row, never from the request: `<gyms.storage_folder_prefix>/exercises/images/<id>-<sanitized name>[-thumbnail].png`, with the name capped so neither URL can outgrow its column. Nothing is uploaded and nothing is written until both files pass, so a refused upload cannot disturb the image already there; the previous objects are deleted only afterwards, only when `isGymOwnedImageUrl()` says they sit under this gym's own prefix (a `cordel/…` System object, another gym's and an external link are never deleted), and only when no other exercise still references them (`duplicate`/`clone`/`import` copy media *references*, so two rows can share one object). `DELETE /exercises/:id/image` clears both references with the same ownership rule and **does not fall back** to the Base Exercise's media — re-importing is how the System image comes back (#719 §12). A `PUT` that sets `image_url` clears `image_thumbnail_url`, which belonged to the master it replaced. Both references ride on every exercise-shaped response, and every list/detail surface renders the thumbnail rather than the master. **Gym Exercise video, MP4 + poster (#719 part 2)**: the same shape one folder over. `exercises.video_thumbnail_url` (migration 188) joins `video_url`, both VARCHAR(1024), and `POST /exercises/:id/video` is the only writer of that pair: the body is JSON `{ video, poster }` (base64), the **browser** captures the 512×512 poster from one frame of the clip (`lib/exerciseVideoUpload.ts` — the same #719 Q2 answer), and the server re-validates each file from its own bytes — the MP4's `ftyp` brand, its `moov` and its `stsd` video sample entries (`domain/mp4Video.ts`, so a QuickTime `.mov` renamed to `.mp4` and an audio-only file are both refused), the poster's PNG signature and exact 512×512 (`domain/exerciseVideos.ts`) — never from the `Content-Type` header or the file name. A poster is *not* required to be transparent: a frame of video is opaque. Keys come from the row: `<gyms.storage_folder_prefix>/exercises/videos/<id>-<sanitized name>.mp4` and `…-thumbnail.png`. `EXERCISE_VIDEO_MAX_MB` (default 50, clamped at 200) bounds the upload, which is buffered in the API process — there is no multipart or presigned path — and the route mounts its own `express.json` limit in `app.ts` for the same reason the image route does. Replace/remove follow part 1's rules exactly (`DELETE /exercises/:id/video` clears both references, deletes only the gym's own objects, never a `cordel/…` System object, and never one another exercise still references), with no fallback to the Base Exercise's video. A `PUT` that sets `video_url` clears `video_thumbnail_url`. **Import and re-import media copying (#719 part 3)**: `POST /exercises/import` copies the Base Exercise's four media references into the new copy (§2 — references only, no System object is duplicated into the gym's folder), and from then on the copy owns them (§3: a later Base Exercise edit never reaches it, and nothing resolves media across the two at read time). An id the gym **already has** is how the System media comes back (§12 — there is deliberately no separate "restore System media" action): the copy's media references are refreshed from the Base Exercise's current ones and it comes back under `refreshed`, while a copy that already carries them stays `skipped` — so the response is `{ imported, refreshed, skipped }`. `domain/exerciseMediaImport.ts`'s `planExerciseMediaRefresh()` is the one rule, and two things it does are deliberate: the image pair and the video pair move **independently**, and a pair the Base Exercise does not have restores nothing — a base row with no `image_url` leaves the gym's own uploaded image where it is, because re-import exists to *obtain* System media and must not delete work the gym did (removing it on purpose is `DELETE /exercises/:id/image`). A refresh touches media only: name, description, defaults and `cloned_from_id` belong to the copy, so a row matched by name alone (a pre-#718 copy with no provenance) gets its media restored without becoming **System sourced**. The objects it stopped pointing at are swept *after* the transaction commits, through the same `isGymOwnedImageUrl()` + still-referenced check a replacement uses, and each refresh is audited as an `update` on the gym's exercise. `GET /exercises/base` carries all four references plus `media_refreshable` — one SQL mirror of the same rule, `ORDER BY id LIMIT 1` over the same copy `imported_exercise_id` names — so the Import modal enables an already-imported row only when a re-import would actually move something, badges it **Media update**, and never compares URLs or resolves System-vs-gym media itself. *Select all matching* deliberately excludes those rows: a re-import overwrites media the gym may have uploaded. **Base Exercise image, master + thumbnail (#716)**: the platform library gets the same pair through `POST`/`DELETE /platform/exercises/:id/image` (`requireSuperadmin`), with one difference — where the objects go. A base exercise is a `gym_id IS NULL` row and belongs to no gym, so its keys are built off `PLATFORM_STORAGE_ROOT`: `cordel/exercises/images/<id>-<sanitized name>[-thumbnail].png` (`domain/baseExerciseImages.ts`, the same `exercises/images` leaf a gym uses inside its own tree, and the same `sanitizeExerciseImageName()`), and the first upload writes the `cordel/`, `cordel/exercises/` and `cordel/exercises/images/` markers. No new column and no object-key column (#716 §7): the resulting URLs go in the same `image_url` + `image_thumbnail_url` the gym side uses, and the row's `gym_id` is what decides the folder. Validation is not duplicated either — #716 §2/§3 are #719 §5 word for word, so the platform route calls the same `validateExerciseImagePair()` over the browser-made pair, and nothing is uploaded or written until both files pass. Each router answers for its own rows only: the platform route is **404** for a gym's exercise, the gym route **403** for a base one. Stale objects are swept afterwards with the mirror of the gym rule — `isPlatformOwnedExerciseImageUrl()` (a gym object and an external URL are never deleted by a platform operation) and then a reference check that deliberately spans **every** tenant, because `POST /exercises/import` copies media *references* so each gym that imported the exercise points at the platform's own object. Removal clears both references with no fallback. **Generating the artwork is out of scope for #716** (the thread's answer to its Q1/Q2): there is no `exerciseImageArt.ts` and no backfill script — an existing base exercise stays image-less until an administrator uploads a master, which the expanded card on Cordel → Base Exercises does (thumbnail over a checkerboard, `Upload Image`/`Replace`/`Remove`, the 2048×2048 master reached only by `View full size`, and inline editing rather than a modal — #716 Q4). **Base Exercise video, MP4 + poster (#717)**: the same construction one folder over, and nothing about it is new except the root. `POST`/`DELETE /platform/exercises/:id/video` (`requireSuperadmin`) write and clear `video_url` + `video_thumbnail_url` — no `video_object_key` column (§2 as answered in Q5) — with keys built off `PLATFORM_STORAGE_ROOT` by `domain/baseExerciseVideos.ts`: `cordel/exercises/videos/<id>-<sanitized name>.mp4` and `…-thumbnail.png`, the first upload writing the `cordel/`, `cordel/exercises/` and `cordel/exercises/Videos/` markers. Q2–Q4 settled the rest by reusing part 2 verbatim: the same `EXERCISE_VIDEO_MAX_MB` (default 50, clamped at 200) over the same buffered `PutObject` path, with the platform route mounting its own `express.json` limit in `app.ts`; the same browser-captured 512×512 poster (`lib/exerciseVideoUpload.ts`), mandatory because a failed capture must fail the whole upload and because #719 part 3's re-import has nothing to copy without it; and the same `validateExerciseVideoPair()` over `domain/mp4Video.ts`, so a QuickTime `.mov` renamed to `.mp4` and an audio-only file are refused and no `ffprobe` enters the API image. The platform route is **404** for a gym's exercise, the gym route **403** for a base one. The stale-object sweep is shared with the images above and asks two questions before deleting: `isPlatformOwnedExerciseImageUrl()` **or** `isPlatformOwnedExerciseVideoUrl()` (a gym object, and an external YouTube link, are never deleted by a platform operation), then whether **any** other non-deleted exercise in **any** gym still references the object through **any** of its four media columns. Removal clears both references with no fallback, and a `PUT` that sets `video_url` still clears `video_thumbnail_url`. The expanded card on Cordel → Base Exercises gains a **Media** section holding both blocks: the stored poster with a play affordance, `Upload Video`/`Replace`, `Remove` behind the page's `ConfirmDialog`, and no `<video>` mounted until the player is asked for — then `controls` + `preload="metadata"`, never autoplay (§8, §9). A row whose `video_url` is a link rather than an uploaded `.mp4` is rendered as a link, never handed to a `<video>`. Per Q6 the **gym** Exercises card's media section is now spelled **Media** and no longer offers `video_url` as an editable text box — the video is managed by the control that writes the reference and its poster together, and the edit `PUT` no longer submits the column at all (a stale value captured when the editor opened would otherwise repoint the reference and drop the poster on save); the creation form keeps its URL field, and the view section still shows the link a row carries. **Inline creation (#805)**: `+ Add Exercise` opens an inline card at the top of the list rather than a `CrudModal`, and it renders the same form body as the `⋮ → Edit` editor — `[locale]/exercises/exerciseForm.ts` declares the section order (General with Name/Description/Status, Configuration, Allowed Result Types, Muscles, Media last with Image and Video side by side), the row → form mapping, and both payloads, which is what keeps the `video_url` asymmetry above a parameter (`toExerciseCreatePayload()` carries the column, `toExerciseUpdatePayload()` does not) rather than two copies of the form drifting. The staged-media flow is unchanged: there is no exercise to upload to until Save returns, so the prepared image and video pairs are posted to `POST /exercises/:id/{image,video}` immediately afterwards. Allowed Result Types renders translated `exercises.result_type_<slug>` labels for migration 073's nine slugs, falling back to the catalogue row's own `name` for a slug added later — the modal asked for keys no locale file had, and next-intl prints a missing key verbatim, so the checkbox list showed `exercises.result_type_repetitions` on screen. **One editor for both screens (#806)**: the gym Exercises page and the platform Base Exercises page render the same `components/exercises/ExerciseEditor.tsx`, with `useExerciseEditorState()` beside it owning the form values, the muscle roles, the selected result types, the validation, the error line and the `saving` flag, and `exerciseForm.ts` moved up out of `app/[locale]/exercises/` to sit with them. The Base Exercises editor therefore gained the whole field set it never had (Status, the five Configuration defaults, Allowed Result Types, Muscles, Media) and its media upload/removal controls moved off the read-only expanded card into the editor behind `⋮ → Edit`. No endpoint was merged (#806 §6): the editor names no route, `onSave` does, so `/exercises` keeps its module permissions and `/platform/exercises` its `requireSuperadmin`; `ExerciseImageField`/`ExerciseVideoField` take `basePath` (defaulting to `/exercises`) and `requiresGymStorage` (false for a Base Exercise, whose objects live under `PLATFORM_STORAGE_ROOT` and are gated by no gym's bucket). Removing media now asks for confirmation on **both** screens — #717 §7's rule, generalised, since unification forces one behaviour. `GET /platform/exercises/lookups` (`requireSuperadmin`, registered **before** `/:id` or Express reads `lookups` as an id) answers `{ muscles, result_types }` for that editor: the gym-facing `/muscles` + `/result-types` sit behind `tenantContext` + `requireModuleAccess('TRAINING')` + `requireFeatureEnabled('training.exercises')`, and a platform screen must not stop working because the superadmin's *selected* gym has exercises switched off. Neither catalogue is gym-scoped, so it is the same data under a different gate. **Free Exercise DB import (#964)**: the Base Exercise catalogue is populated from the public [free-exercise-db](https://github.com/yuhonas/free-exercise-db) dataset (876 exercises) by `npm run exercises:import-free-db` (`api/src/scripts/import-free-exercise-db.ts`), which reads the repository's structured `dist/exercises.json` — never a scraped page — from `--from <file>`, `--url`, `FREE_EXERCISE_DB_URL` or `FREE_EXERCISE_DB_DEFAULT_URL`, in that order. Migration 209 is what makes it repeatable: `exercises.source` (`free-exercise-db`) + `source_id` carry the dataset's own identifier, `slug` is the application handle generated once from the name (`alternate-incline-dumbbell-curl`) and never regenerated, and `equipment`, `category`, `level`, `mechanic` and `force_type` preserve the five source metadata values **verbatim** (`force` is reserved in MySQL 8, hence `force_type`). Uniqueness is two VIRTUAL generated columns in migration 183's shape: `base_source_key` (one Base Exercise per source id, deleted rows included, so a soft-deleted import is *skipped* rather than re-created) and `base_slug_key` (one slug among live base rows). Matching is #964 §12's order — provenance, then slug, then an exact case-folded name — and the two fallbacks only ever adopt a row carrying **no** provenance of its own; anything else is reported as a potential duplicate and left alone. `domain/freeExerciseDb.ts` is the one place every mapping lives and is pure (the script is only I/O): the muscle aliases (`quadriceps` → `quads`, `abdominals` → `core`, everything else its own key — `MUSCLE_KEYS` gained `lats`, `middle_back`, `lower_back`, `traps`, `forearms`, `adductors`, `abductors`, `neck` with labels in all three locales), and the instructions, which become one numbered-per-line `description` with the wording untouched. **There is no exercise-level Exercise Type mapping any more**: #964 shipped one onto `exercises.exercise_type`, a column migration 074 (#154) had already dropped, and #1009 removed both it and the `?exercise_type=` filter it fed — the dataset's own axis is `exercises.category` (`strength`/`cardio`/`stretching`), stored verbatim and read through `?category=` exactly as `equipment` is. **The importer never overwrites what an administrator can edit**: an update fills an empty name or description, keeps the source's own facts in step and adds missing muscle links without removing any. **No image is imported** (§10): the dataset's `images` field is not carried into the parsed shape, so no media column and no raw GitHub URL can be written, and a unit test greps both files for them. Gym rows are untouched throughout (`gym_id IS NULL` in every statement). The list is filtered server-side over the new columns: `GET /platform/exercises` takes `q` (name, any stored translation **or** slug), `muscle` / `primary_muscle` / `secondary_muscle` (comma-separated or repeated, multi-select), `equipment`, `category`, `level`, `mechanic` and `source` — the expanded view's new fields are #965's and the inline filter UI is #969's, below. **The compact filter toolbar (#969 stage 1)**: the vocabulary and the `WHERE` fragment moved into one pure module, `domain/exerciseListFilters.ts` (`parseExerciseListFilter`, `exerciseListFilterSql`, `exerciseFacetsSql`, `groupExerciseFacets`), so the three screens the ticket names — Base Exercises, the Import modal and a gym's own Exercises — share one reading of `?muscle=` rather than three (§19). It adds `slug` as a field of its own (partial, which is also how an exact slug matches — §4) and `muscle_match=any|all`, the OR/AND the checkbox group needs within itself (§6, default `any`, which is the behaviour #964's query already had); the role stays the three parameters the endpoint already understood (§7), and `all` becomes one `EXISTS` per muscle. A closed set refuses an unknown value (`status`, `muscle_match` → 400) while a free-text metadata value is simply applied, because #964 preserves those verbatim and there is no set to compare against. `GET /platform/exercises/facets` (`requireSuperadmin`, registered **before** `/:id`) answers `{ total, equipment, category, level, mechanic, source }`: the distinct values **present** among the rows in scope, which is what the Equipment and Category dropdowns offer (§8 — declaring their values would be inventing the taxonomy the ticket forbids) and a facet that comes back empty is a control the page does not render at all (§9). `total` is the unfiltered count `Showing 42 of 612 exercises` is quoted against (§14). There is deliberately **no pagination** (the thread's `Q3` answer, "do not paginate, display all"), so the list response is still the plain array every caller already reads. On the admin side the state is one JSX-free declaration (`lib/exerciseFilters.ts` — the control values, the query they become and the active-filter chips, each chip carrying the state that removing it produces) and the markup is one component (`components/exercises/ExerciseFilterBar.tsx`) built from the app's own chrome: `FilterBar`/`FilterField`/`filterControlStyle` (#724), the existing `MultiSelectFilter` popover (#350, which gained an optional pinned search box, a footer slot for the Match/Role radio groups and a trigger `style` override), `StatusFilter`, and `listNameBadgeStyle` for the chips — no new badge, no new colour and no second filter-bar look. The free-text facet labels are humanized from the stored value (`exerciseFacetValueLabel`) rather than looked up, because a value the dataset adds tomorrow has no locale key and next-intl prints a missing key verbatim. The page owns the fetch and debounces it, and filters nothing in the browser. Stage 2 brings the same toolbar to `GET /exercises` and `GET /exercises/base` — a gym's own exercises carry no slug and no source metadata, so `withSlug: false` and an empty facet set are what express that there (§9, §19). **One read-only view and one Details modal, and the actor to show in it (#965)**: the expanded card is `components/exercises/ExerciseReadOnlyView.tsx` — the editor's read-only counterpart, same five sections from the same `exerciseForm.ts` declaration, with the field chrome in `exerciseFieldChrome.ts` so neither half can be restyled alone — rendered by **both** pages, with the media shown by one `ExerciseMediaPreview` (the 512×512 thumbnail, the stored poster, and no `<video>` until the player is asked for; `playing`/`onPlay` are the page's, so one clip plays at a time). It carries no technical metadata and no Audit Log link: `⋮ → Details` is a shared modal (`components/exercises/ExerciseDetailModal.tsx`, `scope` supplied by the page) holding the internal id, Created/Modified At & By and `ViewAuditLogButton`, and it renders the list row it was handed rather than a detail endpoint of its own. **Migration 208** is what gives it an actor to show: `exercises` gains `created_by_name`/`created_by_type` + `modified_by_name`/`modified_by_type` (nullable, with `chk_exercises_{created,modified}_by_type` mirroring `staff`|`superadmin`), because a Base Exercise is written by a superadmin with no `gym_memberships` row for `created_by` to point at — the same reason `tax_rates` (126), `themes` (178) and `nutrition_library_items` (196) snapshot theirs. The **platform** router is the only writer, stamping the pair on create, on `PUT /:id` and on all four media routes (a media change is a modification; bumping `modified_at` alone would attribute an upload to whoever last saved the form). There is no backfill — `/platform/*` runs behind `requireSuperadmin` with no `tenantContext`, so `recordAudit()` wrote nothing to reconstruct from — and a pre-208 row reads as the em dash. The **gym-facing** `SELECT` masks the pair on a `gym_id IS NULL` row (`GET /exercises/:id` serves base rows, and a shared catalogue's rows are not the reading gym's to attribute — `maskPlatformActors`' rule) while keeping the `gym_memberships` join as a gym exercise's own actor; those four aliases deliberately shadow the columns `e.*` now expands to, so they must stay after `e.*` and every use of that `SELECT` must stay top-level. |
| Workout templates | `workout-templates.ts` | `[locale]/workout-templates/` | Reusable, block-based workout blueprints (blocks + per-block exercises); admin/coach. Tree-grid page (#63): expandable rows lazy-load the full hierarchy (`GET /:id` aggregates blocks+exercises via JSON_ARRAYAGG), and dnd-kit drag-and-drop covers block reorder, exercise reorder, and block moves **between** templates (`PUT /:id/blocks/:blockId/move`, transactional reparent + position recompaction in both parents). List endpoint returns the paginated `{items,total,limit,offset}` shape when `limit`/`offset` are passed (name/created_by/status filters + sort) and the legacy plain array otherwise — the Training Plan editor's workout selector depends on the latter. `created_by_membership_id` (migration 053) feeds the Created By column/filter. Status `active/inactive/deleted` (#62): selectors only offer active templates (`?status=active`), delete is soft, and `GET /workout-templates/:id/references` powers the dependency dialog. Block field visibility is driven by block type via `blockFieldConfig.ts` — see Feature Patterns' config-driven form pattern. **One configuration per block type (#672)**: `BLOCK_TYPE_CONFIG` gives each type at most one block-level numeric field — Standard/Superset/Triset/GiantSet none, Circuit `Rounds`, EMOM/AMRAP `Minutes`, Tabata `Intervals` — and `getBlockConfig()`/`blockConfigInput()`/`blockConfigPatch()` are the only way the three editors (`WorkoutBlockBuilder`, `WorkoutTemplateTree`, `members/PlanWorkoutBlocksModal`) read and write it. Minutes are a display unit over the existing `duration_seconds` column and Tabata's Intervals are stored in `rounds`, so no column was added and no migration runs; `work_seconds`/`rest_seconds` are no longer offered by any type (the columns stay, values already stored are neither displayed nor cleared). Sets stays an exercise-level field. Exercise limits per block type (#71): `BLOCK_TYPE_MAX_EXERCISES` in `blockFieldConfig.ts` (Standard=1, Superset=2, Triset=3, rest=unlimited); enforced on the API (422 `MaximumExercisesExceeded`) and in the UI (button hidden, `(n/max)` badge). **Fully inline editing (#130)**: all fields auto-save on blur/change; no modals. **Result type at exercise level (#154)**: `result_type` removed from blocks; `workout_template_exercises` now has `result_type_id` (FK→`result_types`, SET NULL on delete), `target_value`, `min_value`, `max_value`, `unit`; `exercises.exercise_type` dropped. `ExerciseRow` adapts columns by `result_type_slug` (reps/weight → sets + min–max reps; duration/distance/other → sets + target value + unit). New duplicate endpoints: `POST /:id/blocks/:blockId/duplicate`, `POST /:id/blocks/:blockId/exercises/:exId/duplicate`. Exercise selection uses a custom filter-as-you-type combobox. Rest stored as `rest_seconds`, displayed as minutes. `BlockModal`/`ExerciseModal` removed. `[locale]/workouts/` (old flat Workout catalog) is now just a redirect here (#55); `workouts.ts` was removed. **Exercise media in workout rows (#720)**: every workout-exercise projection (the JSON trees here, in `training-plans.ts`, `training-plan-templates.ts` and `platform-workout-templates.ts`, the block-exercise list, and each single-row create/update/move/duplicate response) also returns `exercise_image_url`/`exercise_video_url` from `exercises`, and `components/ExerciseMediaThumbnails.tsx` renders them at the right of the row in all four admin views. The component is deliberately dumb — it reads the URLs the exercise carries and holds no System-vs-gym, import or fallback logic — and never mounts a `<video>`: a YouTube URL gets its poster from `img.youtube.com` (`components/exerciseMedia.ts`), anything else gets a play tile. `exercise_image_thumbnail_url` is produced as of #719 part 1 — every projection listed above also selects `exercises.image_thumbnail_url`, so a workout row draws the 512×512 thumbnail and never the 2048×2048 master. `exercise_video_thumbnail_url` is produced as of #719 part 2 — every projection also selects `exercises.video_thumbnail_url`, so a row draws the stored poster of an uploaded MP4 and falls back to the YouTube still (or a play tile) only when there is none. |
| Training plan templates | `training-plan-templates.ts` | `[locale]/training-plan-templates/` | Groups Workout Templates into an assignable plan template; tree-grid editor (`TrainingPlanTree.tsx`, #61) with drag-reorder and per-workout weekday. Block/exercise summary formatters are shared with the Workout Templates tree (`workout-templates/summaries.ts`). **Shared builder**: `workout-templates/WorkoutBlockBuilder.tsx` is a self-contained block/exercise editor (its own dnd-kit `DndContext`, `ExerciseCombobox`, inline block/exercise fields, block Duplicate/Delete, cross-block exercise move via drag-and-drop). It accepts `workoutKey` (drag-ID namespace), `blocksUrl` (all API calls are relative to this), `blocks: HierBlock[]`, `canWrite`, `onChanged`. Used by Training Plans workout cards (#164); not used by `WorkoutTemplateTree` (which keeps its own outer `DndContext` for cross-template block moves). **`GET /:id/hierarchy` is the result-type level's one remaining drift, fixed (#966)**: it still projected `b.result_type`, a column migration 074 (#154) dropped when it moved the result type from the block down to the exercise instance, so *every* expand answered `500 Unknown column 'b.result_type' in 'field list'` and the row stayed on `Loading…`. The block object no longer carries it and each exercise now carries `result_type_id` + `result_type_slug`/`result_type_name` (LEFT JOIN `result_types`, so an exercise with no result type stays in the tree) + `target_value`/`min_value`/`max_value`/`unit` — `workout-templates.ts` `GET /:id`'s own projection, which is what the shared `summaries.ts` `HierExercise` and `exerciseSummary()` have expected since #154. So the Training Plan Template tree also reads a duration/distance/weight exercise correctly for the first time, instead of falling through to the repetitions branch. The frontend keeps a failed load per template (`hierError`) and renders an application-level error with **Retry** rather than `Loading…` for ever, and what that error *says* is the global handler's new rule (see "What the global error handler answers"). |
| Training plans (assigned) | `gym-training-plans.ts`, `training-plans.ts`, `training-plan-creation.ts`, `member-training-plans.ts`, `exercise-logs.ts` | `[locale]/training-plans/` (list, no separate editor page) · also reachable from `[locale]/members/` (`PlanWorkoutBlocksModal.tsx` / `PlanBlockExercisesModal.tsx`) | Per-member cloned plan/workout/block instances (#67, #112, #164). Gym-level list at `/training-plans` renders expandable cards (matching Training Plan Templates UX) inside one list surface whose labelled filter bar and column-header band come from the shared `FilterBar` and `listChrome.ts` (#724 — the same chrome `DataTable` draws for Assigned Plans; header and rows share one `LIST_COLUMNS`-derived grid and scroll together) — inline metadata editing, workout drag-reorder, block/exercise CRUD **fully inline** (no modals; each workout card embeds `WorkoutBlockBuilder` — see Workout templates row), all of it **behind `⋮ → Edit`** since #971: expanding a plan renders the same tree read-only (the weekday as a chip, workout names and blocks as values, the exercise table with its sets, targets and media) and offers no `+ Add Workout`, `+ Block`, `+ Exercise`, drag handle or per-workout `⋮`, which is the app-wide expand-reads rule applied to a tree; the tree's own chrome is `components/workoutChrome.ts`. Context menu per plan: Edit (inline), Details (read-only audit modal), Duplicate (`POST …/duplicate` deep-clones the full tree), Complete (`POST …/complete` stamps end_date and locks the plan read-only), Delete. Two-step **New Training Plan** dialog stays on the list after creation. Cross-parent moves via `PUT …/blocks/:id/move` and `PUT …/exercises/:id/move`. `training-plan-creation.ts` extracts the clone/write-history transaction shared by `POST /training-plans` and legacy `POST /members/:id/member-training-plans`. Lifecycle `draft/active/expired/completed/deleted` on `training_plans.status` (migration 054 adds `expired`; migration 066 adds `completed`); completed plans reject all content mutations with 403. `start_date` required, `end_date` stamped on expire or complete; `member_training_plans` remains the append-only assignment history. `PlanWorkoutBlocksModal.tsx` / `PlanBlockExercisesModal.tsx` are still used on the Members page per-member plan editor (not in the Training Plans list). |
| Audit | `audit-logs.ts` | `[locale]/audit/` (System, gym-scoped, admin+) · `[locale]/cordel/audit/` (Cordel, platform-wide, superadmin) | Both render the shared `AuditLogView` component; platform mode passes `scope="all"` and adds a Gym column. |
| Gyms (platform) | `gyms.ts` (platformRouter) | `[locale]/system/gyms/` (linked from **Cordel → Gyms**) | Superadmin only. Route path kept for URL stability; grouping is Cordel (#66). Migration 095 adds lifecycle/audit columns (`description`, `status` CHECK 'active'/'inactive'/'deleted', `created_by_name`, `modified_at`, `modified_by_name`, `deleted_at`, `deleted_by_name`). Endpoints: `GET /platform/gyms?status=` (list non-deleted, optional status filter), `GET /platform/gyms/:id` (single, including deleted), `POST /platform/gyms` (slug optional, auto-generated), `PUT /platform/gyms/:id` (name/description/status/theme_id, stamps modified_at/modified_by_name), `DELETE /platform/gyms/:id` (soft-delete, stamps deleted_at/deleted_by_name), `POST /platform/gyms/:id/duplicate`. `requireSuperadmin` attaches `req.superadminName` for audit stamping (no extra Clerk API call). Admin page: expandable-row UX, inline creation, context menu Details/Edit/Duplicate/Delete, inline edit with Save/Cancel, Details modal with full audit info. **Cloudflare R2 storage (#417 stage 1)**: `infra/storage.ts` wraps a lazily-constructed S3-compatible client (`@aws-sdk/client-s3`) against one **platform-wide, shared R2 bucket** configured via env vars only (`CLOUDFLARE_R2_ENDPOINT`/`CLOUDFLARE_R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID`/`CLOUDFLARE_R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`/`CLOUDFLARE_R2_BUCKET`) — no per-gym credential storage, mirroring the MONEI/Clerk pattern (see `payments/index.ts`). `isStorageConfigured()` reports whether this deployment has R2 configured at all; every gym response includes it as `storage_configured` (not a DB column — computed per request). Migration 135 adds `gyms.storage_folder_prefix` (nullable, unique) and `gyms.storage_initialized_at` (nullable) — set only by `POST /platform/gyms/:id/storage/initialize` (superadmin), which creates the gym's folder structure (`gyms/<gym_id>-<sanitized_gym_name>/{,nutrition/,exercises/,exercises/images/,exercises/videos/,themes/}`, one zero-byte "folder marker" object per path via `initializeGymBucket()` — parents as well as leaves so the R2 browser matches the ticket tree). **#826** fixes the first level at exactly the folders something writes into — `nutrition/`, `exercises/`, `themes/` (lowercased by **#1035**, which also dropped the `Nutrition/Images/` leaf, since a food's image is the only thing it ever held, and pinned that initialization creates nothing under `cordel/` — that tree is built by hand in the Cloudflare console) — plus `goals/` since **#1035 stage 2** gave it a writer (a Personal Goal's image, `domain/personalGoalImages.ts`: appended last so existing gyms' markers keep their order, and with no leaf, since a goal's image is all that branch holds) — and drops `Branding/` (with its `Logo/`/`Images/` leaves) and `Members/`, which nothing has written to since #824 moved the theme logo to `themes/<theme_id>-<name>/logo/` and #725 put a theme's Members App slots under `themes/<theme_id>-<name>/members_app/`; the leaves under the roots that have them are unchanged, and the marker names stay spelled exactly as the key builders write them (`domain/personalGoalImages.ts` — `goals/` has one spelling, `GOALS_FOLDER` in `infra/storage.ts` — `domain/exerciseImages.ts`, `domain/exerciseVideos.ts`, `domain/themeFolders.ts`), since a marker differing in case alone would add a *fourth* first-level folder rather than rename one. Markers an earlier run already wrote are not removed — see `docs/go-to-production.md`. `themes/` (#735) is the gym-level root only: a Custom Theme's own `themes/<theme_id>-<name>/members_app/` branch cannot exist before the theme does, so it stays with `ensureStorageFolders()` at upload time (#725), and the folder name has one spelling — `THEMES_FOLDER` in `infra/storage.ts`, re-exported by `domain/themeMemberImages.ts` as `THEME_STORAGE_FOLDER`. A gym initialized before a folder was added to the list gets it by re-running the action, which rewrites the whole marker set under the prefix already captured inside the shared bucket; 503 if storage isn't configured for the deployment, 502 if the R2 call itself fails. **Failure diagnostics (#542)**: both responses carry a `diagnostics` object from `infra/storage.ts`'s `getStorageDiagnostics()` — the endpoint split into `endpointProtocol`/`endpointHost`/`endpointPath` (a non-empty path means the bucket name was wrongly appended) plus `endpointMalformed`, `bucket`, `missingConfig`, and the *lengths* of the access key id and secret (never their values, so a truncated/whitespace-padded credential is visible without disclosing it). The 502 additionally carries `details` from `describeStorageError()`, which flattens the `@aws-sdk/client-s3` rejection — `name`/`code`, `$metadata.httpStatusCode`/`requestId`/`attempts`, the exact folder-marker `key` that failed, and the `err.cause` chain (`initializeGymBucket()` wraps each `PutObjectCommand` in a `StorageOperationError` carrying that key). The raw error is also written to the pino logger server-side. Admin page renders all of it in a pinned, dismissible panel under the button with a "Copy diagnostics" button, instead of the toast alone. Re-running the action is idempotent — it reuses the `storage_folder_prefix` captured on first init rather than recomputing it from the gym's (possibly since-renamed) current name. Admin page: read-only "Storage" section in the expanded row (init status, initialized-at timestamp, folder prefix) + "Initialize Cloudflare Bucket" button (always shown; disabled with a warning when the deployment isn't configured). **`gyms/` root (#668)**: `buildGymFolderPrefix()` returns `gyms/<gym_id>-<sanitized_gym_name>` (it was `<gym_id>-<sanitized_gym_name>`), so the whole gym tree hangs off a single `gyms/` prefix inside the shared bucket and the bucket root stays free for other platform-level trees. The prefix is a plain object-key prefix — bucket, endpoint, credentials and everything *below* the gym root are unchanged — and because it is the value captured in `gyms.storage_folder_prefix` at initialize time, uploads (`uploadGymImage()`) inherit it with no second concatenation site. There is no data migration: a gym initialized before #668 keeps its old root (and its objects) until someone moves it deliberately — see `docs/go-to-production.md`. **Stages 2 and 3 (generic upload + rewire `exercises.image_url`; nutrition meal images) landed** — see the Storage row below. |
| Storage (image uploads) | `storage.ts` (`storageRouter`, mounted at `/storage`), `infra/storage.ts` | — (consumed via `apps/admin/src/components/ImageUploadField.tsx`, not a page of its own) | #417 stages 2–3. Generic per-gym image upload, one route per upload target so each carries its own module/feature guard: `POST /storage/uploads/exercise-image` (`requireModuleWrite('TRAINING')` + `requireFeatureEnabled('training.exercises')`) uploads into `<gym's storage_folder_prefix>/exercises/images/<uuid>.<ext>` (lowercase since #1035). Its stage-3 sibling `POST /storage/uploads/nutrition-image` is **retired** (#1035): its `.../Nutrition/Images/<uuid>.<ext>` key could carry neither the food's id nor its name, so it was replaced by the per-row `POST /nutrition-library/:id/image`, which writes `<prefix>/nutrition/<food_id>-<sanitized name>.<ext>` (`buildGymNutritionImageKey()`, `domain/baseNutritionImages.ts`, shared with the platform key one root over) and returns the whole item rather than `{ url }`. `handleImageUpload()` therefore has one caller left. Returns `{ url }` built by `buildStorageObjectUrl()` on `CLOUDFLARE_R2_PUBLIC_URL` (falling back to `${CLOUDFLARE_R2_ENDPOINT}/${CLOUDFLARE_R2_BUCKET}/<key>` when unset; `infra/storage.ts`'s `uploadGymImage()`, an `@aws-sdk/client-s3` `PutObjectCommand` wrapper). Request body is the raw file bytes via `express.raw({ type: (req) => contentType.startsWith('image/'), limit: '6mb' })`, same pattern as the theme logo upload in `themes.ts`. Allowed mime types: png/jpeg/webp/gif (415 otherwise); max 5MB (413). 503 if the deployment has no R2 configured (`isStorageConfigured()`); 409 with the exact message `"Cloudflare storage has not been initialized for this gym, therefore images cannot be uploaded."` if the calling gym's `storage_folder_prefix` is still null (stage 1's `/platform/gyms/:id/storage/initialize` hasn't been run for it — superadmin-only, so gym-level staff can only wait for a Cordel admin to do it); 502 if the R2 call itself fails — the 502 body carries the same `details` object as the gyms route (#542), but **not** `diagnostics`, since this route is gym-staff-facing and that snapshot describes platform infrastructure (it goes to the server log only). Since #713 `infra/storage.ts` also exposes the key-explicit primitives the theme logo needs — `uploadStorageObject()` / `getStorageObject()` / `deleteStorageObject()` (`uploadGymImage()` now delegates its PutObject to the first, keeping its own operation name in #542's error details), plus `extensionForMime()`, `buildGymLogoKey()` and `buildStorageObjectUrl()`. The shared `S3Client` pins `requestChecksumCalculation`/`responseChecksumValidation` to `WHEN_REQUIRED` (#542): aws-sdk-js v3 ≥ 3.729 defaults them to `WHEN_SUPPORTED`, which sends a trailing CRC32 checksum with `content-encoding: aws-chunked` — a combination R2 rejects, failing every `PutObject` including the zero-byte folder markers. Frontend: `ImageUploadField` is a drop-in replacement for a plain URL `<input>` — shows a thumbnail preview, an Upload/Replace/Remove button group, and the not-configured/not-initialized warning inline (reads `activeGym.storage_configured`/`storage_folder_prefix` from `GymContext`, extended with those two fields off the existing `/gyms` response); used by Exercises (`image_url`) and, since stage 3, by the gym-facing Nutrition Library page (`nutrition_library_items.image_url`, migration 138 — gym-owned items only, since system/global catalog items have no gym storage folder to upload into); since #1035 that page passes the food's own route as `uploadPath`, so the control reads the new URL from the returned item's `image_url` as well as a generic upload's `{ url }`, and the **create** form offers no image control at all — the key needs the row's id, so the food is created first and its image uploaded from Edit. |
| Payment Providers (platform) | `payment-providers.ts` (`/platform/payment-providers`) | `[locale]/cordel/payment-providers/` (**Cordel → Payment Providers**) | #636. Superadmin-only CRUD over the platform-level `payment_providers` catalogue (migration 175, **no `gym_id`**): `name`, `provider_key` (the adapter key `getPaymentProvider()` switches on, validated against `SUPPORTED_PAYMENT_PROVIDER_KEYS` in `payments/index.ts`), `description`, `status` (`active`/`inactive`, CHECK `chk_payment_providers_status`), `is_default`, soft-delete + actor-name columns. **No credentials are stored** — API keys and webhook secrets stay in the API's environment (CLAUDE.md's env-var constraint), so `GET /platform/payment-providers/deployment` reports the env-derived status (`provider_key`, `environment`, `credentials_configured`, `missing_config`, `webhook_url`) that the retired Finance → Payment Providers page used to render from the *admin* container's env. Two invariants are held by VIRTUAL generated-column unique indexes rather than by the router alone: `payment_providers_one_default` (at most one non-deleted default — a new default is set by clearing the old one in the same transaction) and `payment_providers_unique_active_name` (names unique among non-deleted rows, so a retired name can be reused). `DELETE` is a soft-delete and answers **409** when gyms still point at the row (with `usageCount` + up to 20 gym names, also served by `GET /:id/references`) or when it is the default; deactivating a provider gyms use is the same 409. The tenant side is `gyms.payment_provider_id` **NOT NULL** with a RESTRICT FK: `POST /platform/gyms` resolves the default when the caller sends none (400 when the catalogue has no default), `PUT /platform/gyms/:id` validates an explicit value and rejects `null`, duplicate keeps the source's provider, and the joined row comes back as `payment_provider` on the superadmin gym reads (`PLATFORM_GYM_SELECT`/`_JOIN`) — the member-readable `GET /gyms` selects neither, so the key is absent there rather than null. Every other INSERT into `gyms` (`test/helpers.ts`, `infra/seed.ts`) takes the default the same way. Billing still resolves the adapter from `PAYMENT_PROVIDER`; wiring it to the gym's row waits for a second adapter (`docs/go-to-production.md`). |
| Superadmins | `superadmins.ts` | `[locale]/system/users/` (linked from **Cordel → Users**) | Superadmin only — grant/revoke platform role. Route path kept for URL stability; grouping is Cordel (#66). |
| Themes | `themes.ts` (superadmin Base Themes) + `gym-themes.ts` (gym admin Customer Themes + assignments) | `[locale]/system/themes/` (Cordel → Base Themes, superadmin) + `[locale]/themes/` (System → Themes, gym admin) | #68 + #97 + #117 + #120. `themes.gym_id` (migration 065) distinguishes Base Themes (NULL) from Customer Themes (gym-owned). Gym admins see both via `GET /system/themes`; can clone and edit Customer Themes; can assign any theme (base or customer) as org default or to individual centers. Inline expandable editor has sections: Assignments / Branding / Colors / Typography / Members App — the same set for every theme since #678, with a Base Theme's read-only on the gym screen. The **Members App** section (#833) is the one shared `ThemeMembersAppEditor` both screens render, and since **#1038** the Members App background images are its first **subsection** (`Members App → Images`) rather than a top-level section of their own: the page passes the rendered `ThemeMembersImagesEditor` as that editor's `images` prop and the editor draws it with the same collapsible header its five setting groups use, so the two sections that both read *Members App* became one, the images editor keeps owning nothing (the page still holds the draft and performs both calls on Save), and the Base Themes screen still withholds the subsection for a theme that does not exist yet; see the Theming section above for its overrides-only `tokens.membersApp` map and its per-setting inheritance from the Admin settings. Assignments section (#985): org-default checkbox, then an **inline checkbox list** — `All Centers` above a hairline, then every Center of the gym, each box being that Center's own `centers.theme_id`, with an `Inherited` tag beside a Center the theme reaches as the Gym Default. The draft is the card's (`apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/themes/centerAssignments.ts` holds the `All Centers` rule and the dirty comparison, JSX-free), so it joins the card's dirty state and its unsaved-changes guard and is persisted by the section's own `Save changes` through one replace-all `PUT` — which is why a **Base Theme** now gets that Save pair too (its configuration stays read-only and the settings `PUT` is not attempted, but its assignments are the gym's). The `+ Assign Centers…` button, its modal, the modal's search field, its Assign/Cancel pair, the per-row `Restore Inheritance` button (unticking a box is that action now), the center search field and the show-all disclosure are all gone; a gym with no Centers reads `No centers available.` rather than an empty list. Delete blocked when theme is in use (409). Theme resolution per center: explicit `centers.theme_id` → `gyms.theme_id` → first alphabetically. #488: `logo_contains_gym_name` checkbox under the Logo field on both editors — gates whether the gym name renders next to the logo in Admin/Member Web/Payment headers (see Theming section above). #713/#824: `POST /system/themes/:id/logo` uploads into the **theme's own** folder inside the gym's R2 tree, at `<storage_folder_prefix>/themes/<theme_id>-<sanitized name>/logo/logo.<ext>` (extension derived from the validated MIME type — the uploaded file name never reaches the key), writes `themes.logo_object_key` and clears `logo_bytes`; `DELETE .../logo` removes the object first and reports a failed removal as 502 rather than dropping the reference. The theme folder and its `logo/` leaf are written on demand (`themeLogoFolderKeys()` + `ensureStorageFolders()`), the gym-level `themes/` root deliberately is **not** — it belongs to Gym Bucket Initialization (#735), and until it exists the admin control is disabled (#823) and the route answers 409. Storage failures reuse #417's conventions (503 not configured / 409 gym folder not initialized / 502 R2 failure, `details` but no platform `diagnostics`), and since #824 each also carries `stage` (`domain/storageFailureStage.ts`) and the `path` it was working on. **#1042** adds the other half of that diagnostic — *why* it failed: every storage answer of both Theme routers carries a `cause` from the closed set in `domain/storageFailureCause.ts` (`not_configured` for the 503, `not_initialized` for the gym-folder 409, and for a 502 whatever `storageCauseFromDetails()` reads off the S3 error itself — `NoSuchBucket`/404 ⇒ `not_initialized`, `AccessDenied`/401/403 ⇒ `access_denied`, anything else ⇒ `unreachable`), with `cause: 'not_found'` on the routers' `Theme not found` 404s and `cause: 'invalid_file'` on the type/size/signature refusals. The admin turns it into the block's *Cause* and *What you can do* lines (`apps/admin/src/lib/storageFailureCause.ts` mirrors the vocabulary and diagnoses the failures that never reached a route from their status, answering `null` — and so rendering #824's block unchanged — for an ambiguous 400 or 409) and uses it to offer the `Initialize bucket` action beside the error, for `not_initialized` alone. The same ticket brought the platform Members-image upload's answers into line with their gym-side twins (they named neither the step nor the object) and put the diagnosed cause and the authenticated user into every storage failure log. Because the key names the **theme**, an upload takes nothing from its siblings: it deletes only what *this* row pointed at, which is how a pre-#824 `Branding/Logo/` key moves into the theme folder the next time the logo is replaced (existing rows are not rewritten and keep rendering meanwhile). **#829:** `POST /platform/themes/:id/logo` is the same route one root over — `cordel/themes/<theme_id>-<sanitized name>/logo/logo.<ext>` off `PLATFORM_STORAGE_ROOT`, the same columns (`logo_object_key` set, `logo_bytes` cleared), the same 512 KB / PNG-SVG-JPEG-WebP validation, the same best-effort sweep of the key it replaced and the same `stage`/`path` diagnostics, minus the 409: the platform root is a constant gated by no gym's bucket, so the upload writes `cordel/` and `cordel/themes/` itself (`themeRootFolderKeys()`) as the Base Theme Members upload has since #732. It is 404 for a gym's Custom Theme, as the gym route is 404 for a Base Theme, and `shapeTheme()` on that router now returns the derived `logo_url` too. `GET /themes/:id/logo` is unchanged and still serves either storage mode, which is what keeps a Base Theme logo uploaded before #829 rendering from its blob. #713's gym-wide hand-over — the uploading theme taking the slot and every sibling dropping its reference — is gone with the gym-wide key it existed for. `GET /themes/:id/logo` filters `deleted_at IS NULL`, so a soft-deleted theme's logo is unreachable. #725: a Customer Theme also carries the **Members App background images** — `training` / `nutrition` / `calendar` / `bookings` / `background` / `membership`, and `personal_goals` since #1038 (migration 219 swaps `chk_theme_member_images_slot` for the seven; no backfill, no existing key touched, and the My Goals object is `members_app/personal_goals.png` like every other slot) — as rows of `theme_member_images` (migration 181, one per configured `(theme_id, slot)`), each holding the R2 object key `<storage_folder_prefix>/themes/<theme_id>-<sanitized theme name>/members_app/<slot>.png`. `POST /system/themes/:id/members-images/:slot` writes the four folder markers of that branch (`ensureStorageFolders()`) and then the object; the key is derived server-side from the tenant's own folder prefix, the theme row and the slot, so the uploaded file's name, type and any request parameter play no part in it (`my-training.jpg` still lands on `training.png`, with the object's `Content-Type` carrying the real type). Allowed types are PNG/JPEG/WebP, validated by **signature** and not by the `Content-Type` header, ≤ 4 MB. `DELETE .../members-images/:slot` deletes only the row — deliberately unlike the logo, #725 requires the object to survive a Remove, and the row (not the object) is what makes a slot configured; re-uploading writes the same key again. A rename is the one case that leaves an orphan, swept best-effort on the next upload. Every theme-shaped response (`GET /system/themes`, `GET /me/gyms`, `GET /me/gym`) carries `members_images: { <slot>_url }` — one field per slot, `null` for an unconfigured slot — resolved from the key by `domain/themeMemberImages.ts` with the same `?v=` stamp the logo uses. #732 gives a **Base Theme** the same slots, in the platform's own folder: `cordel/themes/<theme_id>-<sanitized theme name>/members_app/<slot>.png`, where `cordel` is `PLATFORM_STORAGE_ROOT` (`infra/storage.ts`), the sibling of the `gyms/` root. Migration 182 widens `theme_member_images.gym_id` to nullable — a NULL row belongs to the platform — and `POST`/`DELETE /platform/themes/:id/members-images/:slot` (superadmin, `themes.ts`) are the gym routes' counterpart, resolving the theme with `gym_id IS NULL` so each router answers 404 for the other's themes. Same validation (PNG/JPEG/WebP by signature, ≤ 4 MB), same deterministic key, same "Remove clears the row and leaves the object" rule. A gym is *served* a Base Theme's slots and cannot write them: `loadMemberImagesByTheme()` includes `gym_id IS NULL` rows only for theme ids the caller already named, so `GET /system/themes`, `GET /me/gym(s)` and the member app paint a Base Theme's backgrounds with no change of their own. A **gym-side clone copies the source's configured slots** since #1041 (see below); a Base→Base clone still starts unconfigured, because §14 forbids writing into the platform root as part of that operation. **#827**: cloning is also where a Custom Theme's storage is initialized, and it is refused when the gym's is not. `POST /system/themes/clone/:sourceId` resolves the gym's `storage_folder_prefix` **before** the INSERT — 503 for an unconfigured deployment, 409 for a gym that was never initialized, both carrying `stage: 'resolve_path'` — and then writes the new theme's own folder and its `Logo/` and `Members/` leaves (`themeStorageFolderKeys()` in `domain/themeFolders.ts`, which also owns both leaf names now, re-exported by `themeLogo.ts`/`themeMemberImages.ts`) through `ensureStorageFolders()`. Storage first and the row second is what makes §7's partially created theme impossible: an R2 failure answers 502 (with the failing marker named by position — `themeFolderStageForKey()`) and leaves no `themes` row, while every marker key ends in `/`, so re-writing one is idempotent and never overwrites an object. The gym-level `themes/` root is still Gym Bucket Initialization's (#735) and is never created here, and the clone's markers carry the *new* theme id, so no clone can share its source's path. A **Base** Theme is not gated on any gym's bucket (its objects are `cordel/themes/…`); on the admin side `⋮ → Clone` is disabled with the reason while `gymStorageBlock()` holds (#823) and a failed creation renders `formatStorageError()`'s block like every other storage-backed save (#824). **#828** adds the manual counterpart to that initialization, on both context menus: `POST /system/themes/:id/storage/initialize` (gym admin) and `POST /platform/themes/:id/storage/initialize` (superadmin) write exactly the same `themeStorageFolderKeys()` markers — the theme's folder plus its `Logo/` and `Members/` leaves — off the gym's `storage_folder_prefix` and off `PLATFORM_STORAGE_ROOT` respectively, so a Theme created before #827 or renamed since (a rename moves its folder) can get its tree back without uploading anything. Each router answers for its own rows (a Base Theme is 404 on the gym route, a Custom Theme 404 on the platform one), both are idempotent and write nothing but `/`-terminated zero-byte markers, and neither touches the `themes` row — so the action can never delete a file or change a Theme's configuration. The gym route keeps 409/503/502 with `stage`/`path`/`details` and the gym-level `themes/` root stays #735's (a gym with no prefix is a 409, and the menu item is disabled through `gymStorageBlock()`); the platform route has no 409 counterpart, since the platform root is a constant no gym's bucket governs. `ensureThemeStorage()` in `gym-themes.ts` is the shared folder-writing step (clone + initialize), and the admin reports a failure through the new one-line `formatStorageErrorLine()`, because `Toast` renders its message as a single text node. **#1041** completes the clone: between those folder markers and the INSERT, `POST /system/themes/clone/:sourceId` now **copies every asset the source Theme owns** into the clone's own folder — its logo and one object per configured Members App slot — and stores the *new* keys on the clone, so nothing of the clone's ever points at the source (§7/§9/§11). `api/src/domain/themeAssetClone.ts` is the one place that decides what moves: `planThemeAssetCopies()` names no file name at all (a background is planned from the source's own `theme_member_images` row and keyed by `buildThemeMemberImageKey()`, the logo by `buildThemeLogoKey()` off the stored `logo_mime`), so a slot added to `MEMBER_IMAGE_SLOTS` later is copied with no change to it (§5) and an unconfigured asset produces no copy rather than an empty object (§4). The copies are server-side `CopyObjectCommand`s — `copyStorageObject()` in `infra/storage.ts`, the object's `Content-Type` travelling with it — with one exception: a legacy `themes.logo_bytes` logo is not in the bucket at all, so its bytes are *uploaded* into the clone's key, which is also the only way such a clone gets an R2 logo (and still adds no writer to `logo_bytes`, #829). Failure is the point of the ordering: a copy that fails sweeps the destination objects already written, answers 502 with `copy_logo`/`copy_members_image` as its `stage` and the destination `path`, and leaves **no** `themes` row — so §15's “theme created + only 4 of 7 images copied + clone appears successful” is unreachable. The row and its references commit in one transaction (the `theme_member_images` rows carry the clone's own `gym_id`, even when the source is a Base Theme whose rows are the platform's, #732), and a DB failure sweeps the copies too. Cloning a Base Theme therefore *reads* `cordel/` and writes only under the gym's prefix — §14 — which is why the Base→Base `POST /platform/themes/clone/:sourceId` is untouched. On the admin side the Clone modal's Save reads `clone_saving` while the request is in flight (`CrudModal` disables both buttons on `saving`, so a second click cannot start a second clone, §17) and a success raises `toast_cloned`; the two new stages have `storage_stage_copy_logo`/`storage_stage_copy_members_image` keys in both theme namespaces in en/es/ca. |
| Recycle Bin | `recycle-bin.ts` | `[locale]/recycle-bin/` (System nav, admin only) | #248. No migrations — all entity tables already have soft-delete columns. `GET /recycle-bin` runs a UNION over `membership_plans`, `promotions`, `centers`, `spaces`, `product` (products WHERE is_system=0), `exercises`, `workout_templates`, `training_plan_templates`, `nutrition_plan_templates`, `themes`, `activity_type`, `professional_service` (professional_services WHERE is_system=0, #484), returning `{ items, total, limit, offset }` with filters `entity_type`, `q`, `deleted_by_name`, `from`, `to` and sort `entity_type\|name\|deleted_at`. `GET /recycle-bin/:entityType/:id` returns full entity detail bypassing soft-delete guard. `POST /recycle-bin/:entityType/:id/recover` (requireModuleWrite SYSTEM) clears soft-delete columns and restores appropriate status; records audit with `action='restore'`. Two runtime quirks: (1) MySQL 8.4 rejects `?` params for LIMIT/OFFSET on derived-table queries — interpolated as integer literals; (2) `themes` table uses `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` collation, others use `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci` — `name`/`description` in the themes UNION branch are wrapped with `CONVERT(... USING utf8mb4)`. Entity type `class_package` renamed to `product` (#271) — the branch now queries `products WHERE is_system=0`. Admin page: type-filter select, name/deleted-by text search, date range, paginated table, Details modal with entity-specific fields, Recover button. |
| Dashboards | — | `membership`, `organization`, `training`, `nutrition`, `financials` | Placeholder "coming soon" shells. |

Shared admin components (`apps/admin/src/components/`): `DataTable`, `CrudModal`, `ConfirmDialog`, `ReplacePlanDialog`, `StatusBadge`, `StatusFilter`, `FilterBar`, `listChrome.ts`, `formChrome.ts`, `workoutChrome.ts`, `CardSection`, `CardDetailRow`, `Toast`, `Sidebar` (+ `lib/sidebarCollapse.ts`), `NavGroup`, `AppShell`, `TopHeader`, `GymSelector`, `LanguagePicker`, `headerChrome.ts`, `ThemeProvider`, `ui.tsx`. Use these in every new page — don't hand-roll tables/modals/status chips.

**`FilterBar` and `listChrome.ts` (#724)** are the two halves of a list's presentation, extracted from Assigned Plans so a card list can look like a table list without copying its values. `FilterBar`/`FilterField` (+ `filterControlStyle`, `filterLabelStyle`, `filterButtonStyle`) are the labelled filter bar #411 introduced — label above the control, one control height, wrapping instead of overflowing on a narrow viewport. `listChrome.ts` holds the surface, header band, cell insets and row dividers `DataTable` itself is now built from (`listSurfaceStyle`, `listHeaderRowStyle`, `listHeaderCellStyle`, `listCellStyle`, `listRowDividerStyle`, `listExpandedStyle`, `LIST_PADDING_X`, and since #894 `listNameBadgeStyle` — the small grey `System`/`Mandatory` pill a row's name cell carries, as opposed to `StatusBadge`, which carries a row's state in its own column — beside which #913 added `listNameBadgeAccentStyle`, the same pill in amber for metadata that has to be noticed, spreading the neutral style so only its two colours differ; `Mandatory` reads from it), so a page that cannot use a `<table>` — Training Plans' expandable cards (#724), Members' since #928 — still wears exactly the same chrome, and a change to the header band moves both. `StatusFilter` takes an optional `style` (spread over its default) for a page whose filter bar sets its own control height. Since **#1011** the same module holds a list's **mobile half**: `LIST_MOBILE_MEDIA_QUERY` (mirroring AppShell's own 768/769 split, as `lib/sidebarCollapse.ts` does for the sidebar), the `ListColumnMobile` vocabulary (`name` · `keep` · `actions` · `secondary`), `listCellClass()`/`listScrollerClass()` and `LIST_RESPONSIVE_CSS`, which `components/ListResponsiveStyles.tsx` mounts once from `AppShell`. It has to be a global stylesheet rather than a style object because every cell in these lists is inline-styled and only `!important` in a sheet wins over an inline `display`. `DataTable` reads the declaration: each `<th>`/`<td>` carries its column's class, a `mobile: 'name'` cell wraps its value so it can be truncated and keeps the full value in the cell's `title`, and the table sits in one wrapper whose mode is derived from whether the row expands — `collapse` (secondary columns hidden, their values one tap below in the expanded row) for an expandable list, `scroll` (every column kept, the name and the actions pinned to the edges, the block between them scrolling *inside the list*) for a flat one, since hiding a column there would make it unreachable on a phone rather than one tap away. Above the breakpoint none of those rules exist, so desktop and tablet are byte-identical. A page laid out from its own `LIST_COLUMNS` declaration (#637's grid — Products, Members and Training Plans, joined by Promotions, Membership Plans, Activities, Exercises, Spaces, Centers, Staff and Professional Services when #1011 stage 3 converted those card lists from a `rowStyle` flex row plus a hand-sized header band, and by Training Plan Templates, Workout Templates, Gyms, Themes, Taxes, Nutrition Plans, Nutrition Plan Templates and Payment Providers when stage 4 finished the set — Themes and the two Nutrition plan lists carry no header band at all, so there the declaration is the row's alone and no band was invented for it, which would have been a desktop change) reads the same vocabulary, but cannot be hidden the same way: the tracks are declared on the row, so a hidden cell would leave its track behind and slide every cell after it under the wrong title. Below the breakpoint such a row becomes a flex line instead (`LIST_GRID_ROW_CLASS` — the identity `flex: 1 1 auto; min-width: 0`, the actions `margin-left: auto`) and `LIST_MIN_WIDTH_CLASS` releases #637's track-sum minimum, since a row carrying only the columns a phone has room for has nothing to scroll. Those pages write their cells by hand, so `listCellClasses(LIST_COLUMNS)` maps each column's key to its class and both halves of a column — the header cell and the row cell — read it from there; the `title` a truncated identity needs sits on the name cell rather than in the declaration. The adoption gate is `api/src/test/admin-list-mobile-columns.unit.test.ts` (in the **API** suite, because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only): every column of every list — `Column<T>[]` and `ListColumn[]` alike — declares a behaviour, exactly one is the row's identity, and no page spells a `gd-list-*` class or a media query of its own. Since stage 4 its grid-declaration count is an exact 19, which is every card list in the app, so a page that went back to hand-sized cells drops it.

**`ReplacePlanDialog` (#956 stage 2)** is the one confirmation a gym owner sees before a Membership Plan assignment replaces the one a Member already holds, and it is the shape to copy for any warn-then-confirm answered by a `409` + `confirm: true`. The split is the usual one: `lib/activePlanConflict.ts` is JSX-free and holds the reading — it recognises the conflict by **shape** (`status === 409` *and* `error === 'active_plan_exists'` *and* a `current_plan`), so the router's other 409s (`/close`'s `unused_value_impacted`, a duplicate key) still fall through to the caller's own error line — plus what the dialog says about it (whether a conflict is a family plan somebody else owns, the plural wording keyed on how many plans Continue cancels, the `DD/MM/YYYY` that never goes through `Date`). The component is only the markup, and it draws through `ConfirmDialog` rather than a second overlay, modal or destructive button pair; `ConfirmDialog` gained one optional `details` slot for it, since the warning names two Plans and a date rather than being a longer sentence. It names no endpoint and decides no permission (#806), so the four admin entry points own the request and the resend: the first attempt never sends `confirm` (a click handler passed by reference would hand the submit function a `MouseEvent` as its `confirmReplacement` argument and confirm it), and Cancel clears the conflict and nothing else — no request, no close, no reload. The copy is one set of `common.replace_plan_*` keys in en/es/ca, resolved by the component, because the rule is one rule and the words must not differ per screen.

**`workoutChrome.ts` (#971)** is the same rule one level further in, for the Training Plan → Workout → Day → Block → Exercises tree that three screens render (the Assigned Training Plans card, the Training Plan Templates card, and Workout Templates through the shared `WorkoutBlockBuilder`). It holds the two controls each of them had declared for itself in a lilac no Theme setting reached: `weekdayChipStyle` / `weekdaySelectStyle`, the training-day pill — **one object for both halves of the read-only/Edit split**, so the value and the `<select>` are the same box and switching modes does not move the row, wearing the Theme's `--gd-input-border` / `--gd-input-bg` at a pill radius — and `treeAddBtnStyle`, which is `formChrome`'s `dashedAddBtnStyle` at the tree's denser size for `+ Block` / `+ Exercise` (secondary actions; the filled `primaryBtnStyle()` is `+ Add Workout`). The same ticket put the tree behind Edit mode: every control in it keys off the `canWrite` it is handed, so the card passes `canWrite && editing && !isCompleted` and an expanded plan reads rather than edits, per CLAUDE.md's "Expanding a list card reads; `⋮ → Edit` writes". Covered by `apps/admin/src/test/training-plan-editor-theme.test.ts`.

**#1031 widened it to the whole tree, and swept the third screen onto it.** #971 reached the Assigned Training Plans card and the Training Plan Templates card; `WorkoutTemplateTree.tsx` — the tree the **Workout Templates** card renders — kept its own copy of every control, so `+ Block` / `+ Exercise` were a dashed lilac link, the block header's input and select sat on a hardcoded `#ddd` over `#fafafa`, the block card on a `#ececf0` border under a themed background, the picker's selected option on `#f0eeff`, and the drop target that accepts a block dragged in from another template highlighted `#eef0ff` behind a `#6c63ff` dashed outline. `workoutChrome.ts` now declares `treeControlBox` and everything built from it (`treeHeaderInputStyle`, `treeHeaderSelectStyle`, `treeCellInputStyle`, `treeComboTriggerStyle`), the picker's panel/search/list/options with `TREE_COMBO_ITEM_SELECTED_BG` (the Theme's `--gd-app-bg`), the exercise table (`treeTableStyle` / `treeThStyle` / `treeTdStyle`, its rule on `--gd-card-border`), both drag handles, `treeBlockCardStyle` (`cardSurfaceStyle`), the draft row's `✕` (`treeDraftRemoveBtnStyle`, neutral rather than `formChrome`'s red `rowRemoveBtnStyle` — it abandons an unsaved row) and `treeDropTargetStyle()`; both tree files spread them and neither declares a style of its own. The Workout Templates page itself moved onto `formChrome` (`formFieldLabelStyle` / `formControlStyle` / `formErrorStyle` / `secondaryBtnSmall`, `CardSection` + `CardDetailRow` for its three sections and its read-only rows, `filterControlStyle` for its two filters) and its `+ Add`, like Base Workout Templates' `+ New Template`, is `primaryBtnStyle()`. Frontend only: no endpoint, payload, validation, handler or locale key moved. Covered by `apps/admin/src/test/workout-template-theme.test.ts`.

**`formChrome.ts` (#929)** is the same one-place rule one level in, for what a card draws around its *fields*: `cardSectionLabelStyle`/`cardSubLabelStyle`, `cardSectionStyle`/`cardSectionDividedStyle` (a section's spacing and the hairline above it — the first section of a card takes the undivided one), `innerCardStyle`, `inlineEditorStyle`/`inlineEditorTitleStyle`, `cardMutedTextStyle`/`cardHintStyle`, `formFieldLabelStyle`, `formControlStyle` (the single box an `<input>`, a `<select>` and a `<textarea>` all wear), `formValueStyle`, `formCheckboxLabelStyle`, the help/field-error/form-error lines, the two action rows, `secondaryBtnStyle`/`secondaryBtnSmall` (the neutral half of a button pair whose primary half is `ui.tsx`'s `primaryBtnStyle()`/`primaryBtnSmall()`, #912), `dashedAddBtnStyle` and `cardTextLinkStyle`. `formValueStyle` deliberately carries `formControlStyle`'s padding, type size and border width (transparent): a read-only value that sits flush under a label whose input is inset by 10px moves every field sideways as `⋮ → Edit` opens, which is what the Member card did until #929. The Member card is the reference adopter — its nine files had each grown their own copy of the same input, card, section header and Save button — and a new card spreads these rather than re-picking a padding, a radius and a lilac. **#924 stage 5** added the two pieces a card had been re-implementing *around* those styles, as components rather than objects: `CardSection` (the section's heading, the hairline above it when it is not the card's `first`, and an `action` slot for its `SectionEditButton`) and `CardDetailRow` (one horizontal `Label: Value` pair, at the Membership Plan card's own 200px label width and 13.5px type, over the new `cardDetailRowStyle`/`cardDetailLabelStyle`/`cardDetailValueStyle`). Both are presentational and entity-free in `BillingDurationSummary`'s sense — they resolve no locale key, name no endpoint and decide no permission — because whether a section carries an `Edit` button at all is the card's decision, not the section's (#897). The Assigned Plan card is their first adopter: its four files had three copies of that `Label: Value` row at a 140px label width, two section headers with no hairline at all, a `#111` Save no Theme could reach and a page-local lilac text link for the subsection `Edit` action.

**`Tabs.tsx` (#961)** is the admin app's one tab strip, promoted out of the Nutrition Library's own `LibraryTabs` when the Member card needed the same one. A consumer supplies the tabs (`{ id, labelKey }[]`), the active one, a change handler and a `label` resolver; the component owns the look, the `role="tablist"` semantics with `aria-selected` and roving `tabIndex`, the ← → / Home / End handling and the phone-width horizontal scroll (it scrolls, never wraps). It resolves no label of its own — each page translates in its own namespace (#901) — and declares no colour of its own: the active tab follows `var(--brand, …)`, so a gym's Theme reaches it and no screen may give tabs a custom active colour (#912/#954). `LibraryTabs` is now the binding of it to `LIBRARY_TABS`; the Member card's binding is `MEMBER_TABS`.

**The Member card is five tabs (#961 — frontend only, no migration, no API change).** Expanding a Member used to render ten sections as one very long column. `apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/members/memberTabs.ts` is the one declaration of the split: `MEMBER_TABS` names the tabs in order (Profile · Products & Services · Nutrition · Personal Goals · Training Plan) and the `members.section_*` keys each one renders — Profile keeps the Profile fields and Account, Products & Services takes Membership Plans (moved out of Profile by **#1051**, and first in the tab), Additional Products, the Billing Simulation, PT Class Slots, Session Packages and Billing Events (the rest being #961's own `Q2`), and the last three tabs take the section they are named after. A section belongs to exactly one tab, which `member-tabs.test.ts` asserts against the sections `MemberExpandedRow` actually renders, so none can be shown twice or lost. The page owns the selected tab per expanded Member and mirrors it in the URL (`?member=<id>&tab=<tabId>`, with `memberTabFromParam()` falling back to Profile for anything else), and `MemberExpandedRow` is *handed* its tab and renders only that tab's sections — the first of which carries `divider={false}` so each tab opens without the card's hairline. The inline Edit form renders in the Profile pane, because that is the field set it writes, and `⋮ → Edit` selects that tab before opening it; a tab change discards no draft, since the form values are the page's state. Nothing else moved: every section reads, writes and gates exactly as before.

**`headerChrome.ts` (#808)** is the same one-place rule for the top header: `HEADER_BG` and `HEADER_TEXT` are the exact token chains `TopHeader` paints its band with, and `headerOptionStyle` is the pair an `<option>` of a header `<select>` carries. The popup a browser opens for a native select is painted outside the header element and takes the UA's defaults, so an option that sets no colours of its own gets the header's (white) text on the UA's (white) popup — the language dropdown was unreadable under every theme until #808. `GymSelector` still styles its options with the pre-token `--chrome`/`#fff` pair, which stays readable under every theme; #808 was scoped to the language selector, so adopting `headerOptionStyle` there is a ticket of its own. (`CenterSelector` was the other such control until #930 moved center selection into the Calendar's own filter bar, where a page control wears `filterControlStyle` instead.)

### Member app (`apps/member`)

The member PWA is built out (no longer just a stub). Member endpoints live under `/me/*` (`me.ts`, all `requireRole('member')`).

**Navigation (#361)**: there is no bottom tab bar. Home (`/:locale`) is the primary navigation surface — a personalized dashboard (`components/TopBar.tsx` replaces the old `BottomNav`: a slim bar with a "← Home" back link on every non-Home page and persistent Notifications (#503 stage 8) and Profile entry points, since neither is reachable via a Home tile). Schedule is not a user-facing concept; `/schedule` still exists as a route (renamed "My Bookings" in the UI) but is only reachable via the Home nav tile, never from a tab bar. Packages and Payments are not top-level destinations — they're shown inside `/membership` ("My Membership"), which already surfaces benefits, packages and upcoming/historical billing events.

| Route | Backend (`/me/*`) | Notes |
|-------|-------------------|-------|
| Home (`/:locale`) | `GET /me/schedule`, `GET /me/membership`, `GET /me/notifications`, `GET /me/nutrition-plan` | Public when signed out (sign-in CTA). Signed-in dashboard: time-based greeting, Alerts card (only when an unread notification exists), Next Booking (**since #503 stage 8** also shows `center_name`, additive next to `room_name`), Today's Nutrition Plan (only when the active plan has meals for today's weekday or the "All Days" sentinel — see `/nutrition` below), a 2×2 nav tile grid (Calendar / My Training Plan / My Bookings / My Nutrition), and a My Membership summary card.
