Since **#932** that card's goal line uses My Nutrition's own `goalLabel()`/`goalDetail()`, so it no longer prints the stored slug (`weight_loss`) or an unspaced `1.00l`. **Since #728** each of those five surfaces paints its own Members App theme image as its background through `MembersSectionCard` (the Next Booking and Alerts cards keep their plain surfaces); nothing else about the dashboard changes. **Since #982** a tile whose slot is configured shows that artwork at full opacity and **hides its default emoji**, the label staying where the tile design puts it; a tile with no artwork renders exactly as before. |
| `/link` (first login) | `POST /me/link` | `[locale]/link/page.tsx` — mirrors the admin app's `GymContext` self-heal (`POST /staff/link`). Reads `gym_id` from the invite `redirectUrl` query string (`api/src/api/members.ts`'s invite creation sets `redirectUrl: ${memberAppUrl}/en/link?gym_id=...`) and passes it as `x-gym-id` (the `AppContext`-derived gym isn't known yet at this point, since no `gym_memberships` row exists until this call succeeds). On success, stores it as `activeGymId` in `localStorage` so `AppContext` picks the right gym on the redirect to Home; on 404 (no matching unlinked `members` row for that email+gym) or any other error, shows a message and redirects Home after a couple seconds. |
| (app bootstrap) | `GET /me/gym` | Resolves the caller's gym + assigned theme in one call; no `x-gym-id` required. `AppContext` calls this on mount. |
| `/schedule` ("My Bookings" in the UI) | `GET /me/schedule`, `POST /me/bookings`, `DELETE /me/bookings/:id`, `GET /me/bookings`, `GET /me/activity-history` | Upcoming section: browse/book/cancel sessions in the next 14 days via `GET /me/schedule` (joins `activity_types`+`spaces`, returns `space_name`, `trainer_name`, `can_cancel` — **since #503 stage 8** the page also renders the `center_name` stage 7 already added to the response, alongside `space_name`; (#324) `?activity_type_id=` filter, `allows_shared_booking`/`is_shareable`/`my_shared_request_id`/`my_shared_request_status`, computed `availability_state` enum — 8 values: UNAVAILABLE/BOOKED_BY_MEMBER/AVAILABLE/WAITLISTED_BY_MEMBER/SHARED_REQUESTED_BY_MEMBER/SHARED_REQUEST_AVAILABLE/WAITLIST_AVAILABLE/FULL; since #503 stage 2 a full session reports WAITLIST_AVAILABLE only when its effective `waitlist_mode` is `open`, and FULL otherwise, so members are never offered a waitlist the booking endpoint would 409). **Since #503 stage 5**, each row also carries three fields additive next to `availability_state` (which stays the source of truth for booking-action UI): `status` (`scheduled`/`running`/`completed`/`cancelled` — `running` derived live from `now` falling inside `[starts_at, ends_at)`, never stored), `occupancy_status` (`available`/`few_spots_left`/`full`/`unavailable` — capacity-only, `few_spots_left` at ≤20% of capacity remaining with a minimum of 1, `unavailable` gated on the same condition `bookMemberOnSession` enforces so it never reports a bookable state the booking endpoint would reject), and `waitlist_status`+`waitlist_count` (the existing effective `waitlist_mode` plus an aggregate count of `waitlisted` bookings — never identities, per the issue thread's privacy requirement). **Since #503 stage 6**, `GET /me/schedule` also accepts two optional local-filter query params, additive next to the existing `x-center-id`-header-driven scoping (never replacing it): `?center_id=` narrows to one center the caller is already authorized for — 403 if it falls outside the header-derived `allowedCenterIds`/`centerId` — while still keeping `center_id IS NULL` rows visible (#478); `?trainer_membership_id=` narrows to one trainer's occurrences. Both are request-scoped only, per the issue thread's requirement that a calendar-local filter never move the global `CenterSwitcher` or affect any other section. A new `GET /me/trainers` gives members a read-only `{id, name}` list to populate the filter panel's trainer dropdown — the existing `/trainers` router is staff-only (`requireModuleAccess('ORGANIZATION')`), so it can't be reused here. **Since #986** both read the same set through `domain/trainerAssignment.ts` (every active Staff record), so the member's filter cannot offer a different list of trainers from the one the admin assigns from. **Since #503 stage 7**, `GET /me/schedule` also returns `center_id`/`center_name` (an additive `LEFT JOIN centers`, mirroring the admin `calendarEventsRouter`'s own join) — `center_id` was already usable for filtering since stage 6 but never returned for display; both are `null` for a center-less occurrence. **Since #976** it also returns `color` — `COALESCE(calendar_events.color, activity_types.color)`, `null` when neither is configured, which is what tells the member calendar to fall back to the theme's own Calendar event colour rather than invent one. The `/calendar` page (below) is the frontend consumer of stage 6's filter params and both display fields. Past Bookings section (#362): `GET /me/activity-history` (paginated, newest first), showing attendance/cancellation status; each list item (upcoming or past) expands inline on tap to show fuller detail (description/capacity, or cancellation date) — there is no separate single-booking detail endpoint. `GET /me/bookings` lists all of the caller's bookings unfiltered by date; not currently called by any frontend page, but kept correct since it's part of the public `/me/*` surface. All three of these plus `GET /me/upcoming` (next 30 days of confirmed bookings, also unused by the frontend today) resolve the member via `resolveMemberId` and are center-scoped via `getCenterContext`, matching `/me/schedule`. `GET /me/bookings` previously joined the pre-#059-migration `class_types`/`cs.class_type_id` columns (dropped on a fully-migrated DB) and 500'd on every call — fixed to join `activity_types`/`cs.activity_type_id` like every other `/me/*` handler. |
| `/calendar` | `GET /me/schedule`, `GET /me/trainers`, `POST /me/shared-training-requests`, `DELETE /me/shared-training-requests/:id` | (#324) FullCalendar v6 member calendar page at `[locale]/calendar/`. Defaults to `timeGridDay` (today) per #361. Activity-type filter chips. **Since #976** an event's box is painted with the **event's own** colour and never with the member's booking state: `GET /me/schedule` reports `COALESCE(calendar_events.color, activity_types.color)` as `color`, the page sets `backgroundColor`/`borderColor`/`textColor` inline only when that is a usable hex, and sets nothing at all otherwise so the theme's `calendarEventBackground`/`Border`/`Text` paint it (#559 stage 2). The member's own relationship with the event is text inside the box — `Booked`, `Waitlist · Position #2 · 4 waiting`, `Completed`, and **nothing at all** for an event they never booked — and the whole decision (colour resolution, the WCAG-scored readable foreground, the status line's locale key, the hue-free chip) is `apps/member/src/lib/calendarEventDisplay.ts`. The eight-colour `availability_state` palette it replaced (`STATE_COLORS`, yellow for "you are waitlisted", orange for a joinable waitlist, green for "you are booked") is gone: the same class was a different colour for two members standing next to each other. Each event cell also shows an aggregate occupancy count (`booked/capacity`). **Since #981** it also names the trainer *and* the space, as one compact `Trainer · Space` line in the Day and Week views (`memberEventMetaLine()` in that same module, from `ce.trainer_membership_id`/`ce.space_id` — the occurrence's own, never its Activity Type's defaults), with a missing one simply omitted rather than placeheld; every other view keeps the trainer-alone line it had. **Since #503 stage 7**: a compact "Filter" button (kept collapsed to preserve mobile header space, per the issue thread) opens a dropdown panel with independent, combinable Center and Trainer selects (`GET /me/trainers` for the trainer options, `AppContext`'s existing `centers` for the center options) plus Apply/Clear — wiring up stage 6's `?center_id=`/`?trainer_membership_id=` params; a dot on the button indicates an active filter. The center filter defaults once to the member's default center (`AppContext.activeCenterId`, set from Profile; the top-of-page `CenterSwitcher` was removed) but, per the thread's explicit requirement, never writes back to it — changing it only affects this page's own query. The bottom-sheet detail panel shows the three-badge breakdown agreed on the issue thread — lifecycle `status`, `occupancy_status` (+ `booked/capacity`), and (only when not `disabled`) the `waitlist_status` badge with an aggregate `waitlist_count` (never identities) — alongside the existing action buttons, which still key off `availability_state` unchanged: Book (AVAILABLE), Join Waitlist (WAITLIST_AVAILABLE), Cancel Booking (BOOKED_BY_MEMBER), Leave Waitlist (WAITLISTED_BY_MEMBER), Request Shared Training (SHARED_REQUEST_AVAILABLE), Cancel Request (SHARED_REQUESTED_BY_MEMBER). `POST /me/shared-training-requests` validates `is_shareable + allows_shared_booking`, returns 409 on duplicate; `DELETE` cancels own pending request. Double-click day → Day view. Feature-flagged: `calendar.member_calendar`. **Since #984** the page background is the **general** Members App background, not the Calendar tile image: `slotForPathname()` leaves `/calendar` unnamed, so it takes the `background` slot when one is configured and the resolved Members App background colour when it is not. The Calendar tile keeps that image on the home dashboard, and the page's own Calendar theme settings (header, buttons, time column, modal) are unaffected. |
| `/notifications` ("Alerts" in the UI) | `GET /me/notifications`, `GET /me/notifications/count`, `PUT /me/notifications/:id/read`, `PUT /me/notifications/read-all` | In-app notification center (#194). Notifications are written fire-and-forget by booking/cancel/session-cancel/schedule-rule-cancel flows via `sendNotification`/`sendBulkNotification` in `infra/notifications.ts`. `GET /me/notifications` returns `{ items, unread }`. `AppContext` fetches `count` on mount and exposes `unreadNotifications`+`refreshUnreadCount`. Home's Alerts card shows the single most recent unread notification; **since #503 stage 8**, `TopBar.tsx` has its own bell button (any page) linking here with the unread-count badge — previously the badge sat on the Profile button, which links to `/profile`, not here. |
| `/membership` ("My Membership" in the UI) | `GET /me/membership`, `GET /me/billing-events`, `GET /me/class-packages`, `GET /me/promotions`, `GET /me/billing-event-forecast` | Current membership, benefits, class packages and payments all in one page — this is where Packages and Payments live post-#361 (no separate top-level nav item for either). **Since #1123** the page's own *Upcoming payments* and *Payment history* sections are one collapsed **Payments** card holding three subcards — *Next Payment* (expanded), *Past Billing Events*, *Forecast Billing Events* — over `components/MemberPaymentsCard.tsx` and the pure `lib/memberPayments.ts`. The forecast is `GET /me/billing-event-forecast`, which is the Assigned Plan card's own projection (#924 stage 4) scoped to the caller, so *Next Payment* is its **first group** and the member cannot be shown a total the nightly run will not charge; a past event's `status` is derived by `deriveBillingEventStatus()` (#640) and reported per event, never summarised per date. |
| `/training` | `GET /me/training-plans`, `POST /me/exercise-logs`, `POST /me/workout-block-logs` | Assigned training plans + set logging. **#1009:** a block's result is one optional, untyped free-text value — the typed caption and the `result_type`-gated input are gone, since migration 074 (#154) moved the result type to the exercise instance and the page was throwing `undefined.toLowerCase()` while rendering any block (see "A workout block's result is untyped free text"). **#723:** each exercise row also shows the media the exercise carries — `exercise_image_url`/`exercise_video_url` already ride down the plan tree (`PLAN_TREE_SELECT`, #720), so there is no request per exercise. `components/ExerciseMedia.tsx` renders a lazy-loaded image tile and a video tile with a play indicator inside the exercise's own row (nothing at all when it has no media), and `components/ExerciseMediaViewer.tsx` opens the larger view as an overlay — never a route — so the member keeps their place in the plan: the image at full size, a YouTube video in a `youtube-nocookie` embed, an R2-stored video in a `<video controls>`, and a URL the app cannot embed in a new tab. Neither player autoplays and no `<video>` is mounted to draw a row. `lib/exerciseMedia.ts` holds the pure URL helpers (thumbnail-vs-master, YouTube id/poster/embed, video kind) and, like the Admin side, resolves nothing about where the media came from. |
| `/nutrition` ("My Nutrition" in the UI, #361) | `GET /me/nutrition-plan` | Read-only view of the caller's own active `member_nutrition_plans` row with full hierarchy (days → meals → items, goals) — mirrors the staff-facing `member-nutrition-plans.ts` `/:id/hierarchy` but resolves the member from `TenantContext` instead of an arbitrary `member_id`. Returns `{ plan: null }` when there is no active plan or the caller has no linked member row. Home's Today's Nutrition Plan card fetches the same endpoint and filters client-side to `weekday === today \|\| weekday === 7` (the "All Days" sentinel — see `nutrition-plan-templates.ts`). Feature-flagged: `nutrition.nutrition_plans`. No editing — building the plan is still done by staff via `member-nutrition-plans.ts`/`nutrition-plan-templates.ts`. **#722:** each meal item also carries the Nutrition Library item's `image_url` and its `qualities` (`[{id, slug}]`, from `nutrition_library_item_qualities`), read once for the whole plan rather than per item, and the page renders a meal's foods as a horizontal swipeable carousel of image cards (`components/NutritionFoodCarousel.tsx` + `NutritionFoodCard.tsx`) instead of one joined text line. Home's card is unchanged — it still reads names only. **#932:** the plan's **dietary restrictions** are returned too (`restrictions: [{id, nutrition_library_item_id, item_name, image_url, applies_all_days}]`, in `position` order) — a restriction *is* a Nutrition Library food, `member_nutrition_plan_restrictions.nutrition_library_item_id` being NOT NULL, so its name is resolved in the request locale and its image is the library item's own `image_url`, the same column the meal foods read: there is no second image source for My Nutrition. The page renders **Nutrition Goals** and **Dietary Restrictions** as two sections of image-plus-name rows through one shared `components/NutritionItemRow.tsx`, which owns the thumbnail size, aspect ratio, radius, spacing, typography and the `nutrition.no_image` fallback for both, and both sections state their own empty case in words rather than disappearing. A goal's stored `item_name` is a **slug** from the plan routers' closed vocabulary (`weight_loss`), not a label: `goalLabel()`/`goalDetail()` (`lib/nutritionFood.ts`) translate it and render its value and frequency as `1 l · daily`, deciding the fallback *before* calling `t()` (next-intl has no `defaultValue`). `member_nutrition_plan_goals` carries no link to a food, so a goal row shows that fallback until one exists. |
| `/goals` ("My Goals" in the UI, #1036) | `GET /me/personal-goals`, `GET /me/personal-goals/available`, `POST /me/personal-goals`, `PUT /me/personal-goals/:id`, `DELETE /me/personal-goals/:id`, and since #1037 stage 2 `GET /me/personal-goals/:id/readings`, `POST /me/personal-goals/:id/readings`, `POST /me/personal-goals/:id/initial-reading` | The member's own **Assigned Personal Goals** (`member_personal_goals`, migration 212), managed by them: assign an existing Gym Goal, edit the target they agreed, remove it. They cannot create a Goal **definition** — the picker is a read of the gym's catalogue under its own gym-facing predicate (`gym_id IS NULL OR gym_id = ?`, not deleted) and nothing here writes `personal_goals`. Mounted at `/me/personal-goals` (`api/src/api/me-personal-goals.ts`, registered **before** `/me` so Express reaches it) behind `member_web.my_goals` **and** the catalogue's own `nutrition.personal_goals`: a gym that hid Personal Goals did not mean "and let members assign them anyway". Every route resolves the caller through `resolveMemberId()` and constrains on `(gym_id, member_id)`, so another member's assignment is a 404 whatever the payload says, and no body here carries a `member_id`. The read answers `{ goals, past_goals }` split on the very predicate `mpgoal_live_goal_key` generates from, so **Past Goals** is everything removed, achieved or abandoned; `/available` is the selector's list, excluding a retired goal and one the member already holds live (the thread's `Q3`), which is what makes the selector and the unique index agree. Creating inherits the Gym Goal's name and target as the assignment's snapshot (#1034 §7) — an explicit `null` is still a clear — and `status` is **not** accepted: progress is a staff field, and a member ends a goal by removing it. Removal is the soft delete the staff router already performs, so the Gym Goal is untouched and assigning it again creates a new row with its own snapshot. The page is `apps/member/src/app/[locale]/goals/page.tsx` over the pure `lib/memberGoals.ts` (label, target formatting, which list a row is in, the payloads, the client-side field rules as locale keys) and `components/MemberDialog.tsx`, the app's one dialog shell; it paints through `lib/memberChrome.ts` and spells no colour of its own (#983). Its artwork is #1038's `personal_goals` slot, which `slotForPathname()` now names. Since **#1037 stage 3** each live goal's card carries §5's five structured fields and §18's collapsible reading history, both from `components/GoalReadings.tsx` (presentational in `NutritionItemRow`'s sense: every label arrives resolved and every value formatted, so it calls no `t()`), with `Add reading` and `Change initial reading` opening the app's one dialog shell over the two routes above — the goal's unit beside the input rather than a field, the date defaulting to today. A past goal keeps its compact summary and is offered no reading action. Since **#1115** each live goal is an individual **card**, collapsed by default: `components/MemberGoalCard.tsx` owns the card's shape and nothing about goals (the title, the summary line and every menu label arrive already resolved, so it calls no `t()` and spells no colour), its header carries the name over `Target: 70 kg · 65%` (`goalSummaryLine()` leaves out whichever half is absent rather than placeholdering it), and its body is the five fields, the chart and the history underneath. Expanding is presentation and fetches nothing (#955's rule) — the histories still come with the page. Creating and editing are **inline in the card**: `+ Add personal goal` (and the empty state's CTA) opens a draft card at the top of the section with today already in Start date, `⋮ → Edit` turns an existing card's body into the same form, and the only overlays left on the screen are the two that are not a goal form — Add reading and the removal confirmation. The `⋮` is a sibling of the toggling header rather than a child of it (§4 — tapping the menu must not expand the card) and carries Add reading · Change initial reading · Edit · Remove, so every write is reachable with the cards closed. The **unit is read-only** in both forms, because it is the Gym Goal's: a target the member clears takes it with it (`toGoalUpdatePayload()`), which is what keeps the row inside `chk_mpgoal_target_unit` without asking them to fix a field they do not own. |
| `/profile` | `GET /me/profile`, `GET /me/membership`, `GET /me/payment-requests` | Member profile, plus (#361) default gym/center switching (reusing `AppContext`'s `gyms`/`switchGym`/`centers`/`setActiveCenterId` — no new backend), enrollment status (derived from `/me/membership`'s `status`) and a payment status summary (derived from `/me/payment-requests`: any `failed` → failed, else any `pending` → pending, else "up to date"). **#1039:** the same preferences card carries a **Language** selector directly below Default Center, in the same control, persisting on change through `PATCH /me/profile`'s `preferred_locale` (migration 220) rather than behind the phone field's Edit/Save pair — one value, and the app switches to it as soon as it is stored. A member with no stored preference sees the locale the app is already rendering in selected; the switching itself is `MemberLocalePreference`'s, not this page's. |

### Member Web feature flags (#367)

Extends the `feature_flags` infrastructure (migration 110, see "Feature Flags" below) with a `member_web.*` namespace, deliberately separate from the Admin App's own flags (`membership.*`, `calendar.*`, etc.) — toggling one never affects the other, and the existing generic tree UI at `/cordel/feature-flags` renders `member_web` as its own top-level section with zero frontend changes (it groups by dot-namespace automatically). Migration 127 seeds five keys, all `enabled=1` (already-released features): `member_web.my_training_plan`, `member_web.my_nutrition`, `member_web.my_bookings`, `member_web.my_membership`, `member_web.profile`. **#1036** adds a sixth, `member_web.my_goals` (migration 223), seeded enabled like the rest — the thread's `Q2` answer, and there is no predecessor flag to take a value from the way migration 211 does, because the section is new. It gates the My Goals tile, the `/goals` page and the five `/me/personal-goals` routes, which also require the gym-side `nutrition.personal_goals`.

- **Backend**: each flag additionally gates the `/me/*` endpoints specific to that section — e.g. `member_web.my_nutrition` on `GET /me/nutrition-plan`, `member_web.my_membership` on `GET /me/membership`+`/me/billing-events`+`/me/promotions`+`/me/receipts/:id`+`/me/class-packages`, `member_web.my_bookings` on `GET/POST/DELETE /me/bookings`+`/me/upcoming`+`/me/activity-history`, `member_web.my_training_plan` on `/me/training-plans`+`/me/exercise-logs`+`/me/workout-block-logs`. `requireFeatureEnabled` is chained after any pre-existing flag on the same route (e.g. `calendar.calendar` stays on `/me/bookings`) — both must pass.
- **Superadmin bypass and impersonation precedence (#439)**: `requireFeatureEnabled` bypasses the check only in a superadmin's **native** capacity — `req.tenantCtx.isSuperadmin && !req.tenantCtx.impersonatedUserId`. `tenantCtx.isSuperadmin` itself stays `true` under impersonation (unchanged — other permission checks still treat an impersonating superadmin as having full access), so the exclusion is explicit at the feature-flag gate. This means a superadmin impersonating a member sees exactly that member's flags, not their own bypass — matching the frontend behavior below.
- **Shared-endpoint exception**: `GET /me/schedule` (also used by the un-flagged `/calendar` page under `calendar.member_calendar`) and `GET /me/payment-requests` (used by both `/membership` and `/profile`) are deliberately left ungated at the API layer — gating them under one Member Web flag would incorrectly block a different, independently-flaggable section that shares the same endpoint. `GET /me/profile` is likewise never gated: it's the core bootstrap call `AppContext.loadMemberData` uses to resolve `isLinked` for the whole app, not just the Profile page. These three cases rely on the frontend route guard alone for "no direct URL access"; every other `member_web.*` flag has both frontend and backend enforcement.
- **Frontend**: `apps/member/src/context/FeatureFlagsContext.tsx` mirrors the admin app's context (`GET /feature-flags`, no gym scoping) and exports `isFeatureEnabled(flags, key)` (ancestor-cascade lookup, same semantics as the backend's `requireFeatureEnabled`, defaults to enabled when a key has no row). It polls every 30s (matching the backend's in-memory cache TTL) so a toggle in Cordel → Feature Flags reaches an already-open session without a reload. Home (`page.tsx`) hides the My Training Plan / My Bookings / My Nutrition nav tiles, the Next Booking section, and the My Membership summary card when disabled (Calendar's tile is never gated — it isn't part of this flag set). Each of `/training`, `/nutrition`, `/schedule`, `/membership`, `/profile` redirects to Home in its existing `appLoading`/`isLinked` bootstrap effect when its flag is disabled. **Impersonation precedence (#439)**: each of these bypass checks uses `isSuperadmin && !isImpersonating` (`isImpersonating` from `useImpersonation()`), not raw `isSuperadmin` — while impersonating a member, the bypass does not apply and the page/tile follows the impersonated member's own flags, consistent with the backend. **(#438)** The Next Booking section explicitly gates on `member_web.my_bookings` in the frontend even though the `GET /me/schedule` call backing it is intentionally ungated at the API layer (see the shared-endpoint exception above) — without this, disabling the flag left the widget (and its `DELETE /me/bookings/:id` cancel button, which *is* backend-gated) visibly stuck rather than hidden.

---

## Planned: CalendarEvent Unification (#360, stage 3 landed)

**Status: stage 3 (API consolidation) landed; stage 4 partially preempted by #437.** This section documents the target shape agreed for #360; see [decisions.md](decisions.md) #10 for the settled open questions. Tracked as a 5-PR staged rollout (design → schema+booking support → API consolidation → frontend consolidation → cleanup). Stages 1–3 have landed: `class-sessions.ts`/`calendar-events.ts`/`bookings.ts`/`me.ts`/`shared-training-requests.ts`/`plan-allowances.ts`/`package-credits.ts` all read/write `calendar_events`/`calendar_event_bookings`/`calendar_event_shared_training_requests` now — see the Domain Modules table above (Calendar events + Class sessions, Bookings, Shared training requests rows) for the current shape of each router. `class_sessions`/`bookings`/`shared_training_requests` are frozen (no application code touches them) but not yet dropped — that's stage 5 (cleanup). Frontend consolidation (the admin Calendar page's `_type`-branching and the separate `[locale]/schedule/` page collapsing into one detail panel) is stage 4 and hasn't fully landed — but #437 (UI cleanup) removed the `[locale]/schedule/` page outright rather than collapsing it into a detail panel: session create/edit/cancel/roster is already covered by the Calendar page's `ClassSessionDetailPanel`, but shared-training request approval (`SharedTrainingPanel`) has no replacement admin UI yet — stage 4 still needs to add that to the Calendar page.

**#503 stage 1 (2026-09) — the `kind` discriminator was removed.** #503 ("Members App: Align Calendar Event Information with Admin Calendar") requires every `calendar_events` row to be equally bookable, with no session/event distinction anywhere in the data model, queries, or business logic. Migration 152 drops the `kind` column (and both its CHECK constraints — a historical duplicate, `chk_ce_kind` and `chk_calendar_events_kind`, from the two migration-134 files below). `bookMemberOnSession` (`bookings.ts`) no longer gates on kind — any calendar_events row can be booked at the primitive/API level. The admin-facing `classSessionsRouter`/`calendarEventsRouter` split (see API surface below) is **not** merged by this stage — that requires a UI redesign of the admin Calendar page's two detail panels (`ClassSessionDetailPanel` vs `EventDetailsPanel`) that hasn't been specified yet, tracked as open work for a later #503 stage. Extending member-facing discovery (`/me/schedule` etc.) to rows without an `activity_type_id` was originally deferred to stage 5 ("Unified member read model") under the plan's numbering at the time. **Stage 5, once implemented (see the roadmap's #503 entry), scoped narrower than that**: it added computed read-model fields (`status`/`occupancy_status`/`waitlist_status`/`waitlist_count`) to the rows `/me/schedule` already returns, but did not touch its `INNER JOIN activity_types` — rows with `activity_type_id IS NULL` (manually created calendar entries) remain invisible to members, and unbookable (`bookMemberOnSession` has the same inner join). That discovery/booking gap is still open and would need its own stage.

**#503 is now complete as of stage 9 (2026-09-18)** — all 9 stages of the plan agreed on the issue thread have landed (`kind` removal, configurable waitlisting, activity→future-event field propagation, activity end-date diffing, the unified member read model, local calendar filters, the member calendar UI, Home/My Bookings, and this stage's tests/docs pass). See [decisions.md](decisions.md) #14 for the closing summary. **#503 closing does not close #360** — they are separate staged plans that both operate on `calendar_events`: #503 aligned member-facing presentation with Admin on top of the table #360 already unified; #360's own stage 4 (frontend consolidation, the admin Calendar page's `SharedTrainingPanel` replacement) and stage 5 (cleanup/drop `class_sessions`/`bookings`/`shared_training_requests`) remain open, tracked above in this same section. The `INNER JOIN activity_types` discovery gap noted above is also still open by design — #503's 9 stages never scoped to close it.

**Why unify**: today `class_sessions`+`bookings` (capacity, waitlist, attendance, package-credit debiting, shared-training approvals) and `calendar_events` (space/trainer conflict-checking, soft delete, audit) are two asymmetric scheduling entities. Every bookable calendar occurrence should resolve to one entity instead of the frontend/backend branching on `_type: 'session' | 'event'`.

**`calendar_events` shape** — absorbed the fields formerly split across `class_sessions`/`bookings`:

```text
calendar_events                                        (migration 131, gaps closed in 134)
├── (existing) id, gym_id, space_id, trainer_membership_id,
│              activity_type_id, title, starts_at, ends_at, status,
│              schedule_rule_id, audit columns, soft-delete columns
├── center_id                                       (migration 134 — nullable; app-required for a session-like row)
├── capacity INT UNSIGNED                          (from class_sessions.max_capacity_override)
├── allows_shared_booking TINYINT DEFAULT 0         (from class_sessions, #324)
├── cancellation_reason TEXT                        (from class_sessions)
└── effective_trainer_membership_id / _confirmed_at (from class_sessions, #193)
    — concurrent-group limits stay on spaces.max_concurrent_groups /
      gym_memberships.max_concurrent_groups (#323), unchanged — calendar_events
      already FKs both.

calendar_event_bookings (replaces `bookings`; migration 132, gaps closed in 134)
├── id, gym_id, calendar_event_id FK→calendar_events
├── member_id FK→members
├── center_id, modified_at, modified_by_membership_id      (migration 134)
├── status ('booked'|'waitlisted'|'cancelled'), waitlist_position,
│   booked_at/waitlisted_at/cancelled_at
├── attendance_status ('pending'|'present'|'absent'), attendance_recorded_at/by
├── user_class_package_id FK (credit debit/refund — see Class packages row)
├── active_booking_key generated column + unique(calendar_event_id, active_booking_key)
│   — one non-cancelled booking per member per occurrence (mirrors bookings' pattern)
└── audit columns

calendar_event_shared_training_requests               (replaces shared_training_requests;
                                                         migration 133, FK→CASCADE fixed in 134)
└── same shape, FK renamed calendar_event_id
```

`class_package_transactions` gained `calendar_event_booking_id` (migration 134, nullable FK → `calendar_event_bookings`) alongside its legacy `booking_id` FK → `bookings` — a CHECK constraint (`chk_cpt_single_booking_ref`) keeps at most one of the two set per row; `booking_id` is left dead rather than repointed, since there's no id correspondence between the old and new booking tables to migrate against.

**Recurrence**: `activity_type_schedule_rules` remains the one recurrence mechanism (materializes `calendar_events` via `domain/scheduleEngine.ts` — setting `activity_type_id`, `center_id` (activity type's `default_center_id`, falling back to the gym's sole center), and `capacity` from `activity_type.max_capacity`, closing a bug where capacity was never set on generated occurrences). `calendar_event_series` (#191) — already dead (no code references it; `recurrenceEngine.ts`/`calendar-event-series.ts` don't exist) — has its table dropped in the stage-5 cleanup, not before.

**Booking/capacity/attendance logic**: `bookMemberOnSession`, waitlist auto-promote, the #372 cancellation-timing refund rule, and the shared-training approval transaction all operate on `calendar_event_id`/`calendar_event_bookings` now, with no behavior change from the `class_sessions`/`bookings` era.

**API surface**: `class-sessions.ts` and `calendar-events.ts` merged into a single file (`calendar-events.ts`, exporting both `classSessionsRouter` and `calendarEventsRouter`) exposing `calendar_events` with the fields above at their existing mount paths. `GET` responses keep the existing field names consumers already read (`class_session_id`, `max_capacity_override`, `booked_count`, `attendance_present/absent/pending`, `availability_state`, etc.) so `/me/schedule`, the admin Calendar page, and `ClassSessionDetailPanel`/`EventDetailsPanel` didn't need to change. `calendar-event-series.ts` was already gone before this stage. **Since #503 stage 1**, with `kind` dropped, `classSessionsRouter` (`/class-sessions`) and `calendarEventsRouter` (`/calendar-events`) partition `calendar_events` by `activity_type_id` instead — `IS NOT NULL` (an occurrence of an activity type) vs `IS NULL` (a manually created calendar entry) — which is a mutually-exclusive, exhaustive split of the same shape `kind` gave them, so the admin Calendar page's dual-fetch needed no change. `SESSION_SELECT`'s `INNER JOIN activity_types` already enforced this filter structurally in most read paths; a few bare mutation guards (`UPDATE ... WHERE id = ? AND activity_type_id IS NOT NULL`) make it explicit where there's no such join.

**Frontend**: the admin Calendar page's `_type`-branching (#326) is unchanged in this stage. The separate `[locale]/schedule/` page was removed by #437 (ahead of a full stage 4) rather than collapsed into a detail panel — session management now lives solely in the Calendar page's `ClassSessionDetailPanel`; shared-training request approval still needs a home there.

**Event color (#541)**: the Calendar page colors both `calendar_events` and `class_sessions` blocks exclusively from the persisted `status` column, via the centralized `getCalendarEventStatusColor()` map in `apps/admin/src/lib/calendarEventColors.ts` — replacing the prior `activity_type.color`/per-event `color`-based fill. Reuse that map (don't hand-roll a new status→color lookup) for any future calendar-adjacent status display.

**Event execution status (#977)** — what happened to the *event*, as distinct from what happened with its members. Four values, derived on every read by `eventExecutionStatus()` (`api/src/domain/eventExecutionStatus.ts`) and reported as `execution_status` on every admin-facing read of `calendar_events` (both routers, list and single-row, plus the rows the mutations return — `withEventExecutionStatus()` wraps them all):

| Value | When |
|---|---|
| `scheduled` | the event has not finished, **or** it finished with bookings and nobody has confirmed it |
| `not_used` | it finished with no `booked` bookings at all |
| `completed` | a human confirmed it took place (`POST /class-sessions/:id/complete`) |
| `cancelled` | a human cancelled it (`POST /class-sessions/:id/cancel`, or the status select on a manual event) |

Three properties are the rule rather than the implementation. **Nothing is stored**: there is no `execution_status` column and no sweep — `not_used` is a function of the clock and the booking count, so a stored copy would need a nightly writer, and a night it missed would report yesterday's empty slots as still `Scheduled`. Only the two values a *person* chooses are persisted, in the `status` column that already carried them. **An explicit decision outranks the clock in both directions**, which is what keeps `Completed · 0 attendees` expressible (a teacher who held the session with nobody there) separate from a slot that simply passed unbooked. And **a `draft` manual event has no execution status at all** (`null`): it was never put on the calendar, so it neither ran nor went unused, and no fifth value is invented for it.

It is reported, never re-derived: the admin Calendar page and `ClassSessionDetailPanel` read the field and the badge's `full` (a scheduled session at capacity, still derived in the UI) is gated on it, so the calendar, the panel and any later report cannot disagree about which slots went unused. `EVENT_SELECT` gained `booked_count`/`waitlist_count` so a manual entry — as bookable as a session since #503 stage 1 — is classified by the same rule; the waitlist count is also what §9's `Waitlist: N` line reads, per occurrence and never per activity type.

It is deliberately **not** `computeCalendarEventStatus()` (`api/src/api/me.ts`, #503 stage 5), which answers the member-facing lifecycle (`scheduled`/`running`/`completed`/`cancelled`). `Not used` is an Admin operational state and members may not be shown that an unused slot existed (#977 §15, and #976 §2/§6/§7 for the member-side half) — which holds structurally, since both routers sit behind `requireModuleAccess('CALENDAR')` and a `member` has `NONE` of it. Two vocabularies for two audiences, each decided in one place.

The status reaches the UI as text, never as a colour: #559 stage 3's `CalendarStatusBadge` carries it (`not_used` taking the palette's existing neutral tone, shared with `draft`) and the event box keeps the theme's Calendar event tokens. §8's "the execution status must never change the event's configured background color" is therefore satisfied by where the status is drawn, not by leaving the box unpainted: #975 has since given the box the event's own `COALESCE(calendar_events.color, activity_types.color)`, and because that paint module cannot see a status (`apps/admin/src/lib/calendarEventPaint.ts`), §8 holds unchanged.

Both explicit transitions record **previous → new status** through `recordAudit` (`entity_type = 'class_session'`), which is also where "who marked it completed and when" lives — there is no `completed_by`/`completed_at` column beside it. `POST /:id/complete`'s pre-existing guards are unchanged: pending attendance or a session with no trainer answers `400` with `pending_count`/`missing_trainer`, which the panel renders as an actionable line rather than a generic toast.

**Reactivating a cancelled event (#979)** — `POST /class-sessions/:id/reactivate` is the only way back from `cancelled`, and it writes `status = 'scheduled'` (plus clearing `cancellation_reason`) and nothing else: `Completed` stays reserved for an event that actually took place, so the lifecycle is `Scheduled → Cancelled → Scheduled` and no fifth state appears. The route is a 400 (`code: 'not_cancelled'`) for an event in any other status, which is also what the loser of two concurrent calls gets, since the `UPDATE` carries `status = 'cancelled'` in its own WHERE.

**Nothing is restored, because nothing was removed.** `POST /:id/cancel` writes the event's status and never touches `calendar_event_bookings`, so the enrolled members, the waitlist and its `waitlist_position` ordering are still exactly as they were; reactivation inserts nothing at all, which is what makes a duplicate booking, a duplicate waitlist entry or a second event structurally impossible (#979 §4/§5/§11). The booking read in the route exists only to *report* what came back — the `event_reactivated` alert and the audit row's `restored_member_ids` — never to rebuild it. A booking the member cancelled themselves stays cancelled.

Two things it does guard. The cancellation freed the trainer/space slot (every conflict check in the router skips `cancelled` rows), so re-occupying it goes through `assertSlotAvailable()` — the same `FOR UPDATE` shared-slot rule `POST /` and `PUT /:id` use, extracted in #979 so there is one copy rather than three — and answers the same `409` codes (`slot_fully_occupied`, `slot_not_shareable`, `activity_not_shareable`, `sharing_not_authorized`) when another session has taken it meanwhile. And the members who held a booking are alerted with `event_reactivated` (migration 216 — a new `member_notifications.type` goes in two places, the `NotificationType` union *and* `chk_member_notifications_type`), deliberately **not** `booking_confirmed`: the member booked nothing. A waitlisted member is not alerted, because they were never told the class was cancelled either and have no booking to have restored. A *past* cancelled event is reactivatable on purpose — "cancelled by mistake" is usually noticed after the class should have run — and #977's rule then classifies it (`not_used` if nobody is on it), which is this bullet's one interaction with the table above.

A manual calendar entry has no reactivate route: its status is edited directly in `EventDetailsPanel`'s own status select, which is also how it is cancelled, so the explicit action pairs with the explicit `POST /:id/cancel` that only sessions have.

**Editing an occurrence from the event (#980 stage 1)** — the Trainer and the Space a session runs with are the occurrence's own columns (`calendar_events.trainer_membership_id` / `space_id`, migration 082), so staff edit them from the event's own detail panel and the Activity Type's `default_trainer_membership_id` / `default_space_id` are untouched (§12 — an occurrence overrides the defaults, it does not rewrite them). No new route: `PUT /class-sessions/:id` already accepted both fields, already runs its one `UPDATE` (so §9's "atomic, or unchanged" is the statement rather than a saga) and already applies `validateSessionRefs()` (a space must be active and belong to this occurrence's center) plus `assertSlotAvailable()`'s four `409` codes when the move re-occupies a slot another session holds.

What the stage added on the API side is §11's audit row. The route wrote none at all, so a trainer change was invisible in the one log a gym reads to find out who moved a class. It now records `action: 'update'` on `class_session` with **previous → new** for the fields that actually moved, from `diffAuditedFields()` (`api/src/domain/calendarEventChanges.ts`) over `SESSION_AUDITED_FIELDS`. Three of that module's answers are the rule rather than the implementation: the diff is taken between the row *before* the write and the row *after* it, not from the request body, because the route is a partial write and a staff screen sends the whole form; an edit that changed nothing writes **no row**, so re-saving an unchanged trainer does not fill the log; and values are reported as stored, because the audit registry enriches `space_id`, `trainer_membership_id`, `activity_type_id` and `professional_service_id` into `{ id, name }` and a stringified id would lose the name. Both write paths (the transactional shared-slot branch and the plain one) end at one `readSessionAndAudit()` helper, so an edit is audited the same way whichever branch wrote it. A field added to the route is added to that list or it changes silently.

On the admin side `ClassSessionDetailPanel` gains the app's read-then-edit split (#797): the info card shows the occurrence's Professional Service, Trainer, covering trainer (#193's `effective_trainer_name`, only when it is somebody else) and Space as values, `Edit details` in the Actions block is the one entry point into the form, and the two fields become `formChrome` selects in the card they were values in with `primaryBtnSmall()`/`secondaryBtnSmall` beside them and no colour of their own (#912/#954). The lookups are the Calendar page's own `/spaces` and `/trainers` reads passed down, not a second fetch; a space or trainer the occurrence already holds but that is no longer selectable (deactivated since, or in another center) is offered as a **disabled** option so the stored value still reads correctly, and the save sends only the fields the admin changed, so an unrelated edit cannot 400 on a value nobody touched. The Professional Service stays a value: #980's `Q1 multi` makes it a multi-valued relation in a later stage, modelled once with #973's activity-level one, and a control the `PUT` cannot carry does not belong in a form (#974). 

**The Waitlist setting on an occurrence (#980 stage 2)** — `calendar_events.waitlist_mode` (migration 154, #503 stage 2) is nullable and three-valued, falling back to `activity_types.waitlist_mode`, and until this stage no API wrote it: it was read and honoured only. It is now editable from the same `PUT /class-sessions/:id`, and `api/src/domain/waitlistMode.ts` is the one place the vocabulary and its meanings live — `parseWaitlistModeInput()` (the three modes, plus `null`/`''` for "follow the Activity Type again"; anything else is a 400, never a coercion), `waitlistModeClosesQueue()` and `effectiveWaitlistMode()`. The browser mirror is `apps/admin/src/lib/waitlistModes.ts`, which both the Activity Types page and the event panel read, and the SQL mirror is migration 154's `chk_ce_waitlist_mode` / `chk_at_waitlist_mode`, so a new mode goes in three places.

`disabled` is the one mode that writes member-facing state: `emptyWaitlistQueue()` cancels every `waitlisted` row under `FOR UPDATE` (the existing `chk_ceb_status` vocabulary — there is no `removed` status and none was added), promotes nobody (a closed queue is not a freed place, which is why this is not `cancelBooking()`), touches no `booked` row and refunds no package credit (a waitlisted row holds none). `closed` deliberately empties nothing — it stops new joins and keeps every `waitlist_position` — which is what the three states are for, and `bookMemberOnSession()`'s `waitlist_not_open` already refuses a staff add under it. Because the emptying must be atomic with the write (§9), the route's plain `UPDATE` branch now runs in a transaction like its shared-slot branch.

Two `member_notifications` types came with it (migration 217, in the union **and** the CHECK): `waitlist_closed` for the members a disable removed — never `event_cancelled`, since the class is still running and the member never held a booking (§4) — and `waitlist_removed` for a member staff take off a list that is still open. They are two types because they are opposite promises: one says there is nothing to rejoin, the other that there is. The thread's `Q2` ("every update in the waitlist reaches the Members App") also closed a gap on the staff side: `POST /bookings` with `waitlist: true` raised no alert at all, so it now sends `waitlist_joined`, and the staff `DELETE /bookings/:id` sends `waitlist_removed` when the row it cancelled was waitlisted (`cancelBooking()` reports `previousStatus`/`memberId` for that). A member cancelling their own place gets nothing — they did it themselves.

§11: `waitlist_mode` is in `SESSION_AUDITED_FIELDS`, so a move reads `open → disabled`, and `null → closed` is a real change rather than an absence (the column's NULL is a value — "inherit"). The member ids a disable removed ride that same audit row as `removed_waitlist_member_ids`, because they are the consequence of the change rather than a second action.

In the panel the setting is a value in the read-only card — the *effective* mode, marked `(inherited from the activity)` while the occurrence's own column is NULL — and a `formChrome` select in Edit mode beside Trainer and Space. §3's confirmation is a step of Save rather than a control: it carries the live waiting count, nothing is written until it is confirmed, and changing the choice invalidates it. The draft is compared against the **effective** mode and not the stored column, which is what stops a save made for an unrelated reason from writing the inherited mode onto the occurrence and silently ending its inheritance. Adding and removing waiting-list members needed no new UI: the panel already had both.

Professional Services on the occurrence stays #980 stage 3's, after #973's stage 1, so the occurrence-level and activity-level relations are modelled once.

### Booking alerts: whose action raised it (#1113)

**A member's own booking, cancellation or waiting-list exit raises no alert at all**, and the 2-hour **training reminder** is the one booking alert the platform raises on its own. §4's distinction is *who acted*, applied where the notification is generated rather than by hiding a row in the Alerts page: `POST /me/bookings` used to write `booking_confirmed` / `waitlist_joined` fire-and-forget and now writes nothing — the response is the member's confirmation and the persistent row told them what they had just done. `DELETE /me/bookings/:id` already behaved this way: it alerts only the member it **promotes** off the waiting list, which is not that member's own action. Nothing changes on the staff side, where every one of those alerts *is* news: `POST /bookings` with `waitlist: true` still sends `waitlist_joined`, the staff `DELETE` still sends `waitlist_removed`, and #979's `event_reactivated` and #980's `waitlist_closed` are untouched.

The reminder is `booking_reminder_2h` (migration 226, in the `NotificationType` union **and** `chk_member_notifications_type`) — a **third** reminder value rather than a reuse of `booking_reminder_24h` / `booking_reminder_1h`, which migration 087 declared and nothing has ever written: their copy says *"tomorrow"* and *"starting soon"*, so writing a two-hour reminder under either would word the alert as something else. They stay declared and unwritten, which the CHECK permits (it may be a superset of the union, never a subset). The same migration adds **two** indexes, because the run has two hot halves and it executes every 15 minutes across every gym: `mn_reminder_dedupe_idx (gym_id, type, entity_type, entity_id, member_id)` for the duplicate prevention (migration 087's only index narrows to the member and then scans their whole alert history; the order keeps this one off that index's prefix, since every predicate is equality and column order buys a distinct access path rather than usability), and `ce_status_starts_idx (status, starts_at)` for the **candidate scan**, which is the half that would otherwise have been missed: the query is deliberately cross-gym, so it carries no `gym_id` predicate, and every `calendar_events` index is `gym_id`-leading (migration 082) — without it each pass full-scans one of the largest tables in the schema, and with it the booking side is already covered by `ceb_gym_event_idx`. The first one's cost is write amplification on an append-only log every request path writes to, which is the stated trade rather than an oversight.

What raises it is `POST /booking-reminders/run` (see its row in the endpoint table), fired every 15 minutes by `.github/workflows/booking-reminder-run.yml`: this is the one scheduled run that is **not** nightly, so it claims no run-log slot — #780's guard is *one completed run per UTC date* and would silence every pass but the first of the day. What makes a repeated pass safe instead is that the alert row **is** the record: a booking whose reminder exists is no longer a candidate, so §5's "at most one per booking" and §6's "the live booking, not the replaced one" both fall out of the query and no column stores that a member was reminded. The payload carries the occurrence's own `calendar_events.title` and its `starts_at` and **no sentence** — `type_booking_reminder_2h` + `detail_booking_reminder_2h` in `apps/member/locales/base/{en,es,ca}.json` are what the member reads, and the Alerts page shows the second because the type is in its `DETAIL_TYPES` (next-intl prints a missing key verbatim, so a `t()` fallback is not one). The lead time is a declared constant in `api/src/domain/bookingReminders.ts` rather than configuration, precisely because that sentence says *"2 hours"* out loud: the copy and the number have to move together. Gate: `api/src/test/booking-reminders.unit.test.ts` (in the API suite, because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only) for the rules, the two removed alerts and the Members App half, with `booking-reminders.test.ts` for the run end to end.

**Migration**: hard cutover, as decided (no dual-write/backfill — see decisions.md #10): stage 3 repointed every router at the new tables directly, with no data migration from `class_sessions`/`bookings`/`shared_training_requests` (there was no production data to preserve). Stage 5 (cleanup, not yet done) drops `class_sessions`, `bookings`, `shared_training_requests`, and `calendar_event_series`.

---

## Mobile app shell (decision #18, 2026-10-04)

**Status: WP1 (the API contract for push), WP2 (the Members App's own native half) and WP3 (the shell) have shipped; it has not been built on a device or published.** Full plan, spike findings and work packages are in `docs/mobile-app.md`, and the manual checks no suite can make are `docs/mobile-runbook.md`; this section is only the architectural summary.

- **A Capacitor shell, not a second front end.** The workspace `apps/mobile` holds the `ios/` and `android/` projects and loads the *deployed* Members App through `server.url`. The UI stays in `apps/member`; a web release reaches the app with no store review, a native change does not.
- **The shell is parameterised by an app profile (WP3, #1074 — built).** `apps/mobile/profiles/<id>.json` is the one place an app identity may be spelled, and `MOBILE_*` environment variables override it per field (`src/appProfile.ts`, pure; `src/loadAppProfile.ts`, the I/O half) — stage 1 has one profile, `cordel-fitness`. `capacitor.config.ts` reads nothing else, and `npm run profile:apply` (`src/nativeProjectFiles.ts`, pure text transforms) is the only writer of a native identity: the Xcode project's `PRODUCT_BUNDLE_IDENTIFIER`, `Info.plist`'s display name and URL schemes, Gradle's `applicationId` and the four `strings.xml` values — which `cap add` seeds once and `cap sync` then leaves alone, so without that step a profile change would ship the previous app's identity. A transform that cannot find its anchor throws, because the failure that matters is the silent one. Google's URL scheme is **derived** from the client id rather than configured (the reversed form), a build with no client id registers none, and the Android `namespace` is read from the project rather than written — it is the package the checked-in `MainActivity` lives under, and `applicationId` is what identifies the app. The native side carries exactly two things beyond Capacitor's template, both behind `#if canImport` so the project compiles without either SDK: `GIDSignIn.handle` for Google's callback (asked from both the app delegate and the **scene** delegate, since `Info.plist` declares a scene manifest and a running app's URL arrives there), and the APNs→FCM token swap, because delivery is FCM HTTP v1 and an APNs token is one FCM will never deliver to. `api/src/test/mobile-shell-profile.unit.test.ts` is the gate: no identity literal outside `profiles/`, the committed native projects matching the stage-1 profile, the shell's app id equal to the API's `DEFAULT_APP_ID`, and the plugin versions equal to the Members App's.
- **Everything native is behind two modules (WP2, #1073 — built).** `apps/member/src/lib/native.ts` is the **decision** half — pure, no React, no plugin import — and answers `isNative()`, the platform, the `POST /me/devices` body, the `appUrlOpen` path, the Google configuration and the ID-token extraction; `apps/member/src/lib/nativePlugins.ts` is the **access** half and is the only file in either app allowed to import `@capacitor/*` or `@capgo/*`, which `api/src/test/members-app-native.unit.test.ts` enforces by scanning the whole app (in the API suite, because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only). Every plugin import is dynamic, because the app is server-rendered and a plugin evaluated at module scope would run during SSR and in every browser — `await import()` still ships the package, which it must (a page loaded from a remote URL gets no `registerPlugin` from the injected bridge), and a plugin the shell lacks resolves to `null` rather than throwing. Detection and the registered platform are **one** answer: `isNative()` is true for exactly the platforms `chk_mdt_platform` accepts, so a future Capacitor target reads as not native instead of registering a value the API would refuse. A component that *renders* differently resolves it after mount (`useIsNative()`), since asking during render is a hydration mismatch; safe areas (`safeArea`/`withSafeArea()` in `lib/memberChrome.ts`) are **not** behind the flag at all, because `env(safe-area-inset-*)` is `0px` wherever there is no inset — the top inset is `TopBar`'s own padding so the status-bar strip carries the header's themed background, and the bottom inset is the layout's. A plain browser never runs native code.
- **Social sign-in is native inside the app.** Clerk's default OAuth redirect would leave the app and drop the session in the system browser, so the app obtains a Google ID token natively (`@capgo/capacitor-social-login`) and hands it to Clerk with `authenticateWithGoogleOneTap`. The token's `aud` must be the **web** OAuth client (the one Clerk holds), so the plugin is initialised with both `iOSClientId` and `iOSServerClientId`. Sign in with Apple (iOS) follows the same shape after its own spike. The button is **absent rather than broken** where it cannot work: no bridge, or no `NEXT_PUBLIC_GOOGLE_IOS_CLIENT_ID`/`NEXT_PUBLIC_GOOGLE_WEB_CLIENT_ID` in the build (both are build args of `apps/member/Dockerfile`, per the no-hard-coded-identity rule), renders nothing and leaves the ordinary email-and-password form, and Clerk's own Google button is hidden through `appearance` only when native — so the web sign-in screen is unchanged. Registration of the push token runs when a linked member is known and is simply re-run on every sign-in (the API's upsert makes it idempotent), while removal has to run **before** `signOut()`, since `DELETE /me/devices/:token` is authenticated as the member whose device it is. A tapped notification opens `/notifications` and no per-type route: the `data` block carries the type and entity for a later ticket.
- **Push (WP1, #1072 — built)**. `member_device_tokens` (migration 221: `gym_id` NOT NULL, `member_id`, `platform` with `chk_mdt_platform` = `ios`|`android`, `app_id` default `com.cordel.fitness`, `token`, `last_seen_at`, `created_at`; `UNIQUE (platform, token)`; both FKs `ON DELETE CASCADE`) plus `POST /me/devices` and `DELETE /me/devices/:token` on the `me` router (`requireRole('member')`, the member resolved from the session and never from the request, every query filtered by `gym_id`). The unique key is **global rather than per gym** on purpose: an FCM token identifies an app *installation*, so the `POST` is an upsert that re-points `gym_id`/`member_id` and refreshes `last_seen_at` — a shared handset is taken over by whoever signed in last, instead of pushing one member's alerts to a phone that is now someone else's. The `DELETE` is scoped by gym **and** member, which is the whole of its authorization: another member's token is a 404. Delivery is `api/src/infra/push.ts` over the pure `api/src/domain/pushDelivery.ts`, called by all three `member_notifications` writers (`sendNotification`, `sendBulkNotification`, `recordNotifications`) *after* the insert and never awaited — the row is the durable fact and the push is a copy of it, so no credentials, an unreachable Firebase or a member with no device each cost the push alone. No notification type is added, so `chk_member_notifications_type` is untouched. FCM **HTTP v1** with no SDK: a service-account JWT signed with `node:crypto` is exchanged for an access token (cached per project) and one POST goes out per token, which is why `firebase-admin` is not a dependency (and the legacy server-key API is decommissioned anyway). Credentials are one environment variable, `FCM_SERVICE_ACCOUNTS` — a JSON object keyed by **app id**, optionally base64-encoded because `deploy.yml` writes the API's environment as inline quadlet `Environment=` lines — so a stage-2 per-gym app is a new key and not a code change; a token whose `app_id` has no entry is skipped rather than failed. A failed send deletes the token **only** where FCM says it can never work again (`UNREGISTERED`, `SENDER_ID_MISMATCH`): `INVALID_ARGUMENT` is deliberately kept, because FCM answers it for a malformed *message* too and the alternative is emptying the table on the first bug in the message builder, and a quota or 5xx is an unknown outcome rather than evidence against the token (#785's `provider_error` judgment). The push carries the payload's own `title` and a `data` block for routing, and no sentence: the per-type copy a member reads is the Members App's locale keys, and composing it server-side would be a second copy of it in a module that has to pick a language. One consequence of the global unique key is recorded in migration 221's header rather than left to be rediscovered: since the `POST` takes the token from the body, a member who *learns* another member's registration token could re-point the row at themselves. Per-gym uniqueness would confine that to one gym without removing it and would reintroduce the double delivery, so the key stays global — a registration token is device-scoped, is never echoed by the route, never logged, and read only by the sender, and what a push carries is a copy of an alert the member can already see in the app. The table pins `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci` (an inherited default differing from `gyms`' fails the FK with errno 3780) with `platform` and `token` on `utf8mb4_bin`, because both are opaque identifiers and a case-insensitive unique key would keep a stored token the sender then pushes and FCM rejects.
- **Universal links / App Links (WP4, #1076 — built).** An invitation link (`/{locale}/link?gym_id=…&__clerk_ticket=…`) opens the app rather than the browser, which both platforms grant only when the domain publishes a file naming the app *and* the app declares the domain. The web half is two route handlers in `apps/member` (`app/api/well-known/apple-app-site-association/route.ts`, `…/well-known/assetlinks/route.ts`), both `force-dynamic`, over the pure `lib/appAssociations.ts` (what the files say) and `lib/wellKnownResponse.ts` (how they are served: `application/json`, a short `Cache-Control`, and `404` when nothing is configured). `next.config.js` maps the canonical `/.well-known/…` paths onto them with a **rewrite** — Apple refuses an association file reached through a redirect, and a rewrite keeps the `200` on the URL iOS asked for; the middleware matcher already excludes anything containing a dot, so neither path meets Clerk or the locale redirect. The Apple Team ID and the Android SHA-256 signing fingerprints are one environment variable, `MOBILE_APP_ASSOCIATIONS` — a JSON object keyed by **app id**, optionally base64-encoded — the same shape and the same reason as `FCM_SERVICE_ACCOUNTS`: a stage-2 app is a new key, not a code change. It is env rather than a profile field because the Members App's Docker build cannot see `apps/mobile/profiles/`, and because a Team ID and a release certificate belong to the store account. A malformed entry is **dropped, logged and never published** (a wrong `appID` or a SHA-1 where a SHA-256 belongs claims an association that silently cannot work, and these files are CDN-cached for a day), and a file with nothing to say is a `404` rather than an empty `details` array. The native half is written from the app profile by `npm run profile:apply`, still the only writer of a native identity: the `associated-domains` entitlement on iOS and `@string/app_link_host` for an `autoVerify` App Links `intent-filter` on Android, both on the host **derived** from the profile's own `serverUrl`, since that deployment is what serves the files. Which paths open the app is one declaration (`APP_LINK_PATH_SEGMENT`) spelled in each platform's syntax — the invitation path and not the whole domain, which would swallow the OAuth and Clerk redirects a sign-in bounces through — and `api/src/test/mobile-app-links.unit.test.ts` is the gate that keeps the manifest and the entitlement equal to it, because `apps/mobile` cannot import the module. WP2's `appUrlOpenPath()` already reads an `https` URL, so there is no second link rule, and the custom URL scheme stays registered for a link a universal link misses (an in-app browser that does not trigger one, a build whose domain is not verified yet). With the variable unset the files 404, verification fails and a tapped invitation opens in the browser and completes there.
- **Designed for per-gym apps (stage 2), built only generic (stage 1).** No app identity is hard-coded (Bundle ID, name, `server.url`, OAuth client IDs, Firebase config are all the app profile's), `app_id` is in the first migration, and the universal-link association files list every app from one variable keyed by app id. A second app is a second profile file plus its own store plumbing, and no change to any `.ts`, `.swift` or `.kt` — `docs/mobile-runbook.md` §6 is how that is rehearsed without a Mac. After sign-in the gym already comes from the member's own memberships (`GymSwitcher` is hidden for a single-gym actor); only the login screen's branding before sign-in would need the app to know which gym it is.

---

## Deployment (dev)

All traffic enters through **Traefik on corfront** (`10.0.2.100`), which terminates TLS (Let's Encrypt) for the `vdicube.com` subdomains. Both VPSs are **Oracle Ampere aarch64** — images are built `linux/arm64`-only on GitHub's native ARM runner (`ubuntu-24.04-arm`; never add amd64 back — QEMU emulation times builds out).

| Piece | Public URL | Runs on | Container / image |
|-------|-----------|---------|-------------------|
| API | `https://api.vdicube.com` → corback `10.0.2.101:3000` | "corback" VPS (`150.230.157.145`) | `fitness-api` / GHCR `fitness-api` |
| Admin | `https://admin.vdicube.com` → corfront `:8081` | "corfront" VPS (`10.0.2.100`) | `fitness-admin` / GHCR `fitness-admin` |
| Member | `https://members.vdicube.com` → corfront `:8082` | "corfront" VPS | `fitness-members` (**plural**) / GHCR `fitness-members` |
| Payment | `https://pay.vdicube.com` → corfront `:8083` | "corfront" VPS | `fitness-pay` / GHCR `fitness-pay`. Traefik route assigned. Oscar owns `fitness-pay.container`; `deploy-payment.yml` pulls + restarts. |

**Ownership split (important):**
- **Oscar owns the runtime**: rootless Podman under VPS user `podman`, one **Quadlet unit** per container at `/home/podman/.config/containers/systemd/<name>.container` defining ports, env vars, and restart policy. Containers are managed with `systemctl --user {start|stop|restart} <name>`.
- **Our workflows own build + release only**: build arm64 image → push to GHCR → SSH as `podman` → (API: `podman image prune -f` of dangling images, then abort if < 1 GB free — see #729) → `podman pull` → (API only: run Knex migrations from the image — the DB is VCN-private, CI can't reach it) → `systemctl --user restart <unit>` → health check.
- Workflows must **never** `podman run` the app containers or `podman generate systemd` — that fights the running unit for the port (exit 126) or overwrites Oscar's env config. To change a runtime env var or port, ask Oscar to edit the unit.

Notes:
- API runtime DB config = `CORDEL_FITNESS_DB_HOST/_USER/_PASSWORD/_NAME` (split vars, set in Oscar's unit). Knex migrations still use a single `DATABASE_URL` (deploy passes `DATABASE_URL_MIGRATIONS`).
- Frontends reach the API via `CORDEL_FITNESS_API_URL=https://api.vdicube.com`. API invite emails use `CORDEL_FITNESS_MEMBERS_URL=https://members.vdicube.com`; team invites use `CORDEL_FITNESS_ADMIN_URL`.
- corfront also runs Oscar's `traefik` (:80/:443) and `wordpress_eforge` (:8080) under the same `podman` user — visible in `podman ps`, don't touch.
- Inbound ports are controlled by the **OCI VCN Security List** (cloud console); OS firewalls are disabled. If a port times out from outside but works on localhost, it's the Security List.
- Traefik config lives on corfront at `/srv/containers/traefik/config/dynamic/backend.yml` (managed by Oscar). `pay.vdicube.com` → `:8083`. PCI: strip query strings from access logs on that host so `page_token` is never stored — the container's own access log already does (`log_format pay_no_query`, #1083).
- The payment container's config is an **`envsubst` template** (`apps/payment/templates/default.conf.template`), substituted by the official nginx image at start, because one image tag serves dev and pro: `CORDEL_FITNESS_API_PUBLIC_URL` is what the browser-facing proxies call and `CORDEL_FITNESS_API_INTERNAL_URL` is what the `/webhooks/payment` relay calls (#1083 — the API's internal address, no trailing slash). The Dockerfile carries the dev values as image defaults, and the reference quadlet (`infra/payment-app/fitness-pay.container`) carries them as `Environment=` lines for Oscar's live unit. `deploy-payment.yml` probes the relay with the same `GET` Monei's dashboard preflights with, so a wrong internal address fails the deploy instead of silently losing payment confirmations.
- Diagnostics: `test-ssh.yml` / `test_ssh_corfront.yml` (workflow_dispatch) print identity/ports/`podman ps`; `debug-vps.yml` does deeper corback checks.
- Legacy names are fully retired: `gymdesk-*` containers/images, VPS user `github`, Vercel, `backend-dev.gymdesk.uk` — do not reference them.

---

## CI/CD Configuration (GitHub Actions)

Config is split by scope. **Environment-dependent** values live in GitHub *Environments* (repo Settings → Environments); workflows read them via `secrets.*` / `vars.*`. `ci.yml`, `deploy-alloy.yml` and `debug-vps.yml` declare `environment: dev`; the billing run, the recurring booking run and the four deploy workflows declare `environment: ${{ inputs.environment || 'dev' }}`, with a `workflow_dispatch` choice input (`dev` | `production`, default `dev`) (#784). When PRO arrives, create a `production` environment with the same names and flip the scheduled workflows' fallback to `'production'` — the owner steps are in `docs/go-to-production.md` §1.

### Environment-scoped (per env: `dev` today, `production` later)

| Name | Kind | Used by |
|------|------|---------|
| `CORDEL_FITNESS_DB_HOST/_USER/_PASSWORD/_NAME` | secrets | (reserved — runtime DB config lives in Oscar's unit; kept in sync here) |
| `DATABASE_URL_MIGRATIONS` | secret | `deploy.yml` — Knex migrations on the VPS (DDL user `fitness_deploy`) |
| `DATABASE_URL_MYSQL` | secret | `debug-vps.yml` — connectivity probe (DML user `fitness`) |
| `CLERK_SECRET_KEY`, `CLERK_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` | secrets | CI + deploys (Clerk test instance in dev) |
| `CLERK_WEBHOOK_SIGNING_SECRET` | secret | `deploy.yml` → API `/webhooks/clerk`. **Must equal the Signing Secret of the Clerk webhook endpoint** — since #1085 that endpoint is the admin app's relay (`https://admin.vdicube.com/api/webhooks/clerk` on dev, `https://admin.cordel.tech/api/webhooks/clerk` on pro), not the API's own URL; the secret itself is unchanged by that move and stays in the API, which is still the only thing that verifies a signature. The endpoint must be **enabled** and subscribed to `user.created` and `user.deleted` (#709). A mismatch makes every event fail with `400 Invalid signature` — the dev endpoint sat disabled that way from 2026-09-08 to 2026-09-23. Changing the secret needs a redeploy (`gh workflow run deploy.yml --ref main`); missed events can be resent from the endpoint's *Replay → Replay missing messages*. |
| `NEXT_PUBLIC_CLERK_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` | secret | Frontend builds (baked as build arg) |
| `APP_ENV_LABEL`, `APP_BRAND_NAME` | variables | #1114 — the browser title's two configurable parts, passed by `deploy-admin.yml` / `deploy-member.yml` as the `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_ENV_LABEL` / `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_BRAND_NAME` build args and composed with each app's own role by `src/lib/appTitle.ts` into `(<label>) <role> - <brand>`. Dev: `Dev` + `Cordel.tech Fitness` (⇒ `(Dev) Admin - Cordel.tech Fitness`); production: **no label** + `Cordel.tech` (⇒ `Admin - Cordel.tech`). Variables, not secrets. Neither has a code default — unset, the title is the role alone (`Admin`, `Members`), never an invented brand — and both are baked in at **build time**, so a change needs a rebuild. |
| `CORDEL_FITNESS_MEMBERS_URL`, `CORDEL_FITNESS_ADMIN_URL` | variables | App URLs for invite emails |
| `API_BASE_URL` | variable | #784 — the origin `billing-run.yml` and `recurring-booking-run.yml` call. Since #1086 that is the **admin app's relay prefix** (`https://admin.vdicube.com/api/internal` on `dev`, `https://admin.cordel.tech/api/internal` on `pro`) rather than the API's own host; the workflows still call `$API_BASE_URL/<path>` unchanged. A variable, not a secret; no hardcoded fallback, so a run in an environment without it goes red at its first step. The variable was repointed on 2026-10-05, so the scheduled runs fail until an admin app carrying `/api/internal` is deployed. |
| `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS`, `PUBLIC_REGISTRATION_IP_LIMIT_PER_HOUR`, `PUBLIC_REGISTRATION_GYM_LIMIT_PER_DAY`, `API_PUBLIC_URL` | optional, not set today | #599 — all have code defaults (`1`, `60`, `200`, and the origin of `PAYMENT_NOTIFICATION_URL`), so `deploy.yml` does not pass them. Add them there only to override. |
| `CLOUDFLARE_R2_ENDPOINT`, `CLOUDFLARE_R2_BUCKET` | variables | R2 storage (#417) — platform-wide bucket config |
| `CLOUDFLARE_R2_PUBLIC_URL` | variable | The bucket's public origin (r2.dev Public Development URL, or a custom domain in production), with no trailing slash and no bucket name. `buildStorageObjectUrl()` builds every media URL on it. `CLOUDFLARE_R2_ENDPOINT` is the S3 API the API uploads through and answers a browser's GET with `400 Authorization`, so without this variable the URLs fall back to that private composition and render broken. Dev: `https://pub-bfe67b11c1114125bba78a43cf826252.r2.dev` (bucket `cordel-patform-dev`, enabled 2026-09-27). |
| `CLOUDFLARE_R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `CLOUDFLARE_R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY` | secrets | R2 storage (#417) — optional; `deploy.yml` writes them empty if unset, so the API just reports storage as unconfigured rather than failing to deploy |
| `MONEI_API_KEY`, `MONEI_WEBHOOK_SECRET` | secrets | MONEI payments (Phase 8) — required; `deploy.yml` fails the deploy if either is empty. **Both are the same value**: MONEI signs webhooks with the account's API Key, not a separate per-webhook secret (see `docs.monei.com/guides/verify-signature`), so `MONEI_WEBHOOK_SECRET` should be set to the same value as `MONEI_API_KEY` (MONEI Dashboard → Settings → API Access → Your API Key) |
| `MONEI_ACCOUNT_ID` | variable | Required — this MONEI account is a **Connect partner (master account)**, not a plain merchant account (confirmed: the API key is prefixed `pk_test_`, and the Webhooks dashboard describes itself as receiving events "for this master account"). The partner API key alone only authenticates partner-level endpoints; creating a payment on behalf of a merchant needs that merchant's sub-account ID sent as the `MONEI-Account-ID` header on every request (`payments/providers/monei/client.ts`), or MONEI returns 401 — this is what every real "Pagar ahora" attempt hit until traced back to this. Find the value under MONEI Dashboard → **Accounts**. |
| `PAYMENT_PAGE_URL`, `PAYMENT_NOTIFICATION_URL`, `PAYMENT_OK_URL`, `PAYMENT_KO_URL` | vars (per environment) | `deploy.yml` → the payment flow's public URLs: hosted checkout page, Monei webhook, success/error redirects (the last two with **no locale segment**, #1081 — one value for every member, localized by the Members App's middleware). Environment variables, never literals, so a `production` deploy cannot point its payment flow at the dev hosts; the deploy's first step fails and nothing is deployed if any is empty |
| `PAYMENT_PROVIDER` | literal in `deploy.yml` | `monei` — the adapter name, not a host |
| `FCM_SERVICE_ACCOUNTS` | secret (optional) | #1072 — FCM HTTP v1 service accounts for push, a JSON object keyed by **app id**, accepted raw or base64-encoded. Unset means the API sends no push (every alert still reaches the Members App). Set it base64-encoded in GitHub: the quadlet unit's `Environment=` lines are inline and unquoted, and the raw JSON carries double quotes, braces and a PEM's escaped newlines. |
| `MOBILE_DEFAULT_APP_ID` | variable (optional) | #1072 — the app id `POST /me/devices` records when a registration names none; defaults to `com.cordel.fitness`. It must match a key of `FCM_SERVICE_ACCOUNTS`, or that app's tokens are skipped at send time. |

### Repo-scoped (cross-env)

| Name | Kind | Notes |
|------|------|-------|
| `GHCR_PAT` | secret | Container registry access, shared |
| `CORBACK_SSH_HOST`, `CORBACK_SSH_PRIVATE_KEY` | secret | SSH as user `podman` on corback |
| `CORFRONT_SSH_HOST`, `CORFRONT_SSH_PRIVATE_KEY` | secret | SSH as user `podman` on corfront |

CI (`ci.yml`) runs migrations against a **throwaway MySQL 8.4 service container** (schema `fitness`, plain `DATABASE_URL`) — the real HeatWave DB is unreachable from CI runners.

### Workflows

| Workflow | Purpose |
|----------|---------|
| `ci.yml` | API tests + admin typecheck/build + `apps/payment` Docker smoke build |
| `codeql.yml` | CodeQL analysis (`security-and-quality`) on push to `main`, on every PR, and weekly — see *Code scanning* below |
| `deploy.yml` | Build/push `fitness-api`, run migrations on the VPS, restart |
| `deploy-admin.yml` | Build/push/restart `fitness-admin` |
| `deploy-member.yml` | Build/push/restart `fitness-members` |
| `deploy-payment.yml` | Build/push/restart `fitness-pay` (corfront `:8083`) |
| `exercises-import.yml` | **(#964)** `workflow_dispatch` only — runs the Free Exercise DB importer from the VPS against the selected environment's database. Same reason `deploy.yml` runs migrations there: the database is VCN-private and a GitHub runner cannot reach it. Defaults to `--dry-run`, so a dispatch nobody thought about reports rather than writes, and it runs the image that is *deployed* (no `podman pull`) so the catalogue is written by the code serving the API |
| `debug-vps.yml`, `test-ssh.yml`, `test_ssh_corfront.yml` | Diagnostics (workflow_dispatch) |

### Operator scripts (`api/src/scripts/`)

One-off and repeatable data jobs — `exercises:import-free-db`, `nutrition:base-images`, `storage:rewrite-urls`, `memberships:multi-active`, `plans:percentage-benefits` — run through `npm run` in `api/`. Two rules, both learned the hard way in #964:
